# Audit findings index

This index is populated only after a report contains an evidence-backed
finding.  Each entry links to the owning per-file report and gives a stable
identifier (`SR-AUD-###`), severity, status, and concise remediation target.
`confirmed` means the finding remains open; `remediated` means its original
evidence is retained but a bounded repair and its closure gates have passed.
`confirmed (design-complete)` is a qualifier, not a separate status: the
finding is still open and still counted as `confirmed`, but a completed
design-only ticket has recorded the selected repair and its blocked or inactive
implementation ticket.

| [SR-AUD-006](modules/core/src/System/DateTime.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-006--high--calendar-constructors-accept-invalid-time-components-and-can-violate-the-tick-invariant) | high | remediated | `modules/core/src/System/DateTime.cpp`, `DateTimeOffset.cpp` | `dateToTicks` validated year/month/day and then multiplied hour/minute/second/millisecond straight into the tick sum, so every invalid time component was silently normalized, `DateTime(9999,12,31,24,0,0)` stored **MaxTicks + 1** and `DateTime(1,1,1,-1,0,0)` a **negative** tick count, and `hour * TicksPerHour` was UBSan-confirmed signed-integer-overflow UB reachable from a plain constructor **and** from `DateTime::TryParse("2024-06-15 2000000000:00:00")`, which returned `true` with negative ticks. Ticket #1877 (CCF-002 class A) validates all four components before any arithmetic, in .NET's order, with the messages and `paramName`s `TimeOnly::validateHms` already carried; after that, the sum cannot exceed `MaxTicks` by construction, exactly as `DateTime.cs:1129` asserts. The same ticket (class B) also splits `validateOffsetAndRange` so `DateTimeOffset`'s three delegating constructors validate the **offset first**, matching `DateTimeOffset.cs`'s argument-evaluation order; `Calendar::ToDateTime` inherits the repair with no `modules/globalization` edit. UBSan clean post-fix, +32 tests, mutation-checked. The port's pre-existing year/month/day exception identity (`paramName="year"`/`"day"`) is a deliberate exclusion (`docs/DateTimeValidationBoundaryPlan.md` §16.1). |
| [SR-AUD-007](modules/core/src/System/DateTime.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-007--medium--tryparse-accepts-malformed-input-and-silently-replaces-invalid-time-text-with-midnight) | medium | remediated | `modules/core/src/System/DateTime.cpp`, `DateTimeOffset.cpp` | Both parsers accept malformed text; DateTimeOffset additionally normalizes impossible offset minutes. **SPLIT by cause (CCF-002, `docs/DateTimeValidationBoundaryPlan.md` §8), following the SR-AUD-035 -> #1857/#1858 convention. 007a REMEDIATED (#1878):** the offset field's two numeric components are range-checked before the `TimeSpan` is built, which closed three defects at once - impossible minutes silently absorbed (`"+02:75"` meant +03:15), sign inversion (`"+-05:00"` meant **minus** five hours, `"--05:00"` **plus** five), and `TryParse` **throwing** `OverflowException` from outside its own try/catch for a large hour field, with `Parse` surfacing it instead of `FormatException`. Every valid offset parses to the identical value; the accepted grammar is unchanged; +6 tests, mutation-checked. **007b REMEDIATED (#1879, 2026-07-31, approved per `docs/RemainingApprovalDecisions.md` §C.8):** `std::sscanf` was replaced by the full-consumption `System::detail::DateTimeTextScanner` in all four parsers, so every row of the plan §8.2/§20.1 table now returns `false` — including `"2024-06-15junk"` and the fabricated midnight of `"2024-06-15 10:xx:00"` — while every documented shape keeps its exact value. Two of §20.1's fifteen rows rest on an **incorrect .NET premise**, corrected in the plan §20.3 and left implemented as approved: .NET's `ParseFraction` accepts `".1234567"` and its `ParseTimeZone` accepts `"+2:5"` as 2h05m — the very 125 minutes the port already produced — so those two rejections are deliberate narrowings of this port's fixed-width millisecond subset, not the removal of a divergence (inactive ticket #1929). SR-AUD-007 is now fully `remediated` (007a #1878, 007b #1879). |
| [SR-AUD-008](modules/core/src/System/TimeSpan.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-008--high--timespan-parse-can-return-success-with-a-wrapped-wrong-duration) | high | remediated | `modules/core/src/System/TimeSpan.cpp` | `TryParse` overflows unbounded day counts and returns a successful wrapped duration; `Subtract` detects overflow only after an undefined signed subtraction. **Post-audit #1929 row 5 (2026-08-01):** surrounding invariant whitespace is now accepted under exact approval; grammar/range taxonomy and this finding's remediated status are unchanged. |
| [SR-AUD-009](modules/core/src/System/TimeOnly.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-009--medium--timeonly-tryparse-reports-success-for-malformed-fixed-format-input) | medium | remediated | `modules/core/src/System/TimeOnly.cpp` | The fixed-format parser accepts trailing/nonnumeric fractional text and a bare decimal point. **Remediated (#1879, 2026-07-31, CCF-002 class D, approved per `docs/RemainingApprovalDecisions.md` §C.8):** `std::sscanf` was replaced by the full-consumption `System::detail::DateTimeTextScanner`, so `"10:20:30junk"`, `"10:20:30."`, `"10:20:30.abc"` and `"10:20:30.1234"` all returned `false` and `Parse` threw `FormatException`. **Post-audit correction (#1929 rows 5–6, 2026-08-01):** the exact approval now accepts surrounding whitespace and one-through-seven fraction digits at 100ns; `.1234` is consequently valid at exact ticks, while junk, bare/non-numeric dots and eight digits remain rejected. SR-AUD-009 remains remediated. |
| [SR-AUD-010](modules/core/src/System/Random.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-010--high--randomshared-is-documented-as-thread-safe-but-has-a-c-data-race) | high | remediated | `Random.cpp`, `Guid.cpp` | `Random::Shared` and `Guid::NewGuid` exposed unsynchronised mutable static PRNG state; independent TSan probes confirmed the races (6 in `Random::internalSample()`, 13 at `Guid.cpp:344`). Tickets #1901 and #1902 (CCF-009) give each site a real ownership boundary, both entirely inside `.cpp` bodies: `Guid`'s engine and distribution become per-thread, and `Random`'s shared instance keeps **one stable address on every thread** while `internalSample()` — the sole writer of `seedArray_`/`inext_`/`inextp_` and the funnel every entry point passes through — routes the draw to the calling thread's own generator. The per-thread-*instance* shortcut was rejected for the reason .NET states at `Random.cs:755–759`. TSan clean at both sites after; a seeded `Random(seed)` stream is byte-identical across 4,928 dumped values; 100,000 concurrent `NewGuid()` yield zero duplicates. No header touched, `sizeof`/`alignof` unchanged and asserted, external symbols identical. +14 tests, ASan/UBSan/LSan clean, mutation-checked against the .NET-rejected shortcut (race-free, so TSan cannot see it) and against identically-seeded per-thread engines. Ticket #1903 closes the finding once both landed, per the cross-cutting record's rule that repairing one singleton leaves the other unsafe. `docs/SharedPrngConcurrencyPlan.md` §11–§16. |
| [SR-AUD-011](modules/core/include/System/Version.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-011--medium--versiontostringfieldcount-serializes-unspecified-components-as--1) | medium | remediated | `modules/core/include/System/Version.hpp` | Requesting Build/Revision that the instance does not define emitted the `-1` sentinel rather than throwing. `ToString(intcs fieldCount)` validated only the numeric interval 0–4 and then appended Build and Revision unconditionally. Measured over every (defined-component-count, fieldCount) pair — eight subjects × seven field counts — the before-state read **48 OK / 8 BAD**: the finding's own `Version(1,2).ToString(3)` → `"1.2.-1"` reproduced exactly, and the probe added three facts the finding did not state — the **parsed** spellings reach the same cells, `Version()` is itself a two-component version whose `.ToString(3)` was bad, and zero is a *defined* component that must keep formatting. Ticket #2258 adds two guards testing `Build` and then `Revision` **independently, in .NET's order** (`Version.ToString(int)` checks `_Build` first, bound 2, then `_Revision`, bound 3) rather than deriving a component count, because this port's public mutable fields can hold an undefined `Build` beside a defined `Revision` — a state no .NET constructor produces. They test `< 0`, not `== -1`, matching the predicate the no-argument `ToString()` already applies. The pre-existing out-of-interval branch keeps its exact text and still runs **first**, so no already-correct rejection changed; .NET's own resource text (`ArgumentOutOfRange_Bounds_Lower_Upper`) and its instance-dependent out-of-interval bound are unverifiable with `/rv` absent and are deferred to #2260. After: **56 OK / 0 BAD**, `OK`-cell diff shows additions only, no-argument control byte-identical. +15 tests; 5 mutations, all caught. No signature, layout (`sizeof`/`alignof` 16/4), vtable, `noexcept` or symbol change; 14 dependent translation units, **no first-party call site uses the overload**. Deliberately separate: SR-AUD-053 (a different header and a single constant) and SR-AUD-063 (a public source break needing approval), both still `confirmed`. `docs/CoreVersionFieldCountPlan.md`. A **separate** divergence found while testing — the no-argument `ToString()` omits an undefined leading component while still emitting a defined trailing one, where .NET short-circuits its field count — is ticket #2259 and carries no `SR-AUD-*` identifier (plan §13). |

| [SR-AUD-015](modules/core/src/System/String.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-015--medium--stringformat-rejects-valid-escaped-braces-and-accepts-malformed-closing-braces) | medium | remediated | `modules/core/src/System/String.cpp`, `FormattableString.hpp` | Bespoke composite-format parsers mishandle escaped/malformed braces; FormattableString also reinterprets inserted brace text and silently retains missing indices. **SPLIT by compatibility (CCF-012, `docs/CompositeFormatBoundaryPlan.md` §9), following the SR-AUD-035 -> #1857/#1858 and SR-AUD-007 -> #1878/#1879 convention. Compatible portion REMEDIATED (#1882 String, #1883 FormattableString):** both engines now derive their output from one left-to-right pass over the format string instead of rewriting a buffer that already held substituted argument text. That closed four defects the finding never named, all measured: `String::Format("{0}", "{0}")` **did not terminate** (and neither did `Format("{0:D999999999}", 7)`, by a second quadratic-padding mechanism); `std::abs(std::stoi(...))` on `INT_MIN` was UBSan-confirmed undefined behaviour at `String.cpp:149`; `std::out_of_range`/`std::invalid_argument` escaped a System-shaped public API; and each occurrence of an index silently reused the *first* occurrence's specifier (`"{0:X}/{0:D3}"` -> `"FF/FF"`). Argument text is no longer reinterpreted as format syntax in either engine. +54 tests, mutation-checked four ways, ASan/UBSan/LSan clean over 3,675 + 145 cases. **Approval-gated portion REMEDIATED (#1884, 2026-07-31, approved per `docs/RemainingApprovalDecisions.md` §C.8 item 2):** this finding's own headline claims — `{{`/`}}` escaping and rejection of a malformed closing brace — plus alignment padding and FormattableString's missing-index `FormatException` all landed, exactly as the plan §20.1's fourteen rows specify. One shared scanner, `System::detail::runCompositeFormat`, transcribed from `ValueStringBuilder.AppendFormat.cs`, now drives **both** engines, which additionally closes a divergence the finding never named: before #1884 `String::Format` and `FormattableString::ToString` disagreed about braces, about a missing index, and about whether an alignment component existed at all. Measured before/after against a worktree checkout of the pre-change tree over 36 cases. Premise correction: row 5's reason is `Format_UnclosedFormatItem`, not `Format_UnexpectedClosingBrace`, because a **trailing** `}` runs MoveNext off the end first — the port matches .NET and pins both spellings. Whitespace inside a format item stays rejected (a widening §20.7 does not authorise), recorded in the plan §21.4. +22 tests; no signature, layout or ABI change. **CCF-012 is complete.** |

| [SR-AUD-017](modules/core/include/System/Char.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-017--medium--charparse-accepts-invalid-overlong-utf-8-as-a-valid-bmp-character) | medium | remediated | `modules/core/include/System/Char.hpp` | The UTF-8 parser accepts an overlong encoding as U+0000 instead of rejecting invalid input. |
| [SR-AUD-018](modules/core/tests/System/ObjectTests.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-018--low--a-hash-test-forbids-a-valid-collision-for-different-values) | low | remediated | `ObjectTests.cpp`, `HashCodeTests.cpp`, `MemoryTests.cpp`, `ReadOnlyMemoryTests.cpp`, `ArraySegmentNewTests.cpp`, `StringComparerTests.cpp`, `TupleNewTests.cpp`, `BinaryDataTests.cpp` | Tests require distinct values to have distinct/nonzero/nonnegative hashes, although valid hash collisions and negative or zero final hashes are allowed. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2283); STILL CONFIRMED - implementation deferred to #2284.** Test-only, yes: no production code is involved at any site. But **not** the small singleton an inherited ranking called it, and not the same class as SR-AUD-112, which was one file, one mechanism and one mechanical replacement. Measured across every test tree rather than inherited from this Source column: **45 hash-inequality assertions in 31 files** plus **7 nonnegative/nonzero assertions**, spanning at least core, buffers, io, numerics, uri, net, net-sockets, runtime, collections, globalization, time-zone and tests/integration - against the **8** files listed here. **Two premise corrections:** the owning file has **two** over-constrained assertions, not one (`ObjectTests.cpp:166` `GetHashCode_IsNonNegativeValue` forbids exactly the negative signed hash the finding says is legal, and is unrecorded), and at least two further sites are unrecorded anywhere in the audit (`DecimalNewTests.cpp:105` and `:109`) while `VectorMatrixTests.cpp:166` appears only as an "extends" note in a report owned by SR-AUD-276. **The repair is not mechanical:** blanket deletion would remove real coverage, because `HashCodeTests.Combine2_OrderMatters` pins .NET `HashCode.Combine`'s designed order sensitivity and `OrdinalComparerTests.GetHashCode_CaseSensitive_DifferentCaseDifferentHash` pins the comparer's case sensitivity, while the nonnegative assertions are the only pin on `BinaryData` masking its hash positive. **REMEDIATED (#2284, 2026-08-11), test-only, no production file touched.** `docs/HashAssertionContractRule.md` is the design record: six rules (R1-R6), the complete inventory, and a per-site A/B/C disposition. Re-measured live before editing: **51** hash-inequality assertions in 28 files plus **14** nonzero/nonnegative in 12 files (35 files in union) - a superset of the review's 45/31 + 7, whose file and assertion counts cannot both be reproduced from one definition; no inventory file had changed since `274d284`, so the gap is a counting boundary, not drift. **Two production facts drive the dispositions, both read and neither altered.** (1) `System::HashCode` mixes a `std::random_device` seed into its initial state once per process, by design, mirroring .NET (`HashCode.hpp:34-39`), so nine assertions here were not merely permitted-to-fail but **unreproducible across runs**; they become statements over families of inputs, and `CombineN_ResultDependsOnEveryArgument` now covers every argument of every arity 1-8 rather than only the last of each. (2) Six types end their hash with `& 0x7fffffff` - `Object`, `Memory`, `ReadOnlyMemory`, `ArraySegment`, `Decimal`, `BinaryData` - which answers #2284's open question about `BinaryData`: the masking is intended and is the same idiom six times over, so those assertions are **kept**, renamed `GetHashCode_MasksTheSignBit` and pointed at the line they pin. **Both properties #2283 warned must survive, do.** `HashCode::Combine`'s order sensitivity survives as non-commutativity over 28 pairs ("a commutative combiner would give 0"); `OrdinalComparer`'s case sensitivity survives on `Equals`/`Compare`, where it already lived, and its hash side now checks agreement with `Equals` over every pair of a vocabulary in **both** modes. A third property the review did not name was at greater risk and also survives: `IPAddress`'s IPv6 hash regression had no observable but the hash, so it is reformulated as "eight addresses in one /64 do not all share a hash" - 1 distinct hash before the repair, 8 after. **Retained deliberately:** all five `TotalOrderIeee754Comparer` inequalities (its header states the distinction as behaviour and the float/Half hash is the bit pattern widened, injective by construction) and two `std::hash<double>` preconditions in the collections contract suite, relabelled as the mutation-M8 guards they are. **Excluded with reason:** the 13 `io-hashing` assertions of the same shape are known-answer checks on standardised checksums, not equals/hash-contract sites, and `io-hashing` is absent from #2283's own module list. **Newly found occurrence, no new identifier:** `ObjectTests.cpp:171` `GetHashCode_WithinIntRange` asserted `int <= INT_MAX` and could not fail; folded into the masking pin. **After: 0 class-A sites and 0 nonzero claims remain** anywhere in the repository. -5 tests (five deletions, each because a strictly stronger statement already stood beside it; three additions offset three further deletions), 44 hash inequalities and 8 nonzero assertions removed or replaced, 28 tests rewritten in place. Seven valid mutations, seven caught - including the pre-repair IPv6 hash and the removal of `Decimal`'s mask; one labelled equivalent, two discarded as non-compiling. Sanitizers deliberately not run: no site involves memory, lifetime, threading or overflow. |

| [SR-AUD-020](modules/core/include/System/UInt128.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-020--high--uint128-shift-operators-pass-out-of-range-counts-to-undefined-native-shifts) | high | remediated | `modules/core/include/System/UInt128.hpp` | `UInt128` forwarded counts of 128+ (and negative counts) to native shifts rather than masking to .NET's modulo-128 semantics, producing sanitizer-confirmed UB. Ticket #1843 masks the count with `& 127`, matching .NET `UInt128.operator <<` (`shiftAmount &= 0x7F`) and the sibling `Int128`; UBSan is clean post-fix and in-range shifts are byte-identical (`docs/NumericWrapperBoundaryPlan.md`). |
| [SR-AUD-021](modules/core/include/System/Int128.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-021--medium--invalid-numeric-formats-are-silently-accepted-or-leak-std-exceptions) | medium | remediated | `Byte.hpp`, `SByte.hpp`, `Int16.hpp`, `UInt16.hpp`, `Int32.hpp`, `UInt32.hpp`, `Int64.hpp`, `UInt64.hpp`, `Int128.hpp`, `UInt128.hpp` *(integer slice)*; `Single.hpp`, `Double.hpp` *(float slice → CCF-006 / #1849)* | **Integer slice remediated (#1847):** unknown format specifier → `FormatException` for all ten integer wrappers (was silent decimal); an explicit `G`/`g` branch and a guarded width `std::stoi` were added to the two 128-bit types. **Float slice remediated (#1849, CCF-006):** `Single::ToString`/`Double::ToString` now wrap the precision `std::stoi` in try/catch → `FormatException("Format specifier was invalid.")` (was leaking `std::invalid_argument`/`std::out_of_range`) and reject an unrecognised specifier loudly instead of a silent round-trip fallback, matching the integer wrappers; `F/E/G/R/N` stay valid. +12 tests. The `N`=`F` group-separator and `E` exponent-width fidelity gaps are the separate CCF-007 review. Both slices now closed. |
| [SR-AUD-022](modules/core/include/System/Int64.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-022--medium--numeric-clamp-does-not-reject-inverted-intervals-and-reaches-invalid-stdclamp-use) | medium | remediated | `Byte.hpp`, `SByte.hpp`, `Int16.hpp`, `UInt16.hpp`, `Int32.hpp`, `UInt32.hpp`, `Int64.hpp`, `UInt64.hpp`, `UInt128.hpp`, `Decimal.hpp`, `MathF.hpp` | Inverted Clamp bounds were not rejected; the eight fixed-width wrappers passed them to `std::clamp` (a `[alg.clamp]` precondition violation reproduced as a `_GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS` abort), while UInt128, Decimal, and MathF returned a bound. Ticket #1846 adds a `min > max` → `System::ArgumentException("min cannot be greater than max.")` guard to all eleven overloads (matching the pre-existing Int128/Single/Double/Math contract), replaces `std::clamp` with the manual guarded form, and removes `noexcept` where a throw was added; per-type inverted-interval and equal-bound tests added (`docs/NumericWrapperBoundaryPlan.md` §15). |
| [SR-AUD-023](modules/core/include/System/UInt64.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-023--medium--unsigned-integral-binary-formatting-is-missing-and-silently-returns-decimal) | medium | remediated | `SByte.hpp`, `Int16.hpp`, `UInt16.hpp`, `UInt32.hpp`, `UInt64.hpp`, `UInt128.hpp`, `Int128.hpp` | Integral `ToString("B"/"b")` silently fell back to decimal for six types. Ticket #1845 adds the binary branch (raw two's-complement bits at natural width, width-padded for `Bn`, replicating the already-correct Byte/Int32/Int64 pattern). **Premise correction:** `Int128` was *also* missing the branch (the finding listed only `UInt128`); #1845 fixes both 128-bit types, so all integral wrappers now support `B`/`b` (`docs/NumericWrapperBoundaryPlan.md` §15.3). |
| [SR-AUD-024](modules/core/include/System/SByte.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-024--medium--signed-816-bit-ispositive0-contradicts-the-net-generic-math-contract) | medium | remediated | `SByte.hpp`, `Int16.hpp` | `IsPositive(0)` was false in both signed small-integer wrappers, contrary to the .NET generic-math `value >= 0` definition (`Int32`/`Int64` were already correct). Ticket #1844 changes `SByte::IsPositive`/`Int16::IsPositive` to `value >= 0`, corrects the two tests that pinned the wrong `false`, and adds negative/min/max vectors. Stays `noexcept`; no signature change (`docs/NumericWrapperBoundaryPlan.md` §15.2). |
| [SR-AUD-025](modules/core/include/System/IntPtr.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-025--high--intptradd-and-subtract-execute-signed-overflow-ub-at-native-boundaries) | high | remediated | `IntPtr.hpp` | `Add(MaxValue, 1)` and `Subtract(MinValue, 1)` reach sanitizer-confirmed signed-overflow UB instead of explicitly implementing .NET's unchecked native-width wrap. |
| [SR-AUD-026](modules/core/include/System/Convert.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-026--high--checked-convert-integral-overloads-silently-wrap-instead-of-rejecting-loss-of-rangesign) | high | remediated | `Convert.hpp` | `ToByte(long)`, signed-to-UInt32/UInt64, and `ToChar(int)` used unchecked casts and silently wrapped invalid values rather than throwing `OverflowException`. Ticket #1853 (CCF-005) adds the range guard to all six overloads (`ToChar(int)` uses `[0,255]` for this 1-byte-char port, matching the `ToChar(long)` sibling); every in-range value is unchanged; +26 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-027](modules/core/src/System/Convert.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-027--high--non-finite-floating-convert-inputs-bypass-checks-and-return-spurious-integers) | high | remediated | `Convert.cpp`, `Convert.hpp` | NaN passed comparison-based checks in four floating-to-integer converters and reached a `static_cast` to the integer type. Two files, not one: `ToInt32/ToInt64(double)` in `Convert.cpp`, `ToUInt32/ToUInt64(double)` inline in `Convert.hpp`. +15 tests. |

| [SR-AUD-029](modules/core/include/System/Single.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-029--medium--roundfloat-digits-accepts-invalid-precision-and-can-return-a-spurious-value-or-nan) | medium | remediated | `Single.hpp`, `Double.hpp` | Binary-float `Round(value, digits)` omits its type-specific 0–6/0–15 validation, returns a value or NaN for invalid precision, and cannot throw because it is `noexcept`. | **Remediated — ticket #1862 (2026-07-31):** approved per `docs/RemainingApprovalDecisions.md` §A.10; both overloads dropped `noexcept` and throw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("digits", …)` with .NET's verbatim per-type message before the `pow`. | [SR-AUD-030](modules/core/include/System/Single.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-030--medium--ispow2-rejects-every-valid-subnormal-power-of-two) | medium | remediated | `Single.hpp`, `Double.hpp` | Binary-float `IsPow2` applies the normal significand rule to subnormals, rejecting `Epsilon` and every other subnormal power of two. **Remediated (#1860, CCF-007):** `Single::IsPow2`/`Double::IsPow2` add a subnormal branch (biased exponent 0 ⇒ `popcount(trailingSignificand)==1`), matching .NET; `Epsilon`/`2·Epsilon` now return true, a two-bit subnormal false, normal powers of two unchanged, zero/negative/NaN/Inf still false. +2 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-031](modules/core/include/System/Single.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-031--medium--ilogbnan-leaks-the-c-library-sentinel-instead-of-the-net-result) | medium | remediated | `Single.hpp`, `Double.hpp`, `Math.hpp` | Direct `std::ilogb` forwarding maps NaN to `Int32.MinValue`, whereas .NET reserves that value for zero and returns `Int32.MaxValue` for non-finite inputs. **Remediated (#1859, CCF-007):** `Single::ILogB`, `Double::ILogB`, and `Math::ILogB` now classify zero→`INT_MIN` and NaN/±Inf→`INT_MAX` before the finite `std::ilogb`, matching the already-fixed `MathF::ILogB` and .NET `Math.ILogB`; `noexcept` unchanged. +3 tests (subnormal exponent, both infinities, NaN, zero, normal on all three types). |
| [SR-AUD-032](modules/core/include/System/Single.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-032--medium--pi-scaled-trigonometric-apis-lose-exact-turn-boundary-results) | medium | remediated | `Single.hpp`, `Double.hpp` | Binary-float Pi-scaled trigonometry multiplies a rounded `Pi` then calls C++ trigonometry, losing exact turn-boundary zeros and their signs. **Remediated (#1861, CCF-007):** `Single`/`Double` `SinPi`/`CosPi`/`TanPi`/`SinCosPi` were rewritten to the .NET integral/fractional-turn reduction (amd/aocl-libm-ose kernels), so integer turns return a sign-carried zero, half turns return exact `±1`/`0`/`±Infinity`, non-finite inputs return `NaN`, and ordinary values stay within libm ULPs; `noexcept`/signatures/layout unchanged. +20 add-only tests. Premise corrected: `TanPi(+1)==-0` (not `+0`), `TanPi(-1)==+0`, per the reference. |
| [SR-AUD-033](modules/core/include/System/Single.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-033--medium--public-single-parsing-and-standard-formatting-implement-a-c-subset-rather-than-net-defaults) | medium | remediated | `Single.hpp`, `Double.hpp` | Binary-float parse/format use a narrow C++ literal and stream subset: valid default whitespace/grouping/overflow inputs fail and `N`/`E` output is incompatible. **Parse half remediated** (#1864 whitespace, 2026-07-30; #1865 grouping and overflow, 2026-07-31, approved per `docs/RemainingApprovalDecisions.md` §B.8): `tryParseCore` now implements `NumberStyles.Float | AllowThousands` through the shared `System/detail/FloatParseGrammar.hpp` — invariant-culture `,` group separators by .NET's exact scanner rule, and an out-of-range magnitude saturating to ±Infinity (overflow) or ±0 (underflow) instead of throwing. **Format half remediated** (#1863, 2026-07-31, approved per `docs/RemainingApprovalDecisions.md` §D.5): `E`/`e` emit a sign and at least three exponent digits, `N`/`n` insert invariant-culture group separators, and `G` becomes shortest-round-trip while `G<n>` uses `std::to_chars`' general form — all through the shared `System/detail/FloatTextFormat.hpp`, with the `to_chars` default/`R` fast path pinned unmoved. Two premise corrections: `G17`/`G9` **already** round-tripped (the guarantee was missing, not the behaviour, and their text is unchanged), and `G` with no precision was **not** the `to_chars` fast path §D.3 claimed it was — it was six significant digits. `Half` inherits by delegation. +11 tests; no signature or layout change. **CCF-007 is complete.** |
| [SR-AUD-034](modules/core/include/System/Single.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-034--medium--ispositive-incorrectly-excludes-positive-sign-nan) | medium | remediated | `Single.hpp` | `Single::IsPositive` rejects positive-sign NaN despite the raw-sign-bit generic-math contract; the local Double counterpart already handles it correctly. **Remediated (#2230, 2026-08-10, family #2229):** the `&& !std::isnan(value)` term is removed, so `IsPositive` is the bare `!std::signbit(value)` .NET defines (`SingleToInt32Bits(value) >= 0`) and the `Single`/`Double` pair now agrees on both NaN signs. Verified before the edit rather than assumed: no production consumer and no test pinned the wrong answer, so the repair corrects no existing assertion. `±0`, finite, subnormal and infinite results unchanged; signature, `noexcept` and layout unchanged. +4 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-035](modules/core/src/System/Decimal.cpp.audit.md) | medium | remediated | `Decimal.cpp` | Decimal's narrow parser rejects valid default whitespace/grouping, reports range overflow as `FormatException`, and discards rather than rounds excess fractional precision. **Partially remediated (#1857, CCF-005 Decimal slice):** `TryParse` now skips leading/trailing whitespace (pure widening) and rounds excess fractional precision beyond scale 28 half-to-even instead of discarding it (`0.0…06` → `1e-28`); +4 tests. **Fully remediated (#1858, 2026-07-31, approved per `docs/RemainingApprovalDecisions.md` §B.8 items 2 and 3, delivered as the two separate commits §B.5 required):** a private three-state `ParseStatus` lets `Parse` throw `OverflowException` (`SR.Overflow_Decimal`, verbatim) for a well-formed oversized magnitude while malformed text stays `FormatException`, and `,` became the invariant-culture group separator per `NumberStyles.Number`. Measured correction: **two** values change, not one — `Parse("1,5")` 1.5→15 **and** `Parse(",5")` 0.5→`FormatException`; both tabulated in `docs/Migration-DecimalCommaGroupSeparator.md`. No layout, vtable or existing mangled name changed. +15 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-036](modules/core/src/System/Decimal.cpp.audit.md) | medium | remediated | `Decimal.cpp`, `Math.hpp`, `MathF.hpp` | Decimal, Math, and MathF silently reinterpret an invalid public `MidpointRounding` value rather than throwing `ArgumentException`. **Remediated (#1855, CCF-005 Decimal slice):** all three `Round(…, MidpointRounding)` funnels now reject a value outside `[ToEven=0 .. ToPositiveInfinity=4]` with `ArgumentException(paramName "mode")`, message `The value '{n}' is not valid for this usage of the type MidpointRounding.` — Decimal via `(uint)mode > (uint)ToPositiveInfinity` before its early-out, Math/MathF via the `switch default`. Every named mode 0-4 still returns its exact value; +9 tests. **Closes CCF-008** (all three types validated). |
| [SR-AUD-037](modules/core/include/System/Decimal.hpp.audit.md) | medium | remediated | `Decimal.hpp` | `Decimal::ToOACurrency` truncates its scaled value instead of performing the documented nearest four-decimal currency rounding. **Remediated (#2231, 2026-08-10, family #2229):** the value is rounded to four decimals through the existing `Decimal::Round(d, 4, MidpointRounding::ToEven)` funnel before scaling, so the two values the .NET documentation tabulates now return `1235` and `-792282` (they returned `1234` and `-792281`) and no magnitude below `0.00005` collapses to zero. **The tie rule is the one element the documented examples cannot decide** — neither is a midpoint; `ToEven` was chosen because .NET reduces the scale through `DecCalc.VarCyFromDec` → `InternalRound(…, ToEven)` and because every other rounding funnel in this port defaults to it, with `/rv` absent to confirm. It is **pinned by tests** and the residual is deferred-verification ticket **#2234**. Overflow was measured, not assumed: both doors already threw `OverflowException`, so only the message moves to .NET's Currency text. Signature and exception specification unchanged. +18 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-038](modules/core/include/System/Decimal.hpp.audit.md) | medium | remediated | `Decimal.hpp`, `Decimal.cpp` | Raw construction, parsing, and `CopySign` erase Decimal negative zero even though it is publicly observable through `GetBits`. **Remediated (#1856, CCF-005 Decimal slice):** the raw ctor now sets the sign bit unconditionally (`negative_ = isNegative`), `CopySign` copies the sign unconditionally (`sign.negative_`), and `TryParse` preserves the parsed sign for a zero magnitude (`Parse("-0")` → −0), matching .NET's `Decimal(int,int,int,bool,byte)`/`CopySign`/`NumberBufferKind.Decimal` parser. `−0m == 0m` stays true and both still hash to the same value (equality/hash were already sign-agnostic for zero). +6 tests. `normalize()`/unary-`−` negative-zero *production* remains out of scope (deferred broader decision). |
| [SR-AUD-039](modules/core/src/System/Math.cpp.audit.md) | medium | remediated | `Math.cpp` | `Math::Log(double, base)` omits .NET's base-one/zero/positive-infinity special cases and returns infinity or signed zero instead of NaN. **Remediated (#2232, 2026-08-10, family #2229):** the overload applies .NET's four guards in .NET's order, exactly as the already-correct `MathF::Log(float, float)` sibling did, so `Log(5,1)`, `Log(5,0)` and `Log(5,+Inf)` return `NaN` instead of `+Inf`, `-0` and `+0`. **Premise note:** the `a != 1` term means an argument of one falls *through* to the quotient, so `Log(1,0)` is `-0` and `Log(1,+Inf)` is `+0` — signed zeros the port already returned, now pinned as the over-rejection control. That correction came from the probe's `MathF` control disagreeing with the probe's own first draft. `Log(d)`, `Log2` and `Log10` untouched; signature and exception specification unchanged. +14 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-040](modules/core/include/System/MathF.hpp.audit.md) | medium | remediated | `MathF.hpp` | MathF ties-to-even rounding depends on C++'s mutable process-wide floating-point mode and returns upward-rounded values under `FE_UPWARD`. **Remediated (#2233, 2026-08-10, family #2229):** `MathF` gains `roundToEvenImpl(float)`, the single-precision mirror of the `Math::roundToEvenImpl(double)` funnel this finding points at, and every affected door routes through it. **PREMISE EXTENSION:** the defect reaches **four** production doors, not two — the finding calls *"the sibling double Math API"* mode-independent, which is true of `Math` and **false of `Double`**, whose `Round(double)` was `std::nearbyint`, as was `Single::Round(float)`; the repository-wide `nearbyint`/`rint` grep outside tests returns exactly those four sites. It is also **not `FE_UPWARD`-only**: `FE_DOWNWARD`/`FE_TOWARDZERO` turned `Round(3.5f)` into `3` and `Round(-2.5f)` into `-3`. A measured **residual** is recorded rather than hidden — under those two modes the *digits* overloads differ in the last ULP, but `Math::Round(2.25, 1)`, the untouched reference sibling, deviates identically and matches a bare `22.0 / 10.0` to the bit, so it is ambient-mode arithmetic and not a rounding-rule defect. `MidpointRounding` validation (CCF-008) and the round-limit guards are untouched; all six doors keep their signatures and `noexcept`; the only surface change is the added inline static mirroring the public `Math` one. +14 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-041](modules/core/include/System/BitConverter.hpp.audit.md) | high | remediated | `BitConverter.hpp` | All typed vector decoders omitted index/remaining-width validation; negative and short `ToInt32` inputs reached ASan-confirmed out-of-bounds reads. Ticket #1851 (CCF-005) routes all 14 decoders through a shared `validateDecodeRange` throwing `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("startIndex")` on a negative/over-large index and `ArgumentException(value)` on insufficient width, before any read; `ToBoolean` throws only `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` (width-1 quirk). ASan clean post-fix; +46 tests (`docs/ConversionBoundaryFamilyPlan.md`). |
| [SR-AUD-042](modules/numerics/include/System/Numerics/TotalOrderIeee754Comparer.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-042--medium--total-order-comparer-omits-the-equality-comparer-contract) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `TotalOrderIeee754Comparer.hpp` | The comparer implements ordering only, omitting .NET's equality-comparer contract required to preserve total-order equality in local hash-based APIs. Split by review #2167: **ticket #2169 (done)** adds `Equals`/`GetHashCode` to all three specializations with exactly `IEqualityComparer<T>`'s signatures — bit-pattern equality agreeing with `Compare == 0` on every vector, `-0`≠`+0`, distinct NaN payloads distinguished, the binary64 hash folded rather than truncated — and is **layout-neutral (8/8, static_asserted)**. The remaining *polymorphic binding* half is **ticket #2170, needs_user**: adding the base is a measured public object-layout change **8 → 16 bytes** with a second vptr (`build-probe/2167_probe2_layout.log`), the class this repository has gated since #1788/#1789 (`docs/SystemNumericsNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §6.1, §12). |
| [SR-AUD-043](modules/core/include/System/HashCode.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-043--high--addbytes-converts-a-negative-public-span-length-into-an-unbounded-raw-read) | high | remediated | `Span.hpp`, `Memory.hpp`, `ReadOnlyMemory.hpp`, `ArraySegment.hpp`, `HashCode.hpp` | Span/memory-view constructors and ArraySegment's vector constructor accept negative length metadata after narrowing; HashCode casts it to `size_t`, and ASan confirms an out-of-bounds raw read. **043a — ticket #1852 (CCF-005):** `Span`/`ReadOnlySpan` pointer ctors reject a negative length and the four vector ctors reject a `size()>INT32_MAX` source (shared `detail::checkedSpanLength`), keeping `intcs length_` (no layout change). This closes the *reachable* exploit — a negative-length span can no longer be constructed, so it can never reach `HashCode::AddBytes`; ASan clean post-fix. **043b — ticket #1854 (2026-07-31, approved per `docs/RemainingApprovalDecisions.md` §A.10):** the three `ReadOnlyMemory` ctors and `HashCode::AddBytes(ReadOnlySpan)` dropped `noexcept` (and the pointer ctor its `constexpr`) and now throw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. Measured correction: the `ReadOnlyMemory(const T*, intcs)` half was directly **reachable** and stored `-1`/`INTCS_MIN` verbatim, not defence in depth; the `ArraySegment` and `AddBytes` halves are unreachable and their tests pin the exception specification, not a throw. No signature/layout/symbol change. |
| [SR-AUD-044](modules/core/include/System/Span.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-044--high--copyto-and-trycopyto-corrupt-overlapping-nontrivial-spans) | high | remediated | `Span.hpp`, `Memory.hpp`, `ReadOnlyMemory.hpp`, `MemoryExtensions.hpp`, `Array.hpp`, `ArraySegment.hpp` | Forward copies lose source data when overlapping Span/ReadOnlySpan, Memory, ReadOnlyMemory, static MemoryExtensions, Array, and ArraySegment nontrivial values, violating .NET's overlap-safe contract. |
| [SR-AUD-045](modules/core/include/System/SpanSplitEnumerator.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-045--high--an-empty-sequence-separator-produces-an-infinite-stream-of-empty-spans) | high | remediated | `SpanSplitEnumerator.hpp` | Empty exact-sequence splitting never advances, making range iteration infinite instead of yielding the source once. |
| [SR-AUD-046](modules/core/include/System/MemoryExtensions.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-046--medium--order-sensitive-memoryextensions-methods-use-c-operators-instead-of-the-net-comparison-contract) | medium | remediated | `MemoryExtensions.hpp`, `Array.hpp`, `Nullable.hpp`, `ValueTuple.hpp`, `Tuple.hpp`, `Linq.hpp` | Default Array/MemoryExtensions sort and binary search plus Nullable, ValueTuple, Tuple, and LINQ comparison/equality use raw C++ operators, mishandling float NaN rather than .NET default comparer/equality-comparer behavior. **Remediated (CCF-010, tickets #1904-#1910, 2026-07-31):** one shared policy, `System/detail/ComparisonPolicy.hpp`, now states `Comparer<T>.Default`/`EqualityComparer<T>.Default` once, and all 66 sites across the six headers route through it. `Array::Sort` and `MemoryExtensions::Sort` adopt .NET `ArraySortHelper`'s NaN pre-pass, which also closes a **strict-weak-ordering precondition violation the finding does not name**: raw `<` over a NaN-bearing range left the *finite* elements unsorted in **64 of 196** measured shapes, worst case 3,874 inversions, invisible to ASan, UBSan and `_GLIBCXX_DEBUG` alike. All 28 measured defect rows now return .NET's value and the 8 already-correct rows are unchanged and pinned, including `Nullable<T>::operator==`, which is C#'s lifted `==` and must stay raw IEEE. +70 tests; ten mutations, one of them a negative control (`docs/ComparisonContractPlan.md`). |
| [SR-AUD-047](modules/core/include/System/MemoryExtensions.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-047--high--memoryextensionscopyto-writes-past-a-shorter-destination) | high | remediated | `MemoryExtensions.hpp` | Static `CopyTo` omitted a destination-length check; a two-element-to-one-element ASan probe reported a heap-buffer-overflow write. Ticket #1850 (CCF-005) adds a `source.length > destination.length` guard that throws `ArgumentException("Destination is too short.")` before the `std::copy`, matching .NET and the member `Span::CopyTo`; ASan clean post-fix (`docs/ConversionBoundaryFamilyPlan.md`). The `std::copy` overlap hazard (SR-AUD-044) stays open. |
| [SR-AUD-048](modules/core/include/System/MemoryExtensions.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-048--medium--character-span-whitespace-trim-recognizes-only-c-locale-byte-whitespace) | medium | remediated | `MemoryExtensions.hpp`, `ArgumentException.cpp` | UTF-8 trim and `ThrowIfNullOrWhiteSpace` use byte `std::isspace`, retaining .NET whitespace such as U+00A0. |
| [SR-AUD-049](modules/core/include/System/ReadOnlyMemory.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-049--high--readonlymemoryslicestart-performs-signed-overflow-before-range-validation) | high | remediated | `ReadOnlyMemory.hpp` | `Slice(INT_MIN)` subtracts before validation and reaches UBSan-confirmed signed-overflow UB instead of directly throwing. |
| [SR-AUD-050](modules/core/src/System/Guid.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-050--high--newguid-uses-a-predictable-standard-prng-instead-of-nets-os-csprng) | high | remediated | `Guid.cpp` | `NewGuid` and `CreateVersion7` derive output from a seeded Mersenne Twister instead of the current .NET OS-CSPRNG/strong-entropy contract. **REMEDIATED (#2228, 2026-08-17), and this was the last `high` finding in `modules/core`.** `NewGuid` now fills its sixteen bytes from the platform CSPRNG -- `BCryptGenRandom` with `BCRYPT_USE_SYSTEM_PREFERRED_RNG` on Windows, `getentropy()` elsewhere, chunked at 256 bytes because that limit is a documented `EIO` rather than a silent truncation -- mirroring `RandomNumberGenerator.cpp` rather than inventing a fourth shape. **THE BLOCKING QUESTION DISSOLVED ON MEASUREMENT.** #2228 was blocked on a user decision because a CSPRNG appeared to make a non-throwing public function able to throw on Emscripten; it does not. Emscripten's libc declares `getentropy()` in `<unistd.h>` and implements it as `__wasi_random_get()` (`system/lib/libc/musl/src/misc/getentropy.c`), backed by the host's `crypto.getRandomValues`, and .NET reaches the same source there (`src/native/minipal/random.c:83-93`). All three platforms have a real CSPRNG and nothing new throws. The fill loop deliberately never falls back to a weaker source, because that fallback IS this defect. **`Core.Base` cannot depend on `Security.Cryptography.Random`** -- the edge runs the other way -- so the function is file-local to `Guid.cpp`: no new public header, no new component edge, no layout, vtable, signature or `noexcept` change. **The finding's own invisibility is what made it hard to test**, and it is now demonstrated rather than asserted: a userspace PRNG has state and `fork()` duplicates it, so the pre-#2228 implementation made a parent and child produce the IDENTICAL GUID after a fork. Two new cases assert they differ, +1 pinning the RFC 4122 version/variant bits over the new bytes; the mutation restoring the per-thread Mersenne Twister fails **both** fork cases and nothing else. No test claims to measure entropy quality -- none can. **#1901's concurrency property is preserved BY CONSTRUCTION**: there is no engine, `thread_local`, function `static` or `std::random_device` left on the path, so the race surface is empty and its 100,000-GUID tests are kept unchanged; TSan was deliberately not re-run, for the reason recorded in the plan. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero `NewGuid`/`CreateVersion7` sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. `docs/CoreGuidEntropySourcePlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-051](modules/core/include/System/Array.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-051--high--raw-pointer-arraycopy-applies-unchecked-memcpy-to-arbitrary-objects-and-negative-lengths) | high | remediated | `Array.hpp`, `Buffer.hpp` | Raw-pointer `Array::Copy` and generic Buffer typed-vector BlockCopy use unchecked byte copy for arbitrary types; ASan confirms invalid nontrivial destruction. Array additionally accepts negative signed metadata. |
| [SR-AUD-052](modules/core/include/System/Array.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-052--medium--array-delegate-overloads-do-not-validate-empty-stdfunction-at-the-public-boundary) | medium | remediated | `Array.hpp` | Empty `std::function` inputs yield `bad_function_call` only if reached, or a silent normal result for empty arrays, instead of a deterministic argument error. |
| [SR-AUD-053](modules/core/include/System/Array.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-053--low--arraymaxlengthproperty-reports-int32max-not-the-net-runtime-limit) | low | confirmed | `Array.hpp` | `MaxLengthProperty()` exposes `INT32_MAX`, 56 higher than current .NET `Array.MaxLength`, without a documented vector-adaptation decision. **Reviewed 2026-08-12 (#2327); STILL CONFIRMED, nothing implemented — `needs_user`.** Live: `Array.hpp:38` returns `std::numeric_limits<intcs>::max()` = 2,147,483,647 against .NET's `0x7FFFFFC7` = 2,147,483,591; the gap is 56. **Measured consumer surface: nothing uses it** — across `modules/`, `tests/`, `test/` and `bench/` the name occurs once outside its own definition, `ArrayTests.cpp:386` `EXPECT_GT(..., 0)`; no production call site, no `static_assert`, no array sizing, no allocation or indexing path, no serialized form. **Premise addition the finding does not state: the port already disagrees with itself.** `System::Buffers::MemoryPool<T>::MaxArrayLength` (`MemoryPool.hpp:42`) is a **public** `static constexpr intcs = 0x7FFFFFC7` documented "Matches .NET's Array.MaxLength (Array.cs)" and pinned to that literal by `MemoryPoolTests.MaxBufferSize_MatchesArrayMaxLength`; `ArrayBufferWriter<T>::MaxArrayLength` is the same value, private. The .NET number is already the repository's answer everywhere the limit is *enforced*; the only place publishing a different one is the property named after the .NET constant, where it is enforced nowhere. **Why it is still a decision:** the value is a public `constexpr` in a shipped header, so lowering it changes a downstream `constexpr` context's answer and makes a downstream `n > MaxLengthProperty()` guard newly reject 56 values — a surface this repository cannot measure (CNA/mobile-eggbert are outside its boundary), and the precedent for a public constant's value changing is `needs_user` (SR-AUD-130/#2326). The finding's own remediation is a disjunction, matching `CLAUDE.md` checklist item 5. **Deliberately not done:** pinning `== INT32_MAX`, which would encode the undecided branch as the tested contract. Options priced in #2327. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-054](modules/core/include/System/ArraySegment.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-054--high--default-arraysegment-operations-silently-succeed-or-dereference-null-instead-of-throwing) | high | remediated | `ArraySegment.hpp` | Default-segment operations omit .NET's invalid-state guard; `ToArray` can silently succeed and `Slice(0)` reaches sanitizer-confirmed null dereference. **Residual (#2215, needs_user):** `begin()`/`end()`, the `GetEnumerator()` counterpart, still iterate a default segment zero times because guarding them needs an approved `noexcept` drop. |
| [SR-AUD-055](modules/core/include/System/ArraySegment.hpp.audit.md) | medium | confirmed | `ArraySegment.hpp` | Vector `CopyTo` resizes an undersized destination rather than preserving ArraySegment's fixed-capacity destination/error contract. **Reviewed 2026-08-12 (#2328); STILL CONFIRMED — `needs_user`; one doc clause landed as #2329.** **Premise correction: four tests pin the resize, not one.** Besides `CopyTo_VectorWithOffset_ExpandsDest`, `ArraySegmentTests.CopyTo_Vector_CopiesAllElements` and `CopyTo_Vector_PartialSegment` both copy into an **empty** `dest` through the one-argument overload the finding treats only as "forwards to that behavior", and `CoreMemorySafetyOverlapTests.NonOverlappingCopiesKeepTheirPreviousResults` (`CoreMemorySafetyTests.cpp:837-838`) carries the comment `// SR-AUD-055's resize, unchanged` — ticket #2214 repaired this file's default-state and overlap defects and **deliberately preserved** the resize. **Premise addition: there is a repository convention and this is the one place that breaks it.** Of the **26** `void CopyTo(std::vector<...>&, ...)` overloads in `modules/`, **20 reject** a short destination — 13 with their own guard, five through the shared `System::Collections::detail::requireValidCopyDestination`, and `ImmutableList`'s two forwarding forms — and `ArraySegment`'s two-argument overload is the **only** one that grows a caller-sized destination. (`BitArray`'s two forms *replace* the destination outright: a third shape, a different .NET member, not this finding.) `modules/core` may not depend on `Collections`, so a repair writes its own guard rather than reusing the helper. **Why it is a decision:** the reject branch is a runtime behaviour break on currently accepted input — calls that succeed today start throwing and four first-party tests invert — the class this repository has always escalated. Both options priced in #2328. **#2329 (`done`, documentation only):** the one-argument overload's comment required "capacity for at least Count elements" and described writes "via `push_back`", both false and directly contradicted by the overload it forwards to; it now states what the code does and records that the resize-versus-reject question is open. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-056](modules/core/tests/System/InterfaceTests2.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-056--medium--observable-fixture-discards-the-subscription-handle-and-permits-post-completion-delivery) | medium | remediated | `InterfaceTests2.cpp` | The direct observable fixture returns a null subscription and permits `OnNext` after completion, leaving required unsubscription and terminal-state behavior unasserted. **Remediated (#2301 review, #2302 implementation, 2026-08-11)** by all five of the report's own suggested assertions plus the fixture behaviour they require. This is a test-contract defect, so the fixture and its assertions are the whole of it and **no production file was touched**. Re-measured first: the production interfaces were the right oracle all along - `IObservable<T>::Subscribe` already returns `std::shared_ptr<IDisposable>` and documents what it is for, and `IObserver<T>` already documents that no `OnNext` or `OnCompleted` follows a terminal call; only the fixture contradicted them, and the finding's own note that no first-party `IObservable<T>` implementation exists still holds, so nothing shipped was wrong. `IntObservable2::Subscribe` now returns a real `Subscription` that unsubscribes exactly its own observer, rejects a null observer with `System::ArgumentNullException`, and is a no-op on a second `Dispose()`; the provider records a terminal state so `Emit`/`Complete`/`Fail` deliver nothing after one, and `Fail` supplies the `OnError` path the report notes was never covered. Two documented design choices: the observer list lives in a `State` the provider and every subscription **co-own** - the `SortedSet<T>`/#1786 pattern - so disposal is safe in any destruction order and there is **no ownership cycle**, answering the last "other missing assertions" bullet; and `~Subscription()` deliberately does **not** dispose, because unsubscription is tied to `Dispose()` rather than to the handle's lifetime - measured, since an RAII destructor made both pre-existing cases fail, both discarding the handle. **Seven cases added, none retired** (2 -> 9); the two pre-existing keep their names, with a non-null subscription assertion added to the first. Both defects the finding names were re-introduced as mutations, rebuilt and relinked: returning `nullptr` from `Subscribe` fails `Subscribe_ReturnsADisposableSubscription` **and** the pre-existing `Subscribe_AndReceiveValues`; removing the terminal guard from `Emit` fails `NoValueIsDeliveredAfterCompletion` **and** `OnError_IsTerminalToo` - so the report's "a future implementation can copy this fixture's wrong behavior without a failing regression" risk is closed in both directions. Deliberately **not** pinned: subscription ordering, which no interface documents. Still open and outside this finding: the `ParseableInt` and provider-`nullptr` bullets. `docs/CoreObservableFixtureContractPlan.md`. |

| [SR-AUD-059](modules/runtime/include/System/Runtime/CompilerServices/FormattableStringFactory.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-059--low--factory-documentation-promises-an-exception-for-a-valid-empty-format) | low | remediated | `FormattableStringFactory.hpp` | Ticket #1978 removes the false `@throws` claim and pins the accepted empty format; the behaviour was always correct and the claim was the defect. |
| [SR-AUD-060](modules/core/src/System/DateOnly.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-060--high--dateonly-public-arithmetic-performs-signed-overflow-before-required-range-validation) | high | remediated | `DateOnly.cpp` | `FromDayNumber`, `AddDays`, `AddMonths`, and `AddYears` calculate with signed C++ arithmetic before range handling; UBSan confirms four reachable extreme-input overflow paths. |
| [SR-AUD-061](modules/core/src/System/DateOnly.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-061--medium--dateonly-parser-accepts-arbitrary-trailing-text-after-an-iso-date) | medium | remediated | `DateOnly.cpp` | `TryParse` accepts arbitrary trailing text after a valid ISO prefix via unchecked `std::sscanf` prefix conversion; `Parse` inherits the false success. **Remediated (#1879, 2026-07-31, CCF-002 class D, approved per `docs/RemainingApprovalDecisions.md` §C.8):** the `std::sscanf` prefix conversion was replaced by the full-consumption `System::detail::DateTimeTextScanner`, so `"2024-06-15junk"` and — the worst row — `"2024-06-15 10:20:30"`, a full timestamp silently truncated to its date, both return `false`. Every well-formed date keeps its exact value. **Post-audit #1929 row 5:** surrounding invariant whitespace is now valid; internal/timestamp garbage still fails, so SR-AUD-061 stays remediated. |
| [SR-AUD-062](modules/core/include/System/Tuple.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-062--high--tuplehashcombine-overflows-signed-intcs-for-reachable-hash-inputs) | high | remediated | `Tuple.hpp` | `tupleHashCombine` adds signed 32-bit operands after shifting; UBSan confirms an overflow through reachable `Tuple2::GetHashCode` input rather than defined .NET unchecked hash arithmetic. |
| [SR-AUD-063](modules/core/include/System/Tuple.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-063--medium--tuple-components-are-publicly-mutable-despite-the-immutable-net-contract) | medium | confirmed | `Tuple.hpp` | `TupleN` uses public mutable fields despite .NET Tuple's readonly components, allowing post-construction mutation without a documented adaptation. **Reviewed 2026-08-12 (#2330); STILL CONFIRMED, nothing implemented — `needs_user`.** **Measured consumer surface:** no first-party code *writes* an `ItemN` (zero matches for the assignment form outside `ValueTuple`), but **75 first-party sites read one** — `TupleTests.cpp` 33, `TupleNewTests.cpp` 26, `SystemTypesRemainingTests.cpp` 16 — and all 75 break under the only .NET-matching repair, because `CLAUDE.md` rule 5 turns `t.Item1` into `t.getItem1Property()`. `ValueTuple` is **not** affected and must not be changed with it: .NET's `ValueTuple` fields really are public and mutable, so its 52 sites are correct parity. **Premise addition: the cheap-looking shortcut is the wider break.** Making the members `const` deletes the implicitly declared copy-assignment operator, so whole-tuple assignment, `std::vector<Tuple2<…>>::push_back`, `std::sort` over tuples and every other assignable-value use stop compiling — a larger break than the getter migration, with diagnostics pointing at the standard library. No first-party site does any of that, so the cost would land downstream and unmeasured. Every route is a public source break, which is why the SR-AUD-011 remediation note already excluded this finding as "a public source break needing approval". Options A/B priced and option C (const members) rejected on measurement in #2330. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-064](modules/core/include/System/Lazy.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-064--medium--lazy-constructors-silently-accept-an-invalid-thread-safety-mode) | medium | remediated | `Lazy.hpp` | Mode-taking constructors retained any `LazyThreadSafetyMode` value and `getValueProperty()`'s `default:` label then dispatched it as PublicationOnly. Measured before the fix (`build-probe/2235_probe1_before.log`): `Lazy<int>(static_cast<LazyThreadSafetyMode>(99))` constructed, `getModeProperty()` returned 99, and two accesses over a throwing factory produced **two** factory invocations — no fault caching, which is PublicationOnly's contract and neither None's nor ExecutionAndPublication's, so the invalid instance did not merely survive, it silently acquired another mode's semantics. Ticket #2236 (family #2235) adds a private `requireValidMode()` with .NET's three cases and its `default: throw ArgumentOutOfRangeException(nameof(mode), SR.Lazy_ctor_ModeInvalid)`, called from the two mode-taking constructors **after** `requireFactory()`, matching .NET's argument order (pinned by a test). The five other constructors cannot produce an invalid mode and are untouched. +8 tests, mutation-checked three ways; no signature, template-parameter, `noexcept`, layout or vtable change, and `Lazy<T>` is header-only so there is no exported symbol to move. |
| [SR-AUD-065](modules/core/include/System/Lazy.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-065--medium--lazy-accepts-an-empty-factory-and-defers-failure-to-stdbad_function_call) | medium | remediated | `Lazy.hpp` | Empty `std::function` factories are accepted then fail at first `Value` with native `std::bad_function_call`, rather than deterministic constructor validation. |
| [SR-AUD-066](modules/core/include/System/Lazy.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-066--medium--publicationonly-wrongly-rejects-recursive-value-access) | medium | remediated | `Lazy.hpp` | An unconditional reentrancy guard throws for PublicationOnly even though .NET reserves this recursive-`Value` exception for None and ExecutionAndPublication. **Remediated by this finding's own second stated repair (#2237, family #2235): the divergence is RETAINED BY DESIGN and is now documented and pinned, not matched.** The finding authorises either implementing the PublicationOnly rule without deadlock **or** documenting and deliberately exposing the restriction; the first is not available without a user decision, because `checkNotReentrant()` is load-bearing for memory safety — a recursive `getValueProperty()` would re-`lock()` the non-recursive `publicationOnlyMutex_`, which `[thread.mutex.requirements.mutex]` makes undefined behaviour — and both structural routes to .NET's behaviour (publish-only locking, or same-thread reentrancy with a first-publication-wins discard rule) either reverse the header's already-documented PublicationOnly serialisation deviation or let an unconditionally recursive factory recurse without bound, trading a catchable `InvalidOperationException` for stack exhaustion. So the class doc-comment now carries an explicit second deviation paragraph naming the .NET rule not implemented, the `std::mutex` reason and the reopening ticket; `getValueProperty()`'s `@throws` clause records that .NET raises this for None and ExecutionAndPublication only; and +5 tests pin the behaviour in **all three** modes plus the non-recursive, fault-retry and different-instance controls a future change must not break. No executable statement changed. The behaviour change itself is ticket **#2238 (`needs_user`)**. `docs/CoreLazyThreadSafetyModeFamilyPlan.md` §4.2. |
| [SR-AUD-067](modules/core/include/System/Buffer.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-067--high--raw-bufferblockcopy-converts-negative-metadata-into-an-unbounded-memmove) | high | remediated | `Buffer.hpp` | Raw-pointer BlockCopy accepts negative offsets/count; negative count casts to `size_t` and reaches ASan-confirmed unbounded `memmove` instead of deterministic argument validation. |
| [SR-AUD-068](modules/core/include/System/ValueType.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-068--medium--valuetype-is-publicly-constructible-and-defaults-to-identity-rather-than-net-value-semantics) | medium | confirmed | `ValueType.hpp`, `ValueTypeTests.cpp` | Public C++ `ValueType` is constructible and uses identity/address defaults, where .NET has an abstract fieldwise-value base; direct tests lock in the divergent fallback. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2322); STILL CONFIRMED, nothing implemented — `needs_user`.** Live: `Equals` is `this == &other`, `GetHashCode` is the object address narrowed to `intcs`, `ToString` is the literal `"System.ValueType"`, and the implicit public default constructor makes `System::ValueType v;` compile. **Measured consumer surface: nothing derives from it in production** — the only derived types anywhere are `SimpleValueType` and `ConcreteValueType` in `ValueTypeTests.cpp`, and `System::Void` documents that it deliberately does *not* derive from it, so the finding's harm has no in-repository instance and the exposure is entirely downstream. **Not autonomous, on two different grounds.** Field-by-field `Equals`/`GetHashCode` and a runtime-type-name `ToString` are reflection, which `CLAUDE.md` lists as a **permanent deviation, out of scope** — not a TODO. What remains is making the incompatible default unreachable, and every route is a **public source break** in a shipped header (a `protected` constructor, matching .NET's own `protected ValueType()`, or an abstract class) — the same class as SR-AUD-063. Options priced in #2322. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-069](modules/core/include/System/SequencePosition.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-069--medium--sequenceposition-exposes-mutable-public-representation-instead-of-an-opaque-readonly-position) | medium | confirmed | `SequencePosition.hpp`, `Batch6BuffersTests.cpp` | Public mutable `void*`/integer components let callers rewrite a returned position, unlike .NET's private readonly opaque representation; tests never protect that boundary. **Reviewed 2026-08-12 (#2331); STILL CONFIRMED — it is a CONJUNCTION, and its value-contract clause is REMEDIATED while its representation clause is `needs_user` (#2332).** **Measured consumer surface:** seven files mention the type, and **direct field access exists in exactly one place — inside `SequencePosition` itself**; every other use already goes through the constructor and `GetObject()`/`GetInteger()`, so the encapsulation repair's first-party cost is zero. The two closest siblings settle the convention: `System::Index` and `System::Range` are classes with private fields and public `Equals`/`GetHashCode`. **#2331 (`done`), purely additive:** `Equals(const SequencePosition&)` and `GetHashCode()` are added and `operator==`/`!=` delegate to `Equals`, so the named and operator forms cannot drift. The hash is `((h1 << 5) + h1) ^ h2` evaluated in `uintcs` — signed overflow here would be UB, the CCF-004 class already recorded for `detail::tupleHashCombine` — with the pointer fold guarded by `if constexpr (sizeof(std::uintptr_t) > sizeof(uintcs))` because `bits >> 32` on a 32-bit target is a shift at the operand width. **It is deliberately not .NET's hash value** and the header says so; that is *not* the unverifiable-reference-text class of #2321/#2323, because a hash value is documented unstable in .NET too and this port already ships two hashes that differ from .NET's (`Range::GetHashCode`'s `397`, and `System::HashCode`'s per-process `std::random_device` seed). **+7 tests**, closing the finding's own list — equal and unequal **non-null** segments (every pre-existing direct test used `nullptr`), default equality, hash agreement, hash stability, and the caveat that component equality is not sequence-location identity — all obeying `docs/HashAssertionContractRule.md` (R1 asserted, no R2 pair, segment dependence stated over a family of eight, one R3 pin naming its property: a null segment folds to zero so the hash reduces to the integer's own bits). Three mutations, three caught. No layout, vtable, signature or `noexcept` change. **#2332 (`needs_user`):** making `object_`/`integer_` private is a public source break (structured bindings, designated initialisers, direct assignment) with zero first-party cost and unmeasurable downstream cost; layout is unaffected because all members keep the same access. The header now documents the divergence without taking the decision. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-070](modules/buffers/include/System/Buffers/ArrayBufferWriter.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-070--medium--arraybufferwritert-silently-requires-a-default-constructible-t) | medium | remediated | `ArrayBufferWriter.hpp`, `MemoryPool.hpp` | Vector resize and `Clear` impose an undocumented default-constructor requirement on `T`; a valid non-default-constructible type fails to compile at `GetSpan`, and MemoryPool repeats the constraint.  **Remediated (#2054, 2026-08-04, family B-C, `docs/BuffersNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.4/§23.4):** each requirement is now stated in the owning type's Doxygen block and `static_assert`ed **at the point where it was already enforced**, so *exactly the same set of programs compiles* and only the diagnostic changes — measured site by site against the pre-change headers materialised from `b294738`. **Two premise corrections.** (a) The site count is **six**, not two: the review already promoted `ArrayBufferWriter::checkAndResizeBuffer`, `::Clear`, `MemoryPoolHeapOwner_`'s constructor and `SharedArrayPool<T>::Rent` + `ArrayPool<T>::Return(clearArray=true)` to first-class sites, and implementation found the sixth, `ArrayBufferWriter(intcs initialCapacity)`, which resizes on its own. `SequenceReader<T>::TryRead`/`TryPeek` are two further `T{}` sites whose requirement was **already documented** (the CCF-014 contract); they gain the assert but were never silent. (b) "fails to compile at `GetSpan`" understates where it bites: `GetSpan`/`GetMemory` are `virtual` overrides and `ArrayPool`'s `Rent`/`Return` are `virtual`, so their bodies are instantiated **for the vtable** — measured, `ArrayBufferWriter<NoDefault> w;` and `ArrayPool<NoDefault>::Shared().Return(v, false)` were already rejected before this ticket. Naming the type and `sizeof` stay legal for any `T`, which is why no assert is at class scope. Copy-assignability, which `Clear` and `Return(…, true)` also require, was undocumented too and is now asserted. 13 negative consumer sites (`test/consumer/buffers_generic_requirements_negative.cpp`) prove the rejected half; 9 tests in `BuffersGenericRequirementsTests.cpp` pin the accepted half. No runtime code, signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` specification changed. |

| [SR-AUD-077](modules/buffers/include/System/Buffers/SearchValues.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-077--medium--searchvaluest-documents-equality-only-support-but-imposes-an-undocumented-stdhash-requirement) | medium | remediated | `SearchValues.hpp`, `Batch16BuffersTests.cpp` | Public template documentation promises equality-only support, but unordered_set requires `std::hash<T>` and equality-only values fail to compile.  **Remediated (#2054, 2026-08-04, family B-C, `docs/BuffersNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.4/§23.4):** the `@tparam` sentence that promised equality-only support is replaced by an explicit statement that this port requires equality **and** a usable `std::hash<T>` — strictly more than .NET's `IEquatable<T>` — with the workaround (supply a `std::hash<T>` specialization) named. Both constructors `static_assert` the two halves separately, so an equality-only `T` now fails with that sentence instead of `'std::__hash_enum<…>::~__hash_enum()' is private within this context`. The asserts are in the constructor bodies, not at class scope: `SearchValues<EqualityOnly>*` and `sizeof(SearchValues<EqualityOnly>)` compile today and still do. Two negative consumer sites (one per constructor) and the `EqualityOnly` predicate pins in `BuffersGenericRequirementsTests.cpp` hold both directions. Storing the values in a hash container remains this port's design; making the requirement true rather than the documentation is a separate, unraised question. |

| [SR-AUD-087](modules/buffers/include/System/Buffers/ReadOnlySequenceSegment.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-087--medium--readonlysequencesegment-cannot-construct-the-advertised-multi-segment-sequence) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `ReadOnlySequenceSegment.hpp`, `ReadOnlySequence.hpp`, `Batch17BuffersTests.cpp` | Linked segment nodes cannot construct or back any C++ ReadOnlySequence; only vector/raw-pointer constructors exist despite the public multi-segment claim. |

| [SR-AUD-089](modules/core/include/System/ArgumentNullException.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-089--high--argumentnullexception-null-c-string-parameter-crashes-during-message-construction) | high | remediated | `ArgumentNullException.hpp`, `.cpp`, direct tests | The public `const char*` parameter-name constructor passed null to string concatenation before its base fallback; UBSan/ASan confirmed a null-read crash. Ticket #1776 removed the unsafe local `makeMsg()` concatenation and routes straight through the already null-safe `ArgumentException(const char*, const char*)` base constructor, so a null `paramName` no longer reaches `char_traits::length`. |
| [SR-AUD-090](modules/core/include/System/ArgumentNullException.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-090--low--argumentnullexception-duplicates-its-parameter-marker-in-what) | low | remediated | `ArgumentNullException.hpp`, `.cpp`, direct tests | Parameter-name constructors precomposed and then base-appended the same suffix, producing two `(Parameter 'name')` markers in `what()`. Ticket #1776 passes the raw default message straight to `ArgumentException(message, paramName)`, matching .NET's own `ArgumentNullException(paramName) : base(SR.ArgumentNull_Generic, paramName)`, so the marker is appended exactly once. |
| [SR-AUD-091](modules/core/include/System/ArgumentOutOfRangeException.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-091--medium--generic-argumentoutofrange-guards-silently-require-stdto_string-in-addition-to-their-declared-comparison-contract) | medium | remediated | `ArgumentOutOfRangeException.hpp`, direct tests | Generic comparison/equality guard templates call `std::to_string`, so documented comparison-only user types fail to compile despite satisfying their declared operators. **Remediated (#2253 review, #2254, 2026-08-10, `docs/CoreArgumentOutOfRangeGuardDomainPlan.md`).** The nine guards now share one private ordered `if constexpr` formatter, and each carries a `static_assert` for the one comparison expression it evaluates, so the whole compile-time domain is **declared and diagnosed** *and* **widened** — the finding's two offered routes taken together. Measured one `(type, guard)` pair per translation unit over 22 candidate types x 9 guards (`build-probe/2253_probe1_matrix.log` -> `2253_probe2_after.log`, `2253_probe3_extension.log`): **99 OK / 99 FAIL before, 135 OK / 63 FAIL after, with 0 of the 99 previously accepted pairs regressed** and **0 of the 63 remaining rejections reported inside libstdc++** — every one is now a sharp-runtime sentence. **Compatibility is structural, not incidental:** the formatter's FIRST branch is the same `std::to_string(value)` call the guards made before, so all 11 previously accepted types take the identical path and emit byte-identical text, and all 121 first-party call sites instantiate an arithmetic `T`. **Four premise corrections.** (1) The rejected surface is far wider than the finding's synthetic `OrderedOnly` reproducer: `enum class`, `std::string`, `std::string_view` and every pointer type were all rejected, so `ThrowIfEqual(std::string("a"), std::string("a"), "p")` did not compile. (2) 14 of the 99 failures are **not** this defect — they are `EqualityOnly`/`InequalityOnly` failing on an operator they genuinely lack, which is correct behaviour a repair must preserve. (3) The port already carried an invisible split: an *unscoped* enumeration compiled and printed its integer through integral promotion while a *scoped* one did not compile at all. (4) The three unary guards carry a second undeclared requirement the finding does not mention — they compare against `T{}`, so `T` must be default-constructible. **Two deliberate narrowings, both declared:** raw pointers are excluded (a `std::string_view` from a null pointer is undefined behaviour, and `ThrowIfEqual("a", "b", "p")` deduces `const char*` and would compare addresses rather than text), and a comparison-only type with no rendering is still rejected — now by a sentence naming the six supported shapes and the ADL `to_string` extension point that lets it opt in. **`operator<<` was rejected on a measurement**, not a preference: it forces `<sstream>` into a header **404** translation units depend on, at +2,819 preprocessed lines (+4.6%) each, where the ADL branch gives the same reach for +0. **+26 tests** (`ArgumentOutOfRangeGuardDomainTests`), a **new 6-site negative consumer fixture** (`test/consumer/core_argument_out_of_range_guard_domain_negative.cpp`) and a widened positive fixture (`test/consumer/core_base.cpp`), taking the inventory 14 files/120 sites -> **15 files/126 sites**. Four mutations, all caught: swapping the enumeration and ADL branches, dropping the pointer exclusion (site 2 compiled again; site 1 correctly did not fire), deleting one comparison assertion (the site still failed to compile but on the wrong diagnostic — a whole-file check would have passed falsely), and truncating an embedded NUL. **No signature, layout, vtable, `noexcept` or symbol change**, and no new `SR-AUD-*` identifier. Sanitizers are inapplicable: a translation failure produces no binary. |
| [SR-AUD-092](modules/core/include/System/Exception.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-092--medium--default-exception-exposes-an-empty-message-instead-of-the-net-fallback-diagnostic) | medium | confirmed | `Exception.hpp`, `.cpp`, direct tests | Default C++ Exception stores/returns an empty message, while current .NET provides a nonempty `Exception_WasThrown` fallback; two direct tests lock in the empty result. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2323); STILL CONFIRMED, deferred (`todo`).** Live: `Exception::Exception() : message_("")` (`Exception.cpp:12-14`). **Premise measurement the finding does not state:** across every `*Exception.cpp` in `modules/core/src/System` with a no-argument constructor, **18 subclasses supply a non-empty default message and exactly one type does not — `System::Exception` itself**, so the blast radius is the base type constructed directly, not "every exception". **Two blockers.** (1) .NET's fallback is the *formatted* `Exception_WasThrown` resource and its exact wording cannot be read with `/rv` absent — the same class as **#2252/#2260**, and inventing it would create a divergence no later test could distinguish from the real thing. (2) Its format argument is the **runtime type name**, which this port cannot produce (reflection is a permanent deviation); a hard-coded `"System.Exception"` would be worse than empty for any subclass reaching the base fallback, though per the survey none does. **No behaviour-only sub-clause exists to land first** — choosing what to put there *is* choosing text. `ExceptionTests.DefaultCtorEmptyMessage` and `ExceptionNewTests.DefaultCtor_MessageEmpty` pin the current behaviour. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |

| [SR-AUD-097](modules/core/include/System/AggregateException.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-097--high--null-inner-exception_ptr-is-accepted-and-later-crashes-in-aggregate-message-processing) | high | remediated | `AggregateException.hpp` | Null `std::exception_ptr` inner values were accepted, and `std::rethrow_exception` is undefined for null: one accepted null armed **three** ASan-confirmed crash paths (`buildMessage`, `Flatten`'s `collectLeaves`, `GetBaseException`) plus two silent ones, where `Handle` passed the null to the caller's predicate and `Unwrap` returned it. Ticket #1807 validates in every constructor that takes inner exceptions, reproducing .NET's own type split — `ArgumentException("An element of innerExceptions was null.")` for a null collection **element**, `ArgumentNullException("innerException")` for the null single **argument**. SR-AUD-098 and SR-AUD-099, which share this file, stay `confirmed`. |
| [SR-AUD-098](modules/core/include/System/AggregateException.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-098--medium--aggregate-constructors-and-transformations-lose-net-causal-diagnostics-and-flatten-ordering) | medium | confirmed | `AggregateException.hpp`, direct tests | Message/vector constructors omit first-inner and contained diagnostics; `Handle` / `Flatten` discard custom message and Flatten uses different leaf order. A green direct test locks incomplete text. **Partially remediated 2026-08-11 and deliberately still `confirmed`** — the finding is a conjunction of six clauses (`docs/AggregateExceptionCausalPlan.md`, ticket #2306). C1 (base `innerException_` never named the first cause) closed by #2307; C5 (Flatten leaf order) closed by #2308 plus #2310, which repaired a non-terminating queue #2308 shipped. C2/C3/C4/C6 — composing contained messages and preserving the caller's message through `Handle`/`Flatten` — are deferred to #2309 (`needs_user`): the composed text is not available here, and preserving it needs a raw-message field, a public representation change. |

| [SR-AUD-100](modules/core/include/System/DuplicateWaitObjectException.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-100--medium--duplicatewaitobjectexception-inherits-argumentexception-hresult-and-uses-a-divergent-default-diagnostic) | medium | remediated | `DuplicateWaitObjectException.hpp`, direct tests | All constructors retain `ArgumentException`'s `COR_E_ARGUMENT` (`0x80070057`) instead of `COR_E_DUPLICATEWAITOBJECT` (`0x80131529`); default text also misses the wait-array diagnostic, and direct tests omit both diagnostics. |
| [SR-AUD-101](modules/core/include/System/IO/IOException.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-101--medium--io-and-crypto-exception-ports-omit-public-error-context-overloads-and-have-no-direct-test-coverage) | medium | remediated | `IOException.hpp`, `DirectoryNotFoundException.hpp`, `CryptographicException.hpp` | Current public IOException custom-HResult, DirectoryNotFound path-plus-inner, and Cryptographic composite-format overloads are absent; their focused test filter selects zero tests. **Remediated (#2277 review, #2278 implementation, 2026-08-11.)** All three constructors added. **The inherited "pure additions" ranking was wrong for two of the three members, and both were measured, not discovered afterwards.** A before/after `std::is_constructible_v` matrix over thirteen argument packs per type shows **no cell anywhere flipping `yes` -> `no`** and nine flipping `no` -> `yes`, but: `IOException(message, 0)` used to select the inner-exception overload, because the literal `0` is a null pointer constant, and now selects `IOException(string, int)`, yielding HResult `0` instead of `COR_E_IO` (message and absent inner unchanged).
