That is **irreducible** - `0` has type `int` - and no first-party call site spells it: all eleven 2-argument `IOException` construction sites pass a real `std::exception_ptr`. `nullptr` is unaffected; both facts are pinned by tests. Adding `CryptographicException(format, insert)` would have **removed** `CryptographicException(message, nullptr)`, which compiles today, because C++23 declares `std::basic_string(std::nullptr_t) = delete` and a deleted function is still a candidate, so `nullptr` reached `std::string` and `std::exception_ptr` through equal-rank user-defined conversions; that public source break was designed out with an explicit `(const std::string&, std::nullptr_t)` overload delegating to the empty-`exception_ptr` meaning the spelling already had, deliberately **not** replicated on `DirectoryNotFoundException`/`FileNotFoundException`/`FileLoadException` where the spelling never compiled. The `DirectoryNotFoundException(message, directoryPath, inner)` member **is** a pure addition. **Two premise corrections.** The zero-test claim is executable-scoped: measured across all 38 executables the focused filter selects **15** tests (11 `SharpRuntimeTests_IO`, 4 `SharpRuntimeTests_Security_Cryptography`), not zero - what asserted nothing was `SharpRuntimeTests_Core_Base`, which owns all three headers; 33 tests were added there and none of the 15 retired, so the filter now selects 48. And the ".NET public `(message, directoryPath, innerException)` overload" could not be verified with `/rv` absent - the port's existing path constructor and `getDirectoryPathProperty()` are not a .NET shape this repository can point at either - so the addition rests instead on the stated user-visible gap and on the `(message, fileName, inner)` shape `FileNotFoundException` and `FileLoadException` already carry. `CryptographicException(format, insert)` composes through `System::String::Format`, i.e. the shared `runCompositeFormat` scanner, rather than a bespoke `{0}` replacement that would have been a fifth composite-format grammar (CCF-012, #2020); a malformed format therefore throws `FormatException` from the new overload only. **Not a family and no CCF minted**: three unrelated omissions in three types that share a shape, not a cause, per the #2270 and #2267 adjacency standard. No member, signature, `noexcept`, layout (`sizeof` 168/200/168, `alignof` 8/8/8), vtable or component-dependency change; three new constructor symbols, none removed. Sanitizers deliberately not run - a missing overload is a compile-domain and message-text defect for which they are not discriminating. Five mutations, all caught, the fifth at compile time naming both ambiguous candidates. `docs/CoreExceptionErrorContextOverloadPlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-102](modules/core/include/System/AppContext.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-102--medium--appcontext-named-data-cannot-configure-basedirectory-or-compatibility-switches) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `AppContext.hpp` | Named data cannot set `BaseDirectory` or feed `TryGetSwitch`; the raw `void*` store has no type/ownership model for the current .NET string-backed configuration behavior. **DESIGN-COMPLETE, APPROVAL-BOUND IN BOTH HALVES (#2255 review, 2026-08-10, `docs/CoreAppContextNamedDataDesign.md`).** Both premises reproduce **exactly as filed** — no correction was needed, which is worth recording because the four preceding Core units each corrected something. `getBaseDirectoryProperty()` never consults the data store, and `TryGetSwitch` never falls back to it. **Neither behaviour has a compatible repair**, and three routes were priced rather than one chosen by default: **(A)** give the store a runtime type — the only faithful route, but a public signature change on **four** members across **two** classes, because #2249 made `AppDomain::SetData`/`GetData` forwarders, and it retires three existing pins; **(B)** `reinterpret_cast` the `void*` for the two special keys — **rejected as undefined behaviour by construction**, since a `void*` carries no type, so the assumption is unfalsifiable at the point of use and the correct and corrupting cases are the same instruction sequence; **(C)** add a separate typed string channel — rejected as inventing public API .NET does not have. A **fourth** obstacle applies to the `BaseDirectory` half alone and is not solved by route A: `getBaseDirectoryProperty()` returns `const std::string&`, so an override sourced from the store would hand out a reference to caller-owned storage with no liveness boundary — the **CCF-019** shape, recorded as an adjacency and **deliberately not extended**. **#2255 (`needs_user`)** carries the one decision with its three priced options. **#2256 landed the compatible remainder**: the header now documents the borrowed-pointer ownership, the replacement rule, both divergences and the reflection deviation, and **+10 tests** (`AppContextNamedDataTests`) pin the current contract — including both divergences as *observable* rather than merely asserted, a stored `nullptr` being indistinguishable from an absent key, key replacement, map independence, the base-directory reference outliving its call, and 4-thread concurrent use of both maps. **No behaviour changed**, so the finding stays **`confirmed`** under the split convention SR-AUD-103 and SR-AUD-259 already use. **#2250 is unreachable from here and untouched**: nothing in #2256 changes what `TryGetSwitch` returns. |
| [SR-AUD-103](modules/core/include/System/AppDomain.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-103--medium--appdomain-discards-public-data-and-switch-state-instead-of-forwarding-it-to-appcontext) | medium | confirmed | `AppDomain.hpp` | `GetData`, `SetData`, and `IsCompatibilitySwitchSet` are stubs, so a one-domain AppDomain cannot observe or set the functioning AppContext state and cannot represent the unset switch result. **DATA HALF REMEDIATED (#2249, 2026-08-10, `docs/CoreAppDomainCompatibleSlicePlan.md` §4.1).** `SetData` and `GetData` are now the `AppContext` forwarding calls .NET implements them as; the bodies moved out of line into `AppDomain.cpp` because `AppContext.hpp` includes `AppDomain.hpp` for `BaseDirectory`, so the include cannot run both ways. Before/after on the same probe (`build-probe/2248_probe1_before.log` → `2248_probe2_after.log`): three `BAD` rows become `OK`, and the two controls (`nullptr` for an absent key, a valid name returned unchanged) never moved. **SWITCH HALF DELIBERATELY OPEN — #2250 (`needs_user`).** `IsCompatibilitySwitchSet` cannot be forwarded compatibly and is left exactly as it was, documented and pinned by two tests. It needs **both** a public return-type change (a `bool` cannot distinguish “explicitly false” from “unset”, which the finding itself states) **and** a `noexcept` drop — measured, not assumed: `AppContext::TryGetSwitch("")` throws `System::ArgumentException`, and it takes a `std::mutex` whose `lock()` can throw, so forwarding from a `noexcept` member would be `std::terminate`. The `noexcept` drop is approval-bound on the precedent of **#2215**. A third route — keep the signature, pre-check for empty, return `false` — was considered and rejected as inventing policy. The finding therefore stays **`confirmed`**, the convention SR-AUD-259 already uses. **SR-AUD-102 is untouched**: this work changes what `AppDomain` forwards, not what `AppContext` does. +5 tests in the new module-owned `AppDomainDataPolicyTests` fixture plus 2 pinning the deferred half, and one **retired pin**: **premise correction — the report's "no dedicated `AppDomain` fixture exists" and "no test invokes any of these public `AppDomain` members" are both wrong.** `tests/integration/Task42Tests.cpp` already had a 16-test `AppDomainTests` suite, and its `SetGetData_Stubs_NoThrow` asserted `GetData(...) == nullptr` immediately after a `SetData` — it **pinned the stub this finding is about**. The auditor's recorded validation filter (`AppContextExtraTests.*:AppDomainSetupTests.*`) does not select it. It is inverted to `SetGetData_ForwardsToAppContext` rather than deleted; the suite's other two touching tests are unaffected. It surfaced only in the full 38-executable gate, after the slice was pushed, so commit `be2f906` did leave one failing test and a follow-up commit repairs it. ABI change is additive only (two new out-of-line symbols), with no signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change; ABI change is additive only (two new out-of-line symbols), with no signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change. Mutation M1 (restore the stub bodies) failed exactly 3 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-104](modules/core/include/System/AppDomain.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-104--medium--applypolicy-accepts-invalid-assembly-identity-strings-that-net-rejects) | medium | remediated | `AppDomain.hpp` | `ApplyPolicy` returns empty and NUL-containing names unchanged instead of validating the representable invalid inputs before applying the no-policy identity route. **Remediated (#2251, 2026-08-10, `docs/CoreAppDomainCompatibleSlicePlan.md` §4.2).** An empty name throws `System::ArgumentException("The value cannot be an empty string.", "assemblyName")` — the message this repository already uses at seven other empty-string doors — and a name whose **first** character is NUL throws `ArgumentException("String cannot be of zero length.", "assemblyName")`. The finding's own reproduction is matched exactly: `nul_policy_length=2` measured as `len=2 [\0x]` before, an `ArgumentException` after. **Premise narrowed, deliberately:** the finding's phrase “embedded/leading NUL” reads as though both should be rejected, but .NET checks `assemblyName[0]` only, so `"a\0b"` is a legal argument and is still returned unchanged — rejecting it would make the port stricter than .NET. Mutation M3 (over-reach to `find('\0') != npos`) fails exactly that pin. **A mutation that first SURVIVED is recorded rather than hidden:** dropping the empty-string guard altogether changed nothing, because for a `const std::string&` `assemblyName[0]` with `size() == 0` is well-defined and yields the null character, so the leading-NUL branch alone already threw — same type, same parameter name, different message. Two message assertions were added and M2 now fails exactly one test; the test matrix was wrong, not the mutation. **One residual, ticketed not claimed:** `/rv` is absent, so the exact `SR.Argument_StringZeroLength` resource text could not be re-verified from source — **#2252** carries that as a deferred verification, and the tests pin the behaviour (which inputs throw, the exception type, the parameter name) rather than a parity that cannot be shown. The null case stays unrepresentable (`const std::string&`), now documented. +7 tests; no signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change. |
| [SR-AUD-105](modules/core/src/System/Environment.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-105--medium--unix-special-folder-resolution-ignores-xdg-verification-creation-and-invalid-enum-contracts) | medium | confirmed | `Environment.hpp`, `Environment.cpp`, direct tests | Unix special-folder routing ignores configured XDG config/data homes, folder verification/creation, and invalid folder/option errors; direct tests lock option ignoring. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2319); STILL CONFIRMED, nothing implemented** — no clause of it is autonomously implementable. Reviewed **alone**: sharing `Environment.cpp` with SR-AUD-106 is adjacency, not a cause, and no CCF is minted. Decomposed into four clauses, all reproduced live (`build-probe/2319_probe1_specialfolder.cpp`): **C1** absolute `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=/xdg/cfg` and `XDG_DATA_HOME=/xdg/data` change nothing (`honoured=0`; no `XDG_` string exists anywhere in this repository); **C2** `None`/`DoNotVerify`/`Create` and an undefined `0x1234` all return the same string and none throws; **C3** `Create` does not create and `None` returns a path that is not on disk; **C4** `0x0001`, `0x0003`, `0x0FFF`, `-1`, `999` all return `""`. **Premise correction, measured:** the finding reads `default:` as the undefined-value path, but the enum declares **47 enumerators / 46 distinct values** (`Personal` == `MyDocuments` == `0x0005`) and **32 of the 46 defined values also fall through to it** — only **14** are mapped. An empty return therefore already means *defined but unmapped on this platform*, so C4 cannot be repaired the way SR-AUD-064 was: there the enum had three values and `default:` was unambiguously the error path, here a repair needs a **definedness table** (46 accepted values, 14 undefined holes inside `0x0000`–`0x003B`, plus everything outside). `docs/CoreEnvironmentCompatibleSlicePlan.md`'s own "same shape as SR-AUD-064" note is thus an even weaker analogy than it claimed. **Consumer surface: none in production** — the only in-repository callers are `getSystemDirectoryProperty()` (itself called only from a test) and `EnvironmentTests`, and `io-isolated-storage` resolves its own paths; the cost of any repair is borne downstream. Existing tests deliberately pin the ignored-option behaviour (`GetFolderPath_WithNone_SameAsWithout`, `_WithDoNotVerify_SameAsWithout`) and must be retired by a C2/C3 repair. **Split by blocker, not by clause count: #2320 (`needs_user`)** for C1+C3, which are **policy** — honouring XDG moves where a shipped build reads and writes user data, and the finding names only two variables while the port also maps Desktop/MyMusic/MyPictures/MyVideos/Templates that .NET resolves through `~/.config/user-dirs.dirs`, so *how far* matters as much as *whether*; and C3 would put a `stat` and a `mkdir -p` inside a `[[nodiscard]] static std::string` getter. **#2321 (`todo`, deferred)** for C2+C4, which are **evidence-blocked on .NET's exact exception identity** with `/rv` absent — the same class as #2252/#2260 — and which are sequenced after #2320 because .NET validates the option precisely because it acts on it. No XDG semantics were assumed beyond the frozen finding's own wording, and no message text was invented. `docs/CoreSpecialFolderPolicyReview.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-106](modules/core/src/System/Environment.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-106--medium--setenvironmentvariable-conflates-a-valid-empty-value-with-deletion) | medium | confirmed | `Environment.hpp`, `Environment.cpp`, `EnvironmentTests.cpp` | The non-nullable C++ value parameter uses an empty string to delete, so callers cannot create a valid present empty variable; the direct test requires deletion. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2311); STILL CONFIRMED.** Re-measured live against the shipped `Environment.cpp`: the empty-value delete reproduces exactly. **Three premise corrections.** (1) The remedy #2239 and this report both name — "an `std::optional<std::string>` overload" — is **not additive**: measured, `SetEnvironmentVariable("A", "b")` becomes **ambiguous**, because `const char*`→`const std::string&` and `const char*`→`std::optional<std::string>` are equal-rank user-defined conversions, so every string-literal call site stops compiling. It is the one option that breaks the most source. (2) The defect is **not setter-only**: `GetEnvironmentVariable` returns `std::string` and answers `""` for a present-empty variable *and* for an absent one (`indistinguishable=1`), a deliberate port-wide convention stated at `Environment.hpp:243-252`, so repairing the setter alone leaves the round trip broken. (3) A fact recorded nowhere: `GetEnvironmentVariables()` **already** represents a present-empty entry (`map_value_len=0`), and the platform supports it — the observability channel exists. **This row's own last clause is wrong.** The test does *not* require deletion: its whole assertion was `GetEnvironmentVariable(...).empty()`, which by (2) holds for removal, for a present-empty value, and for a name never set — so changing the setter to store an empty value would have left the entire suite green. **Test clause REMEDIATED (#2312), test-only:** removal is now asserted through `GetEnvironmentVariables()` membership, with a POSIX-guarded companion pinning that an out-of-band empty value survives in the map while the getter still cannot tell it from absence. +1 test; 2 valid mutations, 2 caught, each by its own test (storing instead of removing; dropping empty-valued entries from the map). No production file, signature, layout, vtable, `noexcept`, symbol or component-dependency change. **Representational clause is #2313 (`needs_user`)**, blocked on two independent grounds: the .NET premise is unverified — if .NET treats an empty value as `null`, this port is already correct and the finding is a false positive — and every way of expressing "no value" costs either a source break, a silent behaviour change, or completeness. Zero first-party production consumers, which does **not** authorise a source break. `docs/CoreEnvironmentEmptyValuePlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-107](modules/core/src/System/Environment.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-107--medium--getcurrentdirectory-loses-valid-paths-longer-than-its-fixed-4-kib-buffer) | medium | remediated | `Environment.cpp`, `EnvironmentTests.cpp` | One fixed 4 KiB `getcwd` buffer converted a real 4,866-byte current directory into an empty string. Reproduced independently before the fix (`build-probe/2239_probe1_before.log`): a 4,868-byte current directory, built from 200-byte components with relative `mkdir`/`chdir` so no pathname argument approaches `PATH_MAX`, returned length 0 while a dynamically sized `getcwd` returned all 4,868 bytes. Ticket #2240 (slice #2239) grows the POSIX buffer on `ERANGE` and gives the Windows branch Win32's documented `GetCurrentDirectoryA(0, nullptr)` sizing call; a 1 MiB runaway ceiling replaces the 4 KiB path ceiling, and **every non-`ERANGE` failure still returns `""`, so the error contract does not move**. `std::filesystem::current_path()` was deliberately not adopted despite the `System::IO::Directory` sibling, because on Windows `path::string()` converts through a different narrow encoding than `GetCurrentDirectoryA`. **Premise correction on the finding's second sentence, measured:** the process-path branch's `readlink` genuinely cannot report truncation — it returns bytes written — but on Linux that defect is **unreachable**: procfs builds the `exe` link target in a page-sized buffer, so a process whose own executable path exceeds `PATH_MAX` gets `ENAMETOOLONG`, reproduced by running a helper binary from a 4,671-byte directory (`build-probe/2240_probe2.log`). Before and after, that case returns the same correct empty string; the loop is kept as defensive correctness, together with the Windows branch's previously discarded zero `GetModuleFileNameA` return, which was a real unconditional defect. Only the `GetCurrentDirectory` half is a reproduced data-loss defect. +3 tests (a POSIX >4 KiB cwd regression that unwinds with `chdir("..")`, skips cleanly and restores the cwd on every exit path; a short-cwd control; a process-path control). Mutation-checked; the `readlink` mutation is labelled **equivalent on Linux** for the reason above. No signature, `noexcept` or layout change. |
| [SR-AUD-108](modules/core/src/System/Environment.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-108--medium--commandline-simple-concatenates-arguments-and-loses-required-quoting-boundaries) | medium | remediated | `Environment.cpp`, `EnvironmentTests.cpp` | Raw space joining made arguments containing whitespace or quotes ambiguous, unlike .NET's PasteArguments quoting and escaping route. Measured before the fix (`build-probe/2239_probe1_before.log`): 16 of 18 rows wrong, half of them **round-trip** rows re-parsed with a `CommandLineToArgvW`-shaped reference parser — `prog` + `two words` came back as three arguments and `prog` + `""` lost an argument entirely. Ticket #2241 (slice #2239) implements both of .NET's rule sets from `PasteArguments.Paste(…, pasteFirstArgumentUsingArgV0Rules: true)`: argv[0] verbatim, quote-wrapped only when empty or containing whitespace; every other argument verbatim when non-empty and free of whitespace and quotes, otherwise quote-wrapped with 2n+1 backslashes before an escaped quote, 2n at the end, and `\"` for a bare quote. Whitespace is an explicit ASCII set, not locale-dependent `std::isspace`. **BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, recorded rather than migrated:** the emitted text moves for an empty argument and for one containing whitespace or a quote; everything else is byte-identical, including the two pre-existing expectations, and no in-repository consumer reads the property. +6 tests, every non-argv[0] case re-parsed to exactly the original vector; mutation-checked two ways (weakening 2n+1 to 2n fails 2 tests; never quoting argv[0] fails 1). One element is **deferred, not guessed**: argv[0]'s rules cannot represent a literal `"`, and whether .NET emits it verbatim or rejects it is not derivable from the finding with `/rv` absent. **#2242 (2026-08-17) settled that residual against the reference tree, which is available again (`docs/StandingApprovals.md` §5.1), and BOTH of #2241's answers here were wrong.** First, the question as asked: under the argv[0] rules .NET **rejects** a literal quote — `throw new ApplicationException(SR.Argv_IncludeDoubleQuote)`, "The argv[0] argument cannot include a double quote." (`PasteArguments.Windows.cs`, `Strings.resx:4049`) — it does not emit it verbatim. Second, and larger than the question: **`PasteArguments` is a partial class with two `Paste` implementations**, and `PasteArguments.Unix.cs` states in its own doc-comment that *"the rules for parsing the executable name (argv[0]) are ignored"* — it routes every argument, argv[0] included, through `AppendArgument`. Only `PasteArguments.Windows.cs` honours the flag `Environment.cs:125` passes. This port applied the argv[0] rules on every platform, so it diverged on **its own baseline platform**, not only in the corner the residual named. Both are now platform-split. The observable Linux change is one row — an argv[0] containing a quote is escaped as `"pro\"g"` instead of emitted raw — because the two rule sets agree on whitespace, backslashes and the empty name; the whole command line, argv[0] included, now round-trips through one parser. +1 test, and the residual's pin is inverted. `docs/CoreEnvironmentCompatibleSlicePlan.md` §4.2–4.3. |
| [SR-AUD-109](modules/core/include/System/Activator.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-109--medium--value-createinstance-changes-forwarded-constructor-arguments-through-braced-initialization) | medium | remediated | `Activator.hpp` | Value `CreateInstance` braces select `initializer_list` over a requested constructor, so vector `(3, 7)` became two elements `{3, 7}` while the pointer form correctly creates three sevens. Reproduced exactly. Perfect forwarding was never at fault — value category, move-only arguments, explicit constructors and constructor exception propagation are all measured identical — the defect is entirely `{}` versus `()`. **Premise correction: brace initialization is NOT the complete cause, and the repair this finding implies is a public source break.** Measured over twelve type categories, switching unconditionally to parentheses stops four categories that compile today from compiling — `std::array<int,3>(1,2,3)`, nested-aggregate brace elision, `std::vector<int>(1,2,3)` and any `initializer_list`-only type — and starts silently **accepting** narrowing conversions braces reject (`Agg(1,2.5)`, `int(2.5)`). Under the SR-AUD-063 precedent that is an approval boundary, so it was **not** implemented and no ticket is opened for it. Ticket #2266 instead forwards to a constructor only where the type has one: `is_class_v<T> && !is_aggregate_v<T> && is_constructible_v<T,Args...>` selects `T(...)`, everything else keeps `T{...}` — an aggregate has no constructor to forward to, and braces alone carry brace elision and still refuse to narrow. Repaired contract, stated exactly: **wherever both creation forms are well-formed they now construct identically**; where only the value form is, it keeps today's behaviour. That domain asymmetry is pre-existing (`make_unique` uses parentheses and already rejected all four), and option A would have "resolved" it by deleting capability. **Zero** compile-domain losses; one widening, `std::string(3,'x')`, ill-formed under braces only because the `3` is a forwarded parameter rather than a constant, which the pointer form already accepted — so it removes a value/pointer disagreement. No first-party production call site exists; all 7 pre-existing `ActivatorTests` pass unmodified. +14 tests, +1 negative fixture / 2 sites pinning that narrowing stays rejected. 4 mutations, all caught, none equivalent — including unconditional parentheses, which fails on `std::array` **and** makes both negative sites compile, the repository's own tooling measuring the source break. A SFINAE probe was written and discarded as vacuous (both overloads are unconstrained, so an ill-formed body is a hard error, not a substitution failure); the same error had made an earlier `make_unique` measurement wrong and every such claim was re-measured by real instantiation. Header-only templates: no layout, vtable, `noexcept` or symbol change. `docs/CoreActivatorConstructionPathPlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-110](modules/core/include/System/RuntimeType.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-110--medium--public-runtimetype-enum-occupies-the-name-of-an-unrelated-internal-net-reflection-class) | medium | confirmed | `RuntimeType.hpp`, `RuntimeTypeTests.cpp` | The public C++ category enum uses the name of .NET's internal sealed reflection `RuntimeType` class and exposes invented values, blocking a source-compatible type representation. **Reviewed 2026-08-12 (#2333); STILL CONFIRMED — its false-claim clause is REMEDIATED, its identity clause is `needs_user` (#2334).** **Measured consumer surface: zero.** Exactly one file includes the header — `modules/core/tests/System/RuntimeTypeTests.cpp` — whose six tests assert the six integer values; no production translation unit mentions the type (the 105 substring hits are `RuntimeTypeHandle`, unrelated). **Premise correction: the finding's stated harm is mostly moot under repository policy.** Its reason is that the collision "prevents a future reflection-compatible runtime-type port from using its source name", but `CLAUDE.md` lists reflection — `System::Type`, `System::Activator`, `Enum.GetNames/GetValues` — as **completely out of scope, a permanent deviation whose stubs are the correct end state**, so the .NET class whose name this occupies is never going to be ported and will never need the name back. What survives is the finding's second half: the type lets callers depend on a semantic surface with no .NET counterpart. **#2333 (`done`, documentation only):** both false claims are withdrawn — .NET's `System.RuntimeType` is an internal sealed **class** deriving from `TypeInfo`, not an enumeration, and the six values are not "documented internal constants used in CoreCLR" but this port's own invention — and replaced with what is true, including the absent consumer and the reflection-deviation note. No code, no value, no test changed. **#2334 (`needs_user`):** keep, rename out of the .NET name, or remove — a public type-identity decision whose first-party cost is zero under every option and whose downstream cost cannot be seen from here. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-111](modules/core/include/System/ModuleHandle.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-111--medium--modulehandle-cannot-be-included-as-a-standalone-public-header) | medium | remediated | `ModuleHandle.hpp`, existing Batch15 tests | The public header **defined** `ResolveTypeHandle` with an incomplete `RuntimeTypeHandle` return type — forbidden by [dcl.fct]/12 for a definition, though legal for a declaration — so a translation unit whose only include was this header failed at `ModuleHandle.hpp:41`; `RuntimeTypeHandle.hpp` masked it by completing itself before including this header. Reproduced exactly. Ticket #2264 declares the method in-class and defines it after a deferred `#include "System/RuntimeTypeHandle.hpp"`, mirroring the idiom `RuntimeTypeHandle.hpp:67-73` already used for the other half of the same cycle, so **either** header may now be named first. Both are `Core.Base`, so the edge is intra-component: boundary validator unchanged at **41 modules / 92 edges**, no component dependency added, catalogue current, selective-components not required. **Premise refinement the finding does not state:** whether this was systemic was measured, not assumed — every `modules/core` public header was compiled as the sole include of its own TU, and **222 of 223 were already self-sufficient**, this header being the only exception and all five sibling handle headers clean, so it is a true singleton and no ordinary defect ticket was minted for a neighbour; after the repair the sweep reads **223/223**. Regression coverage is `ModuleHandleStandaloneIncludeTests.cpp`, whose first include — ahead of `<gtest/gtest.h>` — is this header, with three `static_assert`s and six tests; 4 mutations, 3 caught (revert the in-class body, hoist the deferred include above the class, delete the deferred include) and 1 **equivalent by design** (restore the masking order), which is precisely why `Batch15TypesTests.cpp` could never observe the fault. Behaviour, layout and symbols diffed from one probe source against both revisions: `sizeof`/`alignof` 1/1, message byte-identical, `noexcept` surface unchanged, `ModuleHandle` symbol table identical. Purely additive to the set of TUs that compile. `docs/CoreModuleHandleHeaderSelfSufficiencyPlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-112](modules/core/include/System/ArgIterator.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-112--medium--argiterator-tests-invoke-non-static-methods-on-storage-that-is-not-an-argiterator-object) | medium | remediated | `ArgIterator.hpp`, `Batch12ArgHandleTests.cpp` | Five tests cast raw aligned character storage to ArgIterator without starting its lifetime, then invoke member functions; the green unsupported-feature tests therefore rely on undefined C++ behavior. **REMEDIATED (#2274 review, #2275, 2026-08-11, `docs/CoreArgIteratorTestLifetimePlan.md`).** Reproduced exactly. **Premise refinement: six tests, not five, holding seven fabricated objects** — `Equals_ReturnsFalse` builds two. The cause was measured rather than assumed: declaring the two `[[noreturn]]` constructors suppresses the implicit default one, so `is_default_constructible_v` is `false` and there is no ordinary route to an instance at all — which is why the fixture reached for storage. A legitimate route exists and needs **no production change**: `std::bit_cast<ArgIterator>(static_cast<unsigned char>(0))` is constrained only on trivial copyability and equal size — both measured true, `sizeof` 1, `is_empty_v` true — and **returns an object**, so the missing lifetime actually begins. All seven fabrications became one such object each with **every pre-existing assertion retained verbatim**, +4 tests pinning what the mechanism depends on so that invalidating it fails loudly. 3 mutations, all caught, and giving `ArgIterator` state is caught **at compile time** — the loud failure the design claims. **Sanitizers measured non-discriminating**: an ASan+UBSan reproduction of the fixture's exact shape completes with no diagnostic and exit 0, because UBSan's lifetime-adjacent checks need a polymorphic type or a known allocation and ASan sees an in-bounds access to a legally sized stack buffer — running them for ceremony would have argued the opposite of the truth. The residual this report raises separately — no public construction can succeed, so the instance members are unreachable and "need a deliberate static or unsupported-operation design" — is **#2276 (`needs_user`)**, is documented in the header, and is deliberately **not** absorbed into this finding's frozen text, which is about the fixture's access route. |
| [SR-AUD-113](modules/core/include/System/ThreadStaticAttribute.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-113--medium--threadstaticattribute-has-no-attachment-or-storage-mechanism-and-cannot-provide-its-documented-per-thread-field-value) | medium | remediated | `ThreadStaticAttribute.hpp`, `ThreadStaticAttributeTests.cpp` | The marker has no C++ attachment syntax, registry, or `thread_local` mechanism, so it cannot isolate a static field per thread while tests assert only object construction/inheritance. **Remediated (#2287 review, #2288 implementation, 2026-08-11) by the repair this finding itself names** - "Unlike the STA/MTA headers, this file also does not state that the marker has no C++ runtime effect" - which is checkable and was checked: `STAThreadAttribute.hpp` says "carries no data and has no effect in the C++ port", `MTAThreadAttribute.hpp` matches, and this header said the opposite ("Indicates that the value of a static field is unique for each thread"). Implementing the promise is **impossible, not merely expensive**: attaching an object of a class to a declaration is not something C++ can express, so no registry or `thread_local` plumbing inside this class can reach the field a caller wanted isolated. The header now states that it carries no data and has no effect, that nothing in the repository reads it, that C++ has no attachment mechanism, that `thread_local` on the declaration is the facility which does what the .NET attribute describes (two-line migration shown), and that the class exists so ported code naming it still compiles - the silent contract break is now an explicit boundary. **Nothing in the compiled surface changed** - no member, base, `final`, constructor or include, `sizeof` still equals `sizeof(Attribute)`, vtable inherited and untouched, no executable statement changed; `final` was deliberately **not** added because it would forbid derivation that compiles today. Consumer inventory re-measured: **zero** production consumers, three test files, five cases. **Two tests added, none retired**, answering both of the report's missing-assertion bullets: `CarriesNoDataBeyondTheAttributeBase` pins `sizeof(ThreadStaticAttribute) == sizeof(Attribute)` and `is_base_of_v` - the mutation-sensitive pin, tripping on exactly the shape a future "implementation" attempt would take, and adding `int slotIndex_ = -1;` failed this case and **only** this case; `MarkerDoesNotIsolateStorageButThreadLocalDoes` supplies the concurrent static-field isolation case (a `static` and a `thread_local` counter across one joined thread; the static is shared at 6, the `thread_local` is not, this thread still reading 5) and is **labelled in the source as a language-boundary demonstration rather than a behaviour pin**, so it is not counted as a caught mutation. **Not a family with SR-AUD-117**, ranked beside it: their causes are opposite in the respect that decides the repair - this finding exists because C++ offers **no** mechanism, SR-AUD-117 because C++ offers **exactly** the mechanism (`[[deprecated]]`) and the port declined to use it, at a cost that breaks builds. **The genuine family candidate is SR-AUD-115** (`ObsoleteAttribute` "cannot mark a declaration or produce its documented compiler diagnostic") - the same mechanism, an `Attribute`-derived class in a language with no attribute attachment, with a different promised effect. Recorded, not acted on: it is outside this batch, its header carries SR-AUD-116 too, and minting a CCF over the pair needs authority this batch does not have. `docs/CoreMarkerAttributeAndDeprecationPlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-114](modules/core/include/System/Attribute.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-114--medium--attribute-is-constructible-and-uses-identity-equality-rather-than-nets-abstract-fieldwise-attribute-contract) | medium | confirmed | `Attribute.hpp`, `SystemAttributeTests.cpp` | The publicly constructible base hashes and compares object addresses, so equal payload attributes compare unequal where current .NET's abstract base uses same-type fieldwise equality and hash semantics. **Reviewed 2026-08-12 (#2339); STILL CONFIRMED, nothing implemented — `needs_user`.** **It had no owner at all** until this ticket: the only references were #1766 (the discovering audit) and #2317 (the triage umbrella, which listed it as claimed but never reviewed it), so closing #2317 would have orphaned it — found by re-running the strict ownership analysis. **Measured consumer surface, and it is why this is not a smaller SR-AUD-068: 46 types in the repository derive from `Attribute` and not one overrides `Equals`** (measured by searching every public header for a `bool Equals(const Attribute&` declaration, which occurs only in the base), so all 46 inherit the identity fallback and the finding's `CLSCompliantAttribute(true)` example is real. SR-AUD-068's `ValueType` has **zero** production derivations. The base is also instantiated directly in nine places in `SystemAttributeTests.cpp`, pinning both the instantiability and the fallback. **Not autonomous, on the same two grounds as #2322:** field-by-field `Equals` and a first-field hash are reflection — a `CLAUDE.md` permanent deviation, and in C++ a base class cannot enumerate a derived class's fields at all — and every way of making the incompatible default unreachable (protected constructor, matching .NET's own; or an abstract class) is a **public source break** that retires those nine tests. Options A/B/C priced in #2339. **Observation, not part of the decision:** `GetHashCode` is declared `int` rather than `intcs` (rule 7), the same type on this platform. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-115](modules/core/include/System/ObsoleteAttribute.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-115--medium--obsoleteattribute-stores-an-error-flag-but-cannot-mark-a-declaration-or-produce-its-documented-compiler-diagnostic) | medium | remediated | `ObsoleteAttribute.hpp`, `ObsoleteAttributeTests.cpp` | The ordinary C++ object has no declaration attachment or compiler attribute, so setting `IsError` cannot make use of any symbol a compile-time error. **Remediated (#2293 review, #2294 implementation, 2026-08-11).** The finding reproduces exactly as filed, measured under this repository's own `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` plus `-Wpedantic`: a `const ObsoleteAttribute a("Use modern() instead.", true);` describing `legacy()` draws **no diagnostic** and leaves `legacy()` callable, while `[[deprecated("...")]]` on the same declaration produces `error: ... is deprecated ... [-Werror=deprecated-declarations]`. This is the **same mechanism boundary as SR-AUD-113**, exactly as #2287 predicted, and it is impossible rather than merely expensive: attaching an object of a class to a declaration has no C++ spelling, so nothing inside this class can reach the declaration a caller wanted marked. The header now states that constructing one deprecates nothing, that no code in this repository reads any of the four values, that `[[deprecated]]` on the declaration is the C++ facility (migration shown), and that .NET's `sealed` and `AttributeUsage` target/inheritance restrictions are not expressible where there is no declaration to carry them. **Premise correction that outlives the repair:** the warning/error choice is **not a property of the declaration** - one `[[deprecated]]` declaration is an error under `-Werror` and a warning under `-Wno-error=deprecated-declarations`, so `IsError`, whose whole purpose is to choose between the two, has no C++ counterpart **even if #2289 is approved**. **This finding does not run through #2289**, tested rather than assumed: `ObsoleteAttribute` is not one of that ticket's five prose-deprecated declarations but the class carrying the metadata, and neither outcome changes anything here; **no `[[deprecated]]` was added to any production declaration**. **Nothing in the compiled surface changed** - no member, base, `final`, constructor, setter or include; `sizeof` still 112, `alignof` 8; vtable inherited; no executable statement changed; `final` deliberately **not** added because it would reject derivation that compiles today. Consumers measured: **zero** production (corroborated physically - the header edit recompiled exactly two translation units, both test files), 2 test files, 14 cases; the header is public in `Core.Base`, so that zero licenses nothing. **Four tests added, none retired.** `CarriesItsFourDeclaredMembersAndNoSideChannel` is the mutation-sensitive pin (`sizeof` equals the declared members rounded to `alignof`, computed not hardcoded): adding `void* registry_ = nullptr;` failed this case and **only** this case, all six pre-existing included - stated honestly, a further `bool` would fit the measured seven padding bytes and would not trip it. `AttributeObjectDeprecatesNothingButDeprecatedDoes` supplies the report's missing "never compile a deprecated declaration" case and is **labelled in the source as a language-boundary demonstration, not a behaviour pin**, so it is not counted as a caught mutation; the counted mutation is that deleting its scoped `-Wdeprecated-declarations` suppression fails the build with the diagnostic it claims. `CopyAndMovePreserveEveryComponent` and `TextComponentsAreByteTransparent` answer the copy/move and UTF-8 bullets. "Distinguish warning from error" is documented rather than tested, because per the premise correction it is a compiler flag and not anything this port can express or observe. **Not a family with SR-AUD-116** on the same header: that one is a representation boundary whose repair is possible and costly, this one a mechanism boundary whose repair is impossible and free - the SR-AUD-113 / SR-AUD-117 split one file later. No CCF minted. `docs/CoreObsoleteAttributeBoundaryPlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-116](modules/core/include/System/ObsoleteAttribute.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-116--medium--obsoleteattribute-collapses-nullable-net-string-properties-into-indistinguishable-empty-strings) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `ObsoleteAttribute.hpp`, `ObsoleteAttributeTests.cpp` | Non-nullable `std::string` fields erase .NET's observable null versus explicitly empty `Message`, `DiagnosticId`, and `UrlFormat` states. **Design complete, approval-bound (#2293 review, #2295 `needs_user`, 2026-08-11); still open.** The finding reproduces exactly as filed - `ObsoleteAttribute def;` and `ObsoleteAttribute empty(std::string{});` yield `getMessageProperty()` values that compare equal, and no state in the class can record "never supplied". The boundary also exists **on the way in**, which the finding does not say: the message constructor and both setters take `const std::string&`, so a caller can neither supply an absent value nor return a component to that state. **Options priced by probe, not argument** - both candidate shapes were built and the four call shapes now in the suites compiled against them. **A, `optional<string>`:** distinguishes the states, `sizeof` 112 -> **136**, and **three of the four existing call shapes stop compiling** - `.empty()` (written twice in the current suites), binding to `const std::string&`, and passing to a function taking `const std::string&`; only equality against a literal survives, so any downstream using a component as a string breaks. **B, presence flags beside `isError_`:** distinguishes the states, purely additive, no source break, and `sizeof` stays **112** because the flags fit the measured seven padding bytes - but it ships a shape .NET does not have in a public header, permanently. **C, documentation only:** cannot close the finding. Which of faithful-to-.NET and compatible-with-callers wins is a project decision, so **no representation was selected**; zero first-party production consumers does not license the break in A. Taken anyway because true under every outcome: the header now states that .NET's three properties are nullable, that this port cannot tell an absent value from an empty one, and that .NET's parameterless attribute leaves `Message` null - which **does not close the finding**, since the states are still collapsed. No test was added pinning the collapse, deliberately, and the two pre-existing `.empty()` assertions were left as they are rather than deepened. **Not a family with SR-AUD-115**; no CCF minted. `docs/CoreObsoleteAttributeBoundaryPlan.md` §5. |
| [SR-AUD-117](modules/core/include/System/LoaderOptimization.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-117--low--deprecated-loaderoptimization-values-have-documentation-only-deprecation-and-emit-no-c-compiler-diagnostic) | low | confirmed (design-complete) | `LoaderOptimization.hpp`, `LoaderOptimizationTests.cpp` | `DomainMask` and `DisallowBindings` use Doxygen deprecation prose rather than `[[deprecated]]`, so their use compiles silently unlike current .NET's Obsolete diagnostics. **DESIGN-COMPLETE, APPROVAL-BOUND (#2287 review/design, 2026-08-11, `docs/CoreMarkerAttributeAndDeprecationPlan.md` §5; #2289 `needs_user`).** The finding **reproduces exactly as filed**. **Premise correction - it is not a two-value singleton.** Measured across every production header, the "deprecated in prose, silent to the compiler" pattern is **five sites in three files across two modules, in two declaration shapes**: `LoaderOptimization::DomainMask`, `LoaderOptimization::DisallowBindings`, `AppDomain::GetCurrentThreadId()` (a member function), `CultureTypes::WindowsOnlyCultures` and `CultureTypes::FrameworkCultures`; and there is **no `[[deprecated]]` anywhere in this repository**. The real question is therefore repository-wide policy - does this port map .NET `Obsolete` onto C++ `[[deprecated]]`, and if so at all five sites? - so #2289 is scoped to all five, because deprecating two enumerators alone leaves the port inconsistent with itself. **Measured, not assumed** (throwaway `-fsyntax-only` probe under the repository's own `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` plus `-Wpedantic`, deleted once transcribed): the attribute must follow the enumerator name (`DomainMask [[deprecated("…")]] = 3,`; the leading spelling is a **hard syntax error**); the declaration itself draws no diagnostic; **a use is an error, not a warning** - `error: '…::DomainMask' is deprecated: … [-Werror=deprecated-declarations]`; and deprecation is per **name**, not per value, so `MultiDomainHost` at the same underlying `3` stays silent and the alias can be deprecated without touching the live spelling. That third point is the approval boundary, measured rather than feared: any consumer naming either enumerator and building warnings-as-errors - exactly what this repository does, and what `CLAUDE.md` rule 1 requires - stops compiling. Zero production consumers of the two names (**0** production sites; **9** sites in one test file) does not license it, because downstream consumers exist and this batch may not inspect them. **Selected repair recorded for #2289:** all five sites to `[[deprecated("…")]]` beside the retained prose, the one in-repo test file migrated with the `#pragma GCC diagnostic push/ignored/pop` pattern this repository's tests already use twice, and a `test/consumer/*_negative.cpp` fixture with one site per deprecated name - precisely the "compiler warning-as-error consumer" this report asks for. Deprecating only the two named values was rejected: same breakage, three sites of the identical pattern left behind. **What was done anyway:** the header now states in a `@warning` that the tags are prose rather than a compiler diagnostic, that .NET's `Obsolete` does produce one, that `[[deprecated]]` is measured to make every use a hard error under `-Werror` and therefore needs a decision, and that #2289 covers the other three sites - but **no declaration changed, so a caller still receives no diagnostic and the finding is not closed**. **Not a family with SR-AUD-113**, ranked beside it: shared shape, not shared cause, and opposite in the respect that decides the repair - that finding exists because C++ offers **no** mechanism, this one because C++ offers **exactly** the mechanism and the port declined to use it. No CCF minted. |
| [SR-AUD-118](modules/core/src/System/Delegate.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-118--medium--delegate-composition-accepts-different-concrete-delegate-types-and-converts-every-combinedremoval-result-into-base-delegate) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `Delegate.cpp`, `Delegate.hpp`, `MulticastDelegate.hpp` | Combine/Remove accept different dynamic delegate classes and construct a base Delegate, rather than rejecting mismatch and preserving the concrete multicast delegate type. **DESIGN-COMPLETE, GATED TWICE (#2270 review, 2026-08-11, `docs/CoreDelegateCompositionContractPlan.md`).** Reproduced exactly as filed — `combined_dynamic_type_preserved=0`, `mixed_types_accepted=1`, removal result also base `Delegate` — with no premise correction needed. **The finding's two halves are one coupled repair, measured rather than assumed:** a same-type guard alone would reject step two of `Combine(Combine(a,b),c)`, because step one already returned a base `Delegate` while the third operand is still a `MulticastDelegate` (`chained_step2_operand_types_equal=0`) — the exact shape of two currently green fixtures. Type checking is therefore coherent only *after* results preserve their type, and preserving type needs a representation this port does not have: **route A** a new virtual factory on a public polymorphic base — vtable change, hence ABI break, plus silent degradation for any subclass that does not override it; **route B** reuse of the existing public virtual `Clone()` — no vtable change, but `Combine` would invoke user-overridable code and copy the left operand's subclass state into the result; **route C** the status quo the finding rejects. On top of that, mismatch rejection is **behaviour-incompatible in the tightening direction** — `Combine` calls that succeed today would throw `ArgumentException` — the same class as SR-AUD-178/#2269. **#2271 (`needs_user`)** carries both gates, and also carries one adjacent consequence that is *not* separately implementable and is deliberately **not** absorbed into this finding's frozen text: `Equals` is type-blind as well (`cross_type_single_equals=1`) where .NET's `Equals` begins with `InternalEqualTypes`. **SR-AUD-119 and SR-AUD-120 are not part of this cause** — they were reviewed alongside it and split into #2272/#2273; no CCF is minted, since a shared file is adjacency, not causation (the SR-AUD-177/178 precedent). |
| [SR-AUD-119](modules/core/src/System/Delegate.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-119--medium--delegate-equality-compares-multicast-entries-by-shared-pointer-identity-instead-of-delegate-equality) | medium | remediated | `Delegate.cpp`, `MulticastDelegateTests.cpp` | Independently allocated equal free-function delegates make logically equal invocation lists compare false because lists use pointer identity rather than each entry's Delegate.Equals. **REMEDIATED (#2270 review, #2272, 2026-08-11, `docs/CoreDelegateCompositionContractPlan.md` §5).** Reproduced exactly, and refined by one measurement the finding does not state: the **entries already answered `Equals` correctly** (`entry_pairs_equal=1 1`) — the single-target value-equality path has existed since ticket 345 and only the list loop declined to use it, so this is a wrong comparison *primitive*, not a missing policy, and the repair is one loop body. `Equals` now compares `la[i]` against `lb[i]` with `Equals`, matching CoreCLR's `EqualInvocationLists`; it is a **strict widening**, because pointer-identical entries short-circuit through `Equals`'s own `this == &other` fast path, so no pair equal today can become unequal. Measured after: `equal_function_lists_equal` and `..._hash_equal` both `0 → 1`, while `different_lists_equal`, `reordered_lists_equal` and `lambda_lists_equal` stay `0` and `same_pointer_lists_equal` stays `1`. The finding's own coupling note is honoured in the same change — `GetHashCode` folds each entry's **hash code** rather than its address, or the widened equality would have broken the hash contract the single-target path already honours; the mixing function is deliberately untouched, and an entry with no comparable target still hashes as its own address, so **lambda-entry multicast hashes are unchanged**. Deliberately not fixed here: `Equals` still ignores concrete types (.NET's `InternalEqualTypes`), which belongs to SR-AUD-118/#2271 and is unreachable across types anyway, since every multicast this port creates has dynamic type `System::Delegate`. +13 tests; 2 mutations, both caught; no signature, layout, vtable, `noexcept` or symbol change. **Not a family with SR-AUD-118 or SR-AUD-120** (#2270 §1). |
| [SR-AUD-120](modules/core/src/System/Delegate.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-120--medium--delegateremove-cannot-remove-a-multicast-values-last-matching-invocation-list-subsequence) | medium | remediated | `Delegate.cpp`, `DelegateTests.cpp` | Remove scans one entry at a time and cannot remove a multi-entry value's final matching subsequence; RemoveAll inherits the behavior. **REMEDIATED (#2270 review, #2273, 2026-08-11, `docs/CoreDelegateCompositionContractPlan.md` §6).** Reproduced exactly, and the *reason* the entry loop cannot match was measured rather than assumed: a single-target entry compared against a multi-entry value is rejected by `Equals` on length alone, so the answer is always "not found". That also fixes the repair's regression surface at **empty** — every reachable multicast-value removal shape returns the source unchanged today, including `remove_same_pointer_subsequence_size=4`, where the source's entries are the *identical* `shared_ptr`s the value holds, so the new branch can only fire where the current answer is "unchanged" and **no existing outcome can change**. `Remove` now branches on whether the value is itself multicast, as `MulticastDelegate.RemoveImpl` does, scanning candidate start positions from the last one backwards and deleting the **last** matching contiguous subsequence. Measured after: `remove_multicast_subsequence_size` and `remove_same_pointer_subsequence_size` both `4 → 2`, `remove_entire_list_null` `0 → 1`, `remove_leaving_one_size`/`is_a` `3/0 → 1/1`, `removeall_subsequence_size`/`null` `4/0 → 0/1` — so `RemoveAll` inherits the fix exactly as the finding says it inherits the fault — while `longer_value_unchanged` and the entire single-target path stay put. Boundaries: a longer value leaves the source unchanged, emptying the list returns `nullptr`, and a one-entry remainder is returned as that entry itself, the convention the existing single-entry path already used. The `vl.size() > sl.size()` guard is load-bearing (without it the unsigned start index underflows); its boundary is pinned by a mutation rather than by removing it, which would be undefined behaviour rather than a measurement. **Independent of SR-AUD-118**: the multi-entry result uses the same base-`Delegate` construction the existing removal path already used, so no new type-loss debt is added against #2271. +17 tests; 4 mutations executed and all caught, 1 documented as deliberately not executed; no signature, layout, vtable, `noexcept` or symbol change. |
| [SR-AUD-121](modules/core/include/System/EventHandler.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-121--medium--eventhandler-stores-an-empty-handler-and-defers-failure-to-stdbad_function_call-during-raise) | medium | remediated | `EventHandler.hpp`, `EventHandlerTests.cpp` | An empty `std::function` is stored and later invoked as `std::bad_function_call`, whereas adding a null .NET event delegate is a no-op. |
| [SR-AUD-122](modules/core/include/System/EventHandler.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-122--medium--eventhandler-exposes-event-arguments-as-const-and-cannot-represent-net-handlers-that-mutate-their-event-data-object) | medium | confirmed | `EventHandler.hpp`, `EventHandlerTests.cpp` | The callback's `const TEventArgs&` rejects a handler needing mutable event data even though .NET EventHandler passes a mutable event object. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2324); STILL CONFIRMED, nothing implemented — `needs_user`.** Live: `using HandlerType = std::function<void(Object* sender, const TEventArgs& e)>` (`EventHandler.hpp:88`). **Premise correction: the break is on the raise side, not the subscriber side.** Dropping the `const` is asymmetric — existing subscribers taking `const TEventArgs&` keep compiling, because a `T&` argument binds to a `const T&` parameter; what stops compiling is every **raiser** that passes a `const` event-args object to `Raise()`/`Invoke()`, and the args object must become non-`const` there. That is a public source break in a template used by shipped types: `System::Timers::Timer::Elapsed` is `EventHandler<ElapsedEventArgs>`, and `ObservableCollection`, `ReadOnlyObservableCollection` and `XObject` carry aliases of the same shape. The .NET behaviour named (a subscriber mutating event data for acknowledgement/cancellation) is real, but enabling it changes a published API's const-correctness contract. Options priced in #2324. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-123](modules/core/include/System/ResolveEventHandler.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-123--medium--resolveeventhandler-has-no-representation-for-nets-null-not-resolved-result) | medium | confirmed | `ResolveEventHandler.hpp`, `SystemEventTests.cpp` | A required `std::string` result cannot represent .NET's nullable Assembly “not resolved” outcome or distinguish it from an empty string without an undocumented sentinel. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2325); STILL CONFIRMED, nothing implemented — `needs_user`.** Live: `std::function<std::string(void*, ResolveEventArgs&)>`, a total function into `std::string`, while empty already means "absent requesting assembly" elsewhere in `ResolveEventArgs`, so empty cannot also mean "unresolved". **Measured consumer surface: none** — outside its own header the alias appears only in `Batch4Tests.cpp`, and it is **not wired to any `AppDomain` resolve API** because those are stubs under SR-AUD-103. Adding the missing state is a **public representation change** to a published alias: `std::optional<std::string>` breaks every handler's return statement, a documented sentinel cannot be enforced by the type (which is the defect itself), and leaving it means documenting the reduction. Which is right depends on whether the assembly-resolution surface will ever be more than a stub, so it is sequenced behind SR-AUD-103 rather than fixing the signature of a delegate nothing calls. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-124](modules/core/include/System/ApplicationId.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-124--medium--applicationid-replaces-binarynull-aware-identity-fields-with-undocumented-strings-and-skips-required-name-validation) | medium | confirmed | `ApplicationId.hpp`, `ApplicationIdTests.cpp` | Mandatory strings replace a cloned byte[] token and nullable components without an encoding/sentinel policy, and an empty name is accepted instead of rejected. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2290); STILL CONFIRMED - approval boundary recorded (#2291, needs_user).** **A genuine pair with SR-AUD-125**, unlike the adjacency slices earlier in the same batch, and the dependency runs one way: SR-AUD-125's faithful repair needs this finding's representation decision, so it was deliberately not split out. **Both findings reproduce exactly as filed; no premise correction was needed** - worth recording because the two slices before this one each corrected something. Consumer inventory measured: **zero** production consumers (`ApplicationIdentity` is a different type that neither includes nor mentions this one); two test files, 18 cases, in two **different** executables, so the shared suite name is not the one-binary duplication seen on `Void` and `UnitySerializationHolder`. **Routes priced, none selected - the selection is the decision.** Token: a `byte[]`-shaped parameter and getter (source break on the constructor and the getter), an uppercase-hex contract on the existing `std::string` (no signature change but the two tokens the tests use are not hex, so it is a behaviour break dressed as documentation), or opaque text (no break, closes nothing). Optional components: `std::optional` (source break on two getters and the constructor), added predicates (additive, but the constructor still cannot receive the distinction), or one documented representation (no break, closes nothing). **The separable third half - rejecting an empty name - needs no representation decision**, uses the port's existing `ArgumentException::ThrowIfNullOrEmpty` so no message would be invented, and needs no in-repo migration; it was still left to #2291 because it turns an input that currently succeeds into a runtime throw for every consumer while closing nothing, this finding being a conjunction. **What was done anyway:** the header now states that the token is text stored verbatim with **no encoding applied or assumed** so binary key material cannot be round-tripped, that there is **no null/empty distinction** (the empty string represents both), that the name is **not validated**, and what `ToString`/`GetHashCode` actually promise - **no signature, stored type, emitted text or validation changed, so the finding is not closed**. **No test was added**: every assertion the report asks for is an assertion about the disputed model and each would be retired by whichever option is approved (the #2281 reason). A separate latent defect found while reviewing - `GetHashCode()` is `noexcept` yet calls `Version::ToString()`, which allocates, so an allocation failure calls `std::terminate` - is ticket #2292 with no `SR-AUD-*` identifier. No CCF minted; **not** marked design-complete, because the routes were priced but not selected. `docs/CoreApplicationIdIdentityModelDesign.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-125](modules/core/include/System/ApplicationId.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-125--medium--applicationidtostring-omits-the-public-key-token-and-uses-a-different-identity-grammar) | medium | confirmed | `ApplicationId.hpp`, `ApplicationIdTests.cpp` | ToString drops the token and differs from .NET's quoted lowercase optional identity grammar, so unequal token values can serialize identically. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2290); STILL CONFIRMED - approval boundary recorded (#2291, needs_user).** **Not independently compatible, and that is measured rather than assumed:** .NET writes `publicKeyToken` as **uppercase hex bytes**, which only has meaning if the token is a `byte[]` - while it is an encoding-less `std::string`, the raw text, hex of the storage bytes and hex of a decoded value are three different strings and nothing in the port says which the caller supplied - and .NET **omits components that are null**, which only has meaning if Culture and ProcessorArchitecture can *be* null, which this port cannot express. Both prerequisites are SR-AUD-124, so the two are held under one decision; splitting this one out would mean either changing the emitted text without reaching parity (a break that buys nothing) or waiting for #2291 anyway. The dependency is one-way - SR-AUD-124 can be decided without touching `ToString` - which is why #2291 separates the options. Finding reproduces exactly as filed: `publicKeyToken_` does not appear in the `ToString` expression, and the emitted grammar is `<name>, Version=<v>, Culture=<c>, ProcessorArchitecture=<a>` with unquoted capitalized keys and both optional components always present. **What was done anyway:** `ToString`'s comment now says it is a counterpart **in role only**, spells out both grammars, and states the consequence plainly - *two ApplicationIds differing only by token produce identical text, so this string does not identify an ApplicationId and must not be used as a manifest identity or an equality proxy*, while `Equals` compares all five fields and does distinguish them. **The emitted text is unchanged, so the finding is not closed.** No test was added, because every assertion the report asks for (exact text, token distinction, optional omission, escaping, byte-to-hex) pins the disputed grammar and would be retired by whichever option is approved. No CCF minted; **not** marked design-complete, because the routes were priced but not selected. `docs/CoreApplicationIdIdentityModelDesign.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-126](modules/core/include/System/Func.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-126--medium--func-and-converter-permit-void-result-specializations-that-collapse-the-separate-net-action-category) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `Func.hpp`, `Converter.hpp`, their direct tests | Unconstrained result aliases accept `Func<void>` and `Converter<T, void>`, making them type-identical to `Action` forms that .NET deliberately keeps separate because `void` cannot be a generic argument. **Design complete, approval-bound (#2296 review, #2299 `needs_user`, 2026-08-11); still open.** **The report's second prescription is structurally impossible**: "preventing APIs that require .NET parity from accepting the substitute aliases" cannot be satisfied by any alias-based design, since an alias introduces no type - there is only one type, so no declaration can accept `Action` while rejecting `Func<void>`; preserving the category would mean replacing every alias with a distinct class type, a whole-API break. **The first prescription is mechanically available, measured:** a constrained alias-template parameter (`template<NonVoid R> using Func = std::function<R()>;`) is legal C++23 here and makes `Func<void>` fail with `error: template constraint failure ... constraints not satisfied` - and it is a **compile-domain public source break** at every `Func<void>`/`Converter<T, void>` downstream, across all 17 `Func` aliases and both `Converter` declarations. Consumers measured: **zero** production sites name the `Func`/`FuncT*` aliases and **zero** sites anywhere spell a `void` result, which licenses nothing since the headers are public in `Core.Base`. Measured and **not** a defect: `Converter` is declared identically in `System/Converter.hpp` and `System/Action.hpp`, and including both in one translation unit compiles clean under `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`. **Taken anyway (#2300), true under every outcome:** `Func.hpp` gains the arity-spelling note the report asks for, the `std::bad_function_call` note and a `void` warning carrying the structural reason above; `Converter.hpp` the matching warning and `Action.hpp` a cross-reference. **This does not close the finding** - `Func<void>` still compiles. **Five tests added, none retired**, closing three of the four "other missing assertions" bullets - arities 5-7 and 9-15, empty-target `std::bad_function_call`, target-exception propagation and reference-result preservation; dropping `T11` from `FuncT11`'s signature is caught at compile time by the arity case and by nothing else. **No test asserts `Func<void>` is `Action`**, deliberately, since #2299 may remove it. **Not a family with SR-AUD-128 or SR-AUD-129** - three causes, not one; no CCF minted. `docs/CoreMarshalSlotAndFuncShapePlan.md` §4. |
| [SR-AUD-127](modules/core/include/System/CrashReason.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-127--medium--crashreason-publishes-a-private-nested-nativeaot-implementation-enum-as-a-public-system-api) | medium | remediated | `CrashReason.hpp`, `CrashReasonTests.cpp` | A public top-level `System::CrashReason` copies values from the internal nested NativeAOT `CrashInfo.CrashReason`, reserves an unsupported public name, and has no first-party production consumer. **Remediated (#2279 review, #2280 implementation, 2026-08-11) by this finding's own second alternative**, taken deliberately: the first alternative - moving the enum into an internal implementation - withdraws a published, independently includable `System::` header from the `Core.Base` include tree, a public source break and an approval boundary this batch does not cross. The finding's substantive complaints are both statements about what the header says (it "promises stable consumer access where .NET intentionally makes none" and "provides no visibility or NativeAOT-only diagnostic explaining why an internal runtime concept is exposed from Core.Base") and both are now false: the doc-comment states that .NET publishes no `System.CrashReason`, that the counterpart is the `internal` nested `System.CrashInfo.CrashReason`, that this enumeration is published on the project's own authority so a later change to .NET's internal enum is not by itself a defect here, that there is no first-party production consumer so the constants are a porting vocabulary rather than a runtime contract, why the type stays published, and that the enumeration is not closed over its underlying type. **Nothing in the compiled surface changed** - no enumerator, value, underlying type, name or include - so there is no ABI, layout, vtable, `noexcept`, symbol or component-dependency consequence. Consumer inventory re-measured, not inherited: zero production consumers, one test file. The "no invalid-value or full pairwise-distinctness vector" observation was accurate - the five old tests check each enumerator once and compare **two of the six** pairs - so 4 tests were added (5 -> 9, none retired): all six pairs, contiguity from zero, the `int` underlying type and scopedness, and an unlisted-value vector for `4` and `-1`. Two mutations, both caught. **Not a family with SR-AUD-137**, which was ranked beside it: they share a characteristic (a .NET-internal type republished publicly with no first-party consumer), not a cause - this one is republished verbatim, that one with a different shape plus a false source-compatibility claim, and SR-AUD-137 has no available compatible repair (#2281, needs_user). No CCF minted; the same characteristic also runs through SR-AUD-124/125/126/128/129/136. `docs/CoreCrashReasonAndUnityHolderPlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-128](modules/core/include/System/MarshalByRefObject.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-128--medium--marshalbyrefobject-is-constructible-and-omits-the-remaining-public-remoting-api) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `MarshalByRefObject.hpp`, `Batch3TypeTests.cpp` | The C++ base is directly constructible and omits .NET's abstract/protected shape plus its obsolete throwing remoting members, while a green test asserts the invalid construction succeeds. **Design complete, approval-bound (#2296 review, #2297 `needs_user`, 2026-08-11); still open.** Both halves reproduce, and it is a **conjunction with three separate compatibility costs**, which is why nothing was selected. **Premise correction: the "no first-party production consumer" grouping is false for this finding** - **two** production consumers derive from the class, `AppDomain` (which is `final`) and `ContextBoundObject`. A protected constructor breaks **two in-repo construction sites in two different executables** - `Batch3TypeTests.cpp:90` and `Task42Tests.cpp:1452` - plus any downstream site, uninspectable here. Adding the three members is additive in source, but `InitializeLifetimeService()` is **virtual**, adding a vtable slot to this class *and* to both derived classes; `GetLifetimeService()` is `[Obsolete]` in .NET, colliding with the still-undecided #2289; and `MemberwiseClone(bool)` has **no base to overload** - measured, `System::Object` declares no `MemberwiseClone` at all - so it would be an invention rather than a port. The report's rename alternative is a wider break still, since both derived classes and every downstream base-specifier change. **Taken anyway (#2300), true under every outcome:** the header now states the constructibility divergence and its measured in-repo cost, that the three members are absent rather than unimplemented, that .NET retains two of them specifically so a caller receives `PlatformNotSupportedException`, and that restoring the virtual one is a vtable change for three classes. **This does not close the finding.** No test was added constructing the base beyond the two that already exist, and none rejecting construction, since #2297 may make that a hard error. **Not a family with SR-AUD-129 or SR-AUD-126**; no CCF minted. `docs/CoreMarshalSlotAndFuncShapePlan.md` §2. |
| [SR-AUD-129](modules/core/include/System/LocalDataStoreSlot.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-129--medium--localdatastoreslot-stores-one-shared-stdany-and-has-no-thread-local-slot-api) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `LocalDataStoreSlot.hpp`, `LocalDataStoreSlotTests.cpp` | A default-constructible per-object `std::any` replaces .NET's internally ThreadLocal-backed slot, and no C++ Thread allocate/get/set/named-slot API exists; a child write overwrites the parent's value. **Design complete, approval-bound (#2296 review, #2298 `needs_user`, 2026-08-11); still open.** Reproduces as filed, and the "no `Thread` slot API at all" observation was re-measured and holds: .NET's constructor is *internal* and its doors - `AllocateDataSlot`, `AllocateNamedDataSlot`, `GetData`, `SetData`, `FreeNamedDataSlot` - do not exist here, so the public default constructor and the `getData`/`setData` pair are a project-owned surface wearing a .NET name. Consumers measured: **zero** production, one test file; `sizeof` 16. **Premise correction - the report's third "other missing assertions" bullet is wrong.** `std::any`'s move assignment and `reset()` are **`noexcept` by the standard**, and `setData`'s parameter is taken **by value**, so any allocation for it happens at the call site, outside this function's exception specification; `noexcept(slot.setData(std::move(v)))` is 1 and sound, so **no defect ticket was filed** - unlike the genuinely unsound `noexcept` at #2292. What that bullet understates is the rest of it: there is no synchronization policy at all, so two threads touching one slot with at least one write is a **data race with undefined behaviour**. Routes priced: thread-indexed storage behind a new `Thread` slot API is a substantial new public surface **and a behaviour change**, since `getData`/`setData` stop being shared and any existing caller silently observes something different; a non-public constructor on top of that is the most faithful and adds a public source break; demoting or renaming is a source break that removes the .NET name. None was taken. **Taken anyway (#2300), true under every outcome:** the header now states .NET's internal constructor and this port's absent `Thread` API, the single value shared by every thread, the unsynchronized data race, and that the `noexcept` specifications are themselves sound and why; it also corrects the pre-existing "Use std::thread_local", which named no C++ facility. **This does not close the finding.** No two-thread test was added, deliberately - it would pin the sharing that #2298 may remove. **Not a family with SR-AUD-128 or SR-AUD-126**; no CCF minted. `docs/CoreMarshalSlotAndFuncShapePlan.md` §3. |
| [SR-AUD-130](modules/core/include/System/Diagnostics/Stopwatch.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-130--medium--stopwatch-publishes-a-fabricated-10-mhz-timestamp-frequency-instead-of-the-platform-timer-unit) | medium | confirmed | `Stopwatch.hpp`, `StopwatchTests.cpp` | C++ divides raw monotonic nanoseconds into 100-ns ticks and exposes 10 MHz, while current .NET Unix publishes the native 1 GHz counter/frequency; its test asserts the altered public unit. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2326); STILL CONFIRMED, nothing implemented — `needs_user`.** Live: `static constexpr longcs Frequency = 10'000'000LL` (`Stopwatch.hpp:111`) with `GetTimestamp()` returning steady-clock nanoseconds divided by 100. **Premise addition the finding does not state: there is a second public surface.** `System::TimeProvider::getTimestampFrequencyProperty()` returns `Stopwatch::Frequency` verbatim and `TimeProvider::GetTimestamp()` forwards to `Stopwatch::GetTimestamp()` (`modules/threading/include/System/TimeProvider.hpp:60-67`), so a unit change also changes `TimeProvider` — including `TimeProvider::GetElapsedTime`, whose body already carries the CCF-004 / SR-AUD-131 saturation work and whose scale factor is currently **exactly 1.0 because `Frequency == TimeSpan::TicksPerSecond`**; changing `Frequency` re-enables a non-unit factor there, which must be re-measured rather than assumed. The finding itself offers two mutually exclusive remedies — publish the platform unit and convert only at the elapsed boundary (changing a public `constexpr` constant and the meaning of every stored timestamp), or keep the unit and stop presenting it as a .NET counterpart. Priced in #2326; no CCF minted. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-131](modules/core/include/System/Diagnostics/Stopwatch.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-131--high--getelapsedtime-performs-attacker-controlled-signed-timestamp-subtraction-with-undefined-overflow) | high | remediated | `Stopwatch.hpp`, `StopwatchTests.cpp`, `TimeProvider.hpp`, `TimeProviderTests.cpp` | Stopwatch and TimeProvider `GetElapsedTime(INT64_MIN, INT64_MAX)` directly perform signed subtraction; UBSan confirms undefined overflow before TimeSpan construction. **Remediated (tickets #2218 Stopwatch + #2219 TimeProvider, 2026-08-10) — a CCF-004 *occurrence*; that family stays closed 8/8 and gains no member.** Both subtractions now wrap in the unsigned domain and convert back (class A): every value is byte-identical, measured on both sides. **Premise correction:** `TimeProvider::GetElapsedTime` carries a **second** undefined operation the finding does not name, at a different column of the same line — an out-of-range `double`→`long` conversion that GCC's default `-fsanitize=undefined` set **cannot report** because `float-cast-overflow` is outside that group. It is reachable **without** the subtraction overflow, on the **default** provider: `GetElapsedTime(0, INT64_MAX)` returned `INT64_MIN`, a maximal negative duration for a maximal positive interval, and now saturates to `INT64_MAX`, agreeing with `Stopwatch`. Four public doors reach a defective site, not the one the audit names; `TimeProvider::GetLocalNow` was inventoried and is clear. +20 tests; twelve probe cases exit 0 under `-fsanitize=address,undefined,float-cast-overflow -fno-sanitize-recover=all` with activation proved by an unrepaired control in the same binary; six mutations, two of them killed only by the probe and labelled as the weaker signal. No signature, member-data, `noexcept`, layout or vtable change (`docs/CoreDefinedArithmeticBoundedParseFamilyPlan.md`). |
| [SR-AUD-132](modules/core/include/System/TryWriteInterpolatedStringHandler.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-132--high--public-raw-destination-pointer-can-be-null-and-reaches-an-asan-confirmed-write-crash) | high | remediated | `TryWriteInterpolatedStringHandler.hpp`, direct tests | A positive-length null destination passed the handler capacity check and reached `memcpy`; ASan confirmed the zero-page **write**, while a null literal separately reached `strlen`. Ticket #1810 rejects both at their boundaries with `ArgumentNullException`, restoring by validation what .NET's `Span<char>` parameter provides by construction; a null paired with a **zero** capacity stays valid per ticket #1774's rule. The same change fixes the `size_t` wraparound in the `pos_ + len` capacity test and two zero-length null-pointer uses in `memcpy`/`std::string`. SR-AUD-133, which shares this file, stays `confirmed`. |
| [SR-AUD-133](modules/core/include/System/TryWriteInterpolatedStringHandler.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-133--medium--appendformatted-ignores-format-and-replaces-net-formatting-with-hardcoded-c-spellings) | medium | remediated | `TryWriteInterpolatedStringHandler.hpp`, direct tests | Format is discarded and arithmetic uses `std::to_string`, yielding `1`, `255`, and `3.140000` instead of ordinary .NET `True`, `FF`, and `3.14`; the fallback never calls IFormattable. **REMEDIATED (#2304, 2026-08-11), reviewed by #2303.** `docs/InterpolatedHandlerFormattingPlan.md` is the design record. **One root cause, several subcases:** `formatValue<T>` was a hand-written type map with no parameter for the specifier at all, so both the discarded format and the C++ spellings follow from one fact, and one delegation change closes all of it. All three named examples reproduced.
