Measured over a bounded 75-cell matrix (the 75-cell matrix of `docs/InterpolatedHandlerFormattingPlan.md` §6; `build-probe/` is untracked, so the values are transcribed there), the finding is **understated in four places**: (1) **a string literal lost its value entirely** - `AppendFormatted("lit")` emitted `[?]`, because a literal deduces to `char[N]` and never matched the `const char*` arm, so the most ordinary interpolation argument fell into the placeholder branch; `std::string_view` and any `char[N]` did the same; (2) `float` diverges as well as `double` (`2.5f` -> `2.500000`); (3) **every unrecognised or malformed specifier was silently accepted** (`"Q"`, `"Dz"`, `"Fz"`), the exact fallback CCF-006 removed from all twelve numeric wrappers; (4) a real `System::IFormattable` implementer produced `[?]`, so the class doc-comment promising that fallback was false. **Nothing is invented: every arm delegates** to a sibling formatter this repository already ships in the same component - `Boolean::ToString`; `SByte`/`Int16`/`Int32`/`Int64` and `Byte`/`UInt16`/`UInt32`/`UInt64::ToString` chosen by width and signedness; `Single`/`Double::ToString`; and the value's own `ToString(format)` or `ToString()`. `System::detail::runCompositeFormat` was evaluated and correctly rejected: it parses a composite format string into items, a problem this handler does not have, since value and specifier arrive already split. **Two boundaries are deliberately unchanged rather than guessed** and are documented at the point of decision: `long double` keeps `std::to_string` (no extended-precision formatter exists; narrowing would lose precision) and `char16_t` stays integral (whether that door means a character or a 16-bit integer is unanswerable from this type's contract). A type with no `ToString` still yields `[?]`. **35 of 75 cells changed, 40 byte-identical** - every integer, `char`, `std::string`, a `const char*` lvalue, and **all capacity/failure behaviour and all layout facts**. The one succeeding-call-to-throw transition is five malformed specifiers, inherited from CCF-006's policy rather than invented, and validated **before** any byte reaches `appendRaw`, so a rejected specifier leaves the handler untouched and usable. Two tests pinned the defect and were replaced: `AppendFormatted_Bool` asserted `"1"`, and `AppendFormatted_WithFormat_Ignored` asserted only non-emptiness, so it could never have detected the repair. **No first-party production consumer exists** - changing the header recompiled exactly one object file, its own test. No public signature, overload, template constraint, layout (`sizeof`/`alignof` 32/8), vtable, `noexcept`, exported symbol or module edge changed; the header's transitive `__int128` exposure through `Int64.hpp`/`Double.hpp` is disclosed in the plan §9. Compile-domain behaviour unchanged: the two new concepts are used only inside `if constexpr`, never as constraints. +31 tests (25 -> 56). Nine mutations, eight valid and **all eight caught** - including ASan catching the unsafe `std::string(v)` array read that no test could distinguish - one proved equivalent over eleven wrappers. Sanitizers were run only where they discriminate. **Adjacent to CCF-006 and CCF-012, a member of neither, and no `CCF-*` minted or extended:** the handler contains no brace grammar, so it is not a third composite-format implementation, and it is not a numeric wrapper; the repair needed no new policy, only two that already exist. **A separate defect found in the same member carries an ordinary ticket number and no `SR-AUD-*` identifier (#2305):** `AppendFormatted((const char*)nullptr)` reached `std::string(v)` and **aborted the process, exit 134**, via a `std::logic_error` escaping a `System`-shaped API - ASan and UBSan were silent, which is what established the mechanism is not a memory error. Fixed on the exact terms #1810 settled for `AppendLiteral`. |
| [SR-AUD-134](modules/core/include/System/Linq.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-134--medium--linq-callback-overloads-accept-empty-stdfunction-values-and-make-failure-data-dependent) | medium | remediated | `Linq.hpp`, `LinqTests.cpp` | Empty predicates/selectors return normal results on an empty vector and throw native `bad_function_call` only when an item is reached, rather than rejecting invalid callbacks at entry. |
| [SR-AUD-135](modules/core/include/System/Linq.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-135--high--integral-linq-sum-performs-unchecked-signed-c-addition-and-reaches-undefined-behavior) | high | remediated | `Linq.hpp`, `LinqTests.cpp` | `Sum<int>({INT_MAX, 1})` uses native signed addition; UBSan confirms undefined overflow where .NET's integral Sum must throw OverflowException. **Remediated (ticket #2220, 2026-08-10) — a CCF-004 *occurrence*; that family stays closed 8/8 and gains no member.** One shared step, `System::Linq::detail::addForSum`, drives both overloads: for signed integral `T` (excluding `char` and `bool`) the sum is formed in the unsigned counterpart and the overflow recognised from the operand signs, so nothing undefined runs on the way to a `System::OverflowException` carrying .NET's verbatim message. **Two premise corrections:** there are **two** sites, not one — `:236` and the **selector** overload at `:249`, each needing its own repair; and the finding's domain is wider than the undefined behaviour — `Sum<unsigned>` wraps by definition and `Sum<short>` promotes to `int`, so both are pinned **unchanged**, exactly as the finding's own note asks. The sharpest case is one the framing misses: `Sum<int>({INT_MAX, INT_MAX, INT_MIN})` returned the **mathematically correct** 2147483646 through an undefined intermediate, and now throws, because .NET checks per element. No `Linq::Sum` call site exists in this repository outside its own tests. +13 tests; eight probe shapes exit 0 under ASan+UBSan+float-cast-overflow+LSan with `-fno-sanitize-recover=all` and 1,000 throw/catch cycles leak nothing, with UB and leak controls firing in the same binary; five mutations, one killed only by the probe and labelled as such. No signature, `noexcept`, layout or symbol change (`docs/CoreDefinedArithmeticBoundedParseFamilyPlan.md`). |
| [SR-AUD-136](modules/core/include/System/Void.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-136--medium--void-is-presented-as-a-normal-c-valuegeneric-type-although-c-forbids-the-documented-use-cases) | medium | remediated | `Void.hpp`, `VoidTests.cpp` | C++ construction, equality, vector, and Nullable use are advertised as C#-porting support even though local C# rejects ordinary System.Void variables and generic arguments with CS0673. **Remediated (#2285 review, #2286 implementation, 2026-08-11) by this finding's own first alternative**, with one refinement recorded rather than glossed over: "make it a clearly project-specific type" cannot be taken wholesale, because - unlike SR-AUD-127's `CrashReason` - `System.Void` **is** a public .NET type and the empty field-less shape **is** .NET's; what is project-specific is the *use model*. The second alternative was unavailable: reflection is a permanent project deviation (`CLAUDE.md`), so there is no "metadata-oriented handling" to retain and stripping the type back to it would delete three public members. Both substantive complaints are statements about what the header presents and both are now false: the doc-comment states that .NET's `System.Void` is a public **metadata** type naming the `void` return type with no declared field, that C# rejects construction (CS0143) and rejects the type as a value or generic argument (CS0673) while naming the type object with `typeof(void)`, that **no compiling C# source therefore exists** for the `Nullable<System.Void>`/`Task<System.Void>` patterns so the header no longer claims to help port them, that the whole C++ value API (declaration, copy, comparison, container storage, template-argument use, `ToString`, `operator==`, `operator!=`) is a project extension with no .NET counterpart down to the empty string, that this port's `Void` has no base class and therefore no inherited object text, `GetHashCode`, `Equals(object)`, ordering or `std::hash`, that the only shared property is the compiler-visible shape, and that there is no first-party production consumer. **Premise correction - the comment was wrong in two opposite directions.** The finding names the over-claim; the same comment also carried an *under*-claim, "You cannot use System::Void in C++ code directly as a variable type", which is false in C++ and which this file's own `VoidTest.DefaultConstruct` has been disproving since the type was written - the C# CS0673 restriction transliterated into a language that does not impose it. Both are repaired by the same edit, so no separate ticket was minted; a third, smaller inaccuracy ("System.Void has no documented ToString in .NET", where the .NET struct declares **no member at all**) was corrected in passing. **Nothing in the compiled surface changed** - no member added, removed, renamed, re-signed or re-qualified, `sizeof` 1, `alignof` 1, non-polymorphic, both operators still `noexcept`, still `<string>` only, still `Core.Base` - and no executable statement was changed, so there is no ABI, layout, vtable, `noexcept`, symbol or component-dependency consequence. Consumer inventory re-measured, not inherited: **zero** production consumers, two test files holding ten cases in two suites whose names differ only by a trailing `s` (pre-existing duplication, untouched), both in `SharpRuntimeTests_Core_Base`. Zero consumers did **not** license withdrawing the header or a member - downstream consumers exist and this batch may not inspect them - so withdrawal stays a live approval boundary, deliberately not foreclosed. **One test added, none retired:** the existing ten already pin construction, equality, `ToString`, `vector<Void>` and `Nullable<Void>` twice over, and adding more pins there would harden a surface a later approved change may need to withdraw, so `VoidTest.IsAnEmptyFieldlessTagType` pins instead the one property genuinely shared with .NET that nothing covered - `is_empty_v`, `!is_polymorphic_v`, `is_standard_layout_v`, trivial default-construction/copy/destruction, `sizeof == 1`. The pre-existing `sizeof(Void) == sizeof(unsigned char)` is not a substitute, which mutation 1 confirmed: adding `char pad_ = 0;` left **every** pre-existing case passing and failed only the new one. Mutation 2 (`ToString` returning `" "`) was caught by the two old `ToString` cases; both mutations were rebuilt and relinked. **The inherited "same shape as SR-AUD-127" ranking is superficial**: both are about what a header says and neither type has a first-party consumer, but that characteristic also runs through SR-AUD-124/125/126/128/129/137 and is not a cause - `CrashReason` is a .NET-internal concept republished under a name .NET does not publish, `System.Void` is a name .NET **does** publish given a use model C# forbids. No CCF minted. `docs/CoreVoidValueSurfacePlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-137](modules/core/include/System/UnitySerializationHolder.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-137--medium--unityserializationholder-replaces-the-public-serialization-contract-with-an-unrelated-inspectable-data-object) | medium | confirmed | `UnitySerializationHolder.hpp`, its direct tests | Public raw code/data construction and getters replace .NET's serialization-only constructor/method signatures, internal NullUnity/fields, and interface boundary, without a production consumer. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2279); STILL CONFIRMED - approval boundary recorded (#2281, needs_user).** Neither of the finding's two alternatives is available without a user decision, which is what separates it from SR-AUD-127, ranked beside it and remediated by its own second alternative. The first alternative is a conjunction: "explicitly project-specific" is free documentation, but "make the wrapper internal" withdraws `System::UnitySerializationHolder` - or at minimum its public constant, constructor and two getters - from the `Core.Base` include tree, a public source break whose downstream cost this batch may not inspect. The second needs `SerializationInfo`/`StreamingContext` parameters, and `CLAUDE.md` records serialization infrastructure as a **permanent project deviation**, so implementing it would reverse a standing decision rather than remediate a defect. One thing was corrected anyway because it is wrong under either outcome: the header claimed the public surface "is preserved for source-compatibility", which the finding's own comparison disproves, and it now states what the port actually provides, that an ordinary caller can fabricate a holder state .NET only builds from a stream, and that the shape is under an open decision - **the surface itself is unchanged, so the finding is not closed**. **Not a family with SR-AUD-127**: shared characteristic (a .NET-internal type republished publicly with no first-party consumer), not shared cause - that one verbatim, this one reshaped plus a false parity claim, and only this one blocked. No CCF minted; the characteristic also runs through SR-AUD-124/125/126/128/129/136. Consumer inventory re-measured: zero production consumers, and two test suites whose names differ only by a trailing `s` (7 + 8 cases, pre-existing duplication, untouched); no test was added, because pinning more of a surface #2281 may withdraw would raise the cost of that decision. A separate diagnostic defect found while reviewing - `GetRealObject()` prints the optional data string under the label "Invalid unity type", so `UnitySerializationHolder(999, "UnknownType")` never reports 999 - is ticket #2282 and carries no `SR-AUD-*` identifier. `docs/CoreCrashReasonAndUnityHolderPlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-138](modules/uri/include/System/UriBuilder.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-138--medium--uribuilder-copied-credentials-are-fused-into-username-and-later-password-writes-corrupt-them) | medium | remediated | `UriBuilder.hpp`, `UriBuilderTests.cpp` | Copying `user:pass` assigns the whole value to UserName and clears Password; a later password update serializes `user:pass:replacement` instead of replacing `pass`. **Remediated (#1993, 2026-08-03, cause U-F):** `setFieldsFromUri` splits the copied user-info at its **first** colon, so `UserName`/`Password` are the parts the type publishes and replacing the password no longer serialises the old credentials. Both copying constructors share the one function and both are tested. **Measured correction to the ticket, not assumed away:** `ToString()` is byte-identical for three of the four user-info shapes and **not** the fourth — a user-info of `"user:"` now renders `user@` instead of `user:@`, matching .NET after the identical split (`docs/SystemUriNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §24.3). +8 tests; no signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. |

| [SR-AUD-143](modules/uri/src/System/Uri.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-143--medium--opaque-mailto-uris-bypass-the-documented-default-port-table) | medium | remediated | `Uri.cpp`, `UriTests.cpp` | The opaque parse branch bypasses its own `mailto` default-port table and reports -1 instead of 25. **Remediated (#1989, 2026-08-03, cause U-B):** `defaultPortForScheme` is now consulted on **every** path that produces a port. **Premise corrected:** the finding names only `mailto`, but the opaque branch assigned `-1` unconditionally, so `telnet:` lost 23 too — and a **second, entirely unnamed site** had the same defect, an authority ending in a bare `:` (`http://example.com:/` reported `-1` while `http://example.com/` reported `80`). Both repaired together as one root cause. `getAuthorityProperty()` and the two-`Uri` constructor still suppress a default port, asserted rather than argued. +10 tests; no acceptance, signature, layout or ABI change. |
| [SR-AUD-144](modules/uri/src/System/Uri.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-144--medium--base-uri-resolution-treats-query-fragment-and-network-path-references-as-ordinary-paths) | medium | remediated | `Uri.cpp`, `UriTests.cpp` | Query-only and fragment-only references truncate the base path, and `//authority/path` is merged under the old authority instead of replacing it. **Remediated (#1990, 2026-08-03, cause U-C):** three of RFC 3986 §5.2.2's four reference cases are implemented instead of one. A query-only reference keeps the full base path; a fragment-only reference keeps the base path **and** the base query; a network-path reference keeps only the base **scheme** and takes its own authority. The probe diff shows exactly three changed lines, matching this finding's recorded reference outputs. Two cases are deliberately excluded and pinned: RFC 3986 drops the base fragment for an **empty** reference and this port does not (no surviving evidence for what .NET does), and the **opaque-base** shape — `Uri(Uri("mailto:a@b.com"), "c")` → `mailto:///c` — needs information the object does not record and is ticket #2001. +11 tests; no acceptance change; all six downstream consumers re-run unchanged. |
| [SR-AUD-145](modules/uri/src/System/Uri.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-145--medium--uri-accepts-malformed-ipv6-authority-text-and-invalid-urikind-enum-values) | medium | remediated | `Uri.hpp`, `Uri.cpp`, `UriTests.cpp` | Malformed bracketed IPv6 stores `[:`, and arbitrary UriKind casts silently behave as RelativeOrAbsolute rather than throwing. **Remediated by a pair, 2026-08-03 — the finding has two unrelated halves.** **145a (#1991, cause U-D):** `parse` validates the bracket **structure** of an IP-literal authority before the port split, so the unterminated literal, trailing junk after `]`, and the empty literal `[]` are all rejected with `UriFormatException`. **Premise sharpened by measurement:** `http://[::1/path` fabricated **`port=1`** as well as the host `"[:"` the finding names — a caller that connects to `Port` reached port 1 rather than failing — and two further bracketed shapes were accepted that the finding does not name. The narrowing rests on **repository-contained** evidence: `HttpClient::parseUrl` already rejects an unterminated literal, pinned by its own test. Literal *content* and zone identifiers stay out of scope, pinned by a test. **145b (#1992, cause U-E):** `Uri(string, UriKind)` rejects a value outside the three declared members with `ArgumentException`, `paramName "uriKind"`, before parsing — the same policy already approved for this cause at #1976 (`GCSettings`) and #1954 (`WaitHandle`). A repository-wide search for `UriKind` casts returns **zero** hits, so the narrowing cannot reach a consumer. **Deferred verification, pinned rather than guessed:** `TryCreate`'s catch-all turns that into `false` + `nullptr`; whether .NET propagates instead could not be measured here. +19 tests; no signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. |

| [SR-AUD-169](modules/runtime/src/System/Runtime/InteropServices/PosixSignalRegistration.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-169--high--registering-a-posix-handler-discards-neither-chains-nor-restores-the-processs-prior-signal-disposition) | high | remediated | `PosixSignalRegistration.cpp`, `PosixSignalRegistration.hpp`, `PosixSignalTests.cpp` | Ticket #1975 saves the complete prior `struct sigaction` and restores it; the chaining half stays confirmed under the approval-gated #1979. **#1979 (2026-08-17) completed the CHAINING half this row names.** #1975 had saved and restored the prior disposition; nothing invoked it, because the non-cancelled path raised into `SIG_DFL`. The reference tree is available now (`docs/StandingApprovals.md` §5.1) and `SignalHandler` (`pal_signal.c:215-246`) invokes the original disposition **immediately, inside the raw handler**, for every signal that is not `SIGINT`/`SIGQUIT`/`SIGTERM` — *"For other signals, we immediately invoke the original handler."* The port now does the same, and its non-cancelled default branch restores the **saved `struct sigaction`** rather than imposing `SIG_DFL`. See SR-AUD-171 and `docs/SystemRuntimeNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §10.1.1. |
| [SR-AUD-170](modules/runtime/src/System/Runtime/InteropServices/PosixSignalRegistration.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-170--medium--create-rejects-valid-positive-raw-unix-signal-values-that-current-net-expressly-supports) | medium | remediated | `PosixSignalRegistration.cpp`, `PosixSignal.hpp`, `PosixSignalTests.cpp` | Ticket #1977 accepts positive raw signal numbers and makes both spellings of a signal equivalent. |
| [SR-AUD-171](modules/runtime/src/System/Runtime/InteropServices/PosixSignalRegistration.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-171--high--non-cancelled-job-control-signals-perform-an-os-stop-where-current-nets-unix-path-deliberately-does-not) | high | remediated | `PosixSignalRegistration.cpp`, `PosixSignalRegistration.hpp`, `PosixSignalTests.cpp` | C++ restores SIG_DFL and re-raises SIGTSTP/SIGTTIN/SIGTTOU; a reaped child stops on SIGTSTP, whereas current .NET deliberately no-ops after notification. Design complete in `docs/SystemRuntimeNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §10; blocked implementation ticket #1979. **REMEDIATED (#1979, 2026-08-17).** The gate was evidence, not preference: the design (`docs/SystemRuntimeNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §10.1) could only PROPOSE .NET's rule from a reading with no managed probe, and its own alternative 4 was *defer until the reference tree is available*. It is, and the derivation **corrects the proposal three times** (§10.1.1). (1) The no-op set is **seven** signals, not three: `SystemNative_HandleNonCanceledPosixSignal` (`pal_signal.c:260-315`) never raises for `SIGCONT`, `SIGTSTP`, `SIGTTIN`, `SIGTTOU`, `SIGCHLD`, `SIGURG` or `SIGWINCH`. (2) The chaining is **not** in the non-cancelled path but inside the raw handler (`pal_signal.c:215-246`), which is why the later path breaks with *"We've already called the original handler in SignalHandler"* — putting it where the proposal did would have made the callbacks a GATE IN FRONT OF the process's existing handler instead of OBSERVERS of a signal it was always going to handle. (3) The default branch restores the **saved `struct sigaction`**, not `SIG_DFL`. Three tests plus a child-body driver, all of which **re-exec** the test binary rather than merely forking: this module starts its watcher lazily, and `fork()` gives the child the *already started* flag without the thread, so a forked child never dispatches — the first cut passed in isolation and failed inside the full run. Two mutations, each caught by exactly one case. No signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change; one `.cpp` body. Migration unchanged: a consumer relying on the job-control stop has no opt-in replacement, because .NET offers none. |

| [SR-AUD-173](modules/core/include/System/Globalization/CharUnicodeInfo.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-173--medium--unicode-numeric-apis-recognize-only-ascii-plus-a-few-hard-coded-latin-1-characters) | medium | confirmed | `CharUnicodeInfo.hpp`, `CharTests2.cpp` | Decimal/digit/numeric methods reject Arabic-Indic digits, Roman numerals, and fractions outside six hard-coded Latin-1 cases, unlike .NET's generated Unicode numeric data. **Reviewed 2026-08-12 (#2335); STILL CONFIRMED — its documentation clause is REMEDIATED, its table clause is `blocked` (#2336).** **Measured:** the three methods answer for **sixteen code points** in total — `GetDecimalDigitValue` U+0030–U+0039; `GetDigitValue` plus U+00B9/U+00B2/U+00B3; `GetNumericValue` plus U+00BC/U+00BD/U+00BE — and −1/−1.0 everywhere else, reproducing the audit's probe exactly (U+0665 → −1 vs .NET 5, U+216B → −1.0 vs .NET 12, U+2153 → −1.0 vs .NET 0.333…). **Consumer split the finding conflates:** `System::Char::GetNumericValue` forwards here and inherits the reduction (`Char.hpp:410-411`), but `Char::IsNumber` does **not** — it routes through `GetUnicodeCategory`, which is SR-AUD-174's surface, repaired by #2316. **#2335 (`done`, documentation only):** the finding's own clause "This is not documented as a limited character set" needs no data. The class note gains a numeric-reduction paragraph naming the sixteen code points and the three divergences, and all six method doc-comments are corrected — `@return -1 if @p ch is not a decimal digit` was false, since −1 also means *is* one that this port does not know. Same repair #2316 applied to the category method, applied to its siblings; #2316 is not reopened and no behaviour changed. **#2336 (`blocked`):** the `Numeric_Type`/`Numeric_Value` data is absent from this container (no `UnicodeData.txt`, no `DerivedNumericValues.txt`, no generator, no ICU; `/rv` absent) and the governing decision is **already before the user as Approval F / #2018**. Same gate as #2018 and #2315, **different table** — category-plus-case, general category and numeric values are three payloads behind one approval. No second approval raised; no partial table hand-authored. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-174](modules/core/include/System/Globalization/CharUnicodeInfo.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-174--medium--bmp-category-classification-depends-on-the-c-locale-and-labels-common-assigned-code-points-as-othernotassigned) | medium | confirmed | `CharUnicodeInfo.hpp`, `UnicodeCategory.hpp`, `CharTests2.cpp` | POSIX C-locale predicates map combining marks, dash punctuation, private-use, and surrogates to OtherNotAssigned instead of their Unicode categories; tests cover only ASCII. **Reviewed 2026-08-11 (#2314); STILL CONFIRMED, not remediated.** Re-verified live: the header is 182 lines with **no Unicode data at all**, and the seven-predicate `isw*` ladder can name at most **8 of `UnicodeCategory`'s 30** values. **Premise corrections.** (1) "No ticket references it" is not literally true — #1766 names it as the compressed run `SR-AUD-173/174`, which a substring scan misses; #1766 is the audit that *discovered* it, not an owner, so the substantive premise stands. This search-method fix is what surfaced #2317. (2) It is **not wholly a table port**. Two clauses need no Unicode data: the **locale dependence** (this repository itself installs a non-invariant global locale under RAII in `CultureInvariantFormattingTests`, so the same source gives different categories on a host where those locales exist — a reproducibility defect independent of any Unicode version), and the **surrogate misclassification**, where `Char::IsSurrogate` already fixes `U+D800–U+DFFF` in-repo at `Char.hpp:245` while `GetUnicodeCategory` calls the same code point `OtherNotAssigned` — **the header contradicts itself**, with no external reference needed, and this report's own managed probe records the expected `16 (Surrogate)`. (3) The table decision is **already open**, worded, as **Approval F / ticket #2018** (`blocked`) for `System::Text::Rune` — same root dependency, second type — so it is gated rather than asked again. **Tickets: #2315 (`blocked`)** for the full general-category table, which needs a UCD source, an attribution and a stated Unicode version with an update policy — none of which exists in this repository (no `UnicodeData.txt`, no generator; `UnicodeRanges.hpp` is *block* data); **#2316 (`todo`)** for the two data-free clauses. Deliberately **not implemented here**: #2316 edits a header reached transitively by `Char.hpp`, so its rebuild is near-total and it changes observable classification of real code points. **No Unicode data was hand-authored**, per the same reasoning #2018 records: a partial table is worse than a declared reduction. An evidence boundary carried into #2316: the probe pins `17 (PrivateUse)` for **one** code point, and no private-use range constant exists in this repository, unlike the surrogate range. `docs/CoreUnicodeCategoryTablePlan.md`. **PARTIALLY REMEDIATED 2026-08-11 (#2316); STATUS STAYS `confirmed`** because the table clause is untouched and still blocked on Approval F / #2018 (#2315). Two clauses closed, both measured over the whole code space U+0000–U+10FFFF (1,114,112 code points) with the pre-change header extracted from git and swept against the live one. **Clause 1, locale dependence:** `GetUnicodeCategory` no longer consults any locale facet — the `isw*` ladder is replaced by explicit ASCII ranges that reproduce the `C`-locale answer with **0 mismatches** over all 1,114,112 code points, so the invariant-locale result is provably unchanged. Installing a UTF-8 global locale used to change **287,218** categories (25.8%); it now changes **0**. **Clause 2, surrogates:** U+D800–U+DFFF answer `Surrogate`; `Char::IsSurrogate` disagreed with `category == Surrogate` at **2,048** code points before and at **0** after. In the invariant locale those 2,048 `OtherNotAssigned`→`Surrogate` transitions are the **only** change anywhere in the code space, and **0** `Char::IsNumber`/`IsSeparator`/`IsControl`/`IsSurrogate` answers moved. **Three premise corrections to the #2314 review, all measured.** (a) The locale clause was called demonstrable only “on a host where those locales exist”; **`C.utf8` is installed in this container** and reproduces it directly — `CultureInvariantFormattingTests` skips only because it probes four `en_US`/`de_DE` names and never probes `C.utf8`, so “it skips, therefore none is installed” does not follow. (b) The rebuild is **not** near-total: **7 of 728** tracked object files (0.96%) depend on this header, and the single production one, `XmlConvert.cpp`, uses only `Char::ToString`/`Char::Parse` — `GetUnicodeCategory` has **no first-party production consumer at all**. (c) The header's one documented limitation, “non-BMP code points are mapped to OtherNotAssigned”, was untrue on both axes: with 32-bit `wchar_t` supplementary code points went straight into the locale-sensitive ladder, and with 16-bit `wchar_t` (Windows) the `L'\0'` substitution reached `iswcntrl(0)` and returned **`Control`**, never `OtherNotAssigned` — replayed with `WCHAR_MAX` forced to `0xFFFF`. The documented behaviour is true for the first time on every target. **The price, on a host that installs a UTF-8 global locale only:** 287,218 categories are withdrawn to `OtherNotAssigned` (148,166 were `OtherPunctuation`, 134,531 `OtherLetter`, 4,474 letters, 32 `Control`, 15 `SpaceSeparator`), and `Char::IsSeparator` returns `false` for 15 code points (U+1680, U+2000–2006, U+2008–200A, U+2028, U+2029, U+205F, U+3000). Those answers were unreproducible across hosts and mostly wrong — for every non-ASCII row of this report's own managed probe the UTF-8 answer is also wrong (U+0301 and U+2014 and U+E000 and U+1F600 all came back `OtherPunctuation` against `NonSpacingMark`/`DashPunctuation`/`PrivateUse`/`OtherSymbol`) — and answering them correctly *and reproducibly* is exactly #2315's table. **Deliberately not done:** ASCII punctuation subcategories (all 32 stay `OtherPunctuation`; `$`, `+`, `(`, `-`, `_` and friends keep the reduction, now documented instead of silent), `PrivateUse` for U+E000 (one probed point is not a range — pinned by a test asserting it is **not** `PrivateUse`), and SR-AUD-173. +11 tests; 6 mutations, 5 caught and 1 labelled equivalent (an unreachable branch behind the Control guard), 0 survived. No public signature, layout, vtable, `noexcept` or symbol change; `<climits>`/`<cwctype>` dropped as they served only the removed ladder. `docs/CoreUnicodeCategoryTablePlan.md` Part II. |
| [SR-AUD-175](modules/core/include/System/Numerics/BFloat16.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-175--medium--float-conversion-truncates-bfloat16-payloads-instead-of-performing-current-net-round-to-nearest-even) | medium | remediated | `BFloat16.hpp`, `BitConverterTests.cpp` | `fromFloat` shifted the IEEE float bits right by 16, truncating, where current .NET passes non-NaN float bits through `RoundMidpointToEven(bits, 16)`. Measured over 26 exact float bit patterns the before-state read **14 OK / 12 BAD**; both of the report's own probes reproduced exactly (`0x3F818000` -> `0x3F81` not `0x3F82`, `0x3F808001` -> `0x3F80` not `0x3F81`). The probe added four facts the finding does not state, the sharpest being that **truncation turned a NaN into an infinity**: `0x7F800001` is a signalling NaN whose upper 16 bits are exactly the `+Infinity` pattern. It also established that a rounding bias must not be applied to a NaN either (the bias can carry into the exponent and produce the same silent infinity, which is why .NET rounds only non-NaN bits), that overflow-to-infinity is correct rounding rather than a defect (the tie at max + half ulp rounds to the even neighbour, which is the infinity pattern), and that underflow has its own tie at `0x00008000`. Ticket #2262 handles NaN first (sign kept, quiet bit set) and otherwise rounds half to even by adding `0x7FFF` plus the low bit of the retained payload; the mantissa carry reaches the exponent by itself. **Premise correction:** the report says no test constructs from float or performs arithmetic - true of `BitConverterTests.cpp`, false of the repository, since `tests/integration/Task40Tests.cpp` carries a 14-test `BFloat16Tests` suite that does both. Its conclusion survives: every value those tests use has zero discarded bits, so none pinned truncation and none was retired; all 14 pass unchanged and are the compatibility control. After: **26 OK / 0 BAD**. +17 tests including a sweep of every single-bit NaN mantissa in both signs; 4 mutations, all caught - including the plausible wrong repair of fixing the rounding but forgetting that NaN cannot be rounded. `sizeof`/`alignof` measured 2/2 before and after; no signature, layout, vtable, `noexcept` or symbol change; 4 dependent translation units, and `BitConverter`'s BFloat16 entry points are raw-bit reinterpretation that never calls `fromFloat`. Sibling **SR-AUD-176 deliberately rejected** from the unit and still `confirmed`: it is a large additive API port, not a defect in an existing behaviour, and a shared header is not a shared root cause. `docs/CoreBFloat16RoundingPlan.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-176](modules/core/include/System/Numerics/BFloat16.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-176--medium--public-bfloat16-is-a-narrow-bit-wrapper-rather-than-the-current-net-numeric-value-type-contract) | medium | confirmed | `BFloat16.hpp`, `BitConverterTests.cpp` | The public type lacks parse/format, comparison/hash, classification, conversion, and generic numeric APIs from current .NET BFloat16, while tests cover only raw bit conversion. **Scoped 2026-08-12 (#2340); STILL CONFIRMED, deliberately NOT started — `todo`.** **It had no open owner:** #1766 discovered it, #2261 explicitly rejected it from its unit and recorded it as "left confirmed and unclaimed", and #2317's only mention of it is the methodological example "SR-AUD-175/176" — exactly the false-ownership signal the strict method exists to exclude, so it never owned it. **Disposition: a scoped `todo`, not an implementation unit.** The work is purely **additive** — nothing currently observable changes, so no approval boundary is crossed — but unbounded as one ticket, so #2340 records a six-way decomposition (classification plus `CopySign`; comparison and `GetHashCode` against this port's own `detail::ComparisonPolicy` NaN/signed-zero contract; `BitIncrement`/`BitDecrement`; numeric conversions; `Parse`/`TryParse` and the format overloads; the generic-math contract, which needs its own design note because this port has no static-abstract interfaces) and requires each to become its own bounded ticket first. Units 5 and 6 additionally carry the #2252/#2260 exact-reference-text dependency while `/rv` is absent. #2262 (SR-AUD-175, the rounding defect) is not reopened; CCF-021 and CCF-022 stay unminted. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |

| [SR-AUD-178](modules/core/include/System/detail/IntegerNumberStylesParser.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-178--medium--parser-never-validates-undefined-or-incompatible-numberstyles-flags-before-silently-choosing-a-grammar) | medium | confirmed | `IntegerNumberStylesParser.hpp`, `NumberStyles.hpp`, `NumberStylesExtendedTests.cpp` | Unknown masks parse normally and invalid HexFloat chooses raw hex parsing, where current .NET validates style masks and throws ArgumentException at entry. |
| [SR-AUD-179](modules/core/include/SharpRuntime/Experimental/Property.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-179--medium--property-assignment-writes-through-the-setter-but-returns-a-disconnected-stale-cache-value) | medium | remediated | `Experimental/Property.hpp`, `Task39RemainingTests.cpp` | Assignment updates captured backing state but returns the unsynchronized default cache; a string probe prints `stored=new assignment_result=`. **Remediated (#2244, 2026-08-10, `docs/CoreExperimentalPropertySlicePlan.md` §4.1).** `operator=` returns `T` **by value**, read back through the getter after the setter has run, as `std::atomic`'s assignment operator does; the `cachedValue` member is reachable from nothing. **Three premise sharpenings, all measured** (`build-probe/2243_probe1_before.log`): for a scalar `T` the returned value is **indeterminate**, not merely stale — `T cachedValue;` is default-initialised, and two runs of the same binary printed `535613888` and `-455742320`; the returned `T&` also **silently swallowed writes**, so the lvalue was wrong in both directions; and a *synchronised* cache would **not** have been a repair, because a setter that clamps `-5` to `0` would make it report a value the property does not hold. One consequence the finding does not reach was also measured: chained assignment `pa = pb = 9` propagated the indeterminate cache into `pa`. **One deliberate source break:** `T& r = (p = v);` no longer compiles — that spelling *was* the defect. Every value use (`T x = (p = v);`, streaming, statement form, chaining) compiles unchanged and is now correct, and no first-party call site uses `operator=` on this type. **The layout did not move**, deliberately: deleting the now-vestigial `cachedValue` would take `sizeof(Property<int>)` 72 → 64, an object-layout change needing explicit approval, so it is retained, `[[maybe_unused]]`, documented (including the `T` default-constructible requirement it alone imposes) and pinned by a test, with **#2246** carrying the approval request. `ReadOnlyProperty` is unchanged and its base-reference route still throws. +8 tests; no signature beyond the stated return type, no `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change. Mutation M1 (return the cache again) failed exactly 6 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-180](modules/core/include/SharpRuntime/PortableFromChars.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-180--high--apple-fallback-reads-past-the-declared-fromchars-range-and-can-parse-a-different-value) | high | remediated | `PortableFromChars.hpp`, `Single.hpp`, `Double.hpp`, `XPathAstInternal.cpp`, `PortableFromCharsTests.cpp` | The strtof/strtod fallback ignores last; one-character range `"1"` backed by `"12"` returns value 12 and pointer offset 2, violating the drop-in range contract. **Remediated (ticket #2221, 2026-08-10).** The range is copied into a NUL-terminated buffer (stack for realistic literals, nothrow heap rather than truncation for a long digit run), the C parser runs on the copy, and the returned pointer is rebased; both entry points **gain** `noexcept`, matching `std::from_chars` and preventing a `std::terminate` from the `noexcept` `Single::tryParseCore`. **Three premise corrections. (1) This is NOT ASan-decidable** — the over-read happens inside glibc's `strtod`, which is neither instrumented nor intercepted, so a two-byte heap block with no NUL produces **no report**; a `PROT_NONE` guard page does see it, and the fallback segfaulted where `std::from_chars` survived. **(2) The header's comment that every call site passes a whole NUL-terminated string is false** — `Single`/`Double` trim surrounding whitespace first, so `" 1.5 "` yields a subrange whose `last` is a space; the defect was latent, not hypothetical. **(3) The consequence is a wrong value, not only a wrong pointer** — `"1e3"` over `[0,1)` returned 1000. This finding is **not** a CCF-004 member and not adjacent to it: no arithmetic is involved. +23 tests in a new suite that calls the Apple-only fallback **directly**, so it runs on the Linux gate; the guard-page probe now survives with `std::from_chars`'s exact answer; four mutations killed. No signature or symbol change (`docs/CoreDefinedArithmeticBoundedParseFamilyPlan.md`). |
| [SR-AUD-181](modules/core/include/SharpRuntime/Experimental/Property.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-181--low--advertised-defpropauto-and-custom-property-macros-name-a-nonexistent-property-type) | low | remediated | `Experimental/Property.hpp`, `Task39RemainingTests.cpp` | Experimental DEF_PROP_AUTO/custom macros expand to SharpRuntime::Property although the type is SharpRuntime::Experimental::Property, so documented minimal use does not compile. **Remediated (#2245, 2026-08-10, `docs/CoreExperimentalPropertySlicePlan.md` §4.2).** `DEF_PROP_AUTO` and `DEF_PROP_CUSTOM` now expand to `SharpRuntime::Experimental::Property<type>`; the three `IMPL_PROP_*` macros never name the type and are unchanged. **Premise confirmed and bounded by measurement** (`build-probe/2243_probe2_macros.log`): recompiling the identical minimal class with **only** the two type names re-spelled compiles and runs correctly, so the namespace is the *whole* defect and the finding's “followed by a missing constructor field error” is a consequence of the type name failing rather than a second cause. The durable half of the repair is that the macros are now **compiled by the test build** — `MacroWidget` instantiates all four documented pairs — which is what stops a macro nothing compiles from rotting again. +4 tests; nothing that previously built can break, because nothing previously built. Mutation M2 (restore the old spelling) fails the build with 24 errors. |
| [SR-AUD-182](modules/core/include/System/StringNormalizationExtensions.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-182--medium--public-unicode-normalization-is-an-always-successful-no-op-outside-ascii) | medium | confirmed | `StringNormalizationExtensions.hpp`, `NormalizationForm.hpp`, `StringTests.cpp` | Every input is reported normalized and returned unchanged; `e` + U+0301 stays `65CC81` while current .NET FormC reports false and composes it to `C3A9`. **Reviewed 2026-08-12 (#2337); STILL CONFIRMED — its pin clause is REMEDIATED, its implementation clause is `blocked` (#2338).** **Consumer surface: none in production** — five tests in `StringTests.cpp`, no `String::Normalize`, no module call site. **Measured gap:** all five of those tests use ASCII or the empty string, so **every one passes identically before and after a real normalization implementation** — the divergence had no pin at all, the same gap #2022 found for SR-AUD-294. **#2337 (`done`, test only), +4 gated-behaviour pins:** the decomposed sequence the audit measured (UTF-8 `65 CC 81`, reported normalized for Form C and returned unchanged rather than composed to `C3 A9`), the composed U+00E9 under Form D, the compatibility ligature U+FB01 under Form KC/KD, and the finding's separate observation that an **undefined** `NormalizationForm` value is accepted as a normal success where .NET reports an argument error. Each must be inverted by the repair. No production file touched. **Deliberately not done:** throwing `PlatformNotSupportedException` for non-ASCII input — a runtime behaviour break on accepted input, unapproved, in a type whose disclosure says the opposite. **#2338 (`blocked`):** normalization needs canonical and compatibility decomposition mappings, canonical combining classes, composition exclusions and quick-check properties **and** a UAX #15 implementation — the only one of this repository's four Unicode-blocked tickets that is not a table lookup. The **data-source** decision is nonetheless the same **Approval F / #2018**, recorded as the gate with no second approval raised. `docs/CoreOwnedFindingsReviews2317.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-183](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/WaitHandle.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-183--medium--waitall-and-waitany-silently-accept-invalid-handle-collections-and-invalid-timeout-values) | medium | remediated | `WaitHandle.hpp`, `Batch9ThreadingTests.cpp` | Empty/null collections and invalid timeouts return true/258 or loop instead of managed argument diagnostics; C++/managed probes reproduce each divergence.  **Remediated (#1952, 2026-08-03, cause T-C of `docs/ThreadingNamespaceReviewPlan.md`):** all four static entries now validate in `WaitMultiple`'s order -- empty collection (`ArgumentException`, "Waithandle array may not be empty.", paramName `waitHandles`), then timeout (`ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, `millisecondsTimeout`), then each element (`ArgumentNullException`, paramName `waitHandles[i]`, "At least one element in the specified array was null."). **Three corrections measured by `build-probe/1952_probe1_waithandle_multiwait.cpp`:** (1) there are **three** non-terminating shapes, not one -- `WaitAny({})`, `WaitAny({}, -1)` and `WaitAny({nullptr})` all spun forever, because a null element was skipped and left the poll loop with nothing to observe; (2) a `-2` timeout gave **two different** wrong answers, `true` for an empty collection and `false` for a non-empty one, since the negative deadline was already past; (3) a mixed `{handle, nullptr}` collection returned a plausible index by silently skipping the null -- the one pre-fix call that produced a *usable* answer, and now the sharpest observable change. .NET's `MaxWaitHandles` (64) ceiling is **deliberately not** reproduced: it exists because Win32 `WaitForMultipleObjects` caps at 64 and this port waits sequentially, so adopting it would reject input that works; the report's note about the unrepresentable ceiling is answered by documenting it in the header. Valid-input behaviour, including both `-1` special cases, is unchanged. +7 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-184](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/EventWaitHandle.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-184--medium--eventwaithandle-accepts-invalid-eventresetmode-values-instead-of-rejecting-them-at-construction) | medium | remediated | `EventWaitHandle.hpp`, `EventResetMode.hpp`, `ThreadingRemainingTests.cpp` | C++ accepts underlying mode 42 and gives mixed auto/manual behavior; .NET rejects it with an argument exception.  **Remediated (#1954, 2026-08-03, cause T-C of `docs/ThreadingNamespaceReviewPlan.md`):** the constructor rejects any value that is neither `AutoReset` nor `ManualReset` with `System::ArgumentException("Value of flags is invalid.", "mode")`. Rejection rather than normalisation is deliberate and is **not** unified with `ReaderWriterLockSlim`'s normalisation in the same ticket, because .NET does not unify them -- `EventWaitHandle` validates `mode`, `ReaderWriterLockSlim` stores a bool and derives its property. Measured incoherence before the change (`build-probe/1954_probe1_argument_domain.cpp`): with mode 42 the handle stayed signalled after `WaitOne` (`first=1 second_without_set=1`), because `Set()` took the AutoReset branch *for not being ManualReset* while `WaitOne()` skipped the reset *for not being AutoReset*. **Recorded uncertainty:** the reference tree is absent from this environment and the audit's managed probe records only "an argument exception", so the port throws the **base** `ArgumentException` -- caught by any handler written for either .NET candidate -- and the tests assert the category, not a derived type. +2 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-185](modules/io/include/System/BinaryData.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-185--medium--binarydatatostring-returns-malformed-utf-8-bytes-unchanged-instead-of-utf-8-decoding-them) | medium | confirmed | `BinaryData.hpp`, `Batch7Tests.cpp`, `BinaryDataTests.cpp` | C++ returns invalid byte `FF` as text while .NET UTF-8 decoding returns U+FFFD (`EFBFBD`); all direct cases use valid ASCII. **OWNER: #2106, deferred** (`docs/SystemIONamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4, §16). Establishing .NET's exact decoding needs the reference tree, which is absent, so nothing is changed. **PINNED by #2104** (`BinaryDataDeferredBehaviourPins`, 3 cases): `ToString()` of `0xFF` returns one byte `0xFF` and explicitly not `EF BF BD`, a truncated `E2 82` is equally untouched, and the valid-UTF-8 control is unchanged — so a resolution must edit a test and cannot land silently. `BinaryData.hpp` now states the divergence at the class and at `ToString()` instead of claiming "UTF-8 decoding". |
| [SR-AUD-186](modules/io/include/System/BinaryData.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-186--medium--binarydatafrombytesreadonlymemory-snapshots-bytes-that-current-net-deliberately-wraps) | medium | confirmed | `BinaryData.hpp`, `Batch7Tests.cpp`, `BinaryDataTests.cpp` | C++ preserves byte 01 after its source changes to 02, whereas current .NET stores/wraps the supplied ReadOnlyMemory and observes later contents. **OWNER: #2106, deferred. PREMISE INVERTED** (`docs/SystemIONamespaceReviewPlan.md` §6.1): the behaviour is reproduced exactly, but the finding's implicit direction — that the port is wrong — is backwards. .NET's is the **aliasing** behaviour and this port's is the **defensive** one, so "fixing" it means making `BinaryData` alias caller memory it does not own, introducing a borrowed-pointer lifetime hazard of exactly the CCF-019 shape into a type that today has none. It also needs a decision about which overload is meant: `BinaryData(byte[])` copies, `BinaryData(ReadOnlyMemory<byte>)` wraps. Neither is settleable with `/rv` absent. **PINNED by #2104** across **all four** construction doors, not the one the finding names, plus a test showing the copy outliving a source that is gone entirely. Two `FromBytes` doc-comments that said "by **wrapping** the provided ReadOnlyMemory" while copying were corrected — the header was documenting .NET's behaviour, not its own. |
| [SR-AUD-187](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/ThreadPool.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-187--high--unsafequeueuserworkitem-releases-a-borrowed-work-item-to-a-detached-thread-without-retaining-its-lifetime) | high | remediated | `ThreadPool.hpp`, `IThreadPoolWorkItem.hpp`, `Batch9ThreadingTests.cpp` | Detached work captures a raw pointer; an ASan probe deletes it after Execute starts and reports heap-use-after-free in the queued work item. **REMEDIATED (#1959, 2026-08-17), by OWNERSHIP rather than by a waiting destructor.** `ThreadPool::UnsafeQueueUserWorkItem` now takes `std::shared_ptr<IThreadPoolWorkItem>`. The boundary #2134/#2066/#2088 gave the other CCF-019 async members — the owner's destructor waits — is **not available here**: a DETACHED thread has no owner whose destructor could wait for it. Holding a share IS the boundary, and it is the direct counterpart of the GC reference .NET's queue entry is. **Public source break**, landed under `docs/StandingApprovals.md` SA-2 with all five conditions: `docs/Migration-BorrowedCallbackOwnership.md`, a site in `test/consumer/threading_borrowed_callback_negative.cpp`, downstream ticket **#2353**, the full gate, and the measured downstream report (zero sites in `cna` and `mobile-eggbert`). The test asserts BOTH halves: the item survives the caller dropping its reference, **and** `std::weak_ptr::expired()` becomes true once `Execute()` returns, so the repair is not a leak dressed up as a boundary. |
| [SR-AUD-188](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/RegisteredWaitHandle.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-188--high--registerwaitforsingleobject-accepts-a-null-waithandle-and-starts-a-background-null-dereference) | high | remediated | `ThreadPool.hpp`, `RegisteredWaitHandle.hpp`, `Batch9ThreadingTests.cpp` | Null registration returns into a worker that dereferences null and terminates an isolated C++ process, while .NET rejects input synchronously.  **Remediated (#1953, 2026-08-03, cause T-C of `docs/ThreadingNamespaceReviewPlan.md`):** `RegisteredWaitHandle`'s private constructor now runs `ArgumentNullException("waitObject")` then `ArgumentNullException("callBack")` before the `std::thread` is created, and `ThreadPool::RegisterWaitForSingleObject` runs `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("millisecondsTimeOutInterval")` first -- exactly .NET's split, where the public overload does `ThrowIfLessThan(millisecondsTimeOutInterval, -1)` and the private one it delegates to does the two `ThrowIfNull`s. The empty callback and the `-2` timeout are the two extra gaps the owning per-file report records under "Other missing assertions" and asks to be repaired *with* the null-wait crash path; both are closed here rather than deferred. Probe `build-probe/1953_probe1_null_argument_crashes.cpp` (forked children under `alarm(3)`): `threadpool.register.null_waitobject` moved from `child-signal:11(SEGV)` to a synchronous `ArgumentNullException(waitObject)`; the valid-registration control still fires. ASan/UBSan/LSan clean. |
| [SR-AUD-189](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/ThreadPool.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-189--medium--threadpool-configuration-setters-report-success-while-ignoring-requested-values-and-invalid-arguments) | medium | remediated | `ThreadPool.hpp`, `ThreadingRemainingTests.cpp` | C++ SetMinThreads(7,9) returns true but leaves 1,1 and accepts -1; managed success changes state and invalid input returns false.  **Remediated (#1971, 2026-08-03, split out of #1958 Group A).** `SetMinThreads`/`SetMaxThreads` validate, store, and return false for input they reject; `GetMinThreads`/`GetMaxThreads` report what was stored. **Wider than the probe the report quotes:** besides `SetMinThreads(7,9)` storing nothing and `SetMinThreads(-1,-1)` returning true, `SetMinThreads(0,0)`, `SetMaxThreads(0,0)` and a maximum below the current minimum also all returned true. Every rule's provenance is stated because `/rv/tmp/runtime/src/libraries/` is absent here: negative-rejected and valid-stored-and-observable are **measured** from the audit's managed probe, the min/max consistency rule and maximum >= 1 are **.NET-documented**, and accepting zero as a minimum is **reasoned**. .NET's further rule refusing a maximum below the processor count is **deliberately not adopted** -- unverifiable here and machine-dependent, the same reasoning as #1952's `MaxWaitHandles`. Measured consequence recorded: on this four-core container the default maximum is 8, so `SetMinThreads(7,9)` now returns **false** where the audit's 16-core managed probe got `True` -- the same rule, a machine-dependent default. `ThreadPool` is static-only, so the stored state lives in function-local statics behind one mutex and **no object layout exists to change**; the `int`->`intcs` correction from §3.1 item 4 is folded in and shown mangling-identical by `nm`. Concurrency: 4 threads x 2,000 iterations, 0 invariant violations, TSan 0 races. |
| [SR-AUD-190](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/Timer.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-190--medium--timer-accepts-an-empty-callback-and-silently-creates-a-timer-that-can-never-notify) | medium | remediated | `Timer.hpp`, `TimeProviderTests.cpp` | C++ empty Timer callback creates normally and run skips it; managed construction rejects null callback with ArgumentNullException. **Remediated (#1951, 2026-08-03, cause T-B of `docs/ThreadingNamespaceReviewPlan.md` = CCF-011 in `modules/threading`):** the constructor now throws `ArgumentNullException("callback")` after the `dueTime`/`period` range checks and before any side effect, reproducing .NET's own order (`Timer.cs`'s private constructor validates both ranges, then `TimerSetup` runs `ThrowIfNull(callback)`). Probe `build-probe/1951_probe1_threading_empty_callables.cpp`: `timer.empty_callback` moved from `normal` to `ArgumentNullException(callback)`; ASan/UBSan/LSan clean. |

| [SR-AUD-192](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/Thread.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-192--high--thread-starts-an-empty-stdfunction-on-a-detached-capable-worker-and-terminates-the-process) | high | remediated | `Thread.hpp`, `ThreadingTests.cpp`, `Batch8ThreadingTests.cpp` | Empty C++ start function reaches worker `bad_function_call` and terminates an isolated process, while managed Thread construction rejects null synchronously. **Remediated (#1951, 2026-08-03, cause T-B):** the constructor throws `ArgumentNullException("start")`, matching `Thread(ThreadStart)`'s `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(start)`, before a managed thread id is consumed and long before any OS thread exists. | [SR-AUD-198](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/CancellationToken.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-198--medium--cancellationtoken-register-accepts-an-empty-callback-and-turns-invalid-input-into-a-silent-no-op) | medium | remediated | `CancellationToken.hpp`, `CancellationToken.cpp`, `CancellationTokenSource.hpp` | C++ Register({}) succeeds and Cancel skips it; managed registration rejects null callback with ArgumentNullException. **Remediated (#1951, 2026-08-03, cause T-B):** `Register` throws `ArgumentNullException("callback")` before taking `state_->mutex`, so no id is allocated and nothing is recorded -- including on the already-cancelled fast path, where .NET's `callback ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(callback))` also wins over the immediate invocation. Probe: `cancellationtoken.register_empty` moved from `normal` to `ArgumentNullException(callback)`. |
| [SR-AUD-199](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/CancellationToken.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-199--high--public-null-state-cancellationtoken-construction-reaches-a-null-dereference) | high | remediated | `CancellationToken.hpp` | A public empty shared-state token reaches ASan/UBSan-confirmed null atomic dereference; managed callers cannot supply internal cancellation state.  **Remediated (#1953, 2026-08-03, cause T-C):** an **absent** shared state is now the never-cancellable token that .NET's null `_source` denotes, rather than a dereference -- `IsCancellationRequested` is `_source != null && _source.IsCancellationRequested`, `ThrowIfCancellationRequested` cannot fire, and `Register` gives back an inactive registration, which is .NET's own comment at that line (*"Nothing to do for tokens than can never reach the canceled state. Give back a dummy registration."*). Tolerance was chosen over rejection because it is .NET's model **and** because it is the only form that also closes a second route the finding does not mention: a **moved-from** token, whose implicitly declared move constructor leaves the source's `shared_ptr` empty and which SEGV'd identically (probe rows `cancellationtoken.moved_from.isrequested` / `.register`, both `child-signal:11(SEGV)` before and `normal` after). The constructor keeps its signature and accessibility, so nothing is narrowed or removed and no approval was required. The sibling `CancellationTokenRegistration` already used this idiom for its own empty state. ASan/UBSan/LSan clean; +9 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-200](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/PeriodicTimer.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-200--medium--periodictimer-accepts-fractional-periods-despite-its-whole-millisecond-contract) | medium | remediated | `PeriodicTimer.hpp` | A 1.5 ms period passes C++ validation and truncates to 1 ms although the documented contract requires whole milliseconds.  **Deliberately NOT repaired by #1954; stays `confirmed`, and the open question is carried by inactive ticket #1963 (no new `SR-AUD-*` identifier -- numbering stays frozen at 364).** Two reasons, recorded in `docs/ThreadingNamespaceReviewPlan.md` 18.2. (1) This finding's own per-file report carries **no managed probe** for the row, unlike SR-AUD-184/205/213, so the divergence it asserts is between the port's *doc-comment* and the port's *code*, not between the port and .NET. (2) .NET's `PeriodicTimer` converts its period with `(long)period.TotalMilliseconds`, a **truncating** cast -- the same idiom `Timer(TimerCallback, object?, TimeSpan, TimeSpan)` uses -- so .NET appears to accept 1.5 ms as 1 ms, and rejecting it here would be a *narrowing away from .NET* that refuses input `TimeSpan` arithmetic legitimately produces. The reference tree `/rv/tmp/runtime/src/libraries/` is **not present** in the environment that ran #1954, so neither reading could be confirmed and no behaviour was changed. **REMEDIATED (#1963, 2026-08-17) — and this finding's CONCLUSION IS REFUTED.** The reference tree is available in this container (`docs/StandingApprovals.md` §5.1) and `PeriodicTimer.TryGetMilliseconds` (`PeriodicTimer.cs:110-121`) reads `long ms = (long)value.TotalMilliseconds;` followed by `if ((ms >= 1 && ms <= Timer.MaxSupportedTimeout) || value == Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan)`. The cast is truncating and runs **before** the range test, so .NET accepts a 1.5 ms period and schedules it as 1 ms. #1954's second reason was therefore correct and the finding's remedy — *reject the input* — would have narrowed **away** from .NET. What was actually wrong was the port's **doc-comment**, which promised a whole-millisecond contract .NET does not have; it now states the truncation and quotes the reference. **One real divergence was found while checking, which the finding did not name:** the port compared the *double* against the ceiling where .NET compares the *truncated* count, so a period of `Timer.MaxSupportedTimeout + 0.5` ms was rejected here and is accepted by .NET. The comparison now runs on the truncated value. +1 test; the fractional-period case is renamed `PeriodicTimer_FractionalPeriodIsTruncated_NotRejected` and is no longer a pin on an unendorsed status quo, and `PeriodicTimer_CeilingIsTestedAfterTruncation` covers the ceiling. No signature, layout or vtable change. |
| [SR-AUD-201](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/PeriodicTimer.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-201--medium--concurrent-waitfornexttick-consumers-each-consume-the-same-scheduled-tick) | medium | confirmed | `PeriodicTimer.hpp` | Two simultaneous C++ waits both return true for one 20 ms tick, despite current .NET's one-consumer-at-a-time contract. |
| [SR-AUD-202](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/Monitor.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-202--high--monitorwait-deadlocks-when-the-caller-holds-a-recursive-monitor-more-than-once) | high | remediated | `Monitor.hpp`, `ThreadingTests.cpp` | A two-level Monitor.Wait retains one recursive mutex level, preventing the signaler Enter/Pulse path; bounded probe times out after two seconds.  **Remediated (#2341, 2026-08-12, cause T-E/2, split out of the blocked #1957):** `Wait` now reads the calling thread's recursion depth, releases every level for the duration of the wait and restores exactly that many on reacquisition, so a depth-2 or depth-3 waiter no longer keeps the mutex and the signalling thread can Enter/Pulse. Measured `build-probe/2341_probe1_monitor_depth`: depths 2 and 3 TIMEOUT before, `ok` after, with the caller's depth proved restored (three Exits succeed, a fourth throws, and only then can another thread take the monitor). `Monitor` is a static-only class whose per-pointer state lives in a private static registry, so **no public object layout, signature, vtable or exception contract changes** and §9 approval question 2 -- which names only `ReaderWriterLockSlim` and `PeriodicTimer` -- does not reach it; `docs/ThreadingNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §20.2 item 1 had already recorded that this member could be split out and landed without approval once the depth bookkeeping was proven exact. TSan: 0 reports before and after. **#1957 stays blocked** and keeps SR-AUD-201/204/210. |
| [SR-AUD-203](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/ReaderWriterLockSlim.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-203--high--dispose-races-with-entry-and-permits-disposal-while-the-caller-owns-a-lock) | high | confirmed | `ReaderWriterLockSlim.hpp`, `ThreadingRemainingTests.cpp` | Unsynchronized `disposed_` read/write is TSan-confirmed UB during concurrent entry/Dispose, and Dispose succeeds while its caller owns a read lock where .NET throws SynchronizationLockException.  **SPLIT by cause; race half remediated (#1955, 2026-08-03, cause T-A):** `disposed_` is now `std::atomic<bool>`, release-stored by `Dispose()` and acquire-loaded by `throwIfDisposed()`, so the TSan-confirmed race with every `TryEnter*` route is gone (`build-probe/1955_probe1_shared_state_races.cpp`, scenario `rwls.disposed_vs_entry`: one report before, none after). `sizeof` 120 -> 120. **The dispose-while-held half stays open and is approval-gated ticket #1956** (cause T-G): disposal still succeeds while the caller owns a mode, where .NET throws `SynchronizationLockException`. The finding may not be marked remediated until both halves land. |
| [SR-AUD-204](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/ReaderWriterLockSlim.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-204--high--queued-writers-do-not-block-new-readers-and-can-be-starved-indefinitely) | high | confirmed | `ReaderWriterLockSlim.hpp`, `ThreadingRemainingTests.cpp` | The state machine tracks no queued writers, so a new C++ reader enters while a writer waits; .NET rejects that immediate reader, preserving writer-preference and liveness. |
| [SR-AUD-205](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/ReaderWriterLockSlim.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-205--medium--invalid-lockrecursionpolicy-values-are-retained-rather-than-normalized-to-norecursion) | medium | remediated | `ReaderWriterLockSlim.hpp`, `LockRecursionPolicy.hpp`, `ThreadingRemainingTests.cpp` | An underlying policy value 2 is stored and reflected as 2, whereas current .NET accepts it but exposes NoRecursion (0); valid-ordinal tests omit the boundary.  **Remediated (#1954, 2026-08-03, cause T-C):** the constructor normalises anything other than `SupportsRecursion` to `NoRecursion`, reproducing .NET's `_fIsReentrant = (recursionPolicy == LockRecursionPolicy.SupportsRecursion)` plus a property derived from that bool, so no undeclared value can be read back. **Correction measured by `build-probe/1954_probe1_argument_domain.cpp`:** only the *property* diverged. The behavioural half was already correct -- `isReentrant()` tests for `SupportsRecursion`, so policy 2 already locked like `NoRecursion` and already threw `LockRecursionException` on recursive entry (probe row `rwls.policy2.recursion_rejected` reported "recursion rejected" **before** the change). The stored value, the reported value and the behaviour now agree. `rwls.policy2.property` moved 2 -> 0. +3 tests. |
| [SR-AUD-206](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/Semaphore.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-206--high--release-overflows-signed-count-arithmetic-before-detecting-a-full-semaphore) | high | remediated | `Semaphore.hpp`, `SemaphoreSlim.hpp`, `ThreadingTests.cpp` | Both Release implementations add valid signed counts before their maximum guard; UBSan confirms overflow for count 1 plus INT_MAX and both return normally where .NET throws SemaphoreFullException. |

That is exactly SR-AUD-210, the defect approval-gated ticket #1957 exists to remove, and #1955 must not add a second instance of it. `ThreadingSharedStateTests.Barrier_ParticipantCountReadableFromPostPhaseAction` pins the property remains callable from there. | [SR-AUD-213](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/SpinWait.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-213--medium--spinuntil-skips-required-timeout-and-callback-validation) | medium | remediated | `SpinWait.hpp`, `ThreadingRemainingTests.cpp` | C++ accepts -2 timeout as false and delays an empty callback to native bad_function_call, while .NET rejects the two inputs with ArgumentOutOfRangeException and ArgumentNullException. **SPLIT by cause; callable half remediated (#1951, 2026-08-03, cause T-B):** both `SpinUntil` overloads now throw `ArgumentNullException("condition")` before the first spin, so the infinite-timeout form fails instead of looping forever on an uninvocable condition. **The -2 timeout half stays open and is ticket #1954 (cause T-C)**; when it lands its check goes *above* the condition check, reproducing .NET's `ThrowIfLessThan(millisecondsTimeout, -1)`-then-`ThrowIfNull(condition)` order. The finding may not be marked remediated until both halves land.  **Timeout half remediated (#1954, 2026-08-03, cause T-C); SR-AUD-213 is now fully `remediated`.** `SpinUntil(condition, millisecondsTimeout)` runs `ArgumentOutOfRangeException::ThrowIfLessThan(millisecondsTimeout, -1, "millisecondsTimeout")` **above** the `condition` check added by #1951, reproducing .NET's `ThrowIfLessThan` -> `ThrowIfNull` order, so a call invalid in both ways reports the timeout. A `-2` timeout previously returned `false`, which a caller cannot tell apart from a legitimate expiry (probe row `spinwait.timeout_minus2` moved from `false` to `ArgumentOutOfRangeException(millisecondsTimeout)`). Both valid special cases (`-1`, `0`) are unchanged. +3 tests. |

The report's second, sharper half is confirmed too: a **reentrant** write from inside the handler used to be overwritten by the delayed outer assignment (a handler writing 99 left the value at 5; now 99 survives). The equal-value no-op is untouched and separately pinned. Concurrency capability proved first -- the ordering scenario reported **8,000 violations against the pre-fix header, every iteration, and 0 after**; TSan 0 data races both runs, ASan+UBSan+LSan 0 reports. No signature, layout, vtable or edge change: the repair is the order of two statements. |

| [SR-AUD-217](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/LazyInitializer.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-217--medium--an-empty-native-factory-is-deferred-to-bad_function_call-instead-of-preserving-managed-null-delegate-behavior) | medium | remediated | `LazyInitializer.hpp`, `ThreadingRemainingTests.cpp` | Empty C++ factory input becomes late `bad_function_call` only if target is null, where current .NET faults through its null-delegate path; no local fixture covers it. **Remediated (#1951, 2026-08-03, cause T-B) -- and this is the one family member whose .NET answer is NOT `ArgumentNullException`.** `LazyInitializer.EnsureInitialized` performs no null check: it is `Volatile.Read(ref target) ?? EnsureInitializedCore(...)`, whose core simply calls `valueFactory()`, so .NET raises `NullReferenceException` and an already-initialized target suppresses the fault entirely. Both the exception type and the data-dependence are therefore reproduced deliberately; the defect closed is the *hierarchy* -- `std::bad_function_call` does not derive from `System::Exception`, so ported `catch (const Exception&)` could not see it. Probe: `lazyinit.empty_factory_null_target` moved from `bad_function_call` to `NullReferenceException`, `lazyinit.empty_factory_set_target` stays `normal`. |

| [SR-AUD-219](modules/threading/include/System/Threading/ThreadLocal.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-219--medium--empty-factories-and-isvaluecreated-after-dispose-bypass-the-managed-validation-contract) | medium | confirmed | `ThreadLocal.hpp`, `ThreadingRemainingTests.cpp` | An empty factory is deferred to `bad_function_call` rather than constructor ArgumentNullException, and C++ returns false from disposed IsValueCreated where .NET throws ObjectDisposedException. **SPLIT by cause; factory half remediated (#1951, 2026-08-03, cause T-B):** both factory constructors now throw `ArgumentNullException("valueFactory")` from the constructor body, matching .NET's `ThrowIfNull(valueFactory)`. **Measured correction to this finding's stated consequence:** the stored empty factory did *not* reach `bad_function_call` at first access -- `getValueProperty()` wrote `factory_ ? factory_() : T{}`, so it silently produced a default-constructed value on every thread and raised nothing at all (probe `threadlocal.empty_factory_value=normal` before the change). The observable was a *silent wrong value*, not a deferred crash; the ternary stays because the two non-factory constructors legitimately default. **The IsValueCreated-after-Dispose half stays open and is ticket #1956 (cause T-G, approval-gated).** The finding may not be marked remediated until both halves land. |

A slot has no destructor a caller controls, so — as with SR-AUD-187 — ownership is the only available boundary, and it is what .NET's thread-static GC reference already provides. **Public source break** in three spellings (the setter's parameter, the property's return type, and storing that return in a raw pointer), each with its own negative-fixture site. `SetSynchronizationContext(nullptr)` is unchanged and is deliberately left outside every site, which is what records that.
