| [SR-AUD-230](modules/threading-tasks/include/System/Threading/Tasks/TaskCanceledException.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-230--high--the-public-task-property-retains-a-dangling-raw-pointer-after-its-source-task-is-destroyed) | high | remediated | `TaskCanceledException.hpp`, `TaskCanceledException.cpp`, `TasksTests.cpp` | Task-taking construction stores a raw non-owning Task pointer; ASan reports stack-use-after-scope when an exception exposes it after a local Task dies. **REMEDIATED (#1970, 2026-08-17), at no public-signature cost.** The constructor still takes `const Task*` and the property still returns `const Task*`; what changed is that the exception now OWNS what it points at. A `Task` in this port is a **handle** over a `std::shared_ptr<State>`, so the exception stores a copy of the handle: the state stays alive for as long as the exception is reachable, **and** stays observed, so a status change after construction is visible through `getTaskProperty()`. That second half is what makes it .NET's contract rather than a safe approximation — `TaskCanceledException.Task` there is a GC-tracked reference to the same object, not a snapshot. `std::shared_ptr<const Task>` rather than `std::optional<Task>` because `Task.hpp` includes this header, so a by-value member would be a circular include. **Layout: `sizeof` 192 → 200**, `alignof` 8 unchanged, no vtable or `noexcept` change; landed under `docs/StandingApprovals.md` SA-3 with the figures pinned as a relationship. **One pre-existing test was INVERTED, and the reason matters**: `TaskCtor_StoresTaskPointer` asserted ADDRESS identity, which is precisely the borrowing this finding reports, and is also the wrong analogue of .NET's contract — there `Task` is a reference type, here it is a value-semantics handle, so the counterpart of *the same object* is *the same state*. +4 tests; the mutation that borrows the caller's pointer again through `shared_ptr`'s aliasing constructor is caught. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero `TaskCanceledException` sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. `docs/Migration-TaskCanceledExceptionOwnsItsTask.md`. **CCF-019 is NOT closed by this** — the async-capture members (#1959, #2066, #2088, #2134) and the owned-tree members remain open. |
| [SR-AUD-231](modules/threading-tasks/include/System/Threading/Tasks/Task.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-231--medium--task-entry-points-accept-empty-delegates-and-defer-a-managed-argument-error-into-an-asynchronous-bad_function_call) | medium | remediated | `Task.hpp`, `TaskFactory.hpp`, `Parallel.hpp`, `TasksTests.cpp` | Empty native action/function/continuation values return a task and fault later with bad_function_call, where current .NET synchronously rejects null delegates. **Remediated (#1965, 2026-08-03, cause TC-A — CCF-011 in a third module).** Enumerated by measurement as **22 public entries, not the two named** (eleven distinct bodies edited; the rest inherit the check by forwarding): every `Task`/`TaskT`/`TaskFactory`/`Parallel` entry now decides emptiness at the public boundary, before any worker is started or continuation registered, with .NET's own parameter name (`action`, `function`, `continuationAction`, `continuationFunction`, `body`). Four premises corrected: (1) **`Parallel`'s failure was catchable** — it already arrived as `System::AggregateException`, so CCF-011's "wrong type" consequence applies only to the sixteen Task/TaskT/TaskFactory entries; (2) **`Parallel::Invoke` is not an `ArgumentNullException` site** — .NET reports a null *element* with a bare `ArgumentException(SR.Parallel_Invoke_ActionNull)`, the same §17.1-shaped trap that caught #1951; (3) the failure was **data-dependent in two shapes the finding does not name** — a zero-length range/empty source ran no iteration, and an already-cancelled token short-circuited before the action, so both returned normally; (4) **`ContinueWith` did register** the empty continuation and returned a faulted task rather than an error. Zero `bad_function_call` and zero deferred aggregates remain reachable from any public entry; ASan+UBSan+LSan 0→0; +37 tests (171→208); no signature, layout, vtable or edge change. |
| [SR-AUD-232](modules/threading-tasks/include/System/Threading/Tasks/Parallel.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-232--medium--invalid-maximum-degrees-are-silently-converted-to-hardware-concurrency-instead-of-rejected) | medium | remediated | `Parallel.hpp`, `TasksTests.cpp` | Parallel.For accepts zero and -2 maximum degree by substituting hardware concurrency; current .NET rejects both values and only permits -1 as the sentinel. **Remediated (#1966, 2026-08-03, cause TC-B/1).** Valid values are -1 (unlimited) and every value >= 1; 0 and every value <= -2 now throw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("MaxDegreeOfParallelism")` before any iteration is dispatched (`ran = 0` measured in every rejecting case). Three premises recorded: (1) **the repair cannot be placed where .NET places it** -- .NET validates in the `ParallelOptions` *setter*, but this port's field is a public mutable data member with nowhere to put a check, so validation moves to the one method that reads it; the exception type and parameter name are .NET's and only the point of detection differs, the same shape that gates SR-AUD-235 as #1969; (2) **the degree check must precede #1965's `body` check** -- measured on the intermediate tree it did not, and .NET's setter necessarily runs before its own body null check; (3) **one site**, since this port has no `ForEach` options overload. The -1 -> `hardware_concurrency()` realisation is unchanged and is a *worker* count, not a build-job count. ASan+UBSan+LSan 0 reports; +10 tests (208->218); no signature, layout, vtable or edge change. |
| [SR-AUD-233](modules/threading-channels/include/System/Threading/Channels/ChannelOptions.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-233--medium--valid-zero-capacity-channels-are-silently-changed-into-one-element-buffers) | medium | remediated | `ChannelOptions.hpp`, `Channel.hpp`, `ChannelTests.cpp` | C++ zero-capacity TryWrite buffers and TryRead returns an item, while .NET accepts zero as a rendezvous channel and both immediate calls fail without a peer. **Remediated (#1968, 2026-08-03, cause TC-B/2).** A zero-capacity channel is now a rendezvous: `TryWrite` and `TryRead` return false with no waiting peer, a parked reader is handed a writer's item directly, `WriteAsync` blocks until a reader arrives, and `Count` stays 0. Every non-zero capacity, the unbounded channel, the prioritized channel and the drop modes at capacity >= 1 are byte-identical. **Contradicting prior evidence resolved and recorded:** `Channel.hpp`'s own comment claimed the opposite -- that it was "verified against BoundedChannel.cs" that a capacity-0 channel is "observably equivalent to a capacity-1 channel for every publicly-visible outcome". The finding's managed *behavioural* probe was preferred over the comment's *reading* of source, the comment was replaced rather than dropped, and the limitation is stated: `/rv/tmp/runtime/src/libraries/` is absent here, so the reading could not be adjudicated directly and the repair is revertible without touching a signature. Drop modes at capacity 0 (no evidence either way) discard the item and keep Count at 0, reasoned rather than measured. **Layout:** every public type is unchanged (`Channel<int>` 32, `ChannelReader/Writer<int>` 24, `ChannelOptions` 16, `BoundedChannelOptions` 24, both impls 40, all align 8, now pinned by `static_assert`); only the internal `detail::ChannelState<int>` grew 240 -> 248 for the waiting-peer counter, in an INTERFACE target where nothing can be linked across versions. Round-based TSan probe: 600 wrong outcomes before, 0 after, 0 data races in both runs; ASan+UBSan+LSan 0 reports. **The two zero-capacity tests that pinned the wrong behaviour were rewritten, not deleted**, each old concern keeping a test. +12 tests (52->64). |
| [SR-AUD-234](modules/threading-channels/include/System/Threading/Channels/Channel.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-234--medium--readasync-leaks-the-close-error-instead-of-throwing-channelclosedexception-with-it-as-the-cause) | medium | remediated | `Channel.hpp`, `ChannelClosedException.hpp`, `ChannelTests.cpp` | Error-completed C++ ReadAsync rethrows the raw close error; .NET throws ChannelClosedException whose InnerException retains it. **Remediated (#1967, 2026-08-03, cause TC-C).** `ReadAsync` on an error-completed channel now throws `ChannelClosedException` with the writer's exception retained as its inner exception, on both the FIFO and the prioritized shapes; `WaitToReadAsync`, `WaitToWriteAsync` and `Completion` keep exposing the cause **unwrapped**, and buffered items are still drained before the closure is reported. **Premise corrected: `WriteAsync` has the identical defect**, which the finding explicitly denies ("only ReadAsync loses its API-specific closed channel boundary") -- a cleanly completed channel gave it a `ChannelClosedException` but an error-completed one let the raw producer error escape, from the same `if (!WaitToXAsync()) throw` shape, and .NET routes both through `ChannelUtilities.GetInvalidCompletionValueTask`. Repaired together as the same defect at a second site, with **no new SR-AUD identifier**. A `ChannelClosedException` raised by a consumer subclass is passed through rather than double-wrapped. Round-based concurrency probe (four scenarios x 400 rounds, latch-released completer) reports **3,600 wrong outcomes before, 0 after**, proving the probe schedules the interleaving; TSan 0 races in both runs (fully instrumented from source, 21 `__tsan` symbols), ASan+UBSan+LSan 0 reports. +13 tests (39->52); no signature, layout, vtable or edge change. |

| [SR-AUD-238](modules/timers/include/System/Timers/Timer.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-238--high--an-exception-from-elapsed-escapes-the-background-stdthread-and-aborts-the-process) | high | remediated | `Timer.hpp`, `Timer.cpp`, `TimerTests.cpp` | An exception from native Elapsed escapes the worker std::thread and aborts the process; current .NET catches event-handler exceptions. **Remediated (#2154, 2026-08-09):** one `catch (...)` around the whole callback body in `System::Timers::Timer::startTimerThread`, silent, matching .NET's `Timer.MyTimerCallback` whose `throw_process=alive` this report itself measured. **Two premise extensions:** (1) the escape point is `System::Threading::Timer::run` in modules/threading, not the modules/timers files this finding names -- but the repair still belongs here, because .NET's `System.Threading.Timer` also crashes on an unhandled callback exception, so the layer below is already correct and is left unchanged; (2) the abort was universal AND non-local -- 7 of 7 SIGABRT across `std::`, sharp-runtime and non-`std` exception types, one-shot and periodic, first fire and after three fires, and an entirely unrelated second timer died with it, because the failure is process death rather than thread death. `catch (...)` rather than `catch (const std::exception&)`: `throw 42` is a measured case. +10 tests (9 -> 19), every wait a condition variable rather than a sleep. Mutation testing found and fixed a real test weakness -- narrowing the catch SURVIVED the first version of the suite because the unwind happens on a background thread and the tests stopped waiting when the handler signalled; each case now waits for a SENTINEL timer to fire five more times after the throw. ASan/UBSan/LSan and TSan: 0 reports each. No signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. Pinned, not changed: a second subscribed handler is not reached when the first throws, matching a C# multicast delegate. |
| [SR-AUD-239](modules/timers/include/System/Timers/Timer.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-239--medium--elapsed-reports-a-null-sender-rather-than-the-timer-that-raised-the-event) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `Timer.hpp`, `Timer.cpp`, `TimerTests.cpp` | C++ `Elapsed.Raise(nullptr, ...)` gives handlers null, while current .NET passes the raising Timer instance. **Design complete, implementation BLOCKED (#2155, 2026-08-09):** `nullptr` is not a forgotten argument -- it is the only value that COMPILES. `EventHandler<T>::Raise` types its sender as `System::Object*`, `std::is_convertible_v<Timer*, Object*>` is 0, and `System::Object` is abstract and polymorphic, so passing the timer requires giving `Timer` that base. Measured cost (`build-probe/2153_probe4_layout.log`): `sizeof(Timer)` **104 -> 112**, `std::is_polymorphic_v` false -> true, every data member's offset shifts by 8, and five virtual members arrive with the base, one of which (`GetTypeName`) must be implemented because `Object` is abstract. An object-layout AND vtable change on a public type, which the repository's settled rules (#1788, #1789) require explicit per-action approval for. No compatible alternative exists: there is no `void*` sender overload, the sender is a parameter rather than a property of `ElapsedEventArgs`, and handing out some other `Object*` would be worse than null. Current behaviour is pinned by a test AND by compile-time `static_assert`s in two test files, so the change cannot land silently. |
| [SR-AUD-240](modules/net-security/include/System/Net/Security/SslApplicationProtocol.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-240--medium--valid-alpn-protocol-hashing-executes-signed-overflow-undefined-behavior) | medium | confirmed | `SslApplicationProtocol.hpp`, `SecuritySupportTests.cpp` | Valid 255-byte ALPN data makes the signed C++ hash accumulator overflow under UBSan, where .NET defines unchecked Int32 wrapping. |
| [SR-AUD-241](modules/io-isolated-storage/include/System/IO/IsolatedStorage/IsolatedStorageFile.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-241--high--an-absolute-caller-path-bypasses-the-isolated-storage-root) | high | remediated | `IsolatedStorageFile.hpp`, `IsolatedStorageFile.cpp`, `IsolatedStorageFileStream.cpp`, `IsolatedStorageConfinementTests.cpp` | An absolute POSIX caller path makes `root / path` discard root, so file and directory operations escape their isolated store; current .NET strips leading separators first.  **Remediated (#2204, 2026-08-10, `docs/SystemIOIsolatedStorageNamespaceReviewPlan.md`).** The finding is **wider than the audit measured**: not one door but **thirteen caller path arguments across ten members**, and all four effect classes escape -- an outside file *read*, an outside file and an outside directory *deleted*, files *created and written* outside, store content *moved out*, and outside content *imported in* through `CopyFile`'s source and `MoveDirectory`'s source.  Lexical `..` traversal escapes too (the owning file report explicitly declined to count it), as do statically planted symbolic links at final, intermediate and chained components, so **.NET's leading-separator strip closes the named probe and leaves all of that open**.  `fullPath()` is now a validating resolver run before any filesystem effect: reject an embedded NUL, strip leading separators (.NET `GetFullPath` parity, so a rooted path is *reinterpreted* store-relative), reject what strips to nothing, reject a surviving drive/UNC root, require lexical containment, then require containment again after `weakly_canonical` resolves every link.  The audit's own probe inverts: `escaped_exists` **1 -> 0** and `root_child_exists` **0 -> 1**.  A **real bug was found while testing**: `CopyFile`/`MoveFile`/`MoveDirectory` validated inside the call expression, and C++ leaves call-argument evaluation order unspecified -- GCC evaluated right-to-left, so a doubly invalid call named the *destination* parameter; both are now resolved into locals in declared order.  Two adjacent post-audit defects closed with it: four members missing the disposed guard (#2205) and four doors leaking `std::filesystem_error` (#2206).  The module's **first dedicated test executable** (`SharpRuntimeTests_IO_IsolatedStorage`, 58 tests, gate 37 -> 38 executables) carries a discriminating control proving the raw join still escapes, over-rejection guards, and before/after snapshots proving a rejected call changes neither tree; 7 of 7 mutations killed.  ASan+UBSan+LSan and non-recovering UBSan over the changed body: exit 0, zero reports, with a deliberate out-of-bounds control firing in the same build.  **Two residuals are recorded, not folded in**: the check-then-use TOCTOU race (#2207, blocked) and `IsolatedStorageFileStream`'s unconfined public constructor (#2208, blocked on a public signature change). |

| [SR-AUD-247](modules/net-websockets/include/System/Net/WebSockets/ClientWebSocket.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-247--high--asynchronous-operations-retain-raw-clientwebsocket-pointers-after-destruction) | high | remediated | `ClientWebSocket.hpp`, `ClientWebSocket.cpp` | ASan confirms the task lambda dereferences raw ClientWebSocket this after stack destruction; all client async operations share the capture pattern. **REMEDIATED (#2088, 2026-08-17) with the liveness boundary #2134 established for this family.** Every `*Async` member captured raw `this` in a body `std::async` starts immediately, with nothing keeping the object alive until it ran. .NET needs no boundary because the GC does that job; C++ has no such mechanism, so the destructor waits. **No public signature changed** and the existing layout probe gained exactly one member, under `docs/StandingApprovals.md` SA-3. **The finding's wider half is also repaired**: `SendAsync` captured the CALLER's vector by reference, which the boundary cannot help with because the buffer is not this object, so the bytes it will send are now copied. `ReceiveAsync` keeps its reference deliberately — that parameter is the OUT-parameter the result is written into, so a caller that destroys it has discarded what it asked for. The copy is guaranteed by construction rather than by a deterministic test, and the record says so: a case that mutated the caller's buffer after the call was written, measured against the borrowing mutation, found to **pass anyway** because `std::async` wins that race, and removed rather than kept as a test that cannot fail. Two implementation facts carried over from #2134 and worth not rediscovering: the decrement must be **body-local**, because `std::async` keeps the callable alive until the last future to its shared state is destroyed, so a guard captured BY the lambda is released when the CALLER drops the task and the boundary then waits for the caller; and the boundary must be **crossable**, so it wakes a blocked worker before waiting rather than turning a use-after-free into a hang. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. **CCF-019 is NOT closed** — #1959 remains, as do the owned-tree members SR-AUD-327 and SR-AUD-333. |
| [SR-AUD-248](modules/net-websockets/include/System/Net/WebSockets/ClientWebSocketOptions.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-248--high--setrequestheader-accepts-crlf-and-serializes-caller-text-directly-into-the-http-upgrade-request) | high | remediated | `ClientWebSocketOptions.hpp`, `ClientWebSocket.cpp` | C++ accepts CR/LF header text and serializes it into the upgrade request, permitting injected handshake headers where .NET rejects the input. **Remediated (#2089, 2026-08-04).** Reproduced exactly (`build-probe/2089_probe1_before.log`): a six-field handshake request became an **eight-field** one carrying a smuggled `GET /admin HTTP/1.1`, because `performHandshake` concatenates `name + ": " + value + "\r\n"` verbatim. **SCOPE CORRECTION, the load-bearing one:** the finding names `SetRequestHeader`, but the **request URI is a third door** -- the request line is built from `uri.getPathAndQueryProperty()` and `Host:` from `uri.getHostProperty()`, and `System::Uri` preserves CR/LF/NUL in both (`build-probe/2089_probe2_uri_door.log`). Closing only the options doors would have left request smuggling open. This is the same door `#2063` found missing from `SR-AUD-313`'s paraphrase, in a second namespace. `System::Uri` is NOT modified; the Uri-side defect stays the separate blocked ticket #2003. The predicate has exactly **one body** in the repository: it moved down to `System::Net::detail::ContainsProtocolFieldTerminator` in the `Net` component both protocol modules already depend on, with `System::Net::Http::detail::ContainsProtocolControlCharacter` kept as a forwarder -- no `System::Net::Http` call site, exception type or message changed, and the module graph is unchanged at 41 modules / 91 edges. Exception identity is `System::ArgumentException` (not `System::Net::Http`'s `FormatException`), recorded as **this port's choice** because the sibling `AddSubProtocol` check in the same class already reported invalid characters that way; the rejected text is never echoed. Rejection precedes any byte on the wire: over 20 rejected connections the `/proc/self/fd` delta is 0 and all 20 threw. +16 tests add-only, three mutations discriminating 5/2/3, ASan/UBSan/LSan clean over 52 rejections and 19 acceptances with `ClientWebSocket.cpp` compiled from source plus a live-instrumentation control. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. `docs/Migration-WebSocketProtocolStrictness.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-249](modules/net-websockets/include/System/Net/WebSockets/ClientWebSocketOptions.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-249--medium--addsubprotocol-accepts-http-token-separators-that-net-rejects) | medium | remediated | `ClientWebSocketOptions.hpp` | C++ accepts `chat,evil` as one subprotocol despite HTTP-token separators, then changes the advertised protocol list; .NET throws ArgumentException. **Remediated (#2089, 2026-08-04). PREMISE CORRECTED in the port's favour and against it at once** (`docs/SystemNetWebSocketsNamespaceReviewPlan.md` 6.2): a subprotocol validator **did** already exist and already rejected `c <= 0x20` and `0x7F`, so the finding is right in its example and wrong in implying no validation existed -- the repair **widens** an existing check rather than creating one. Measured before (`build-probe/2089_probe1_before.log`): **not one** of the seventeen RFC 7230 separators `( ) < > @ , ; : \ " / [ ] ? = { }` was rejected, so `chat,evil` and `a;b` were stored as a SINGLE subprotocol and joined into `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` with `", "` -- the caller advertised two protocols where the API reported one. All seventeen are now rejected while every RFC 7230 `tchar` (`-`, `.`, `_`, `+`, `!`, `#`, `$`, `%`, `&`, `'`, `*`, `^`, backtick, `|`, `~`, digits, letters) stays accepted and the pre-existing empty/space/tab/DEL/CR/LF/NUL rejections and the case-insensitive duplicate check are unchanged. A subprotocol never was a CR/LF/NUL door -- all three are `<= 0x20` and were already caught. Mutation M2 (separator set neutered) failed exactly 2 tests. |

| [SR-AUD-257](modules/io-compression/src/System/IO/Compression/DeflateStream.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-257--high--compression-streams-accept-a-null-inner-stream-and-later-dereference-it) | high | remediated | `DeflateStream.cpp`, `GZipStream.cpp`, `ZLibStream.cpp` | Null inner streams constructed successfully and crashed at first use: a large incompressible `Write` in Compress mode and a `Read` in Decompress mode each reached the null inner stream, six ASan-confirmed SEGVs, two per type — the `Read` half was not in the finding text. Ticket #1811 throws `ArgumentNullException("stream")` from all three constructors, before zlib initialisation so a rejected call allocates no compressor state. The `inner_` guards in `Close()` deliberately REMAIN: unlike StreamReader's, they are reachable, because `Close()` itself nulls the member. SR-AUD-258, which shares these files, stays `confirmed`. |
| [SR-AUD-258](modules/io-compression/src/System/IO/Compression/DeflateStream.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-258--medium--compression-streams-silently-accept-invalid-modes-and-post-close-operations) | medium | remediated | stream implementations, `CompressionMode.hpp` | A cast mode 42 creates a deflater while both capability properties are false, and Write after Close silently returns; current .NET rejects the mode and throws ObjectDisposedException after close. **Remediated (#2148, 2026-08-09):** all three constructors reject a `CompressionMode` outside the two declared members with the base `ArgumentException("Enum value was out of legal range.", "mode")` — the category the audit's own managed probe recorded for .NET, `/rv` being absent to narrow it — placed after the null-stream check and before `deflateInit2`/`inflateInit2`. **Two premise corrections, both measured:** (1) the out-of-domain mode is a guaranteed LEAK, not only a capability inconsistency — the constructor splits on `Decompress` while `Close()` splits on `Compress`, so a cast value took the deflate arm in and the inflate arm out, and `inflateEnd` on a `deflateInit2` stream frees nothing; LSan measured 5,952 direct + 65,536 indirect bytes leaked per object in all three types, with the caller's payload silently discarded (`build-probe/2148_probe2_before_after.log`); (2) the closed-state defect is on THREE doors, not the one the finding names — `Read` answered 0 and `Flush` returned silently after `Close()` too, on all three types and both `leaveOpen` values. All three now throw `ObjectDisposedException(type, "Cannot access a closed Stream.")`, with buffer-argument validation still running first (.NET's order) and the closed check ahead of the `count == 0` early return. Two wrong-mode doors on an OPEN stream are deliberately PINNED, not changed, and move to new ticket #2152. +14 tests (75 -> 89); three counting mutations; UBSan 0 reports; no signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. |
| [SR-AUD-259](modules/io-compression/src/System/IO/Compression/DeflateEncoder.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-259--medium--compressionstrategy-is-stored-but-never-reaches-any-encoder-and-stream-option-constructors-are-absent) | medium | confirmed | encoders, streams, `ZLibCompressionOptions.hpp` | Every options encoder hard-codes default strategy; native default/RLE output is identical, and all three C++ stream types omit current .NET's public options constructors. **STRATEGY HALF REMEDIATED (#2149, 2026-08-09); the finding stays `confirmed` for its second half.** Evidence widened from the report's single default-vs-RLE pair to 3 encoders x 5 strategies x 3 payload shapes: 45 of 45 outputs were byte-identical to Default before, with a straight zlib encode of the same parameters diverging in 24 of them; after, 0 of 45 diverge from zlib and the 21 still equal to Default are genuine agreements (Filtered really does equal Default at level 6, and stored blocks ignore strategy). One mapping, Detail::ResolveZLibStrategy, defined once with five static_asserts pinning the enum to zlib's constants; DeflateEncoder(options) stops delegating to the Z_DEFAULT_STRATEGY constructor and GZip/ZLib's MakeDeflateEncoder gains a strategy parameter, with the non-options constructors unchanged. Default-option output byte-identical, diffed and pinned. +9 tests (89 -> 98); two counting mutations plus one honest non-result (always resolving memLevel 8 is observationally equivalent, because the port picks memLevel 7 only at quality 0 where zlib emits stored blocks); ASan/UBSan/LSan 0 reports; no signature, layout, vtable or noexcept change. **REMAINING HALF: the three stream types' absent ZLibCompressionOptions constructors — a public surface addition, owned by the BLOCKED #2150.** |
| [SR-AUD-260](modules/io-hashing/src/System/IO/Hashing/Adler32.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-260--high--hashing-raw-pointer-overloads-dereference-positive-null-buffers-instead-of-reporting-an-argument-error) | high | remediated | raw `Adler32`, CRC, XXH, and base output paths | Positive-length null input reaches UBSan-confirmed loads in Adler/CRC and analogous unchecked raw paths across the module; a claimed long null destination reaches derived writes, where current .NET rejects null arrays or exposes only non-null spans. **Remediated (#2141, 2026-08-09).** Measured far wider than filed: **58 of 102** probe cases died with SIGSEGV, and the **destination** half (35) was larger than the source half (21) and lived in **one** file, the base class every hasher inherits. The **four XXH types were not exempt** — the per-file reports call them repaired, but that repair is SR-AUD-261's negative-length guard; on the null-pointer axis all seven crashed identically. Implementation found **nine more doors the 102-case matrix never reached** — `TryGetHashAndReset` on all seven types and both parameter sets' `WriteCrcToSpan`, a **public** destination door carrying no capacity argument at all — so the real blast radius was **67**, and a further defect UBSan alone could see: `memcpy(buffer, nullptr, 0)` on XxHash3/XxHash128 is undefined **even at zero count**, on the very case the contract requires to be *accepted*. One shared seam now validates every door; a null pointer is rejected only when the length is **positive**, so `default(ReadOnlySpan<byte>)` stays legal, and the destination's **capacity claim is decided first**, so a short buffer still reports *"Destination is too short."*. Sanitizer evidence is present-before/absent-after with the production sources compiled into the instrumented binary and the "before" tree extracted from `c8d8b71`: **60 → 0** and **9 → 0** diagnostics, **58 → 0** and **9 → 0** SIGSEGVs. All eight published check values unchanged. +14 tests; no signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. Two of six mutations survived at first and each exposed a real gap now closed — `EXPECT_THROW(…, ArgumentException)` was never strict here because `ArgumentNullException` and `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` both derive from it, and nothing pinned the destination byte order of Adler32/Crc32/Crc64. `docs/SystemIOHashingNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §17.2. |
| [SR-AUD-261](modules/io-hashing/src/System/IO/Hashing/Adler32.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-261--medium--adler-and-crc-raw-signed-lengths-accept-negative-input-as-a-successful-empty-operation) | medium | remediated | `Adler32.cpp`, `Crc32.cpp`, `Crc64.cpp`, parameter sets | Native `sourceLength=-1` returns normally for Adler/CRC one-shot, streaming, and parameter-set updates because their loops skip the negative count, unlike repaired XXH and managed span boundaries. **Remediated (#2142, 2026-08-09).** Confirmed exactly as filed. The defect was not "an error that goes unreported" but **a successful empty operation**: every loop is `for (intcs i = 0; i < length; ++i)`, false at once, so `HashToUInt32(data, -1)` returned the hash of *nothing* and the caller could not tell. The repair **deletes a divergence rather than inventing a rule** — the guard, its exception type, its `paramName` and its message already existed in XxHash32/64/3/128, and now live in one shared seam that all seven types and both parameter sets call, so the module holds **one** definition instead of five. Applied at three choke points (`Adler32`'s internal `Update`, `Crc32ParameterSet::Update`, `Crc64ParameterSet::Update`) which between them cover every door of the three defective types; validating inside `Update` is also what leaves a **rejected `Append`'s hash state untouched**. Measured: the 102-case matrix moved 25→14 accepted and 19→30 threw, the difference being exactly the 11 negative-length cases and nothing else. Sanitizers are honestly **non-discriminating** here — a negative length in Adler/CRC is not UB at all, which is why a wrong answer survived a healthy suite. +12 tests, three mutations. `docs/SystemIOHashingNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §17.1. |
| [SR-AUD-262](modules/io-hashing/src/System/IO/Hashing/XxHash3Shared.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-262--medium--xxh-implementations-label-native-object-copies-as-little-endian-loads-and-produce-platform-dependent-hashes) | medium | remediated | XXH32/64/3/128 implementations and shared helpers | Functions named `ReadUInt*LE`/`WriteUInt64LE` and XXH32/64 lanes only `memcpy` native integers, so big-endian targets byte-swap algorithm input and no longer match specification/current .NET values. **Remediated (#2143, 2026-08-09), scoped to what this environment can prove.** A new header holds the module's one definition of little-endian, assembling values from bytes; `ReadUInt32LE`/`ReadUInt64LE`/`WriteUInt64LE` become thin delegations and XxHash32/64's block lanes and tail loads use it directly. `grep -rn "memcpy(&"` over the module's sources now returns **nothing** — no integer is loaded in native order any more. **Proved host-independently:** a fixed byte pattern maps to specific values, including unaligned reads, the write round trip and the all-zero/all-ones patterns. **Proved on this host:** every published value unchanged — and a premise the review had not recorded, XxHash32/64's *existing* official vectors (39- and 43/44-byte strings) do exercise the multi-lane block path, so the lane loads are pinned by **published values**, not merely by self-consistency. **NOT proved and not claimed:** correctness when *executed* on a big-endian machine — no such host here and no cross-run in this repository's CI, an environment limit rather than a reference question, stated in the header, the README and the ticket. Mutating the read helper to big-endian (the closest this environment gets to such a host) kills 11 tests including both XXH32/64 official-vector tests while every Adler/CRC vector stays green. +8 tests. `docs/SystemIOHashingNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §17.3. |
| [SR-AUD-263](modules/net-sockets/src/System/Net/Sockets/Socket.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-263--high--socket-asynchronous-operations-start-immediately-with-raw-this-captures-that-can-outlive-the-socket) | high | remediated | `Socket.cpp`, `Socket.hpp`, `Task.hpp` | All four Socket async tasks capture raw `this` and `TaskT` immediately starts `std::async`; destruction or move while any worker runs leaves it dereferencing dead Socket state. **REMEDIATED (#2134, 2026-08-17), at no public-signature cost, and every one of #2139's four pins survives unchanged.** `Socket` does NOT become `enable_shared_from_this`, `~Socket` stays `noexcept`, `Socket` stays move-assignable and non-copyable, and the async return types are untouched — which is the argument for this design over the shared-ownership one. .NET needs no boundary because the GC keeps the object alive for as long as the captured delegate can reach it; C++ has no such mechanism, so the port takes the RAII answer: **the destructor waits for the work**, the shape #2347 gave `FileSystemWatcher`. Move-assignment carries the same boundary, because it replaces the descriptor and every field while a body may still be reading them. **The awkward case is `AcceptAsync`, and it changed the design:** `shutdown()` reliably unblocks `recv()`/`send()` but on Linux does **not** unblock `accept()` on a listening socket (`ENOTCONN`, and the accept stays blocked), while `close()` is unsafe mid-syscall — so a naive boundary would have turned a use-after-free into a HANG. That body therefore waits on this type's own portable `Poll()` in 50 ms slices and re-checks a stop flag between them; an interrupted accept reports `SocketError::OperationAborted` rather than a `Socket` built from a dead descriptor. **A second design error was found and fixed during implementation:** the first cut captured the in-flight guard *in* the lambda, but `std::async` keeps the callable alive until the last future to its shared state is destroyed, so the guard was released when the CALLER dropped the `TaskT` — the boundary waited for the caller instead of for the work and deadlocked in exactly the case it exists to make safe. The decrement is now a body-local scope object. **Layout `sizeof` 24 → 40** (one `shared_ptr` plus the alignment padding the scalar block gains with it), `alignof` 8 unchanged, no vtable or `noexcept` change; landed under `docs/StandingApprovals.md` SA-3 and pinned against shadow structs. +4 tests. Two mutations: removing the move-assignment boundary fails cleanly; removing the destructor's fails its assertion **and then hangs at teardown**, because the orphaned body spins forever on a destroyed socket — both recorded, since the hang is itself the defect. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. `docs/Migration-SocketAsyncLivenessBoundary.md`. **CCF-019 is NOT closed**: #2066, #2088 and #1959 have the same shape and are untouched. |
| [SR-AUD-264](modules/net-sockets/include/System/Net/Sockets/SendPacketsElement.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-264--medium--sendpacketselement-treats-every-negative-buffer-count-as-the-whole-buffer-instead-of-rejecting-invalid-ranges) | medium | remediated | `SendPacketsElement.hpp`, support tests | Native `SendPacketsElement({1,2,3}, 0, -2)` returns with count 3 because every negative count means whole buffer; current .NET rejects -2 through unsigned range validation. **Remediated (#2135, 2026-08-08). CONFIRMED, AND NARROWER THAN THE FINDING READS.** Measured (`build-probe/2133_probe1_before.log`): `count` ∈ {−1, −2, −100, `INT_MIN`} were all accepted with `count == 3`, because the buffer overload computed `count >= 0 ? count : buffer_.size()` **before** any range check, so the sign was consumed by the ternary and never reached one. **Two corrections:** the negative *offset* was **already** rejected — by the unsigned-cast idiom two lines below the ternary, which converts to `uintcs` precisely so a negative value becomes enormous and trips `ThrowIfGreaterThan` — and the **file-path overload was already correct**. So this was one overload's ternary, not a type-wide gap. The repair is explicit `ThrowIfNegative` calls before the unsigned bounds, with the whole-buffer meaning moved to the one-argument constructor where it is meant, so no negative sentinel travels into the checked overload at all. **A diagnostic half no document named, repaired with it:** the offset message reported the *unsigned reinterpretation* of the caller's argument (*"Actual value was 4294967295"* for `-1`); fixing the sign check and leaving the message lying would have been half a repair. Cause **NS-D**. +4 tests (`SharpRuntimeTests_Net_Sockets` 88 → 92; the *+7 / 84 → 91* first recorded here was a mis-transcription, and the sole cause of the inherited 16,005-vs-16,002 gate discrepancy — reconciled in `docs/SystemNetSocketsNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §18); mutations: under-repair fails 2 — the defect test **and** the message test — over-repair (`ThrowIfNegativeOrZero`) fails exactly 1, the valid-counts control, and a semantics-preserving respelling fails 0. ASan+UBSan+LSan clean over 1,458 constructions covering `INT_MIN`/`INT_MAX` at both parameters across buffer sizes 0–5 and both overloads, with a live heap-use-after-free control. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-265](modules/net-sockets/src/System/Net/Sockets/NetworkStream.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-265--medium--networkstream-accepts-an-invalid-raw-descriptor-and-turns-readswrites-into-silent-eofnoop-operations) | medium | remediated | `NetworkStream.hpp`, `NetworkStream.cpp`, socket tests | `NetworkStream(-1)` returns EOF from positive Read and succeeds on positive Write; current .NET rejects null, unconnected, non-stream, and nonblocking sockets at construction and throws after disposal. **Remediated (#2136, 2026-08-08). CONFIRMED EXACTLY AS FILED, AND THERE IS A SECOND DEFECT UNDERNEATH IT.** Measured before the repair (`build-probe/2136_probe1_fdstate.cpp`, log `2136_probe1_before.log`): every descriptor shape constructed — `-1`, `-2`, `999999`, a closed socket, a **pipe end**, `/dev/null`, a datagram socket, an unconnected socket, a listening socket and a non-blocking socket — and the type's whole contract is *I own this handle*. **The second defect is the more interesting one:** `Close()` has always *recorded* the closed state and the capability properties have always *reported* it, while neither data-transfer member consulted it — `Read` answered `0`, indistinguishable from a clean end-of-stream, and `Write`, being `void`, returned normally having written nothing. That is **CCF-022's cause in its strongest form seen so far** (not enforced even at the data-transfer members); **CCF-022 remains UNMINTED** — the obstacle is authority, not evidence (#2109). The repair validates the descriptor in the constructor **body**, which is what makes the non-leak true by construction: a body that throws means the destructor never runs, so `Close()` can never close a descriptor the instance was not allowed to own. Four rules, in .NET's own order after a socket-ness precondition the C++ raw-`int` signature makes necessary: not-a-socket-handle → `ArgumentException` with `paramName == "fd"`; non-blocking / non-connected / non-stream → `IOException` with .NET's three messages. Closed `Read`/`Write` now throw `ObjectDisposedException("Cannot access a closed Stream.")`, this repository's established message (`MemoryStream.cpp:65`, `UnmanagedMemoryStream.cpp:46`). **Argument validation deliberately stays in front of the closed check** and is pinned there. `TcpClient::GetStream` gained a `catch`-and-`close` around its `dup()` because a validating constructor can now reject the descriptor it was just handed. Causes **NS-A + NS-B**. +13 tests (`SharpRuntimeTests_Net_Sockets` 92 → 105); mutations: reverting `Write`'s closed check fails 2, removing the connected check fails 2, **closing the descriptor before rejecting it** fails 2 including the accounting test, and moving the closed check in front of the argument checks fails exactly the ordering pin. ASan+UBSan+LSan clean over **2,200 constructions / 2,000 rejections** with **zero descriptor drift** measured from `/proc/self/fd` and a live heap-use-after-free control. **The exception types are this port's choice**, recorded as such: `/rv` is absent, so the per-file report's list of what .NET rejects is the evidence, not its exact shapes. No layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-266](modules/net-sockets/src/System/Net/Sockets/TcpClient.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-266--medium--tcpclient-local-endpoint-construction-is-inert-and-all-connection-paths-force-ipv4) | medium | confirmed | `TcpClient.cpp`, `TcpClient.hpp`, socket tests | The C++ local-endpoint constructor ignores its argument, while host and endpoint connect paths use AF_INET/sockaddr_in only; current .NET selects/binds the supplied family and local endpoint. **ENDPOINT HALF REMEDIATED (#2137, 2026-08-08); FAMILY HALF STILL OPEN as #2138 (`needs_user`), so this row stays `confirmed`.** This is **two defects wearing one finding number** and they were deliberately not given one ticket. *Endpoint half:* the constructor body was literally `{}` with the parameter name commented out. Measured against the kernel rather than against the object (`build-probe/2137_probe1_before.log`): a client that asked for local port **40601** connected from **40998**. It now binds at construction, as real .NET does, and `Connect()` connects **the socket it already owns** — creating a second socket is precisely how the endpoint used to be discarded. **No new member and no layout change:** `fd_ >= 0 && !connected_` was previously unreachable, so it is available to mean *bound, not yet connected*. A failed `Connect()` on a bound client keeps the socket owned rather than closing it, so nothing leaks and the caller's endpoint is not silently dropped. *Family half (AF_INET only):* unchanged, design, **#2138** — supporting `AF_INET6` reshapes every connect/bind/accept path. Cause **NS-C** (endpoint half) / **NS-F** (family half). Mutations: reverting the constructor to `{}` fails 4; making `Connect(host, port)` ignore the bound socket fails **exactly the hostname-path test**, which is why both overloads are pinned separately. |
| [SR-AUD-267](modules/net-sockets/src/System/Net/Sockets/UdpClient.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-267--medium--udpclient-silently-narrows-invalid-ports-and-implements-every-endpoint-path-as-ipv4) | medium | remediated | `UdpClient.cpp`, `UdpClient.hpp`, socket tests | C++ casts public ports to uint16 and all bind/connect paths use AF_INET/sockaddr_in, so invalid ports lack range errors and IPv6 endpoints are misrepresented where .NET validates/selects the family. **Remediated (#2137, 2026-08-08) for the PORT half; the AF_INET half belongs to SR-AUD-266 and #2138. THE DEFECT SURFACE IS WIDER THAN THE FINDING NAMES.** The review measured `UdpClient(int port)`; this ticket measured every bare-`int` door and found **two more** — `UdpClient::Connect(host, port)` and `TcpClient::Connect(host, port)` — and they failed differently: a **negative** port there was rejected, but by `getaddrinfo`, as *"DNS failed: Servname not supported for ai_socktype"*, a `SocketException` blaming name resolution for an argument the caller controls, while **65536/70000 were truncated** and connected to the wrong port. So one door lied about which argument was wrong and the other did not complain at all. One rule now, in `src/.../PortValidation.hpp`, expressed against `IPEndPoint::MinPort`/`MaxPort` and raising `IPEndPoint`'s own exception shape, so the bare-`int` doors and the endpoint doors cannot drift apart. **The check runs BEFORE the socket is created**, which is what makes the non-leak structural — mutation N2 moves it after and fails exactly the `/proc/self/fd` accounting test. RFC 793's 16-bit port field is the evidence; no `/rv` needed. Cause **NS-C**. +11 tests (`SharpRuntimeTests_Net_Sockets` 105 → 116) with 1,152 rejected constructions leaking nothing and a deliberate-leak discrimination control; ASan+UBSan+LSan clean. No layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-268](modules/diagnostics/src/System/Diagnostics/Process.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-268--medium--processwaitforexitint-breaks-the-negative-timeout-contract) | medium | remediated | `Process.cpp`, `Process.hpp` | `WaitForExit(-1)` returns immediately instead of waiting infinitely, while values below `-1` are not rejected. **Reviewed by #2023 (2026-08-03, cause D-A of `docs/SystemDiagnosticsNamespaceReviewPlan.md`); repair ticket #2024, compatible, `todo`.** Reproduced and sharper than filed: `WaitForExit(-1)` on a 3 s child returned `false` after **0 ms**, and `WaitForExit(INTCS_MIN)` returned normally with no diagnostic. Filed with SR-AUD-272 because both are the same overload pair; the repair is transcribed from this file's own three correct `EINTR` retry loops, so no reference tree is required. **REMEDIATED (#2024, 2026-08-04).** `WaitForExit(-1)` now waits indefinitely and returns `true` (measured 2002 ms against a 2 s child, where it previously returned `false` after 0 ms), and any `milliseconds < -1` raises `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` with `paramName = "milliseconds"` and the message transcribed verbatim from this repository's own `System::Threading::Thread::Join(intcs)` guard. The constant is spelled locally as `TimeoutInfinite` rather than included from `System::Threading::Timeout`, which would have added a module edge for one integer; it is pinned behaviourally instead. Validation runs before any state check, so a running child does not change which exception a caller sees. +13 tests. **UBSan is an honest non-discriminating result here:** the plan §11 row asked it to show `now + milliseconds(INTCS_MIN)` is defined, and it is -- clean **both before and after**, because the millisecond-to-nanosecond conversion of `INTCS_MIN` stays inside `int64`. UBSan was proven live by a deliberate `chrono` overflow control. No signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. |
| [SR-AUD-269](modules/diagnostics/src/System/Diagnostics/Process.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-269--high--process-destruction-leaks-a-zombie-or-blocks-on-redirected-output) | high | remediated | `Process.cpp`, `Process.hpp` | Destroying an unredirected running Process leaves an unreaped zombie; a redirected child instead makes destruction block until pipe EOF. **Design complete, blocked (#2029, cause D-B, 2026-08-03).** **Premise correction (plan §4.1): this is two OPPOSITE defects and which one fires depends on redirection.** Unredirected, `~Process` returns at once and the child was measured in state `'Z'` 500 ms later, with the probe's own `waitpid(WNOHANG)` still returning it. **Redirected, destruction instead BLOCKS** — `~Impl` joins the pipe readers, which cannot finish until the child closes stdout: **2005 ms measured for a 2 s child**, unbounded in general. A repair must satisfy two requirements that pull in opposite directions, which is why this is a design ticket. Four alternatives and the exact approval sentence are plan §14.1. **REMEDIATED (#2029, 2026-08-17) — the BLOCKING half; the zombie half is a documented divergence, see below.** The redirected case blocked for the child's WHOLE LIFETIME (measured 2005 ms for a 2 s child, unbounded in general) because `~Impl` joins the pipe readers and a reader calling a bare blocking `read()` cannot return until the child closes stdout. The readers now wait on `poll()` in bounded slices and observe a stop flag the destructor sets. **The join is deliberately KEPT**: the plan's option C said to DETACH the readers, but a detached reader keeps appending into the `Process`'s own string after the object is gone — a use-after-free, which would trade this defect for a worse one. Bounding the wait gets the same promptness without it, and that correction is the reason the design could not simply be applied as written. **.NET's contract is now read rather than inferred**: `Process.Close()` (`Process.cs:761-805`) stops watching for exit, releases the handle and **cancels** the async read before disposing the stream — it never waits for the child. **The zombie half is NOT repaired, and the reason is structural**: .NET reaps process-wide from a SIGCHLD-driven wait state (`ProcessWaitState.Unix.cs`), which this port cannot replicate without colliding with `PosixSignalRegistration` (#1975/#1979), which owns SIGCHLD here. A child that has already exited is still reaped; one that is still running is left to the OS, and that divergence is documented in `docs/Migration-ProcessLifecycleAndOutputSafety.md` §1.2 rather than papered over. +2 tests, the blocking pin inverted, one mutation (readers ignore the stop flag) caught. `Process` is a PIMPL, so no public layout moved. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. |
| [SR-AUD-270](modules/diagnostics/src/System/Diagnostics/Process.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-270--high--restarting-a-redirected-process-calls-stdterminate) | high | remediated | `Process.cpp`, `Process.hpp` | Calling the documented restart path with active redirected readers assigns over a joinable `std::thread` and terminates the caller. **Reviewed by #2023 (cause D-C); repair ticket #2025, compatible, `todo`.** Reproduced exactly: `terminate called without an active exception`, **KILLED by signal 6**. `Process.hpp`'s own doc-comment advertises the restart. Classified compatible because a call that aborts the whole process has no defined result to preserve, and the evidence is the C++ standard (`std::thread::operator=` on a joinable target), not .NET. **REMEDIATED (#2025, 2026-08-04).** **Premise sharpened by measurement (`build-probe/2025_probe1_before.log`):** the trigger is a **joinable reader thread**, not a *running* child. Restarting after the previous child had already **exited** aborted too (probe case C) whenever the caller had not called `WaitForExit()`/`getHasExitedProperty()` first, because only those join the reader -- so the plan's §10 row "restart after the previous child exited (must work)" was **already broken** in the redirected case. Two further measured facts the finding did not name: the captured text **accumulated** across a restart (`"first"` then `"firstsecond"`, probe case B), and an **unredirected** restart while the child ran silently **abandoned** it unreaped (probe case E, `waitpid(WNOHANG)` returned 0). The repair resolves the previous child's readers **before** the assignment: a still-running previous child is refused with `InvalidOperationException` -- uniformly for both redirection combinations -- and an exited one has its readers joined, its captured text reset and its `isCurrentProcess` flag cleared at the commit point, so no partial state transition is observable. Refusal was chosen because the alternatives are both worse: joining a live child's readers blocks for its whole lifetime (the SR-AUD-269 blocking mode) and detaching them **is** #2029's gated destructor policy. +15 tests; ASan and LSan clean with `Process.cpp` compiled from source into the probe and LSan proven discriminating by a deliberate-leak control. No signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. |
| [SR-AUD-271](modules/diagnostics/src/System/Diagnostics/Process.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-271--high--process-state-and-captured-output-have-internal-data-races) | high | remediated | `Process.cpp`, `Process.hpp` | Public Process state/output access races internal pipe-reader and lifecycle mutations, exposing undefined behavior and invalidated text references. **Design complete, blocked (#2030, cause D-D, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced: the **same** `const std::string&` returned by `getStandardOutputTextProperty()` read **4 bytes mid-run and 8 bytes after exit**. **Post-audit observation (plan §16 row 5):** `getHasExitedProperty()` is `const` and **mutates** `hasExited`/`exitCode` through `reapIfNeeded`, so `const` gives no thread-safety guarantee here. The audit's TSan evidence was **not** re-run by #2023 and #2030 must re-run it. **This is the only ticket in the namespace that changes a public declaration** — the two text getters must return `std::string` by value; the mutex itself is layout-invisible because `Process` is a pimpl. Approval sentence plan §14.2. **REMEDIATED (#2030, 2026-08-17).** Both captured-output getters returned a REFERENCE into storage the internal reader thread was still appending to — the audit measured the SAME reference reading 4 bytes mid-run and 8 bytes after exit. No reference can be made safe there, so both now return `std::string` **by value**, taking the copy under the same mutex the readers append under. Source-compatible for the ordinary spelling (a value still binds to `const std::string&`); `auto&` deduces differently, which is the point. **The post-audit observation is also settled**: `getHasExitedProperty()` is `const` and mutates through `reapIfNeeded` — that is .NET's shape too, since `HasExited` reaps lazily, so the repair is not to stop mutating but to make it safe, and it now happens under a lock. +3 tests including four concurrent readers against the live reader thread. `Process` is a PIMPL, so no public layout moved. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. `docs/Migration-ProcessLifecycleAndOutputSafety.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-272](modules/diagnostics/src/System/Diagnostics/Process.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-272--medium--waitforexit-silently-returns-when-waitpid-is-interrupted) | medium | remediated | `Process.cpp` | EINTR from blocking `waitpid` makes `WaitForExit` return without recording child exit state, leaving ExitCode unavailable. **Reviewed by #2023 (cause D-A); repair ticket #2024, compatible, `todo`.** **Premise correction (plan §4.2): the consequence is stronger than filed.** With a non-`SA_RESTART` `SIGALRM` at 1 s, the **blocking** `WaitForExit()` returned after **1000 ms on a 3 s child** — violating its own doc-comment ("Blocks the calling thread until the associated process terminates"), not merely leaving `ExitCode` unavailable, which it also did (`getExitCodeProperty()` threw). The reader joins then run with the child still alive, so a redirected caller blocks *there* instead. **REMEDIATED (#2024, 2026-08-04).** Both `waitpid` calls now retry on `EINTR` -- the blocking one in `WaitForExit()` and the `WNOHANG` one in `reapIfNeeded()`, which could also be interrupted and would then have reported a child that had in fact exited as still running. Re-measured with the same non-`SA_RESTART` `SIGALRM` at 1 s against a 3 s child: `WaitForExit()` returned after **3003 ms** with the alarm delivered, `getHasExitedProperty()` `true` and `getExitCodeProperty()` returning 0, where it previously returned after 1000 ms and `getExitCodeProperty()` threw. Two permanent tests assert the signal really was delivered, so a run in which it was not cannot pass silently. |
| [SR-AUD-273](modules/diagnostics/src/System/Diagnostics/Process.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-273--medium--killtrue-is-only-a-process-group-kill-not-a-full-process-tree-kill) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `Process.cpp`, `Process.hpp` | `Kill(true)` signals one process group, so a `setsid` descendant survives despite the declared full-process-tree contract. **Design complete, blocked (#2031, cause D-E, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced: after `Kill(true)` the `setsid` grandchild was **ALIVE** and had to be killed by the probe itself. Gated because the repair starts **killing processes it does not kill today** — which is what the contract promises, and is still a change in blast radius — and because the `/proc` descendant walk is Linux-specific, so other POSIX platforms need a documented fallback to today's `killpg`. Approval sentence plan §14.3. |
| [SR-AUD-274](modules/diagnostics/src/System/Diagnostics/Process.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-274--high--fork-child-calls-non-async-signal-safe-code-in-multithreaded-parents) | high | remediated | `Process.cpp`, `ProcessStartInfo.hpp` | After fork in a multithreaded parent, the child calls non-async-signal-safe `setenv` and `execvp`, risking an unrecoverable pre-exec deadlock. **Reviewed by #2023 (cause D-F); repair ticket #2026, compatible, `todo`.** **Post-audit observation (plan §16 row 4):** the parent is multithreaded exactly when a previous **redirected** `Start` left **this class's own** pipe-reader threads running, so the hazard is reachable without the caller ever creating a thread. The repair builds the `envp` array in the parent before forking and `execve`s it, so the child performs no allocating call; evidence is POSIX `fork(2)`'s async-signal-safety requirement, not .NET. **REMEDIATED (#2026, 2026-08-04).** The environment is marshalled in the **parent**: the process's own `environ` is copied, each configured variable replaces a same-named entry or is appended, and the resulting array is handed to `execvpe` under `__GLIBC__` (elsewhere the global `environ` pointer is replaced -- a single store, async-signal-safe -- before `execvp`). The child's remaining pre-exec calls are `close`/`setpgid`/`dup2`/`chdir`/`_exit`/`write` and the `exec` itself; **no allocating call remains**. Name validation still runs in the parent before the fork, so no entry can be empty or contain `=`. No observable change on any path: inheritance, override-replaces-once, an `=` inside a *value*, an empty value, 100 variables, and synchronous `chdir`/`exec` failure reporting are all pinned by +13 tests. ASan and LSan clean with `Process.cpp` compiled from source. No signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. **A separate defect was found while pinning this and filed rather than absorbed:** `WaitForExit(milliseconds)` blocks past its own deadline when a grandchild holds the redirected pipe (measured 29,951 ms against a 5,000 ms bound) -- inactive ticket **#2032**, blocked on #2029 because every repair decides the reader-thread policy that #2029 gates. No `SR-AUD-*` identifier was issued; numbering stays frozen at 364. |
| [SR-AUD-275](modules/diagnostics/include/System/Diagnostics/Debug.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-275--high--global-debug-provider-and-diagnostics-state-are-unsynchronised) | high | remediated | `Debug.hpp`, `Trace.hpp`, diagnostics tests | TSan confirms a `Debug::Write`/`SetProvider` race on the global shared_ptr; Diagnostics global state has no synchronization policy. **Reviewed by #2023 (cause D-G); repair ticket #2027, compatible, `todo`.** Measured shape: `Debug::providerStorage()` is a function-local `static std::shared_ptr<DebugProvider>` written by `SetProvider` and read by every `Write`, with no atomic and no lock; `Debug::indentSizeStorage()` and `Trace::indentSizeStorage()` are plain process-global scalars, while `indentLevel` is correctly `thread_local` in both. The audit's TSan evidence was **not** re-run by #2023; #2027 must show the race present before and absent after. `Debug` and `Trace` have **no data members**, so the repair changes no layout or signature. **This is the first namespace in the programme where TSan applies to the compatible half.** **REMEDIATED (#2027, 2026-08-04).** TSan was re-run as required, against the header itself -- `Debug` and `Trace` are header-only, so the production bodies are **necessarily** instrumented and no stale archive is possible. **The before-state was worse than filed:** 20 data races **and 12 `heap-use-after-free`**, the latter inside `Debug::Write`, because a reader could dereference a provider a concurrent `SetProvider` had already destroyed (`build-probe/2027_probe1_tsan_before.log`). Repair: every provider read goes through a new private `currentProvider()` that takes a `std::mutex` and returns an **owning** `shared_ptr` snapshot, so the object cannot die under the caller; the lock is released before the provider is invoked, so a provider that calls back into `Debug` cannot deadlock; `SetProvider` returns the outgoing provider by value for the same reason; and both indent-**size** globals become `std::atomic` (indent-**level** was already correctly `thread_local` and is unchanged). `setIndentSizeProperty` now reports the **clamped local** to `OnIndentSizeChanged` instead of re-reading the global, which a concurrent setter could have changed in between. After: **0 warnings of any kind, clean exit** (`build-probe/2027_probe1_tsan_after.log`). Single-threaded behaviour is unchanged and +11 tests pin it, including the ownership guarantee via `std::weak_ptr`. `Debug` and `Trace` have no data members, so no layout or signature changed; both are public headers, so consumers recompile. |
| [SR-AUD-276](modules/numerics/include/System/Numerics/Vector3.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-276--medium--degenerate-vector-and-plane-normalization-returns-finite-zero-rather-than-net-nans) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `Vector2.hpp`, `Vector3.hpp`, `Vector4.hpp`, `Plane.hpp`, `Matrix4x4.hpp`, `VectorMatrixTests.cpp` | Zero/degenerate vector and plane normalization returns finite zero rather than .NET NaNs. **Measured by review #2167, the finding is wider and far less uniform than recorded:** the vector guard is `Length() > 0`, so it also fires for **any NaN component** (`NaN > 0` is false — the NaN is swallowed, not propagated) and for **any vector whose squared length underflows to zero**; `{-0,-0,-0}` returns `-0`, not "finite zero"; and **`Plane::Normalize` does not share the behaviour at all** — its `len < 1e-10f` guard *propagates* NaN and returns `{1e-11,0,0}` unnormalized while normalizing `{1e-9,0,0}` with `D` scaled to `1e+09`. The module holds **three** thresholds for one question. **Ticket #2173 (done)** states the real contract in all four doc-comments and pins every measured row with 16 `PIN_` tests including both dependents; the semantic change is **ticket #2175, blocked on evidence** — the finding carries no managed probe (§6.2). |
| [SR-AUD-277](modules/numerics/include/System/Numerics/Complex.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-277--medium--complex-abs-signature-and-default-text-diverge-from-net) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `Complex.hpp`, `ComplexTests.cpp` | `Complex::Abs` returns a Complex rather than the .NET double, and the default textual form diverged. Split by review #2167. **Text half — ticket #2171 (done):** measured, the rendering was worse than "fixed six decimals" — `Complex(1e-9, 0)` printed `<0.000000; 0.000000>`, destroying the value, `1e300` produced a 619-character string, and infinities/NaN used the C library's `inf`/`nan`. Both components now render through `System::Double::ToString`, this port's own .NET-compatible renderer. **Premise corrected:** the audit's "parenthesized pair" target rests on a *constructor documentation example*, while the same report links the current .NET source, which gives `<a; b>` — the two citations disagree, so the **bracket/separator skeleton is deliberately unchanged and pinned**, owned by **#2174**. **Signature half — ticket #2172, needs_user:** changing `Abs`'s public return type to `double` is source-breaking with no conversion path, the class gated as approval D-B in `docs/ConsolidatedApprovalPackage.md` (§4.5, §4.6, §6.3). |
| [SR-AUD-278](modules/numerics/include/System/Numerics/GenericMathInterfaces.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-278--medium--generic-math-static-interface-members-are-unresolved-link-time-stubs) | medium | remediated | `GenericMathInterfaces.hpp`, `Task42Tests.cpp` | Public generic-math static members were declarations with no definitions, so consumer code compiled and then failed with undefined references at final linkage. Measured, the surface is **44 members across 9 templates**, not the 3 the audit's probe named. Ticket #2168 makes every one `= delete`, moving the failure from the linker to the call site, and names the C++ replacement (`<limits>`, `<numbers>`, `<cmath>`, `TSelf{}`/`TSelf{1}`) on each interface. Defining them was rejected: generic-math *conformance* is already a settled documented reduction (`Half.hpp:43-45`, `DivisionRounding.hpp`, the header preamble), and inventing 44 numeric semantics with `/rv` absent is what this programme forbids. **No program that builds today is affected** — a call that exists today already fails to link. Compile-time proof in `test/consumer/numerics_generic_math_negative.cpp` (12 sites); +6 runtime tests (`docs/SystemNumericsNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.7, §5 NM-A). |
| [SR-AUD-279](modules/globalization/include/System/Globalization/StringInfo.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-279--medium--stringinfo-confuses-utf-8-bytescode-points-with-managed-text-elements) | medium | confirmed | `StringInfo.hpp`, `TextElementEnumerator.hpp`, Globalization tests | UTF-8 code points/byte indexes replace .NET grapheme text elements; `SubstringByTextElements` can return an invalid single-byte UTF-8 tail. |
