| [SR-AUD-285](modules/globalization/include/System/Globalization/RegionInfo.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-285--medium--cultureinfo-and-regioninfo-accept-unknown-identifiers-and-fabricate-locale-metadata) | medium | confirmed | `CultureInfo.hpp`, `RegionInfo.hpp`, Globalization tests | Unknown culture/region identifiers succeed and return name-derived pseudo-ISO fields and USD defaults rather than current .NET unsupported-culture/region diagnostics. |
| [SR-AUD-286](modules/text/src/System/Text/ASCIIEncoding.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-286--high--unchecked-signed-raw-decode-ranges-reach-an-out-of-bounds-read) | high | remediated | `Encoding`, ASCII/UTF-8/Latin-1/UTF-16/UTF-32 encodings | Raw `GetString` paths do not consistently reject signed index/count before pointer arithmetic; ASan confirms `ASCIIEncoding::GetString(data, -1, 1)` stack-buffer-underflow. **Remediated (#2007, 2026-08-03, cause T-A of `docs/SystemTextNamespaceReviewPlan.md`).** One shared validator, `System::Text::detail::validateRawDecodeRange`/`checkedRawDecodeRange`, is adopted by all nine decode entries, by `Decoder`'s two overloads, by `GetCharCount` through delegation, and by `DecoderExceptionFallback::GetFallbackString`. The policy is **transcribed from `UTF7Encoding`** — this port's own already-correct entry — so no reference tree was needed. **Premise corrections (plan §4.1):** the finding's named reproduction, `UTF8Encoding::GetString(data, -1, 1)`, does **not** reproduce (`size_t` wrap makes the derived end 0; UTF-8 is still reachable for `count < |index|`), and **five failure modes it does not name** were measured and closed with it — a segmentation fault for a null buffer in Latin-1/UTF-16/UTF-32, `std::length_error` escaping `Latin1Encoding` for a negative count, UBSan-confirmed `signed integer overflow: 2147483647 + 4` in `UnicodeEncoding`'s own `intcs end = index + count`, `Decoder::GetString(vec, -1)` reading five bytes from a four-element vector, and `std::length_error` from `DecoderExceptionFallback::GetFallbackString(nullptr, -1)`. ASan reported five heap-buffer-overflows before and none after, with the production bodies compiled into the probe. **One residual is recorded, not hidden:** `GetString(buffer, INTCS_MAX, 4)` still reads out of bounds, because `INTCS_MAX` is a valid non-negative index and a raw pointer carries no length; what changed is that the addition on the way there is no longer undefined. +19 tests including a generated 9-encodings × 15-input-classes byte-identity block; no signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change. |
| [SR-AUD-287](modules/text/include/System/Text/Encoding.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-287--high--null-fallback-setters-cause-a-later-null-dereference) | high | remediated | `Encoding.hpp`, `UTF8Encoding.cpp` | Public fallback setters accept null; decoding one malformed UTF-8 byte afterwards reaches an ASan/UBSan-confirmed null virtual call instead of argument validation. **Remediated (#2008, 2026-08-03, cause T-B).** Both setters reject a null value with `ArgumentNullException("value")`, as .NET's `Encoding.DecoderFallback` setter does; a rejected call leaves the previous fallback installed. **Premise correction (plan §4.2):** the finding and its per-file report name only `setDecoderFallbackProperty`; measured, `setEncoderFallbackProperty(nullptr)` followed by `GetBytes("\xFF")` is the identical segmentation fault, so repairing one setter would have left the other. +8 tests; no layout or signature change. |
| [SR-AUD-288](modules/text/src/System/Text/Encoding.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-288--high--shared-factory-encodings-are-mutable-and-racy) | high | remediated | `Encoding.cpp`, `Encoding.hpp`, Text tests | Static factory methods return one mutable object; TSan confirms concurrent fallback mutation/read on `Encoding::UTF8()`, whereas managed factory encodings are read-only. **Design complete, blocked (#2013, cause T-G, 2026-08-03).** Structurally CCF-009's shape (`Random::Shared`, `Guid::NewGuid`): a process-wide singleton with publicly mutable state and no ownership boundary; CCF-009's policy is reused rather than a new family opened. Four alternatives, the recommended minimum (.NET's read-only factory encodings plus `IsReadOnly` and `Clone()`) and the exact approval sentence are `docs/SystemTextNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §14.1. Gated because a currently-succeeding setter starts throwing — including in this repository's own `TextNamespaceTests.cpp`. The current behaviour is now pinned by `TextUnitContractTests` (#2012) so the change cannot land silently. **REMEDIATED (#2013, 2026-08-17).** The seven factory encodings are still one shared object each — that was never the defect, and .NET does the same — but they are now **read-only**, so a caller can no longer change what every other caller in the process decodes. Transcribed from `Encoding.cs:485-497`: both fallback setters open with `if (this.IsReadOnly) throw new InvalidOperationException(SR.InvalidOperation_ReadOnly)`, message *"Instance is read-only."*, and the read-only test precedes the null test — an order the port reproduces and pins. `getIsReadOnlyProperty()` is added as the counterpart of `Encoding.IsReadOnly`. **`Clone()` is deliberately NOT added**, although .NET has it: it would be a new **virtual on a public base class**, which `docs/StandingApprovals.md` SA-3 excludes from standing approval, and every concrete encoding here is publicly constructible, which is the migration path. **Layout: `sizeof(Encoding)` 40 → 48** — the vtable pointer and two `shared_ptr`s used all 40 bytes so the flag costs a whole slot — while `UnicodeEncoding` and `UTF32Encoding` are **unchanged at 48**, their own flags having moved from their tail padding into the base's. A pin written as *base + one pointer* would have been wrong in both directions, so it compares against shadow structs. +5 tests, the gated pin inverted; two mutations, both caught by three cases each. Downstream measured under SA-2: `cna` zero sites; `mobile-eggbert` reads through `Encoding::UTF8()` at `Worlds.cpp:304`/`:325` and sets no fallback, so it is behaviourally unaffected but must be rebuilt. `docs/Migration-EncodingReadOnlyFactories.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-289](modules/text/include/System/Text/Latin1Encoding.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-289--medium--latin1encoding-converts-utf-8-storage-bytes-not-iso-8859-1-code-points) | medium | remediated | `Latin1Encoding.hpp` | U+00E9 encodes to `c3a9` rather than `e9`, and byte `e9` decodes to an invalid one-byte UTF-8 tail rather than UTF-8 `c3a9`. **Design complete, blocked (#2014, cause T-H, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced exactly: `GetBytes(u8"é")` → `c3a9`, `GetString({0xE9})` → `e9`. `Latin1Encoding` is the only encoding in the component that does not decode the UTF-8 storage into scalars first; the repair is what `ASCIIEncoding` already does one range wider. Approval sentence in plan §14.2. Gated because produced bytes change for every non-ASCII input. Ticket #2012 has made the header state the divergence outright and pinned it. **REMEDIATED (#2014, 2026-08-17).** `Latin1Encoding` was the ONLY encoding in this component that did not decode the UTF-8 storage representation into scalars first — `ASCIIEncoding`, `UnicodeEncoding` and `UTF32Encoding` all do — so it mapped storage bytes and called the result ISO-8859-1: `GetBytes(u8"é")` returned `c3 a9` where the answer is the single byte `e9`, and `GetString({e9})` returned that byte verbatim, producing an ill-formed `System::String`. Both directions now convert scalars, and the whole 0..255 range round-trips — asserted for every one of the 256 bytes, which is the property that was missing rather than merely wrong. An unrepresentable scalar becomes `?`, and a supplementary-plane one becomes **two** `?`, matching the two UTF-16 code units .NET encodes it from, exactly as this component's `ASCIIEncoding` already did. **A duplication was found and half-repaired on the way**: the same UTF-8 conformance decode existed in FIVE places in `modules/text`, and this ticket needed a sixth — so the rule was factored into `System/Text/detail/Utf8Scalar.hpp` and `ASCIIEncoding.cpp` moved onto it; the three header-inline copies are recorded as **#2354** rather than refactored under a behaviour ticket. +2 tests, both gated pins inverted; the mutation restoring the storage-byte mapping is caught by three cases. Downstream measured under SA-2: neither consumer names `Latin1Encoding` — though `cna` carries its own `Latin1ToUtf8` in `AudioTagParser.cpp`, which is what a caller writes when the library's own conversion is wrong. |
| [SR-AUD-290](modules/text/include/System/Text/Encoding.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-290--medium--getcharcount-reports-utf-8-byte-length-not-managed-utf-16-character-count) | medium | remediated | `Encoding.hpp`, concrete encodings | Base `GetCharCount` returns `std::string::size`; UTF-8 U+1F600 reports 4 where the managed count is two UTF-16 code units. **Disclosure half remediated, semantic half design-complete and blocked (#2012 / #2015, cause T-I, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced: `GetCharCount(U+1F600)` → `4`. #2012 makes `Encoding`'s class doc-comment and `GetCharCount`'s state the unit actually used and pins it by test; #2015 carries the three options (UTF-16 code units, scalar counts, or keep bytes and declare it) and the approval sentence in plan §14.3, and notes that it should not be decided without deciding it for `System::String` too. **DECIDED 2026-08-17 (#2015) as a DECLARED DEVIATION, and closed on that basis.** Plan §14.3 offered three options for what a public index means: (A) UTF-16 code units, (B) scalar counts, (C) keep UTF-8 storage bytes and declare it. **The ticket's own constraint decides it** — the unit *"should not be decided without deciding it for `System::String`, which carries the same adaptation"* — and `System::String` is a UTF-8 `std::string` throughout this runtime, so A and B are not a change to `System::Text` but a re-architecture of every index in the port. **What makes (C) the FAITHFUL answer rather than the cheap one is measured, not asserted**: a byte index into UTF-8 is the exact analogue of a code-unit index into UTF-16 *including* the ability to split a character. .NET's `StringBuilder.Remove` validates only the numeric range (`StringBuilder.cs:1024-1042`) — there is **no** surrogate-pair guard — so it can leave a lone surrogate and an ill-formed string, exactly as this port can leave an ill-formed UTF-8 sequence. Adopting .NET's unit would have moved that hazard to a different character, not removed it. Recorded in `CLAUDE.md`'s permanent-deviations list and pinned by `TextUnitContractTests.Decl2015_*`, which additionally assert the unit is **consistent** across the component — a mixture would be far worse than either unit consistently applied. The disclosure half was #2012. **No production statement changed**; this ticket is a decision and its evidence. |
| [SR-AUD-291](modules/text/include/System/Text/UTF32Encoding.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-291--medium--utf16utf32-getbytes-serializes-configured-bom-as-payload-instead-of-exposing-a-preamble) | medium | remediated | `UnicodeEncoding.hpp`, `UTF32Encoding.hpp` | BOM-enabled constructors insert U+FEFF into every `GetBytes` result; .NET requires callers to prefix `GetPreamble` themselves and states that `GetBytes` does not prepend it. **Design complete, blocked (#2016, cause T-J, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced, and sharper than filed: it is the **default** factory that misbehaves — `Encoding::UTF32()->GetBytes("A")` → `fffe000041000000`, while `Encoding::Unicode()->GetBytes("A")` → `4100`. **Two gates, not one:** emitted bytes change, and the natural repair adds a virtual `GetPreamble()` to `Encoding`, which appends a vtable slot; a non-virtual per-class spelling avoids that gate but cannot be reached through an `Encoding&`, which is how every consumer holds one. Approval sentence in plan §14.4; behaviour pinned by #2012. **Pinned by #2022 (2026-08-03):** the previous pin covered `Encoding::UTF32()` only. Measured, **two** default factories emit a BOM as payload — `Encoding::BigEndianUnicode()` is constructed `UnicodeEncoding(true, true)` and returns `feff0041` for `"A"` — and the **decode** direction silently **consumes** a leading U+FEFF in both UTF-16 and UTF-32, discarding a real ZERO WIDTH NO-BREAK SPACE. Neither is named by the finding or by plan §14.4; a round trip cancels both halves exactly, which is why no test saw them. Folded into #2016; no `SR-AUD-*` identifier issued. **REMEDIATED (#2016, 2026-08-17).** `GetBytes` no longer prepends a byte-order mark, on any encoding or setting; the mark is what the new `GetPreamble()` returns. .NET keeps the two apart (`UTF32Encoding.cs:1113-1128`), and for a reason the port was demonstrating: a mark emitted as payload is concatenated into strings, counted in lengths, and written a second time by anything that also writes a preamble. **The finding named one factory and there were two** — #2022 measured that `Encoding::BigEndianUnicode()` is constructed as `UnicodeEncoding(true, true)` and emitted a mark too, so the port was inconsistent with itself: the little-endian UTF-16 factory did not. The BOM constructor argument now affects `GetPreamble()` and nothing else. **`GetPreamble()` is deliberately NOT virtual and NOT on `Encoding`**, although .NET declares it there: a new virtual on a public base class is what `docs/StandingApprovals.md` SA-3 excludes from standing approval, and a caller holding only an `Encoding&` does not need it, since an encoding with no mark has nothing to write. +3 tests, two gated pins and one integration case inverted; the mutation restoring the prepend is caught by two. No layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. `docs/Migration-EncodingPreambleIsNotPayload.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-292](modules/text/include/System/Text/EncoderFallback.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-292--medium--public-fallback-policies-are-inert-outside-utf8encoding) | medium | remediated | ASCII, UTF-7, UTF-16, UTF-32 encodings and fallback classes | Exception/replacement fallback settings are publicly accepted but ASCII/UTF-16/UTF-32/UTF-7 hard-code replacement behavior; probes show configured exception fallback never throws. **Design complete, blocked (#2017, cause T-K, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced: a configured **exception** decoder fallback never throws in UTF-16, UTF-32 or ASCII (it does in UTF-8), and a configured `"!"` encoder replacement is ignored by ASCII, which hard-codes `'?'`. Filed together with SR-AUD-293 because both are the same cause. Approval sentence in plan §14.5; gated as a fallback-policy change, and the repair must install each encoding's currently hard-coded replacement text in its constructor or default output changes too. Behaviour pinned by #2012. **Pinned by #2022 (2026-08-03):** the previous pin covered the decoder direction only. The encoder direction is now pinned too — `ASCIIEncoding` ignores a configured `EncoderReplacementFallback("!")` and a configured exception fallback, hard-coding `'?'`, while `UTF8Encoding` honours both. **Correction to plan §14.5:** the fallback surface takes a `char` (`EncoderFallbackBuffer::Fallback`, `EncoderFallback::GetFallbackBytes`, `EncoderFallbackException::getCharUnknownProperty`), which cannot carry a non-ASCII scalar, so routing scalars through it needs a public virtual signature change — a second gate §14.5 does not ask about (`docs/SystemTextApprovalPackage.md` §4.3). **REMEDIATED (#2017, 2026-08-17).** Only `UTF8Encoding` consulted its configured fallback; `UnicodeEncoding`, `UTF32Encoding`, `ASCIIEncoding` and `Latin1Encoding` substituted `U+FFFD` or `'?'` **directly**, so a caller who installed `DecoderFallback::ExceptionFallback()` on any of them got silence where .NET throws, and a configured `EncoderReplacementFallback("!")` on ASCII was accepted, stored and ignored — a policy that could be set and never observed. All four now route through one shared dispatch (`System/Text/detail/FallbackDispatch.hpp`), so there is a single place where *what happens to input we cannot convert* is decided. **The DEFAULT output is byte-identical**, and keeping it so required a second transcription: `UnicodeEncoding` and `UTF32Encoding` now install a `U+FFFD` replacement fallback in their constructors, which is what .NET's `SetDefaultFallbacks` does for them (`UnicodeEncoding.cs:56-68`, `UTF32Encoding.cs:65-76`) and what makes the default substitution survive the routing. **The second finding is also repaired**: a truncated trailing fixed-width unit was DISCARDED outright — `UTF16LE.GetString(3 bytes)` returned one character and the odd byte vanished with no diagnostic — and now reaches the fallback like any other undecodable input, so an exception fallback can report it. +3 tests, two gated pins inverted; two mutations, each caught. **One limitation is recorded rather than smuggled**: `EncoderFallback::GetFallbackBytes` takes a `char`, so the unencodable scalar is narrowed — harmless for both shipped fallbacks, which ignore the argument, and filed as **#2355** because widening a public virtual's signature needs SA-2's machinery. No layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. |
| [SR-AUD-293](modules/text/include/System/Text/UnicodeEncoding.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-293--medium--fixed-width-utf-decoders-silently-drop-incomplete-trailing-units) | medium | remediated | `UnicodeEncoding.hpp`, `UTF32Encoding.hpp` | UTF-16 drops an odd final byte and UTF-32 drops one to three trailing bytes without passing them through the configured decoder fallback. **Design complete, blocked (#2017, cause T-K, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced: `UTF16LE.GetString(3 bytes)` → one character with the trailing byte dropped; `UTF32LE.GetString(6 bytes)` → one character with two dropped. Same cause and same ticket as SR-AUD-292. Approval sentence in plan §14.5; behaviour pinned by #2012. **REMEDIATED (#2017, 2026-08-17).** Only `UTF8Encoding` consulted its configured fallback; `UnicodeEncoding`, `UTF32Encoding`, `ASCIIEncoding` and `Latin1Encoding` substituted `U+FFFD` or `'?'` **directly**, so a caller who installed `DecoderFallback::ExceptionFallback()` on any of them got silence where .NET throws, and a configured `EncoderReplacementFallback("!")` on ASCII was accepted, stored and ignored — a policy that could be set and never observed. All four now route through one shared dispatch (`System/Text/detail/FallbackDispatch.hpp`), so there is a single place where *what happens to input we cannot convert* is decided. **The DEFAULT output is byte-identical**, and keeping it so required a second transcription: `UnicodeEncoding` and `UTF32Encoding` now install a `U+FFFD` replacement fallback in their constructors, which is what .NET's `SetDefaultFallbacks` does for them (`UnicodeEncoding.cs:56-68`, `UTF32Encoding.cs:65-76`) and what makes the default substitution survive the routing. **The second finding is also repaired**: a truncated trailing fixed-width unit was DISCARDED outright — `UTF16LE.GetString(3 bytes)` returned one character and the odd byte vanished with no diagnostic — and now reaches the fallback like any other undecodable input, so an exception fallback can report it. +3 tests, two gated pins inverted; two mutations, each caught. **One limitation is recorded rather than smuggled**: `EncoderFallback::GetFallbackBytes` takes a `char`, so the unencodable scalar is narrowed — harmless for both shipped fallbacks, which ignore the argument, and filed as **#2355** because widening a public virtual's signature needs SA-2's machinery. No layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. |
| [SR-AUD-294](modules/text/include/System/Text/Rune.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-294--medium--rune-unicode-category-and-case-members-are-reduced-to-ascii) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `Rune.hpp` | Public Rune letter/digit/case operations recognise only ASCII; U+00E9 is not a letter and does not uppercase despite the Unicode-category counterpart contract. **Design complete, blocked (#2018, cause T-L, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced: `Rune(U+00E9).IsLetter` → `false`, `ToUpper` a no-op, `IsDigit(U+0660)` → `false`. **Premise refinement (plan §4.7):** the port is not silently wrong — seven of the eight member doc-comments already say "ASCII range only" — but it does contradict itself, because `Rune::IsWhiteSpace(U+00A0)` is Unicode-aware and returns `true`. Approval sentence in plan §14.6; gated on a generated Unicode table and a stated Unicode version. Shares CCF-015's subject without being a CCF-015 member: `Rune` classifies scalars, not bytes. **Pinned by #2022 (2026-08-03):** this finding had **no behaviour-pinning test at all** — every `Rune` test in the repository uses ASCII exclusively and passes identically before and after a Unicode repair, so #2018 could have landed unapproved. `TextGatedBehaviourPinTests` now pins `IsLetter`/`IsDigit`/`IsUpper`/`IsLower`/`ToUpper`/`ToLower` for U+00E9, U+00C9, U+0660 and U+03B1, mutation-checked against a Latin-1-widened `IsLetter`. **Premise refinement:** `Rune::IsWhiteSpace` is Unicode-aware **and divergent** — its table contains U+FEFF, which .NET excludes — so the repair is not "make six members match the seventh". Folded into #2018; no `SR-AUD-*` identifier issued. |
| [SR-AUD-295](modules/text/src/System/Text/StringBuilder.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-295--high--stringbuilder-copyto-signed-capacity-arithmetic-overflows-before-validation) | high | remediated | `StringBuilder.cpp`, `StringBuilder.hpp` | `CopyTo` subtracts count from unvalidated `destinationLength`; UBSan confirms signed overflow for `INT_MIN` capacity and count one. **Remediated (#2009, 2026-08-03, cause T-C, CCF-004's fourth module).** A negative `destinationLength` — the one parameter of five with no guard — is rejected before any arithmetic, and the remaining comparison is widened. **Premise correction (plan §4.3):** the consequence is worse than filed. Because `INTCS_MIN - count` wraps to a large positive value, the comparison `destinationIndex > destinationLength - count` evaluated **false**, so the guard **passed** and `std::copy` ran — ASan reported a heap-buffer-overflow writing four bytes into a two-byte destination while the call **returned normally**. The undefined arithmetic did not accompany the bounds check, it **defeated** it. UBSan reported `StringBuilder.cpp:146:50: signed integer overflow: -2147483648 - 4` before and is silent after. +8 tests — also the first `CopyTo` coverage the suite has ever had, which is why the audit recorded the finding as having no regression. |
| [SR-AUD-296](modules/text/src/System/Text/StringBuilder.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-296--medium--stringbuilder-exposes-utf-8-byte-positions-as-managed-character-positions) | medium | remediated | `StringBuilder.hpp`, `StringBuilder.cpp` | Length/index/insert/remove/copy use storage bytes; `éA` has length 3 and removing index 1 yields invalid `c341`, not a character-preserving result. **Disclosure half remediated, semantic half design-complete and blocked (#2012 / #2015, cause T-I, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced exactly: `StringBuilder(u8"éA").Length` → `3`, and `Remove(1,1)` → the ill-formed byte pair `c341`. #2012 makes the class doc-comment, `Length`, `Insert` and `Remove` say "UTF-8 byte" where they said "character", and pins both measurements by test; #2015 carries the approval sentence (plan §14.3). **DECIDED 2026-08-17 (#2015) as a DECLARED DEVIATION, and closed on that basis.** Plan §14.3 offered three options for what a public index means: (A) UTF-16 code units, (B) scalar counts, (C) keep UTF-8 storage bytes and declare it. **The ticket's own constraint decides it** — the unit *"should not be decided without deciding it for `System::String`, which carries the same adaptation"* — and `System::String` is a UTF-8 `std::string` throughout this runtime, so A and B are not a change to `System::Text` but a re-architecture of every index in the port. **What makes (C) the FAITHFUL answer rather than the cheap one is measured, not asserted**: a byte index into UTF-8 is the exact analogue of a code-unit index into UTF-16 *including* the ability to split a character. .NET's `StringBuilder.Remove` validates only the numeric range (`StringBuilder.cs:1024-1042`) — there is **no** surrogate-pair guard — so it can leave a lone surrogate and an ill-formed string, exactly as this port can leave an ill-formed UTF-8 sequence. Adopting .NET's unit would have moved that hazard to a different character, not removed it. Recorded in `CLAUDE.md`'s permanent-deviations list and pinned by `TextUnitContractTests.Decl2015_*`, which additionally assert the unit is **consistent** across the component — a mixture would be far worse than either unit consistently applied. The disclosure half was #2012. **No production statement changed**; this ticket is a decision and its evidence. |
| [SR-AUD-297](modules/text/include/System/Text/Encodings/Web/HtmlEncoder.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-297--medium--web-default-encoders-and-url-decoder-do-not-preserve-the-net-policy-or-diagnostic-contract) | medium | remediated | Html/JavaScript/Url encoder headers, Web tests | Default HTML/JavaScript encoders pass raw non-Basic-Latin UTF-8; additional UrlDecoder exposes `std::invalid_argument` for malformed percent text instead of a defined runtime result/diagnostic. **Diagnostics half remediated (#2011), policy half design-complete and blocked (#2019), causes T-E and T-M, 2026-08-03.** **Premise correction (plan §4.5):** the `Decode` defect is four defects, not one — besides the named `std::invalid_argument` for `%zz`, `std::stoi` also accepted text that is not two hexadecimal digits and produced a **silently wrong byte**: `"%-1"` → `0xFF`, `"% 1"` → `0x01`, `"%+f"` → `0x0F`. `HtmlEncoder::Encode(value, startIndex, characterCount)`, which the index row does not mention at all, threw `std::out_of_range` for a negative start and **silently clamped** an over-long count. #2011 closes all six: RFC 3986 §2.1 two-`HEXDIG` parsing raising `System::FormatException`, and managed range diagnostics raising `System::ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. Truncated `"%4"` keeps its literal pass-through. The default HTML/JavaScript encoders' Basic-Latin allow-list is the gated remainder, ticket #2019, approval sentence in plan §14.7, current pass-through pinned by #2011's own test. **REMEDIATED (#2019, 2026-08-17), and the half the approval package recorded as UNVERIFIABLE is now verified.** The default `HtmlEncoder` and `JavaScriptEncoder` passed every non-Basic-Latin scalar through unchanged. .NET's defaults are built with an **allow-list**: `DefaultHtmlEncoder.BasicLatinSingleton` and its JavaScript sibling are both `new Default…Encoder(new TextEncoderSettings(UnicodeRanges.BasicLatin))` (`DefaultHtmlEncoder.cs:13`, `DefaultJavaScriptEncoder.cs:11`) — U+0000..U+007F and nothing else — so the target set was never a preference, only a missing measurement. The escape forms are transcribed too: `&#x` + the **scalar** in uppercase hex with no padding + `;` (`DefaultHtmlEncoder.cs:98-126`), and `\uXXXX` with four uppercase hex digits, a **surrogate pair** for a supplementary scalar because that is what a JavaScript string literal can express (`DefaultJavaScriptEncoder.cs:129-150`). `UrlEncoder` needed no change: it already percent-encoded every byte outside the unreserved set, which is the same policy in its own syntax. **This is a narrowing and it is the safe direction** — an allow-list escapes a character nobody thought about, which a deny-list ships — and that is asserted rather than only claimed: one case checks that the encoded form is itself entirely Basic Latin. +2 tests, the gated pin inverted; the mutation restoring the pass-through is caught by both. No signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. A relaxed encoder (.NET's `HtmlEncoder.Create(UnicodeRanges.All)`) is deliberately NOT added — that is new API and needs its own ticket. The diagnostics half was #2011. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. `docs/Migration-WebEncoderBasicLatinAllowList.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-298](modules/text/include/System/Text/CompositeFormat.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-298--medium--compositeformat-leaks-native-parse-exceptions-and-accepts-malformed-alignment-grammar) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `CompositeFormat.hpp` | `{2147483648}` exposes `std::out_of_range` and `{0,not-a-width}` parses successfully, rather than normalizing malformed format-item diagnostics. **Diagnostics half remediated (#2010), grammar half design-complete and blocked (#2020), causes T-D and T-N, CCF-012, 2026-08-03.** **Premise correction (plan §4.4):** the same `std::stoi` expression produced a **silent wrong result** one value before the named leak — `Parse("{2147483647}")` succeeded and returned `getMinimumArgumentCountProperty() == -2147483648`, UBSan-confirmed `signed integer overflow: 2147483647 + 1` from `return maxIdx + 1`. #2010 accumulates the index in a wider type bounded at `INTCS_MAX - 1` so neither the leak nor the overflow can occur, and **deliberately stops one value short of** `System::detail::kCompositeIndexLimit`; adopting the shared #1884 grammar (which also rejects `{0,not-a-width}` and `{0,-}`) is #2020, approval sentence in plan §14.8, with all three shapes pinned as still accepted. **Correction to CCF-012 (plan §4.6): its exclusion list states that `System.Text.CompositeFormat` "is not ported". It is** — this is the third composite-format grammar, and CCF-012 cannot close until #2020 lands. **Pinned further by #2022 (2026-08-03), with three corrections to plan §14.8, all measured (`build-probe/2022_probe1_verify.log` §A):** (i) *"any index at or above 1,000,000 begin to throw"* is **false** — `kCompositeIndexLimit` stops digit consumption rather than rejecting, so the shared grammar **accepts** `{1000000}` … `{9999999}` and first rejects eight-digit indices and `{2147483646}`; (ii) adoption also **widens** — `{0 }` and `{0  ,5}` are `FormatException` here today and are accepted there; (iii) `runCompositeFormat` is a **formatting** engine needing an `argCount` and a `render` and it **pads while validating** (`pad=1000000` for `{0,1000000}`), so #2020 must extract a non-rendering scanner into `modules/core` rather than call it. The widening row is now pinned. |
| [SR-AUD-299](modules/text/include/System/Text/EncodingInfo.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-299--medium--encodinginfo-getencoding-ignores-its-declared-code-page) | medium | remediated | `EncodingInfo.hpp`, `EncodingProvider.hpp` | Every EncodingInfo returns UTF-8, including one that advertises ASCII or another code page, giving a successful object with the wrong byte behavior. **Design complete, blocked (#2021, cause T-F, 2026-08-03).** Reproduced: `EncodingInfo(20127, "us-ascii", "US-ASCII").GetEncoding()->getCodePageProperty()` → `65001`, encoding `é` as UTF-8. Re-verified that no first-party caller constructs an `EncodingInfo`, so the risk is external only, and that the header's own doc-comment already states the reduction verbatim (plan §4.7). Approval sentence in plan §14.9; gated because the returned object changes for seven of the eight code pages this port implements and an unknown page starts throwing. **Pinned by #2022 (2026-08-03):** this finding had **no test anywhere**. `TextGatedBehaviourPinTests` now pins the code page, the UTF-8 bytes and the resolution of four code pages, mutation-checked against a `GetEncoding` that resolves 20127 to ASCII. **Correction to plan §14.9:** the object returned is `Encoding::UTF8()` **itself** — the shared, publicly mutable singleton SR-AUD-288 owns — so #2021 and #2013 are one decision about one object (`docs/SystemTextApprovalPackage.md` §2.2). **REMEDIATED (#2021, 2026-08-17).** `EncodingInfo::GetEncoding()` returned `Encoding::UTF8()` unconditionally, so `EncodingInfo(20127, "us-ascii", …)` handed back an object whose `getCodePageProperty()` was **65001** and which encoded `é` as UTF-8 — an object that REPORTS one code page and BEHAVES as another, which is worse than one that refuses. .NET's is `Provider?.GetEncoding(CodePage) ?? Encoding.GetEncoding(CodePage)` (`EncodingInfo.cs:56`): it resolves **its own** code page. It now does, for all seven code pages this component implements, and **rejects** anything else with `ArgumentException` carrying .NET's `Argument_EncodingNotSupported` text rather than substituting. That rejection is the half that matters: this runtime has no `EncodingProvider` registry, so returning *something* was the defect. The finding's own note that #2021 and #2013 must be decided together is discharged — the resolved object is the shared factory instance, which #2013 made read-only on the same day. +2 tests, the gated pin inverted; the mutation restoring the unconditional UTF-8 is caught by both. |
| [SR-AUD-300](modules/net/src/System/Net/SocketAddress.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-300--high--getipendpoint-decodes-undersized-and-non-ip-raw-buffers-without-validating-their-layout) | high | remediated | `SocketAddress.cpp` | Public `GetIPEndPoint` reads past undersized buffers, treats non-IP families as IPv4, and truncates invalid direct-constructor ports. **Remediated (#2035, 2026-08-04, cause N-A of `docs/SystemNetNamespaceReviewPlan.md`).** `GetIPEndPoint` now rejects any family that is not `InterNetwork`/`InterNetworkV6` and any declared size below that family's layout minimum with `ArgumentException`; the minimums are `IPv4AddressSize`/`IPv6AddressSize` — **the same two constants `IPEndPoint::Create` already checked**, so the safe path and the public shortcut can no longer disagree, and no buffer this class itself produces is affected. `SocketAddress(const IPAddress&, intcs)` validates the port against `IPEndPoint::MinPort`/`MaxPort` with `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("port")`, reusing `IPEndPoint::validatePort`'s own domain. **Two premise extensions, measured, no `SR-AUD-*` issued:** (i) the port defect spans the **whole signed domain**, not just the finding's 70000 — `INTCS_MIN` encoded as port **0** and `INTCS_MAX` as **65535**, so a caller could not tell a truncated port from a real one; (ii) a **defect the finding does not name** — `setSizeProperty` can shrink a well-formed 16-byte IPv4 buffer's *declared* size to 4 and `GetIPEndPoint` still decoded `127.0.0.1:80`, reading offsets 4–7 that the type's **own** `operator[]` refuses with `IndexOutOfRangeException`. That case is memory-**safe** (the allocation is intact) so no sanitizer can see it; it is a self-contradiction in the type's contract, and validating the **declared** size closes it and the allocation together because `size_ <= buffer_.size()` always holds. ASan reproduced the overflow at the finding's own line for IPv4 (`SocketAddress.cpp:106`, 0 bytes after a 2-byte region) **and at `:110` for IPv6** (0 bytes after an 8-byte region), **2 reports before, 0 after**, with `SocketAddress.cpp`/`IPAddress.cpp`/`IPEndPoint.cpp` compiled from source into the probe; UBSan is **non-discriminating** over the whole matrix (recorded as a non-result) and LSan reports no leak. Every well-formed IPv4/IPv6 round trip is **byte-identical** to the pre-repair encoding, transcribed into the tests from the probe log. +17 tests. No signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change; the header gained only doc-comments stating the new contract. Raw-buffer construction stays permissive, as the audit's own remediation note requires. Reproduced exactly and at the same line: ASan reports a `heap-buffer-overflow` **READ of size 1**, *0 bytes after a 2-byte region*, at `SocketAddress.cpp:106`, with the allocation stack showing the 2-byte `std::vector<bytecs>` (`build-probe/2034_probe2_asan.log`, `SocketAddress.cpp`/`IPAddress.cpp`/`IPEndPoint.cpp` compiled **from source** into the probe). Also measured: an `AddressFamily::Unix` buffer decodes as `0.0.0.0:0`, `SocketAddress(Loopback, 70000)` yields `127.0.0.1:4464` and `SocketAddress(Loopback, -1)` yields `127.0.0.1:65535`. Classified compatible because reading past the end of a heap allocation has no defined result and a truncated port is a defined-but-meaningless one; the one row that removes a **non-faulting** result (the `Unix` family) is tabulated in plan §7.3 and may be split out. |
| [SR-AUD-301](modules/net/src/System/Net/IPAddress.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-301--medium--raw-ipv6-scope-id-construction-and-mutation-silently-narrow-invalid-values) | medium | remediated | `IPAddress.cpp` | Raw IPv6 scope constructor/setter narrow negative and greater-than-UInt32 values instead of rejecting them. **Remediated (#2036, 2026-08-04, cause N-B of `docs/SystemNetNamespaceReviewPlan.md`).** One shared file-local `validatedScopeId(longcs, const char* paramName)` is adopted by **both** doors, exactly as the audit report's own remediation note asks ("share range validation between the constructor and setter"): a value outside `[0, 4294967295]` raises `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, and the constructor validates **before any field is written** so a rejected construction leaves nothing half-initialised. The `paramName` is each door's **own** parameter -- `"scopeId"` for the constructor, `"value"` for the setter -- matching .NET's `nameof(...)` convention rather than plan §7.3's shorthand, which wrote `"value"` for a combined row. The **IPv4 family guard stays first** and still wins for a wrong-family *and* out-of-domain call, preserving the pre-existing `SocketException` contract, and a rejected set leaves the previous scope id in place -- both pinned. **Premise extension, measured, no `SR-AUD-*` issued:** the narrowing is a **modulo-2^32 wrap, not a clamp**, so it produced a *plausible* scope id rather than an obviously wrong one -- `-4294967295` became **1**, `4294967297` became **1**, `LONGCS_MIN` and `4294967296` both became **0**, and `ToString()` rendered the wrapped value (`fe80::1%4294967291` for scope id `-5`). Two addresses constructed with `-1` and `4294967295` compared **equal**. UBSan is **non-discriminating in both directions** and is recorded as a non-result, exactly as plan §11 predicted for this conversion: a `longcs` → `uint32_t` narrowing is implementation-defined, not undefined. **Every other scope-id door was inventoried and is uint32-sourced**, so none could ever be out of range: the IPv6 text parser's own `from_chars<uint32_t>` (which already rejected `%4294967296` and `%-1`), `Dns::fromSockaddrIn6`, `SocketAddress::GetIPEndPoint`, and `net-sockets`' `Socket.cpp:169` -- verified by grep, not assumed. +9 tests. No signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change; `sizeof(IPAddress)` is 24 before and after. Relink-only in ABI terms; the header gained doc-comments stating the new contract. Reproduced in **both** directions and on **both** doors, as the finding claims: `IPAddress(bytes, -1).ScopeId` = **4294967295**, `IPAddress(bytes, 4294967296).ScopeId` = **0**, and `setScopeIdProperty(-5)` reads back **4294967291**. Cause N-B is one shape in two files — a signed public parameter converted to unsigned with no domain check — shared with SR-AUD-307. |
| [SR-AUD-302](modules/net/src/System/Net/IPEndPoint.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-302--medium--bracketed-endpoint-parser-accepts-and-discards-text-between--and-the-port-delimiter) | medium | remediated | `IPEndPoint.cpp` | Bracketed parsing accepts `[::1]ignored:80` and silently discards `ignored`. **Remediated (#2037, 2026-08-04, cause N-C of `docs/SystemNetNamespaceReviewPlan.md`).** What may follow the closing bracket is now exactly nothing, or `':'` and the port; anything else returns `false` / throws `FormatException`. **Premise extension, measured, no `SR-AUD-*` issued:** the finding names one shape and there are **four**. Besides `[::1]ignored:80` → `[::1]:80`, the old code also accepted **`[::1]ignored`** and **`[::1]x`** — with no colon anywhere after the bracket it simply cleared the port and reported success as `[::1]:0` — and **`[::1] :80`**, dropping a literal **space**, while the *unbracketed branch of the same function* correctly rejected the equivalent `1.2.3.4 :80`. One function gave two answers to the same input shape, which is why plan §12 is right that the repair needs no external reference: it is transcribed from the branch that was already correct — decisive here, since `/rv/tmp/runtime/src/libraries/` is absent from this container. `Parse` and `TryParse` agree on **all 29** probed inputs, before and after. Every previously valid form parses **identically**, including `[::1]` → `[::1]:0`. **One adjacent defect was deliberately NOT absorbed** and is carried by ordinary inactive ticket **#2045**: a trailing `':'` with no digits is accepted as port 0 in **both** branches (`[::1]:` and `1.2.3.4:`), which is cause N-C's *shape* but not this finding's site, and rejecting it would violate #2037's own acceptance criterion that every currently-valid form still parse identically. Both current results are **pinned** so #2045 cannot land silently. +9 tests. No signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change; `sizeof(IPEndPoint)` is 40 before and after; relink-only, the header was not touched at all. Reproduced: `IPEndPoint::Parse("[::1]ignored:80")` returns **`[::1]:80`**. The repair is transcribed from the same function's other two branches, which do **not** discard trailing text, so it needs no .NET reference. |
| [SR-AUD-303](modules/net/src/System/Net/IPNetwork.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-303--medium--ipnetwork-discards-an-already-normalized-ipv6-base-addresss-scope-id) | medium | remediated | `IPNetwork.cpp` | IPv6 network masking rebuilds even unchanged bytes and loses the base address scope ID. **Remediated (#2038, 2026-08-04, cause N-D of `docs/SystemNetNamespaceReviewPlan.md`, the silent-wrong-answer class of #1837).** `clearNonPrefixBits` rebuilds an IPv6 result through the 16-byte + scope constructor, so the scope id the constructor was handed survives masking at **every** prefix length (0/1/63/64/127/128 all measured); IPv4 and scope-0 IPv6 are byte-identical to before. **The half that could have broken silently — and would have — is `Contains`.** `IPAddress::operator==` compares the scope id, so preserving the scope while still writing `IPAddress(candidateBytes) == baseAddress_` would have made containment scope-**sensitive**: `fe80::1%7/64` would have stopped containing `fe80::1%9`, `fe80::1`, **and `fe80::1%7` itself**, because the candidate is rebuilt from bytes and carries scope 0. `Contains` therefore compares masked **bytes**, which reproduces **all fifteen** probed answers exactly (they already reduced to a byte comparison, since both sides always carried scope 0) and is the right semantics independently — a scope id names a link, not a prefix. **Premise extension, measured, no `SR-AUD-*` issued:** the finding names the lost `ScopeId`; it does not name the consequence, which is that two networks on **different links** compared **equal** and **hashed equal** (`fe80::1%7/64 == fe80::1%9/64` was `true`), so a map keyed on `IPNetwork` silently merged them. **Two observable changes on working calls, both intended and both tabulated in plan §17.5:** `BaseAddress.ScopeId` and `ToString()` for a scoped network (`fe80::/64` → `fe80::%7/64`), and equality/hash for two networks differing only in scope. Round trips through `Parse`/`ToString` still hold, including the newly rendered scope. No sanitizer applies (no allocation, index, shared state or undefined conversion) — stated rather than silently skipped. +8 tests. No signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change; relink-only, the header was not touched. Reproduced: `IPNetwork(fe80::1 scope 7, 64).BaseAddress.ScopeId` = **0** — information the constructor was handed is silently lost, the silent-wrong-answer class of #1837. Note this repair changes a **returned value on a working call**, which plan §7.3 calls out rather than hides. |
| [SR-AUD-304](modules/net/src/System/Net/Dns.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-304--medium--dns-ipv4-literal-fast-path-accepts-invalid-text-wildcard-addresses-and-wrong-family-results) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `Dns.cpp` | The duplicate scanf literal parser accepts invalid text, returns wildcard addresses, and skips requested-family filtering. **Three of four halves remediated (#2039, 2026-08-04, cause N-C); the wildcard half stays open as the gated #2043.** The duplicate `sscanf` parser is deleted and the module now has **one** literal parser, `IPAddress::TryParse`; the literal fast path honours the requested family; the resolver hints ask for `SOCK_STREAM` and the result list is deduplicated; and every failure message carries the platform resolver's own text instead of a fabricated Win32 code. **The central premise correction (plan §17.6), measured directly against `getaddrinfo`:** the duplicate results do **not** come from the duplicate parser, which plan §5's cause N-C asserts. They come from `hints.ai_socktype = 0`, which asks for **one `addrinfo` per socket type**, so *every* answer came back **three** times — `localhost`, `runsc`, `vm` and `127.0.0.1` as well as the finding's `1.2.3` — and `GetHostEntry(8.8.8.8)` returned **six** entries for two distinct addresses. Replacing the literal parser alone would have fixed the one named input **by accident** while leaving every resolved **name** still tripled. **Two more premise extensions:** the duplicate parser disagreed with `IPAddress::TryParse` about **valid** input, not just invalid — `0177.0.0.1` was `177.0.0.1` through `Dns` and `127.0.0.1` through `IPAddress::Parse`, i.e. one module gave two answers for one literal — and it also accepted `+1.2.3.4` and `" 1.2.3.4"`, neither named. **One correction to plan §7.3:** its row predicts an **empty** result for `GetHostAddresses("127.0.0.1", Unix)`. It raises `SocketException(HostNotFound)` instead, because that is this repository's **own** established and tested contract for a family-mismatched literal — two pre-existing `DnsTests` pin it and their comment records the reasoning verbatim (an empty vector is *"indistinguishable from 'checked and found nothing'"*). Deduplication uses **binary `IPAddress` equality** (family + bits + IPv6 scope id), preserves the resolver's first-occurrence order, and canonicalises nothing: `1.2.3.4` vs `::ffff:1.2.3.4` and `fe80::1%7` vs `fe80::1%9` are kept distinct, each pinned. **`"0.0.0.0"` and `"::"` are deliberately unchanged** and now pinned so the gated **#2043** cannot land silently. **One residual is recorded rather than hidden** — the family filter reaches a literal but not a resolved name (`("localhost", Unix)` still returns an IPv4 address) — carried by ordinary inactive ticket **#2046**, because narrowing it would remove a currently-succeeding result for every name. LSan is **discriminating**: clean across **12,800** calls over both the success and failure paths, while a control build that abandons one `addrinfo` list per iteration reports the leak. The stale `Dns.hpp` note claiming resolution is *"restricted to IPv4"* and that `InterNetworkV6` *"returns an empty result"* — false on all three counts — is corrected in place. +16 tests. No signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change. The finding's own cases reproduce: `GetHostAddresses("-0.0.0.1")` = `0.0.0.1`, `("0.0.0.0")` returns the wildcard, and `("127.0.0.1", Unix)` returns one IPv4 result with no family filtering. **Two premise corrections (plan §§4.1-4.2):** the finding does **not** name a fourth defect — `GetHostAddresses("1.2.3")` returns **three identical** `1.2.0.3` entries — and `"999.999.999.999"` does **not** reach the literal parser at all but **throws** from real resolution, whose POSIX message is the Win32-shaped `"Win32 error 11001"` (a disclosure defect folded into #2039, no `SR-AUD-*` issued). The wildcard half is the only one that removes a **working, meaningful** result and is therefore gated as #2043. |
| [SR-AUD-305](modules/net/src/System/Net/CookieContainer.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-305--high--adduri-cookie-accepts-an-unrelated-explicit-domain-and-later-emits-that-cookie-for-it) | high | remediated | `CookieContainer.cpp` | A cookie supplied from one origin may be stored and emitted for an unrelated explicit domain. **Design complete, blocked (#2040, cause N-E, 2026-08-04).** Reproduced exactly, including the value: a cookie added from `origin.invalid` with `Domain=.unrelated.invalid` is returned by `GetCookieHeader(http://unrelated.invalid/)` as **`session=isolated`**. Decided **together with SR-AUD-306**, because the implicit flags that finding describes are the *input* to the domain-matching rule this one needs. Gated on approval — `Add` starts rejecting cookies it stores today — **and** on evidence: .NET's exact `Cookie.VerifyAndSetDefaults` domain rule cannot be established in this container. The recommended option validates at insertion and needs **no object-layout change**; storing the origin on `Cookie` would be one, since `Cookie` is header-only. Approval sentence plan §14.1. **REMEDIATED (#2040, 2026-08-17).** The gate was an approval AND missing evidence — .NET's exact `VerifyAndSetDefaults` domain rule — and the reference tree settles it (`docs/StandingApprovals.md` §5.1, SA-5). `Cookie.VerifyAndSetDefaults` (`Cookie.cs:358-424`) reads: if the domain is implicit, `SetDomainAndKey(host)`; otherwise `IsValidDomainName(key) && HostMatchesDomain(host, key)` or `throw new CookieException(SR.Format(SR.net_cookie_attribute, "Domain", m_domain))`. Both halves of this ticket land together because the implicit flags are the INPUT to that rule: the `Cookie` constructors now route through their setters, exactly as .NET's do by writing `Path = path;` / `Domain = domain;` (`Cookie.cs:108-118`), so a caller-supplied value is no longer overwritten by the request URI's; and `CookieContainer::Add` validates an explicit domain and throws `CookieException` carrying .NET's own message text. Defaulting an implicit domain deliberately does NOT clear the flag, matching `SetDomainAndKey` (`Cookie.cs:310-314`). ONE domain rule now serves both the validation and the emission match, as in .NET, which also brought in the two compatibility conditions `HostMatchesDomain` adds on top of RFC 6265 (`Cookie.cs:347-349`): a single-label domain and an IP-literal host both require an exact match. +8 tests, the four #2047 pins inverted; four mutations, all caught, M1's first attempt invalid under `-Werror`. No layout, vtable, signature or `noexcept` change; `CookieContainer` becomes a friend of `Cookie` for the two origin-defaulting setters, which is the boundary .NET draws with `internal`. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero `Cookie`/`CookieContainer` sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. `docs/SystemNetNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §14.1. |

Both halves of this ticket land together because the implicit flags are the INPUT to that rule: the `Cookie` constructors now route through their setters, exactly as .NET's do by writing `Path = path;` / `Domain = domain;` (`Cookie.cs:108-118`), so a caller-supplied value is no longer overwritten by the request URI's; and `CookieContainer::Add` validates an explicit domain and throws `CookieException` carrying .NET's own message text. Defaulting an implicit domain deliberately does NOT clear the flag, matching `SetDomainAndKey` (`Cookie.cs:310-314`). ONE domain rule now serves both the validation and the emission match, as in .NET, which also brought in the two compatibility conditions `HostMatchesDomain` adds on top of RFC 6265 (`Cookie.cs:347-349`): a single-label domain and an IP-literal host both require an exact match. +8 tests, the four #2047 pins inverted; four mutations, all caught, M1's first attempt invalid under `-Werror`. No layout, vtable, signature or `noexcept` change; `CookieContainer` becomes a friend of `Cookie` for the two origin-defaulting setters, which is the boundary .NET draws with `internal`.
