| [SR-AUD-307](modules/net/include/System/Net/CookieCollection.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-307--high--negative-or-oversized-collection-indexes-reach-unchecked-vector-access) | high | remediated | `CookieCollection.hpp` | Signed indices narrow to size_t and direct vector access crashes under ASan. **Remediated (#2041, 2026-08-04, cause N-B of `docs/SystemNetNamespaceReviewPlan.md`).** Both indexers now route through one private `validatedIndex(intcs)` that rejects `index < 0 || index >= Count` with `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("index", "Index was out of range. Must be non-negative and less than the size of the collection.")` — the message `List<T>`, `Array`, `ArrayList`, `ArraySegment` and `ObservableCollection` already use, so no reference tree was needed. The negative half is tested on the **signed** value and the upper half only afterwards, so no `size_t` conversion is ever performed on a negative index and the bound is not truncated. **Premise correction (plan §4.3) re-verified independently, and it is sharper than filed:** with **one probe source and four binaries** (`build-probe/2041_probe1_before_after.log`), *without* a sanitizer the finding's own case `collection[-1]` **returned normally** on **both** overloads with a garbage `Cookie` whose `Name` printed as `(null)`, while `collection[Count]` died with SIGSEGV — and on an **empty** collection `[-1]` crashed instead, so which of the two outcomes appears is itself undefined rather than a property of the index. Eleven indexes were probed across both overloads. **ASan: 9 reports before (`heap-buffer-overflow` and `SEGV`), 0 after.** **Correction to plan §11:** UBSan was predicted non-discriminating; measured, it is **partially** discriminating — silent about the `intcs`→`size_t` conversion as predicted, but reporting *"reference binding to null pointer"* and *"applying non-zero offset 18446744073709551464 to null pointer"* for the two empty-collection cases before and nothing after. +14 tests (`CookieCollectionIndexTests`, 12; `NetLayoutPinTests`, 2 incl. `static_assert` layout pins for all five §10 types). `CookieCollection` is header-inline, so every cross-module consumer recompiled: `SharpRuntimeTests_Net_Http`, `_Net_Http_Json`, `_Net_WebSockets`, `_Net_Sockets`, `_Net_NetworkInformation` and the integration suite were rebuilt and re-run. No signature, `noexcept`, virtual, vtable, data member or layout change — `sizeof(CookieCollection)` is 24 before and after. |
| [SR-AUD-308](modules/net/src/System/Net/CookieContainer.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-308--medium--cookie-storage-is-unbounded-despite-a-public-network-container-role) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `CookieContainer.cpp` | No capacity, per-domain, size, cleanup, or aging policy bounds accumulated cookie state. **Design complete, blocked (#2042, cause N-F, 2026-08-04).** Reproduced: 10,000 distinct cookies from one origin are all retained (`Count == 10000`). Gated because every bound is a number somebody must choose and adding one starts **discarding** stored data, and on evidence: .NET's exact default capacities cannot be established here. If the chosen design adds background aging, TSan becomes applicable to this type. Approval sentence plan §14.2. |
| [SR-AUD-309](modules/net/include/System/Net/WebUtility.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-309--medium--html-encodingdecoding-is-an-incomplete-byte-oriented-subset-of-webutility) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `WebUtility.hpp` | HTML encoding/decoding is a five-entity byte subset rather than compatible Unicode/entity behavior. **Design complete, deferred (#2044, cause N-G, 2026-08-04).** **Premise correction (plan §4.4): the finding describes the ENCODE direction only.** `HtmlEncode` is indeed a five-entity subset that passes all non-ASCII through, but `HtmlDecode` already understands decimal (`&#169;`) and hexadecimal (`&#xA9;`) character references and named entities beyond those five (`&copy;`) — a decoder that understands more than its encoder produces, which is itself the shape of the defect. **Deferred rather than merely blocked**: .NET's exact default escape set cannot be verified in this container, the same gap that defers `System::Text` #2019, and the two must be decided together or two HTML encoders in one repository will diverge. Approval sentence plan §14.4. |
| [SR-AUD-310](modules/net-http/src/System/Net/Http/HttpClient.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-310--high--httpclient-asynchronous-methods-retain-a-raw-client-pointer-after-the-client-may-be-destroyed) | high | remediated | `HttpClient.cpp`, `HttpClient.hpp`, `Task.hpp` | ASan confirms a task that outlives a destroyed HttpClient reads freed `defaultHeaders_` through its captured raw `this`. **Dispositioned by #2062's review (`docs/SystemNetHttpNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.6, cause NH-D): approval-sensitive, blocked ticket #2066, NOT APPROVED and not implemented.** This is **CCF-019** verbatim — a copyable public handle retaining a raw pointer with no owner liveness — the family that also blocks #1888/#1889/#1894/#1899/#1959/#1970. Repair means either `HttpClient` deriving from `enable_shared_from_this` (a public base-class change) or `TaskT` gaining ownership semantics (a `Threading.Tasks` change far outside this module). **Deliberately NOT qualified `confirmed (design-complete)`**, unlike SR-AUD-314 and SR-AUD-315: the review records **two competing options with no selection** and explicitly declined to re-run the reproduction, so no design is complete (correction recorded in the plan §20.7, which supersedes its own §17 criterion 3 for this finding only). **A use-after-free is not a behaviour to pin**, so #2063's suite pins the *ownership model* instead: a `static_assert` that `HttpClient` does **not** derive from `std::enable_shared_from_this<HttpClient>`, mutation-checked (adding the base fires it as a compile error). **CCF-019 is NOT closed.** **REMEDIATED (#2066, 2026-08-17) with the liveness boundary #2134 established for this family.** Every `*Async` member captured raw `this` in a body `std::async` starts immediately, with nothing keeping the object alive until it ran. .NET needs no boundary because the GC does that job; C++ has no such mechanism, so the destructor waits. **No public signature changed** and no layout pin existed to update; `HttpClient` is non-copyable and therefore already non-movable, so the destructor it gains takes nothing away. There is nothing to interrupt for an in-flight request — the socket lives inside the handler and is unreachable from the client — so the bound is the request's own timeout. Two implementation facts carried over from #2134 and worth not rediscovering: the decrement must be **body-local**, because `std::async` keeps the callable alive until the last future to its shared state is destroyed, so a guard captured BY the lambda is released when the CALLER drops the task and the boundary then waits for the caller; and the boundary must be **crossable**, so it wakes a blocked worker before waiting rather than turning a use-after-free into a hang. Downstream measured under SA-2: zero sites in `cna` or `mobile-eggbert`. **CCF-019 is NOT closed** — #1959 remains, as do the owned-tree members SR-AUD-327 and SR-AUD-333. |
| [SR-AUD-311](modules/net-http/src/System/Net/Http/HttpClient.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-311--medium--ad-hoc-url-parser-accepts-malformed-authority-text-and-loses-query-only-request-targets) | medium | remediated | `HttpClient.cpp`, `HttpClient.hpp`, `HttpClientTests.cpp` | Prefix ports, invalid ranges, bracket suffixes, and authority-only queries are accepted or parsed into the wrong host/path. **REMEDIATED (#2064, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemNetHttpNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.2 and §20.6, cause NH-A / CCF-002).** `std::stoi` stops at the first character it cannot use and reports **success** for the prefix it consumed, and it additionally skips leading whitespace and accepts a sign; nothing checked that the whole field had been consumed. Measured: `:80abc` -> **80**, `:-1` -> **−1**, `:99999` -> **99999**, `: 80` -> **80**, `:+80` -> **80**. The port now routes through a full-consumption parser — the entire text must be one to five ASCII digits and the value must fall inside 0…65535 — which is CCF-002's remedy shape reduced to what this module needs; **no new family was minted**. The authority now ends at the first `/`, `?` **or** `#`, closing the finding's worst row: `http://host?q=1` used to parse to host **`host?q=1`** with path `/`, so a query string reached **DNS and the `Host:` header** while the request line asked for `/` — `client.Get("http://api.example?key=secret")` put the secret in the Host header and requested the wrong resource. That is a **value change, not a rejection**, which is what made it the more dangerous half to leave alone. **Premise correction (plan §6.3):** the finding's list is complete but the host was additionally **never lowercased** while the scheme already was, so `HOST.EXAMPLE` and `host.example` were distinct hosts to the cookie container, the `Host:` header and every downstream consumer (RFC 3986 §3.2.2); it is lowercased now. A **fragment** is dropped from the request target in both positions (RFC 9110 §7.1 — client-side only), extending §4.2's authority-only row consistently rather than writing `GET /p#frag` on the wire. `http://host:0` and `:65535` stay accepted; `http://host:`, `http://user:pass@host/p` and `http:///p` keep throwing exactly as before; **not one existing test needed updating**. +9 permanent regressions plus 5 interaction tests with #2063. Seven mutations, each reverted from an exact backup: `break`ing at the first non-digit (i.e. `std::stoi` again) fails 4 tests, reverting the authority split fails 2, removing the lowercasing fails 2, keeping the fragment fails 2. ASan/UBSan/LSan clean with `HttpClient.cpp` compiled **from source** and a control heap-buffer-overflow proving instrumentation (`build-probe/2064_probe2_asan.log`). No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. Deliberate narrowing plus two value changes, documented in the `parseUrl` doc-comment. |
| [SR-AUD-312](modules/net-http/src/System/Net/Http/HttpClient.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-312--medium--status-line-parser-turns-malformed-response-text-into-arbitrary-or-successful-status-codes) | medium | remediated | `HttpClient.cpp`, `HttpClient.hpp`, `HttpClientTests.cpp` | Prefix numeric parsing accepts `200trailer`, invalid versions, and non-three-digit status codes, potentially presenting malformed response text as success. **REMEDIATED (#2064, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemNetHttpNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.3 and §20.6, cause NH-A / CCF-002).** **Premise correction (plan §6.2): the version token was never parsed AT ALL**, so this is not a case of "invalid versions" being accepted too leniently — there was no check to tighten, there was one to **add**. Measured, `GARBAGE 200 OK` yielded **200**: a response that is not HTTP at all was reported as a successful HTTP response. The token must now be exactly `HTTP/<digit>.<digit>` (RFC 9112 §2.3) and the status code exactly three ASCII digits (RFC 9112 §4), through the same full-consumption parser as the port. `200trailer` -> 200, `2` -> 2, `20` -> 20, `-5` -> **−5**, `+5` -> 5 and `99999` -> 99999 are all rejected now; the `−5` row mattered because the handler casts the result into `System::Net::HttpStatusCode`, a **public enum**, which then held a value no enumerator names. **Two choices recorded as this port's, not as .NET matches, and PINNED either way per plan §15:** `HTTP/1.1 099 OK` is **accepted** as the code 99 (three digits is all the grammar asks for), and `HTTP/9.9 200 OK` is **accepted** (a version this port does not speak is the server's behaviour to report, not a parse error). `HTTP/1.1  200 OK` (double space) keeps throwing, unchanged. **A sanitizer premise was corrected by measurement:** the review's §12 nominated UBSan's invalid-enum-value report as the instrument for the `−5` cast. Measured with a dedicated discrimination control (`build-probe/2064_probe3_enumctl.log`), `-fsanitize=enum -fno-sanitize-recover=undefined` **reports nothing and exits 0** for that cast, because `HttpStatusCode` is an `enum class` with the implicit `int` underlying type and every `int` is inside its value range. **UBSan is not a discriminating instrument for this defect** and the clean run is recorded as a non-discriminating confirmation; the behavioural test `NoStatusCodeCanEscapeTheEnumDomain` is the closure evidence. +7 permanent regressions plus the shared interaction suite and an end-to-end test that a `GARBAGE 200 OK` server is now rejected **without** reintroducing #2065's descriptor leak. Mutation-checked: disabling the version check fails exactly 2 tests, dropping the three-digit requirement fails exactly 1. No signature, layout or ABI change. |
| [SR-AUD-313](modules/net-http/src/System/Net/Http/HttpClientHandler.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-313--high--httpmime-serializer-accepts-crlf-bearing-metadata-and-emits-extra-fields) | high | remediated | `HttpClientHandler.cpp`, `HttpClient.cpp`, `HttpRequestMessage.hpp`, `HttpResponseMessage.hpp`, `StringContent.hpp`, `ByteArrayContent.hpp`, `ReadOnlyMemoryContent.hpp`, `StreamContent.hpp`, `MultipartContent.cpp`, `MultipartFormDataContent.cpp` | Raw request/header/media/disposition concatenation accepts CR/LF; a direct multipart probe emits separate injected MIME fields. **REMEDIATED (#2063, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemNetHttpNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.1 and §20.3).** The finding has **ten** public doors, not the four the namespace review's §4.1 scoped the ticket to, and every one of them was measured open against `257106a` (`build-probe/2063_probe1_doors.cpp`, log `2063_probe1_before.log`). Two corrections mattered: the **request path** is an injection vector the review never named — `parseUrl("http://host/pa\r\nX: y")` returned path `"/pa\r\nX: y"`, which `HttpClientHandler::Send` writes into the request **LINE**, so that is request smuggling rather than one extra header field; and the **media type, charset, multipart subtype and form-data name/file name** doors that this finding's own text names were dropped by the review's paraphrase and were all still open — `MultipartFormDataContent::Add(c, "na\r\nX-Injected: yes")` emitted `Content-Disposition: form-data; name="na\r\nX-Injected: yes"`, reproducing the audit's multipart probe exactly. Closing only §4.1's four doors would have marked this finding remediated with three of its own named vectors still open. The repair rejects CR, LF and NUL at all ten doors through one shared `System::Net::Http::detail` helper, and validates the **whole URL string** once at the top of `parseUrl` rather than the parsed components — which also makes #2064's authority/query/fragment re-split safe by construction. `System::FormatException` for the protocol-field doors, `System::UriFormatException` (which **is** a `FormatException`) for `parseUrl`, `HttpRequestException` for `parseStatusLine` and the handler's response-header-line check (a malformed **response** is this module's response error), `System::ArgumentException` for the two multipart doors whose same parameters already report their other defects that way. .NET's exact type is **not** known here (`/rv/tmp/runtime/` absent, re-verified 2026-08-04) and these are recorded as this port's choices. Rejected text is deliberately **not** echoed into the message: it is attacker-controlled and messages get logged. A space, a tab, an empty value, a percent-encoded CRLF and the content **body** all stay accepted. +21 permanent regressions covering each character at the start, middle and end of every door, plus an end-to-end assertion that an injected request URI **opens no socket at all** (the `/proc/self/fd` instrument #2065 established). Mutation-checked: emptying the shared predicate fails **exactly** the 13 rejection tests while all 8 acceptance tests and all 7 pins stay green. ASan/UBSan/LSan clean over 130 rejections and 19 acceptances with the four changed `.cpp` bodies compiled **from source** (the `Net.Http` archive would have been uninstrumented) and a control heap-buffer-overflow proving instrumentation (`build-probe/2063_probe3_asan.log`). No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. This is a **deliberate narrowing** of accepted input, documented in `docs/Migration-HttpControlCharacterRejection.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-314](modules/net-http/src/System/Net/Http/HttpClient.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-314--medium--httpclient-permits-a-request-message-to-be-sent-repeatedly) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `HttpClient.cpp`, `HttpRequestMessage.hpp` | No sent-state guard exists; a no-network counting handler receives the exact same request twice. **Dispositioned by #2062's review (`docs/SystemNetHttpNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.8, cause NH-F): approval-sensitive, blocked ticket #2067, NOT APPROVED and not implemented.** The repair is singular and named — `HttpRequestMessage` gains state, which is an **object-layout change to a public class**, plus a new throw from `Send` — which is what qualifies this finding `design-complete`; the finding itself stays **open** and is still counted as `confirmed`. Behaviour **pinned** by `NetHttpGatedBehaviourPins.Pin2067_OneRequestMessageCanStillBeSentTwice` and by a layout `static_assert` against a same-members probe struct (portable across libstdc++ and libc++, unlike a literal byte count). Both mutation-checked by #2063: adding the member fires the layout assert as a **compile error**, and a deliberately layout-neutral sent-state guard fails the behavioural pin and nothing else. |
| [SR-AUD-315](modules/net-http/include/System/Net/Http/HttpRequestMessage.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-315--medium--requestresponse-header-maps-treat-http-field-names-as-case-sensitive) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `HttpRequestMessage.hpp`, `HttpResponseMessage.hpp`, `HttpClient.cpp`, `HttpClientHandler.cpp` | Raw unordered maps make case-distinct HTTP header names unequal, so lookups fail and defaults can create duplicate semantic fields. **Dispositioned by #2062's review (`docs/SystemNetHttpNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.5, cause NH-E): approval-sensitive, blocked ticket #2068, NOT APPROVED and not implemented.** The repair is singular and named — a case-insensitive comparator — but `getHeadersProperty()` returns `const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>&`, so **the comparator is public surface** and changing it breaks every caller that names the type; the `Migration-CollectionsFloatingComparers` shape. That is what qualifies this finding `design-complete`; it stays **open** and is still counted as `confirmed`. **Premise correction (plan §6.5):** a second defect this finding does not name belongs here — `HttpClientHandler::Send` writes `Host`/`User-Agent`/`Accept`/`Connection` **unconditionally, before** the caller's map, so a caller-set `Host` sends **two** `Host` fields, which is request-smuggling-adjacent. It is folded into #2068 because de-duplicating needs the case-insensitive lookup #2068 introduces. Both halves **pinned** by `Pin2068_HeaderMapsAreCaseSensitive` and `Pin2068_HandlerEmitsADuplicateDefaultHeader`, and both mutation-checked by #2063 (a case-insensitive `getHeader` fails the first and nothing else; skipping the handler's own `Host` fails the second and nothing else). The two public map types are additionally frozen by `static_assert`s on the return type. |
| [SR-AUD-316](modules/net-http/include/System/Net/Http/HttpResponseMessage.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-316--medium--response-messages-accept-out-of-range-status-values-and-unvalidated-reason-phrases) | medium | confirmed | `HttpResponseMessage.hpp`, `HttpClient.cpp` | Public construction accepts -1 and 1000, and reason phrases admit newline text instead of managed range/format diagnostics. **SPLIT by compatibility (#2062's review, `docs/SystemNetHttpNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.4), following the SR-AUD-007 -> #1878/#1879 and SR-AUD-318 -> #2065/#2071 convention. Reason-phrase half REMEDIATED (#2063, 2026-08-04):** `setReasonPhraseProperty` rejects CR, LF and NUL with `System::FormatException`, and `parseStatusLine` rejects a control-bearing status line with `HttpRequestException` **before** the setter is reached, so a malformed **response** still surfaces as this module's response-error type rather than as a format error. **Status-code-domain half NOT remediated:** `HttpResponseMessage(static_cast<HttpStatusCode>(-1))`, `(0)`, `(1000)` and `(99999)` all still construct with `getIsSuccessStatusCodeProperty()` answering `false`. Rejecting an out-of-domain code means making a public constructor that today cannot fail **throw** — an exception-specification and semantic change — or replacing the parameter type, and `EnsureSuccessStatusCode`'s message text would change with it. Blocked ticket **#2069**; nothing about it is approved, and the current behaviour is **pinned** by `NetHttpGatedBehaviourPins.Pin2069_ResponseAcceptsAnyStatusNumber` (mutation-checked: a constructor that rejects a code outside 100–599 fails exactly that pin). SR-AUD-316 therefore stays `confirmed` for its open half. |
| [SR-AUD-317](modules/net-http/include/System/Net/Http/StringContent.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-317--medium--stringcontent-advertises-arbitrary-charsets-while-always-emitting-the-original-utf-8-bytes) | medium | confirmed | `StringContent.hpp`, `HttpClientHandler.cpp` | A UTF-8 payload labelled utf-16 remains `c3 a9`, so declared charset and emitted bytes can contradict each other. **Dispositioned by #2062's review (`docs/SystemNetHttpNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.9, cause NH-F): DEFERRED VERIFICATION, ticket #2070, evidence ABSENT.** Three candidate repairs — encode through `System::Text::Encoding`, reject a charset this port cannot encode, or document the label as advisory — differ in what they break, and choosing between them needs .NET's `StringContent` semantics. `/rv/tmp/runtime/` is absent (re-verified 2026-08-04). This port's `HttpContent` has no encoding parameter at all, only a charset **string**, and `System::Text`'s encoding factories are themselves the subject of blocked tickets #2013–#2017, so the repair is entangled with an unapproved family. Current behaviour **pinned** by `NetHttpGatedBehaviourPins.Pin2070_StringContentEmitsStorageBytesUnderAnyCharsetLabel`, mutation-checked by #2063 (encoding for `charset == "utf-16"` fails exactly that pin). The charset **label** itself did gain CR/LF/NUL rejection under #2063 (SR-AUD-313); the label-versus-bytes question is untouched. |
| [SR-AUD-318](modules/net-http/src/System/Net/Http/HttpClientHandler.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-318--medium--terminal-response-parsing-has-unbounded-buffering-weak-framing-checks-and-leaks-connected-sockets-on-exceptions) | medium | confirmed | `HttpClientHandler.cpp` | Header/body/chunk reads have no limits or strict complete-token framing; exceptions after connect bypass the only socket close. **SPLIT by compatibility (#2062's review, `docs/SystemNetHttpNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.7), following the SR-AUD-007 -> #1878/#1879 convention. Leak half REMEDIATED (#2065, 2026-08-04):** the audit filed the socket clause last; measured, it is the module's most consequential compatible defect. `connectToHost` returned a bare descriptor and `platformClose(fd)` was reached at exactly **one** point, after the whole body had been read, so every throw in between leaked it — and all four of those throws are chosen by the **remote peer**: a garbled status line, a malformed `Content-Length`, a malformed chunk size, and a body shorter than the declared length. Measured before the repair, 20 requests in each of those four modes leaked **20** descriptors and the success path leaked none, so a server answering ~1,024 requests with a garbled status line exhausts a default `RLIMIT_NOFILE`. A `SocketGuard` now owns the descriptor; the **original** close point is kept on the success path, so the only behaviour that changes is that a failing path closes too. After: **0** leaked in all five modes. +5 permanent regressions, mutation-checked — emptying the guard's destructor fails exactly the four failure-path tests with the pre-repair count of 19 while the success-path test stays green, which is what shows the mutation was targeted. ASan/UBSan/LSan clean with `HttpClientHandler.cpp` compiled **from source** into the probe, since the `Net.Http` static archive would have been uninstrumented; **LSan does not cover this** — it tracks memory, not descriptors, so the `/proc/self/fd` count is the instrument and no clean LSan run is substituted for it. No header, signature, layout or ABI change. **Limits half NOT remediated:** `recvAll` and a `Content-Length`-bounded `recvExact` still accumulate without bound and `std::stoul(chunkLine, nullptr, 16)` still accepts a chunk size up to `SIZE_MAX`; imposing a maximum response size is a **public-surface addition** (.NET spells it `HttpClient.MaxResponseContentBufferSize`, which this port does not have). Blocked ticket **#2071**; nothing about it is approved, and the unbounded behaviour is now **pinned** by `NetHttpGatedBehaviourPins.Pin2071_ResponseReadsAreUnbounded` (mutation-checked by #2063: capping `recvExact` at 64 KiB fails exactly that pin). SR-AUD-318 therefore stays `confirmed` for its open half. |
| [SR-AUD-319](modules/net-http-headers/src/System/Net/Http/Headers/NameValueHeaderValue.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-319--high--typed-header-values-accept-forbidden-control-characters) | high | remediated | `NameValueHeaderValue.cpp`, `EntityTagHeaderValue.cpp`, `WarningHeaderValue.cpp`, `ViaHeaderValue.cpp`, `ContentDispositionHeaderValue.cpp`, `HttpHeaders.cpp`, `HttpRequestHeaders.cpp`, `AuthenticationHeaderValue.cpp` | Typed quoted and host-like value paths accept CR/LF/NUL and serialize those controls; probes construct CR/LF header text and embedded-NUL values. **Remediated (#2124, 2026-08-08). CONFIRMED AND WIDER THAN BOTH THE FINDING AND THE REVIEW.** Measured (`build-probe/2124_probe1_doors.cpp`, 414 door/payload pairs; the review's own pre-repair reproduction is `build-probe/2122_probe1_before.log`): the field-terminator check lived in the **`else` branch** of `NameValueHeaderValue::checkValueFormat`, so it ran only for an **unquoted** value; a value beginning with `"` went to a quoting grammar that checks quoting and nothing else. So `NameValueHeaderValue("p", "\"a\r\nX-Injected: yes\"")` was accepted and `ToString()` emitted the terminator verbatim. **Three premise corrections.** (1) `ViaHeaderValue` did **not** already reject CR *and* LF: `isValidReceivedBy` rejects CR because CR is one of its list delimiters and rejects **neither LF nor NUL**, so `ViaHeaderValue("1.1", "safe\nX")` was accepted and serialized a raw LF — the review's reading came from a CRLF probe string that rejects on its CR and hides the LF, and `WarningHeaderValue`'s `isValidAgent` had the identical gap. (2) There are **five** types handing out a mutable parameter/extension vector, not two (`MediaTypeHeaderValue`, `TransferCodingHeaderValue`, `ContentDispositionHeaderValue`, `CacheControlHeaderValue`, `NameValueWithParametersHeaderValue`), plus a **tenth door no document named**: `RangeConditionHeaderValue`'s string constructor. (3) The module already contained **four independently written copies** of the terminator rule (`HttpHeaders`, `AuthenticationHeaderValue`, `HttpRequestHeaders`, `NameValueHeaderValue`) and they disagreed — cause **NH-H** applied to the terminator rule itself. **The repair routes all of them, and the newly closed doors, through `System::Net::detail::ContainsProtocolFieldTerminator` — still exactly one body in the repository** — which required the **new component edge `Net.Http.Headers` → `Net`, declared `PRIVATE_DEPENDENCIES` (41 modules / 91 → 92 edges, catalogue regenerated, no cycle).** Because every parameter *is* a `NameValueHeaderValue`, one check in its constructor/`setValueProperty` closes all five mutable vectors, `ContentDispositionHeaderValue`'s `Name`/`FileName`, and every parameter-carrying `Parse`. A quoted-pair whose escapee is a terminator is rejected too; HTAB, DEL, the other C0 controls and non-ASCII stay accepted, and the new rejection messages do **not** echo the offending text. Cause **NH-H**. **CCF-021 candidate member; CCF-021 is NOT minted** — see #2131. +14 tests (`SharpRuntimeTests_Net_Http_Headers` 380 → 394); mutations M1 (under-repair, 6 failures) / M2 (over-repair rejecting every C0 control and DEL, **exactly one** failure — the over-narrowing control) / M3 (semantics-preserving respelling, 0 failures) discriminate; ASan+UBSan+LSan clean over 167,926 operations (84,379 accepted / 83,547 rejected) with a live heap-use-after-free control. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-320](modules/net-http-headers/src/System/Net/Http/Headers/MediaTypeHeaderValue.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-320--medium--escaped-quoted-delimiters-are-split-as-structural-delimiters) | medium | remediated | Content-Disposition, media type, transfer coding, request/response list parsers | Ad-hoc quote toggling ignores backslash escapes, rejecting or corrupting valid parameters containing escaped quotes and delimiters. **Remediated (#2126, 2026-08-08). CONFIRMED, PREMISE SHARPENED TWICE.** The review already corrected "quote-blind" to "escape-blind", proven by an UNESCAPED `;` inside a quoted parameter being accepted while `text/plain; p="a\\";b"` was rejected. Implementation found the sharpening holds for **six** of the seven; the seventh, `NameValueWithParametersHeaderValue::TryParse`, split on a bare `input.find(';')` with **no quote tracking at all**, so even that control case was split there. All seven now call one body, `src/System/Net/Http/Headers/HeaderFieldSplitter.hpp` (implementation-only, beside the sources). The scanner deliberately does **not** validate: an unterminated quote or a trailing backslash still yields one final segment that the caller's grammar check rejects, so the widening cannot leak into acceptance of malformed quoting; and a backslash **outside** a quoted-string stays an ordinary character, because `quoted-pair` occurs only inside `quoted-string` and `comment`. **This is a WIDENING** -- text valid per RFC 9110 starts being accepted and nothing that parsed before stops parsing (pinned). Cause **NH-H**. **Not a CCF-021 member** (grammar, no field terminator). +7 tests (401 → 408); mutations: under-repair fails 2, over-repair fails exactly 1 -- after first passing everything and exposing the assertion as **vacuous**, because merging two segments into one still-invalid segment leaves `TryParse` returning `false` either way; re-pointed at `Accept`, whose `parseList` skips unparsable elements, the element COUNT discriminates -- and a semantics-preserving respelling fails 0. ASan+UBSan+LSan clean over 200,040 operations. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-321](modules/net-http-headers/src/System/Net/Http/Headers/HttpContentHeaders.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-321--medium--rfc-1123-parser-accepts-trailing-garbage) | medium | remediated | Content/request/response headers, Retry-After, If-Range, Warning, Content-Disposition | Repeated `sscanf` HTTP-date parsers accept a valid `GMT` prefix plus arbitrary tail, unlike the reference full-value parser. **Remediated (#2125, 2026-08-08). CONFIRMED EXACTLY AS FILED, in SEVEN copies rather than six.** The review named six; `WarningHeaderValue`'s date field is the seventh, extracting the date from inside a quoted string and handing it to its own byte-identical copy. All seven are now one body, `src/System/Net/Http/Headers/HttpDateParser.hpp` (implementation-only, beside the sources, so it is not public surface), which appends `%n` to the existing conversion string and requires the whole value to be consumed. **The grammar is deliberately unchanged**: a fixed-width rewrite would also reject text `sscanf` accepts and `/rv` is absent, so full consumption is all this ticket takes. Two additions the ticket did not name: an embedded NUL is rejected before `c_str()` can hide the rest of the value, and trailing *whitespace* stays accepted because it was accepted before. **The obsolete-format pin, measured:** neither the RFC 850 nor the ANSI C `asctime` form was **ever** accepted (the conversion string demands a comma after a three-letter day name), so #2125 narrowed nothing away; RFC 9110 §5.6.7 requires a recipient to accept all three and closing that gap is a widening owned by the deferred **#2130**. Cause **NH-H**. **Not a CCF-021 member** (a consumption defect, no field terminator). +7 tests (394 → 401); mutations: under-repair fails 2, over-repair fails exactly 1 — after the over-repair first exposed the assertion as **vacuous** because `RetryConditionHeaderValue::TryParse` pre-trims its input, so the control was re-pointed at an untrimmed collection door — and a semantics-preserving respelling fails 0. ASan+UBSan+LSan clean over 200,040 operations with a live heap-use-after-free control. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-322](modules/net-http-headers/src/System/Net/Http/Headers/HttpHeaders.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-322--high--tryaddwithoutvalidation-accepts-invalid-header-names) | high | remediated | `HttpHeaders.cpp`, `HttpHeaders.hpp` | The unvalidated-value API accepts CR/LF-bearing field names and serializes them, although .NET returns false for invalid names. **Remediated (#2123, 2026-08-08). CONFIRMED, AND HALF OF IT WAS ALREADY CORRECT.** Measured (`build-probe/2122_probe1_before.log`): `TryAddWithoutValidation("X-Bad\\r\\nInjected: yes", "v")` returned **`true`** and `ToString()` emitted `X-Bad\\r\\nInjected: yes: v\\r\\n\\r\\n` — **two header fields where the caller supplied one**, through a `bool`-returning API reporting success. A NUL-bearing name and a name containing a space were accepted too; only an empty name was rejected. **The correction that shaped the repair:** the validating sibling `HttpHeaders::Add` was **already correct** for CR, LF, NUL **and** space, in **both** name and value, and additionally rejects whitespace-padded names and obs-fold (`"a\\r\\n b"`). So the repair was **not** to write a validator but to route this door's **name** through the `isToken` predicate `Add` had used all along — the same "route the broken door through the sibling that was already right" shape as #2103, #2101, #2111 and #2114. **The value stays deliberately unvalidated**, which is the entire difference between the two doors and is pinned by its own test; mutation **Q2**, which "repaired" the value too, fails exactly that test and nothing else. The vector overload checks the name **once, before anything is stored**, so a rejected call cannot leave the collection partially populated. Cause **NH-I**. **CCF-021 candidate member; CCF-021 is NOT minted** — see #2131 and the cross-cutting appendix. +7 tests (`SharpRuntimeTests_Net_Http_Headers` 373 → 380); mutations Q1/Q2 discriminate 3/1 with controls stable; ASan+UBSan+LSan clean over 53,256 operations (5,969 accepted / 47,287 rejected) across every byte value in both name and value position, every truncation prefix of a rich header value, and 8,000 fuzzed pairs, with a live heap-use-after-free control. **No new component edge** — the predicate used is this module's own. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-323](modules/net-http-headers/src/System/Net/Http/Headers/ContentDispositionHeaderValue.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-323--medium--rfc-5987-decoder-ignores-the-declared-charset) | medium | remediated | `ContentDispositionHeaderValue.cpp`, `.hpp` | RFC 5987 decoding returns percent-decoded bytes without interpreting or validating the charset; ISO-8859-1 output is not converted to UTF-8. **Remediated (#2127, 2026-08-08). CONFIRMED, AND THERE ARE THREE DEFECTS IN THIS DECODER, NOT TWO.** The charset label was parsed only far enough to locate the delimiters and then discarded, so `filename*=iso-8859-1''foo-%E4.html` produced **ten** bytes with a raw `0xE4` in a `std::string` the rest of this runtime reads as UTF-8, and `filename*=bogus''x` was accepted. `UTF-8` and `ISO-8859-1` are now honoured case-insensitively per RFC 5987 §3.2.1, an ISO-8859-1 octet is transcoded (11 bytes, `C3 A4`), and anything else **including an empty label** is rejected. **The §10 split was not needed**: rejection is what the RFC requires of a recipient and is the failure mode this decoder already used for every other malformed input, so it is recorded as **this port's choice** in the property's doc-comment rather than as a claim about .NET. **A third defect neither the finding nor the review names:** the escape bound was `i + 2 < size`, so a TRUNCATED escape at the end (`a%`, `a%C`) fell through to the literal branch and was kept as text -- silently turning malformed input into a plausible-looking file name, at a door whose whole job is producing file names -- and is now rejected for the same reason a non-hex escape already was. The companion post-audit defect #2129 (a decoded raw CR/LF handed to the caller, cause NH-K) landed in the same function and is **deliberately NOT a CCF-021 member**: its terminator travels inward and never appears in serialized text; the scope stays exactly the three framing characters, with a decoded TAB and ESC still returned (pinned). Cause **NH-J**. +8 tests (408 → 416); four mutations discriminate 2/1/1/1, the last of them failing the SCOPE control rather than the defect test. ASan+UBSan+LSan clean over 200,040 operations. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |

| [SR-AUD-327](modules/text-json/include/System/Text/Json/Nodes/JsonNode.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-327--high--jsonnode-children-retain-dangling-raw-parent-pointers-after-parent-destruction) | high | confirmed (design-complete) | `JsonNode.hpp`, `JsonArray.hpp`, `JsonObject.hpp`, `JsonValue.hpp`, `JsonNodeOptions.hpp`, `JsonNode.cpp`, `JsonNodeTests.cpp` | Shared child ownership can outlive the parent held only as a raw pointer; retained-child `getRootProperty` produces an ASan-confirmed heap-use-after-free. **Ticket #1885 (2026-07-30):** design-only, still open and still counted as `confirmed`. Measured surface is **41 public entries across 5 files**, not two accessors: 8 ASan `heap-use-after-free` accesses plus 2 `stack-overflow`s, and 6 further cases that give a wrong answer with **no diagnostic in any build** — including implicitly generated copy operations that alias children and a slicing copy-assign that rewrites `parent_` on a stored node. Selected repair and the exact approval request: `docs/OwnedTreeLifetimeContractPlan.md`. **Ticket #1886 (2026-07-31) — the approved core repair, PARTIAL closure:** `JsonArray` and `JsonObject` now clear the parent link of every child they still own in their own destructor, so a retained child of a destroyed owner is left in exactly the state `RemoveAt`/`Remove`/`Clear` already produce. Re-running the #1885 probe unchanged, **7 of this finding's 8 ASan `heap-use-after-free` cases stop producing one** (J01, J02, J03, J04, J08, J16, J17) and the recoverable-ASan faulting-access count for the JsonNode section falls **9 → 1**; J06's permanent `The node already has a parent.` orphan is also closed. Zero bytes, zero vtable slots, zero allocations added (measured: `build-probe/1886_alloc_*.log`, `1886_layout_*.public.txt`, identical 219 external symbols). +32 permanent tests, mutation-checked two ways. **Remaining, each approval-blocked:** J11 (stale iterator, still an ASan-confirmed use-after-free) → #1889; J08's aliasing copy, J09's slicing copy-assign and J13's public `DetachParent()` → #1888; J10's `SetItem` exception path → #1887; J12's silent stale iterator → #1889; J19c/J19d/X28c deep-nesting crashes → #1893. Stays `confirmed (design-complete)` until those land. **Ticket #1895 (2026-07-31):** J19c's recursive teardown closed. **Ticket #1897 (2026-07-31), approved option B:** J19c's sibling **X28c is closed** — `JsonNode::Parse` builds its tree with an explicit heap worklist instead of recursing, so the finding's last ASan `stack-overflow`, and the only case in the whole of CCF-019 reachable from **untrusted input**, is gone. Fully compatible: identical accepted input, emitted text, public signatures, strong symbols, layout, vtable and exception specification (`docs/OwnedTreeLifetimeContractPlan.md` §44). Option A (a depth bound matching .NET and `JsonDocument::Parse`) was **not** approved and is **not** implemented; that deviation is documented and pinned by two tests. Still `confirmed (design-complete)`: J11/J12 → #1889 (declined), J08/J09/J13 → #1888 (declined), J19d → #1896 (declined). |
| [SR-AUD-328](modules/text-json/include/System/Text/Json/Nodes/JsonValue.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-328--medium--jsonvalue-integer-accessors-silently-truncate-floating-json-numbers) | medium | remediated | `JsonValue.hpp`, `JsonNodeTests.cpp` | `JsonValue::GetInt32/GetInt64` cast floating numbers instead of rejecting nonintegral values; 1.5 returns 1. **Remediated (#2114, 2026-08-08). PREMISE LARGELY REFUTED, AND THE SURVIVING HALF IS WORSE THAN FILED.** The summary implies the whole integer-accessor family truncates. Measured (`build-probe/2114_probe1_before.log`), **`JsonElement` was already correct at every boundary** -- `1.5`, `1.0`, `2e1`, `1e100`, `2147483648` and `99999999999999999999` all threw. Exactly two doors survived: `JsonValue::GetInt32`/`GetInt64`, and **`JsonSerializer::Deserialize<T>`, which the finding does not name** and which is reachable straight from untrusted document text. **AND IT IS NOT ONLY TRUNCATION: IT IS UNDEFINED BEHAVIOUR.** Both surviving doors reached `nlohmann`'s `get<T>()`, which for a `number_float` is a bare `static_cast<int>(double)`. Built with `-fsanitize=float-cast-overflow` the probe produced **three** reports of *"1e+20 is outside the range of representable values"* at `vendor/nlohmann/json.hpp:4279` (`build-probe/2114_probe1_fcorec.log`); after the repair the same probe reports **zero**. **The repair target already existed in the module**: `JsonElement`'s body carried the correct rule *and* a comment naming precisely this UB. #2114 moved that rule into `detail::TryConvertJsonNumberToIntegral` so all three doors share **one** predicate rather than the module gaining a second and third copy -- a duplicated rule drifting apart being cause TJ-E, which #2113 had just repaired one file away. `JsonElement`'s observable behaviour is unchanged; only the rule's home moved, and mutation N3 proves the sharing is real by breaking all three doors at once, including the pre-existing `JsonElement` pin. **Exception types deliberately differ and are pinned**: `JsonValue` raises `InvalidOperationException` (its own doc-comment transcribes `JsonValueOfElement.cs`'s `ThrowInvalidOperationException_NodeUnableToConvertElement`), `JsonElement` keeps `FormatException`, `Deserialize<T>` keeps `JsonException`. That **corrects the ticket's acceptance criterion**, which asked for "the same exception `JsonElement` already raises". **Measured residual, pinned not hidden:** `Deserialize<T>`'s guard is top-level only, so `Deserialize<std::vector<int>>("[1.5]")` still truncates -- an aggregate is deserialized by `nlohmann`'s ADL customization points, the design this port uses in place of reflection, with no hook between them. +7 tests; mutations N1/N2/N3 discriminate 4/1/6 with controls stable; ASan+UBSan+LSan+float-cast-overflow clean over 84,168 conversions (21,426 converted / 62,742 rejected) with a live heap-use-after-free control. One new internal header; no public signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-329](modules/text-json/include/System/Text/Json/JsonEncodedText.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-329--medium--jsonencodedtext-accepts-malformed-utf-8-bytes-unchanged) | medium | remediated | `JsonEncodedText.hpp`, `JsonTests.cpp` | The narrow Encode overload copies malformed UTF-8 unchanged, contradicting its validated JSON-text contract and permitting invalid bytes in the encoded representation. **Remediated (#2113, 2026-08-08). PREMISE BOTH WIDER AND NARROWER THAN FILED.** The finding names the narrow overload's leniency for `C3 28`. Measured (`build-probe/2113_probe1_encodedtext.cpp`), `Encode(const std::string&)` had **no validation at all** and accepted every malformed class -- `C3 28`, the UTF-8-encoded lone surrogates `ED A0 80`/`ED B0 80`, the overlong `C0 AF`, the out-of-range `F5 80 80 80`, a stray continuation byte, a truncated lead, raw control characters and an embedded NUL -- while the `std::u16string` overload rejected malformed UTF-16. That is cause **TJ-E**, sibling overloads disagreeing, the same shape as SR-AUD-345 (#2103). **NARROWER in one respect:** the two overloads can only genuinely disagree on the lone-surrogate class, because that is the only malformed class UTF-16 can also express; the rest can arrive through the narrow door only. **The decisive evidence is the module contradicting ITSELF** -- `Encode` certified as "validated JSON text" five byte classes this module's own `JsonDocument::Parse` rejects. The narrow overload is now the single validator (the UTF-16 overload delegates to it, so the two cannot drift apart again) and reuses the module's existing dependency `System::Text::Unicode::Utf8::IndexOfInvalidSubsequence` rather than a second copy of the rule. **The control-character boundary is MEASURED, not assumed:** a granularity matrix over `0x00`-`0x20` and `0x7F` proved `0x00`-`0x08`, `0x0B`, `0x0C` and `0x0E`-`0x1F` are rejected by this module's parser **both** inside a string and in whitespace position, while `0x09`/`0x0A`/`0x0D` are legal whitespace and `0x7F` is legal inside a string -- so those four stay accepted, pinned by a control test that fails if the boundary is ever widened past its evidence. `Utf8JsonWriter` is deliberately untouched and pinned: it **replaces** ill-formed sequences with U+FFFD and **escapes** control characters, so it is a different kind of door, not a second site. Known remaining deviation, out of scope as a public source break: `Value` is a public field, so assigning it bypasses `Encode`. +9 tests; mutations M1/M2/M3 discriminate 4/2/1 with controls stable; ASan+UBSan+LSan clean over 61,357 inputs (16,543 accepted / 44,814 rejected) with a live heap-use-after-free control. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-330](modules/text-json/include/System/Text/Json/JsonSerializer.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-330--medium--jsonserializer-deserialize-ignores-its-jsonserializeroptions-argument) | medium | remediated | `JsonSerializer.hpp`, `JsonSerializerOptions.hpp`, `JsonSerializationTests.cpp` | Both Deserialize overloads discard options, so configured parse controls such as trailing commas, comments, depth, and duplicate policy have no effect. **Remediated (#2116, 2026-08-08), and the repair is wider than "forward the argument".** Confirmed exactly as filed: both `Deserialize` overloads named their options parameter as an unused comment and discarded it, so `MaxDepth` and `ReadCommentHandling` — **both of which demonstrably work at `JsonDocument::Parse`** — were unreachable through the serializer. **The structural fact behind it:** `JsonDocument::Parse` and `JsonSerializer::Deserialize` were **two independent implementations of "parse a document under document options"**, and they had drifted in two directions at once — the serializer discarded its options *and* (found by #2114's probe, filed as **#2121**) never reached `detail::RejectEmbeddedNul`, so an embedded NUL still silently truncated a document through the template overload after #2112 had closed the other four doors. Both now call one `detail::ParseDocumentText`, which validates the options, runs the NUL guard, parses and enforces the depth; mutation P3 proves the sharing is real by breaking both doors with one deleted line. **All four parse-time options are forwarded, including the two that are currently inert** — forwarding only the two that work would have re-created the divergence, and forwarding all four means whatever **#2115** decides takes effect at both entry points with no further change. **Two documented narrowings, both parity-improving and both pinned:** the serializer's doors now enforce `MaxDepth` (default 64, as .NET's `JsonSerializerOptions.MaxDepth` default of 0 also means) and now reject an embedded NUL. `nlohmann`'s own exception is deliberately allowed to escape the core so each caller keeps its existing message wording — the same discipline #2111 used. **`JsonNode::Parse` is deliberately NOT routed through the core**: #1897 made it iterative and applies **no depth bound**, a `CLAUDE.md`-pinned deviation reopenable only as the still-unapproved option A, and a test pins that exception so a future consolidation cannot silently swallow it. `Utf8JsonWriter::WriteRawValue` stays separate for the same kind of reason — it validates a fragment, not a document under document options. +8 tests; mutations P1/P2/P3 discriminate 3/4/4, and a fourth mutant was **rejected as invalid because it failed to build** rather than being reported; ASan+UBSan+LSan clean over 30,840 parses (805 accepted / 30,035 rejected) with a live heap-use-after-free control. One new internal header; no public signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
