| [SR-AUD-333](modules/xml-linq/include/System/Xml/Linq/XObject.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-333--high--retained-xmllinq-children-dereference-a-destroyed-raw-parent) | high | confirmed (design-complete) | `XObject.hpp`, `XObject.cpp`, `XNode.hpp`, `XNode.cpp`, `XContainer.hpp`, `XContainer.cpp`, `XElement.hpp`, `XElement.cpp`, `XAttribute.hpp`, `XAttribute.cpp`, `XDocument.cpp`, `Extensions.hpp`, `XLinqNodeTests.cpp` | A public shared_ptr child outlives its shared_ptr-owned parent while retaining a raw parent pointer; ASan confirms heap-use-after-free at getParentProperty. **Ticket #1885 (2026-07-30):** design-only, still open and still counted as `confirmed`. Measured surface is **35 public entries across 13 files**, and the severity is understated: `XObject::getParentProperty` dispatches a **virtual call** through the freed parent, so **eight** public entry points abort the process (`pure virtual method called`) with no sanitizer present. 21 ASan `heap-use-after-free` accesses plus 1 `stack-overflow`; `XAttribute::next_` is a **second** unguarded borrowed link; `ReplaceWith`'s exception path **loses** the node it was replacing. Selected repair and the exact approval request: `docs/OwnedTreeLifetimeContractPlan.md`. **Ticket #1890 (2026-07-31) — the approved core repair, PARTIAL closure:** `XContainer` now clears the parent link of every child node it still owns in its own destructor, and `XElement` clears every owned attribute's parent link **and** its intrusive `next_` sibling link. Re-running the #1885 probe unchanged, **19 of this finding's 21 ASan `heap-use-after-free` cases stop producing one** (X01–X14, X18, X19, X22, X25, X26), the XObject faulting-access count falls **48 → 4**, and **all eight** public entry points that aborted the process with `pure virtual method called` stop doing so — every one of them now returns the value a *detached* object already returned, or throws `The parent is missing.` X13's dangling `next_` and X14's write through a stale parent are closed with them. Zero bytes, zero vtable slots, zero allocations added (measured: `build-probe/1886_alloc_*.log`, `1886_layout_*.public.txt`; three weak COMDAT `XContainerD0/D1/D2Ev` definitions are newly emitted, no name removed). +35 permanent tests, mutation-checked three ways. **Remaining, each approval-blocked:** X15 (`Extensions::Ancestors`' raw `XElement*`) and X17 (`getAttributesProperty()`'s reference), both still ASan-confirmed use-after-free and neither reachable through `parent_` → #1892; X20's `ReplaceWith` exception-path data loss → #1891; X27c/X27d deep-nesting crashes → #1893; X21's documented move-on-`Add` is deliberately unchanged. Stays `confirmed (design-complete)` until those land. |
| [SR-AUD-334](modules/xml-linq/include/System/Xml/Linq/XElement.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-334--medium--namespace-uri-semantics-are-discarded-by-xmllinq-parse-and-serialization) | medium | remediated | `XName.hpp`, `XNamespace.hpp`, `XElement.hpp/.cpp`, `XAttribute.cpp`, `XDocument.cpp`, `XStreamingElement.cpp`, `NamespaceScope.hpp`, `XLinqNamespaceTests.cpp` | Parsed prefix text never resolves to an XName URI and writer paths emit only local names; a namespaced programmatic tree serializes/reparses as unqualified XML. **Ticket #2197 (2026-08-10) — remediated.** Five premise corrections. **It emitted MALFORMED XML, not only namespace-lossy XML**: two attributes differing only by namespace serialized as `<r x="1" x="2"/>`, which this module's own parser rejects with `XML_ERROR_PARSING_ATTRIBUTE`. **It destroyed declarations built the .NET way**: `XAttribute(XNamespace::Xmlns + "p", "urn:x")` serialized as `<e p="urn:x"/>`, the declaration becoming an ordinary attribute and silently unbinding the prefix for the whole subtree. **`getIsNamespaceDeclarationProperty()` was wrong for parsed input, inconsistently** — 0 for `xmlns:p`, 1 for `xmlns` (the latter right only by accident). **Six files, not five** — `XStreamingElement::WriteTo` shares the defect. **The DOM layer already resolved namespaces correctly**, including the two rules easiest to get wrong (an unprefixed attribute takes no namespace; the `xml` prefix is built in), and the converter simply never called it — family **X-C**, the same family as SR-AUD-335 in the same module. Repair: parse builds `XName` from the DOM's resolved parts; both serialization doors carry a namespace scope rebuilt from the tree, reusing a declared prefix and generating `p1`, `p2`, … with a declaration where none is; `GetDefaultNamespace()`/`GetPrefixOfNamespace()` added (additive). **Nothing is cached in any object** — deliberate, because caching would need the `XObject` field #1896's approval was refused. +48 permanent regressions; five mutations, of which **one did not discriminate and a test was added for it rather than the gap being hidden**; clean ASan/UBSan/LSan with a firing control. Behaviour-incompatible for namespaced trees and documented in `docs/Migration-XmlLinqNamespaces.md`; unqualified trees are unchanged byte for byte. Deliberately unchanged: an **undeclared** prefix is still accepted and unresolved (#2083 owns that question) and `SaveOptions::OmitDuplicateNamespaces` stays inert. See `docs/SystemXmlLinqNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.2 and §20.2. |
| [SR-AUD-335](modules/xml-linq/include/System/Xml/Linq/XCData.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-335--medium--direct-serializers-emit-or-corrupt-xml-special-lexical-content) | medium | remediated | `XCData`, `XComment`, `XProcessingInstruction` headers/sources, `XmlWriter.cpp`, `XmlLexicalSanitizer.hpp`, `XLinqLexicalSerializationTests.cpp` | CDATA ]]>, comment --, and PI ?> lexical delimiters are emitted without splitting, escaping, or diagnostics; a CDATA round trip loses data. **Ticket #2196 (2026-08-10) — remediated.** Three premise corrections. **The surface is five doors, not three node kinds**: the PI **target** is a fifth and it failed differently — the direct door emitted `<?a?>b d?>` where the sibling `WriteTo()` door already **threw**. **The writer doors were already correct**, so the defect was confined to `SerializeTo` (`ToString()`, `ToString(SaveOptions)`, `Save(fileName)`, and every containing element/document that recurses into them) — family **X-C**, a public door bypassing a validator the module already ships, exactly as `docs/SystemXmlNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §17 predicted for this module. **The consequences were three different shapes**: CDATA lossy (`left]]>right` → value `leftright]]>`), the PI **silently dropped data** (measured: the emitted text re-read with `data == "left"`, `right` gone), the comment silent (this runtime's parser accepts the invalid comment). Repair moves the three self-healing transforms to `System/Xml/detail/XmlLexicalSanitizer.hpp` so the writer door and the direct door **share one definition** and cannot drift; the PI target now routes through the `XmlConvert::VerifyName` the writer already used. `XmlWriter`'s behaviour is unchanged character for character (483/483 `SharpRuntimeTests_Xml` pass unmodified). +28 permanent regressions, four discriminating mutations, clean ASan/UBSan/LSan with a firing control, and no public signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. Deliberately out of scope and carrying their own tickets: **#2200** (`XDocumentType` quoted literals, twin of #2084), **#2201** (embedded NUL, twin of #2085) and **#2202** (the parser accepts a PI only before every other node — measured independent of this finding). See `docs/SystemXmlLinqNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.1 and §20. |
| [SR-AUD-336](modules/xml-linq/include/System/Xml/Linq/XObject.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-336--medium--changed-and-changing-accept-handlers-but-never-notify-mutations) | medium | confirmed (design-complete) | `XObject.hpp`, `XObjectChange*`, mutation APIs, `XLinqChangeNotificationTests.cpp` | Event registration discards handlers; direct mutation leaves a registered Changed callback uninvoked and tests only assert no throw. **Ticket #2198 (2026-08-10) — premise confirmed verbatim and re-measured across NINE doors (including an ancestor observing a descendant, all zero); the CLASSIFICATION corrected — this is NOT compatible-actionable.** Two structural blockers: (1) there is nowhere to store a handler — `sizeof(XObject)` is 16, a vptr plus `parent_` with **no padding**, so a handler field takes it to 24 and grows every derived node type with it, and **that exact growth was declined by the user on 2026-07-31** for #1896; (2) `XObjectChangeEventHandler` is a bare `std::function` alias and `std::function` is **not equality-comparable**, so `remove_Changed` cannot name a registration **at any layout cost** — proved by `static_assert`, not asserted. A process-wide side table avoids (1) but not (2), and #1896 already judged that shape worse. #2198 delivered the compatible half: **+23 permanent regressions** that make the inert contract discriminating at every door, mutation-checked against a deliberate HALF-implementation (which now fails, where the previous single 'does not throw' test would have passed it), plus `static_assert`s and a layout pin that fail if either blocker stops holding. Implementation is **#2199, blocked** on approvals XL-1 and XL-2, stated verbatim in `docs/SystemXmlLinqNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §12.3. **Nothing was implemented and no behaviour changed**; the finding stays open. |
| [SR-AUD-337](modules/io/src/System/IO/StreamReader.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-337--medium--streamreaderstreamwriter-close-with-leaveopen-keeps-the-wrapper-usable) | medium | remediated | `StreamReader.cpp`, `StreamWriter.cpp`, text wrapper tests | Closing a leaveOpen text wrapper does not dispose the wrapper, so later reads/writes proceed instead of throwing while the base stream remains open. **OWNER: #2098, BLOCKED on Approval IO-1** (`docs/SystemIONamespaceReviewPlan.md` §21). Confirmed as filed (§4.1): `StreamReader(&ms, leaveOpen: true)` returns `97` from `Read()` after `Close()`, and `StreamWriter` grows the underlying stream by 11 bytes. Recording the closed state needs storage, and every option is an **object-layout change in a public type**; the measured decision, its three costed options and the exact approval sentence are §21.2–§21.8. The one member of the original ticket needing no new storage, `UnmanagedMemoryStream`, was split out and landed as **#2108** (SR-AUD-344 → `remediated`). **#2104 corrected the doc-comments**: `StreamReader::Close()` said "Closes the underlying stream" and `StreamWriter::Close()` "Closes the writer and the underlying stream", both of which describe the repair Approval IO-1 has not authorised; they now state what the code does and name the gate. Layouts pinned by `IoLayoutPinTests`. **REMEDIATED (#2098, 2026-08-17):** Approval IO-1 was granted as `docs/StandingApprovals.md` SA-3 and the repair landed as option (a) — one private `bool` per leaf, `sizeof(StreamReader)` and `sizeof(StreamWriter)` **unchanged** at 24 because the flag lands in existing tail padding. **HALF OF THIS FINDING DOES NOT REPRODUCE AGAINST THE REFERENCE**, which is available again (`docs/StandingApprovals.md` §5.1): .NET's two stream wrappers deliberately disagree. `StreamReader.Dispose` sets `_disposed` **unconditionally**, outside its `if (_closable)` (`StreamReader.cs:243-268`), so a `leaveOpen` reader **is** disposed — the reader half is a real divergence and is repaired. `StreamWriter` assigns `_disposed` only inside `CloseStreamFromDispose`, under `if (_closable && !_disposed)` (`StreamWriter.cs:221-244`) with `_closable = !leaveOpen`, so a `leaveOpen` writer is **never** disposed and its post-`Close()` growth — this finding's own writer evidence — is .NET's own behaviour. The asymmetry is reproduced and pinned rather than tidied away; what the writer half actually repairs is the non-`leaveOpen` case, where a write after `Close()` reached a closed stream instead of throwing. Exception identity is transcribed: `ObjectDisposedException("StreamReader"/"StreamWriter", SR.ObjectDisposed_ReaderClosed/WriterClosed)`. `getBaseStreamProperty()` deliberately keeps working (.NET's `BaseStream` is a bare field read), as does `Write(const char*)` with a null pointer. A separate defect found while implementing is fixed here: both destructors **re-closed** a stream an explicit `Close()` had already closed, which .NET does not. +14 tests; no vtable, mangled-symbol, signature or `noexcept` change. Downstream measured under SA-2: `cna` zero sites, `mobile-eggbert` one site (`Worlds.cpp:154`) that never calls `Close()` and is unaffected. `docs/SystemIONamespaceReviewPlan.md` §21.10, `docs/Migration-IOLifecycleAndArgumentStrictness.md` §7. |
| [SR-AUD-338](modules/io/src/System/IO/StreamWriter.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-338--high--text-stream-wrappers-accept-a-null-base-stream-and-reach-silent-eof-or-a-null-dereference) | high | remediated | `StreamReader.cpp`, `StreamWriter.cpp` | StreamReader accepted a null stream as EOF and StreamWriter dereferenced it; ASan/UBSan confirmed **five** distinct writer dereferences, not the one the finding named — `Write(string)`, `Write(const char*)`, `Flush()`, `Close()` and `~StreamWriter()`, the last fatal on the default `leaveOpen=false` with no call on the object at all. Ticket #1806 throws `ArgumentNullException("stream")` from both constructors, matching .NET and the sibling BinaryReader/BinaryWriter, and removes the reader's now-unreachable null guards, whose `-1`/`""` results had made a missing stream indistinguishable from an empty document. SR-AUD-337, which shares these files, is untouched. |
| [SR-AUD-339](modules/io/src/System/IO/FileSystemWatcher.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-339--medium--changing-path-while-a-filesystemwatcher-is-enabled-retains-the-old-inotify-watch-and-reports-the-new-path) | medium | remediated | `FileSystemWatcher.cpp` | Changing Path did not recreate the inotify watch; events from the former directory were delivered with a FullPath under the new one. **Remediated (#2344, split from #2102 by measured cause).** Measured before-state (`build-probe/2102_probe1_before.log`): after `setPathProperty(B)` on an enabled watcher a file created in the OLD directory A was reported as `B/sentinelOnA.txt` — **a path naming no file that exists** — while a file created in B raised nothing. **PREMISE EXTENDED, and the finding does not say it: this is also a DATA RACE.** `watchLoop()` reads `directory_` on the watcher thread (`FileSystemWatcher.cpp:242`) while `setPathProperty` writes that `std::string` on the caller thread (`:59`); ThreadSanitizer reported **3 data races (exit 66)** against the pre-repair tree, so the fabricated `FullPath` was the benign-looking face of undefined behaviour. Repair: validation stays **first** (a rejected path still leaves the old watch live and the object unchanged), then the watcher thread is **joined** before `directory_` is written, then the new directory is armed — which removes the race **by construction** rather than by adding a lock, because re-arming already requires retiring the old watch. The re-arm is gated on `enabled_`, not on a thread already running, so the *EnableRaisingEvents-before-Path* ordering the `.cpp` comment deliberately tolerates now actually arms instead of staying permanently inert. A failed re-arm keeps `startWatchingIfPossible()`'s existing contract (`EnableRaisingEvents` false + `Error` raised) and the old watch is **not** retained as a fallback. TSan **3 → 0** over 400 path flips and 1,749 concurrently delivered events; +8 tests, all deterministic with **no sleep used as synchronisation**; 5 mutations, 4 caught by the suite (4/3/1/1 tests) and the fifth — writing `directory_` *before* the teardown, which is functionally identical — caught **only by TSan** (3 races, exit 66), which is why TSan was run here and is recorded as discriminating rather than ceremonial. Descriptor delta **0** over 50 re-arms. No public signature, member, layout, vtable or exception-specification change; header touched for doc-comment only. **Deliberately NOT closed here: SR-AUD-346**, whose remainder is the `NotifyFilters` → inotify mapping policy (#2345 narrow half, #2346 `needs_user`). |
| [SR-AUD-340](modules/io/src/System/IO/RandomAccess.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-340--medium--randomaccess-raw-overloads-silently-accept-invalid-metadata-and-posix-getlength-suppresses-failures) | medium | remediated | `RandomAccess.cpp` | Negative Write counts silently succeed and POSIX GetLength returns -1 for invalid descriptors rather than a System I/O failure. |
| [SR-AUD-341](modules/io/src/System/IO/MemoryStream.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-341--high--memorystream-raw-buffer-constructor-dereferences-null-input-before-it-can-report-an-argument-error) | high | remediated | `MemoryStream.cpp` | Raw MemoryStream construction copied a null nonzero source before validation; ASan confirmed the null read and UBSan the null load. Ticket #1805 initializes `data_` from a validating helper instead of the raw range, so `ArgumentNullException("buffer")` and `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("size", …)` precede any pointer arithmetic; the negative-size `std::length_error` leak found alongside it is closed by the same change, and a null pointer paired with a **zero** size stays valid, per ticket #1774's rule for the same pointer/length shape. |
| [SR-AUD-342](modules/io/src/System/IO/FileStream.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-342--medium--filestream-does-not-enforce-requested-access-or-disposed-state-consistently) | medium | remediated | `FileStream.cpp` | Operations do not consistently check FileAccess/liveness; OpenOrCreate+Read can write a new file, and closed metadata remains usable. **Three claims, three fates (#2099, 2026-08-12, cause I-A, `docs/SystemIONamespaceReviewPlan.md` §6.2/§20.6).** The *write* claim was already repaired by #1825 and is **not reproducible** — `Write` on a `FileAccess::Read` stream throws `NotSupportedException`. The *OpenOrCreate creates a file* claim is **closed by exclusion, not by repair**: whether .NET creates there is unverifiable with `/rv` absent and no repository evidence settles it, so it is recorded as an open question rather than guessed. The *closed metadata* claim is repaired here, and it was **six** members, not the three §6.2 predicted: `getLengthProperty()` returned `5` by re-`stat`ing `path_`, `getPositionProperty()` returned the sentinel **`-1`**, and `setPositionProperty`, `Seek(Begin)`, `Seek(End)` and `Flush` all succeeded outright. **Premise correction:** §6.2's "`Seek()` succeeds outright" is wrong for `SeekOrigin::Current`, which *threw* `IOException("...before the beginning of the stream.")` — the `-1` sentinel does not stay local, `Stream::Seek` adds it to the offset — so a closed stream complained **about the seek target**; a bare `EXPECT_THROW` would have passed before the fix. **Second premise correction:** `setPositionProperty(-1)` and `SetLength(-1)` checked the *argument* before the closed state, the same reversal #2108 found in `UnmanagedMemoryStream`. One private `EnsureNotClosed()` now funnels every dependent member, including the four already correct. **Layout-neutral and therefore NOT gated on #2098's Approval IO-1** — `file_.is_open()` already *is* the closed flag; `sizeof` 576 and `alignof` 8 unchanged, the same split #2108 made. One residue is pinned rather than removed: `FileStream` does not override `Seek`, so a closed `Seek(-1, Begin)` still reports `IOException` — measured **identical** in `UnmanagedMemoryStream`, so overriding it here alone would create a divergence. +9 tests (635 → 644), 5 mutations all caught, ASan/UBSan/LSan clean, descriptor delta 0 over 100 cycles. |
| [SR-AUD-343](modules/io/include/System/IO/StringReader.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-343--medium--stringreader-and-stringwriter-inherit-no-op-close-and-remain-usable-after-disposal) | medium | remediated | `StringReader.hpp`, `StringWriter.hpp`, `TextReader.hpp`, `TextWriter.hpp` | In-memory text wrappers inherit no-op Close and continue to read/write after apparent disposal. **OWNER: #2098, BLOCKED on Approval IO-1** — same storage decision as SR-AUD-337. Confirmed (§4.1): after `Close()`, `StringReader("hello")` gives `Peek()`=104, `Read()`=104, `ReadToEnd()`=`"ello"` — *entirely* unaffected — and `StringWriter` still writes and still returns its text. **PREMISE CORRECTED by #2104:** plan §7.1 states that `TextReader::Close()` is `{}` **and that `TextWriter` has no `Close()` at all**. The second half is wrong — `TextWriter.hpp` has carried `virtual void Close() {}` since this history's first commit (`9b657c85`, verified with `git log -S`), so the two bases are the *same* shape and #2098's base-class option is a symmetric change to both. The conclusion is untouched: a `{}` `Close()` is no disposal contract, which is why this finding exists. Both base doc-comments claimed to close and release resources and now say what they do. **REMEDIATED (#2098, 2026-08-17):** both types now override `Close()` and record the state in a private `bool`. `sizeof(StringReader)` is **unchanged** at 48; **`sizeof(StringWriter)` grows 384 → 392**, the one layout cost Approval IO-1 quoted, authorised by `docs/StandingApprovals.md` SA-3 and now pinned by `IoLayoutPinTests` in its *after* state. Both bases stay at 8 — the base-class option was **not** taken. Exception identity is transcribed from the reference, which is available again: both pass a **null** object name (`StringReader.cs:325-328`, `StringWriter.cs:73-75`), unlike the stream pair of SR-AUD-337, so the message stands alone with no `Object name:` suffix. **`StringWriter::ToString()` and `GetStringBuilder()` deliberately keep working after `Close()`** — `StringWriter.cs:309-312` and `:64-67` have no `_isOpen` check, so guarding them would be a divergence rather than a stricter repair. `docs/SystemIONamespaceReviewPlan.md` §21.10. |
| [SR-AUD-344](modules/io/src/System/IO/UnmanagedMemoryStream.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-344--medium--unmanagedmemorystream-exposes-length-and-mutable-position-after-close) | medium | remediated | `UnmanagedMemoryStream.cpp` | UnmanagedMemoryStream closes data operations but still exposes Length and accepts Position mutation afterward. |
| [SR-AUD-345](modules/io/include/System/IO/FileInfo.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-345--medium--fileinfodelete-can-remove-an-empty-directory) | medium | remediated | `FileInfo.hpp` | FileInfo over an empty directory delegates to filesystem remove and deletes the directory, unlike File.Delete/.NET file semantics. **Remediated (#2103, 2026-08-04). PREMISE SHARPENED:** the finding names only `FileInfo`, but the measured defect is that **two APIs .NET documents as equivalent gave opposite answers for the same input** -- `FileInfo(emptyDir).Delete()` DELETED the directory while `File::Delete(emptyDir)` THREW `"Is a directory."` `std::filesystem::remove` is documented to call the equivalent of `rmdir` on a directory, which is why the two diverged. `File::Delete` **already had the guard**, complete with a comment recording that real .NET calls `unlink()`, which can never remove a directory -- so **the repair target already existed in the module**. `FileInfo::Delete` now delegates to `File::Delete` rather than carrying a second copy of the rule: one guard, one message, and the two siblings cannot drift apart again. **The only finding in `modules/io` that destroys user data.** Cause I-F. Tests assert the directory and its contents SURVIVE, not merely that a throw happened; mutation O2 discriminates exactly 2. One test is honestly recorded as NON-discriminating (`std::filesystem::remove` fails with `ENOTEMPTY` on a non-empty directory anyway). No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-346](modules/io/src/System/IO/FileSystemWatcher.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-346--medium--filesystemwatcher-stores-notifyfilter-but-never-applies-it-to-inotify-registration-or-dispatch) | medium | remediated | `FileSystemWatcher.cpp` | NotifyFilter is stored but ignored; a Size-only configuration still raises `Created`. **STILL OPEN, and deliberately so — SPLIT by evidence, not by symptom (#2345 landed, #2346 `needs_user`).** **PREMISE CORRECTED:** the ticket and the handoff both expected "Size-only raises Created" to be an *independently repairable* defect with its own mechanism (an over-broad mask, a misclassified event, a switch fallthrough). It has **none**. Measured over all ten filter configurations × seven operations (`build-probe/2102_probe2_before.log`), **every configuration produced the byte-identical seven events, including `NotifyFilters(0)`** — one cause (a `constexpr` mask and a property no code reads), and the headline symptom is one cell of a table in which every cell was equal. **#2345 closes only the class-crossing half**, which needs no reference tree: the public values split into a NAME class (`FileName`, `DirectoryName`) and a CONTENT class (`Attributes`, `Size`, `LastWrite`, `LastAccess`, `CreationTime`, `Security`), and no value in one class can justify an event from the other. After: the ten rows read 7/5/5/2/2/2/2/2/2/0 events against 7 across the board before, with the **default** filter byte-identical because it spans both classes (`2102_probe2_after.log`). +8 tests, 6 mutations all caught (3/3/1/1/1/1); no signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change. **What keeps this finding `confirmed` is #2346**: allocating events *within* a class is a policy choice with no repository-contained evidence — `IN_MODIFY` could serve `Size` or `LastWrite` or both, `IN_ATTRIB` could serve any of five, `CreationTime` has **no** inotify event at all, `LastAccess` is unserved because `IN_ACCESS` is in no mask, and `FileName` is not separated from `DirectoryName` although `IN_ISDIR` would allow it. **#2346 LANDED 2026-08-17 and SR-AUD-346 is now fully remediated.** The five allocation questions are the one deferral in this namespace that the reference tree could **not** settle and never will: `NotifyFilters` names the notifications Win32's `ReadDirectoryChangesW` produces, and inotify's event set is not a relabelling of it. They were answered by the user as `docs/StandingApprovals.md` SA-7 — **1(a), 2(a), 3(a), 4(c), 5(b)** — permissive where Linux genuinely cannot discriminate, discriminating where it can. `IN_MODIFY` now serves `Size` and `LastWrite` only, so `Attributes`/`CreationTime`/`Security` alone stop seeing a content write; `IN_ATTRIB` keeps serving all six, which is also how `CreationTime` is approximated since inotify cannot report a btime change at all; `IN_ACCESS` is admitted **only** when `LastAccess` is named, so that filter can finally fire for its own operation without every read waking every content watcher; and `FileName` is separated from `DirectoryName` on `IN_ISDIR` — in **dispatch**, not in the mask, because `IN_ISDIR` travels on the event, reaching `Created`, `Deleted`, both halves of a rename and the unpaired `IN_MOVED_FROM` a cross-directory move leaves behind. Five tests, one per decision, replace the case that asserted the indistinguishability decision 1(a) removes (`SharpRuntimeTests_IO` 689 → 693); three mutations, all caught, with M3's first attempt recorded as **invalid** because `-Werror=unused-function` rejected it. No signature, layout, vtable or `noexcept` change; neither downstream consumer constructs a `FileSystemWatcher`. Priced option-by-option, and answered, in `docs/SystemIONamespaceReviewPlan.md` §21.11. |
| [SR-AUD-347](modules/io/include/System/IO/FileSystemInfo.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-347--medium--fileinfodirectoryinfo-empty-paths-leak-stdfilesystemerror-instead-of-system-argument-validation) | medium | remediated | `FileSystemInfo.hpp`, `FileInfo.hpp`, `DirectoryInfo.hpp` | Empty instance paths leak a raw std::filesystem_error instead of the System argument exception contract. **Remediated (#2101, 2026-08-04), and its real scope was ONE door, not seven.** The review's grep flagged seven candidate escape routes; measured (`build-probe/2101_probe2_before.log`), **all but one were already guarded** -- `DirectoryInfo::GetFiles`/`GetDirectories`/`GetFileNames` over a missing directory and `FileInfo::CopyTo("")`/`DirectoryInfo::MoveTo("")` all already threw `System` exceptions. The only real leak was the `FileSystemInfo` **constructor**, where `std::filesystem::absolute(path)` on an empty path threw `std::filesystem::filesystem_error("cannot make absolute path")` straight out of `FileInfo("")` and `DirectoryInfo("")`, invisible to a caller writing `catch (const System::Exception&)`. The repair rejects the empty case explicitly and routes every OTHER failure through the `std::error_code` overload, so `filesystem_error` cannot escape **by construction** rather than by being caught; the already-guarded neighbours are now pinned by a test. A **whitespace-only** path is deliberately still accepted and pinned -- it resolves cleanly on POSIX and no repository evidence says .NET rejects it, so narrowing would be a guess. Exception identity (`System::ArgumentException`, `paramName` `path`, reusing the wording `FileInfo::CopyTo` already carries) is **this port's choice**. Cause I-C; the same cause as `System::Text`'s T-E/T-M in a different component, and deliberately **not** made a CCF-012 member. +12 tests add-only with #2103, mutation O1 discriminating exactly 4, ASan/UBSan/LSan clean over 600 rejections and 400 acceptances with a live-instrumentation control. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-348](modules/xml/src/System/Xml/XmlReader.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-348--medium--xmlreader-continues-reading-after-close) | medium | remediated | `XmlReader.cpp` | Close only stores state; Read continues parsing and leaves the public lifecycle boundary unobservable. **REMEDIATED (#2078, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemXmlNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.4 and §20.5, cause X-D).** Measured, worse and wider than filed: `Read()` had no guard **and assigned `ReadState::Interactive` on its way out**, so the closed state did not survive one call, and **three** members destroyed it — `Read()`, `ReadStartElement()` and `ReadElementContentAsString()`, the latter two advancing through `Read()` — while `MoveToElement()`, `MoveToNextAttribute()` and `GetAttribute()` all kept answering from a closed reader. **Corrected premise:** the repair needed no new state and no new policy, because this class **already implements one terminal read state correctly** — a reader driven past its last event reports `EndOfFile`, returns `false` from every further `Read()`, and never leaves that state. `Closed` is now the same shape, `ReadState::Closed` itself is the flag (no new field, no layout change), and every accessor reuses the class's own pre-existing "no current node" answer (`None` / `""` / `false`) rather than a value invented for this ticket. `ReadStartElement`/`ReadEndElement` throw the **same** `XmlException` they already threw for any other wrong position — no new exception identity. Note the reader is **not** streaming (`Create` flattens the document into an event vector), so no I/O is consumed; the defect is the lifecycle. +12 permanent regressions, add-only, **not one existing test updated**; three mutations fail exactly 5, 7 and 10 of them and nothing else, and the two control tests (`EndOfFile` unchanged, reading unaffected before `Close()`) never fail. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-349](modules/xml/src/System/Xml/XmlWriter.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-349--medium--xmlwriter-emits-malformed-xml-because-names-and-writer-state-are-not-validated) | medium | remediated | `XmlWriter.cpp` | An invalid name serializes as malformed XML that the paired reader rejects, with no writer-state validation. **REMEDIATED (#2076, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemXmlNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.3 and §20.4, causes X-C + X-D).** Measured, the surface was **wider than the finding's one example**: **four** public doors took an XML name and validated none of them — `WriteStartElement`, `WriteAttributeString`, `WriteProcessingInstruction` (the PI *target*) and `WriteDocType` (the DOCTYPE root-element name) — and an unvalidated PI target containing `?>` closed the instruction early and spilled its remainder into document-level text. **Every** `Write*` member also stayed callable after `Close()`, silently discarding its argument, and an unbalanced `WriteEndElement()` and an attribute written with no element open were discarded too. **Corrected premise confirmed:** the validator already existed and one sibling already used it, so all four name doors now route through `XmlConvert::VerifyName` and the writer door and `XmlDocument::CreateElement` report an identical diagnostic for identical input — no name grammar was invented. Writer state is enforced with `System::InvalidOperationException`; `Close()` stays idempotent and `ToString()`/`Flush()` stay usable after it. +27 permanent regressions, add-only, **not one existing test updated**; nine mutations each fail exactly their own guard's tests. ASan/UBSan/LSan clean over 3,700 rejections with `XmlWriter.cpp`, `XmlConvert.cpp` and `vendor/tinyxml2` compiled **from source** and a control heap-use-after-free proving instrumentation. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. Deliberate narrowings: a leading `:` in a name is now rejected (the module's own validator's pre-existing rule, matching the DOM door), and previously discarded calls now throw. |
| [SR-AUD-350](modules/xml/src/System/Xml/XmlNode.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-350--high--invalid-innerxml-destructively-clears-existing-children-without-an-error) | high | remediated | `XmlNode.cpp`, `XmlNode.hpp` | Invalid InnerXml is accepted after existing children are removed, silently losing document content. **REMEDIATED (#2074, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemXmlNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.1 and §20.1, cause X-A).** `setInnerXmlProperty` called `RemoveAllChildren()` **first** and then discarded `fragmentDoc.Parse()`'s return status, so an element holding `<keep/>` set to `"<bad>"` ended up **empty with no exception** — silent, total content loss on public input. **Corrected premise:** the correct pattern was already one call away in the same module — `XmlDocument::LoadXml` parses the same text through the same substrate and throws a shaped `XmlException` — so the repair is an **ordering change plus an existing error path**, not a new policy, and needed no reference evidence. The fragment is now parsed first, every child is cloned **before** this node is touched (so a failure inside `DeepClone` cannot strip the node either), and only then are the old children removed. Valid fragments, text-only fragments, mixed content and the clearing empty string are all unchanged. +7 permanent regressions; mutation-checked — restoring the original ordering fails **exactly** two of them and nothing else in the 394-test suite. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. Deliberate narrowing: a caller who relied on the clearing side effect must call `RemoveAll()`. |
| [SR-AUD-351](modules/xml/src/System/Xml/XmlNode.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-351--high--dom-mutators-detach-a-node-owned-by-an-unrelated-parent) | high | remediated | `XmlNode.cpp` | RemoveChild accepts a node owned by another parent and detaches it from that unrelated tree. **REMEDIATED (#2075, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemXmlNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.2 and §20.2, cause X-B).** **Corrected premise: FOUR public mutators are affected, not one, and they fail in THREE different ways.** Measured with `root -> {a, b}` and `b -> {childOfB}`: `a->RemoveChild(childOfB)` detached B's child; `a->InsertBefore(n, childOfB)` **silently ignored** `refChild` and put `n` inside `a`; `a->InsertAfter(n, childOfB)` left `n` **nowhere at all**; `a->ReplaceChild(n, childOfB)` did both wrongs at once; and a **parentless** node was accepted silently. Root cause: the port already validated *document identity* (`ThrowIfDifferentDocument`) and *ancestry* (`ThrowIfSelfOrAncestor`) but never **parenthood**, so a same-document node under a different parent passed every check — the two existing guards prove the shape was understood and one case was missed. One shared `ThrowIfNotChildOf` now guards all four; `ReplaceChild` checks **before** delegating to `InsertBefore`, or a foreign `oldChild` would leave `newChild` already spliced in when the removal threw. **The ASan question the review refused to answer by reading is ANSWERED, and the answer is a measured NON-RESULT:** with `XmlNode.cpp`, `XmlDocument.cpp`, `XmlElement.cpp` and `vendor/tinyxml2/tinyxml2.cpp` compiled **from source** (the `Xml` archive would have been uninstrumented), ASan/UBSan/LSan are clean **before** the repair, including over 200 consecutive drops — the dropped node comes from the document's own pool and is freed with it, so it is an **orphan**, not a leak, not a dangling wrapper. The defect is precisely the silent acceptance, and the behavioural test is the closure evidence, not a sanitizer run. Instrumentation proven by a control heap-use-after-free. +8 permanent regressions; mutation-checked — emptying the guard fails **exactly** the five ownership tests and nothing else. The same four calls with a correct child, a null `refChild`, and the pre-existing cross-document and ancestry guards are all unchanged. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. .NET's exact message text is not verifiable here and the wording is recorded as this port's choice. |
| [SR-AUD-352](modules/xml/src/System/Xml/XmlDocument.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-352--medium--xmldocument-node-change-events-are-publicly-exposed-but-never-raised) | medium | remediated | `XmlDocument.cpp`, `XmlDocument.hpp` | Registered DOM node-change handlers are retained but no mutation dispatches them. **REMEDIATED (#2079, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemXmlNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.6 and §20.6, cause X-E).** Measured with the pre-#2079 bodies compiled **from source**, **sixteen** distinct public mutation doors dispatched **zero** handlers. All six are now dispatched by every mutation whose affected node survives the operation: the Insert pair from `PrependChild`/`AppendChild`/`InsertBefore`/`InsertAfter`/`ReplaceChild`/`InnerXml`/`InnerText` and once per child for a `XmlDocumentFragment`; the Remove pair from `RemoveChild`; the Change pair from `XmlCharacterData`'s single `setDataProperty` choke point (which `AppendData`/`InsertData`/`DeleteData`/`ReplaceData`/`setValueProperty`/`setInnerTextProperty` all reach), `XmlAttribute::setValueProperty`, `XmlDeclaration::setValueProperty` and `XmlProcessingInstruction::setDataProperty`. **Corrected premise confirmed:** the handlers are public **data members**, so the dispatcher lives in a `src`-only internal header and adds **no** public surface, type, signature or layout. **One door deliberately stays silent and is pinned:** `RemoveAllChildren` destroys its children, so a `NodeRemoved` handler's `XmlNode*` would name freed storage — the CCF-019 shape, refused rather than introduced, tracked as **#2086**. `*ing` fires before and `*ed` after; a throwing `*ing` handler leaves the tree untouched and a throwing `*ed` handler leaves it fully mutated; no handler installed is still a no-op. +19 permanent regressions, add-only, **not one existing test updated**. Five mutations fail exactly 17, 12, 4, 3 and 1 tests, including one that adds the **unsafe** completion of the `RemoveAllChildren` pair and is caught by the pin. **The dispatcher's snapshot copy is proven load-bearing by ASan, after two non-discriminating attempts were discarded:** a handler that reassigns its own field and then reads a capture is a heap-use-after-free without it, while ASan/UBSan/LSan are clean over 82,500 dispatches with it. The now-false *"not yet wired to call sites"* note on `XmlDocument` was corrected. Migration: `docs/Migration-XmlStrictnessAndLifecycle.md`. |
| [SR-AUD-353](modules/xml/src/System/Xml/XmlNamespaceManager.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-353--medium--hasnamespace-ignores-namespaces-inherited-from-outer-scopes) | medium | remediated | `XmlNamespaceManager.cpp`, `XmlNamespaceManager.hpp`, `XmlNamespaceManagerTests.cpp` | HasNamespace searches only the current scope although its contract covers any active scope. **REMEDIATED (#2077, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemXmlNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.5 and §20.3).** The predicate is now `LookupNamespace(prefix).has_value()`, expressed in terms of the sibling that has always searched outward so the two cannot desynchronise again. The header's own contract already said *"true if @p prefix is declared in any scope"*, so this needed no reference evidence. **Corrected premise:** the finding names user-declared outer prefixes; measured, the built-in `xml` prefix — which the manager installs itself in scope 0 and documents as permanent — was invisible from any inner scope too. **And the defect was PINNED BY A PRE-EXISTING TEST**: `HasNamespace_ChecksCurrentScopeOnly` asserted `EXPECT_FALSE` after `PushScope()`, locking in exactly the behaviour this finding calls wrong, against the type's own contract. That is why it survived. The test was **corrected in place and renamed**, not deleted, with a comment recording the history. +6 permanent regressions including a **property** assertion that `HasNamespace` and `LookupNamespace` agree for every probe prefix over a four-deep push/pop cycle; mutation-checked — restoring the single-scope search fails exactly four tests and nothing else in the 400-test suite. Widening only: nothing that returned `true` returns `false`. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-354](modules/xml/src/System/Xml/XmlConvert.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-354--medium--xmlconvert-timespan-conversion-uses-native-syntax-instead-of-xml-schema-duration) | medium | confirmed | `XmlConvert.cpp` | TimeSpan conversion rejects XML Schema P-duration syntax and serializes a native colon form. |
| [SR-AUD-355](modules/xml/src/System/Xml/XPath/XmlDocumentNavigator.cpp.audit.md#sr-aud-355--medium--xpath-navigator-exposes-adjacent-textcdata-nodes-separately) | medium | remediated | `XmlDocumentNavigator.cpp` | Adjacent text-like nodes are navigated separately instead of as one XPath logical text node. **REMEDIATED (#2081, 2026-08-04, `docs/SystemXmlNamespaceReviewPlan.md` §4.7 and §20.7, cause X-F).** The XPath 1.0 data model has **one** text node per maximal run of adjacent text-like DOM siblings, valued by their concatenation; the navigator returned the native node, so `<r>left<![CDATA[right]]></r>` presented **two** nodes valued `left` and `right`, backward navigation was asymmetric, `IsSamePosition` called two members of one run **different positions**, and `Select("text()")` counted per native node. Repaired with three helpers named after the .NET mechanism the file's **own doc-comment already identified** (`CalibrateText`/`ValueText`): a run's logical position is its **first** member, so `MoveToFirstChild`/`MoveToNext`/`MoveToPrevious` all land there, `getValueProperty`/`getNodeTypeProperty`/`GetNode`/`IsSamePosition` all answer from there, and a navigator constructed **directly on a mid-run DOM node** is calibrated too. Adjacency is DOM sibling adjacency, so a comment, a PI and a node with no XPath representation each split a run. Element and root string-values were already correct and are unchanged. +16 permanent regressions, add-only, **not one existing test updated** (the 74 pre-existing XPath tests stay green). Five mutations: X1 → 10 tests, X3 → 2, X4 → 1, X5 → 1, and **X2 makes navigation non-terminating**, which is itself the proof that stepping off the run *end* and calibrating onto the run *start* are coupled halves. ASan/UBSan/LSan clean over 37,600 characters walked forward, backward, through `Select("text()")` and across DOM mutation between walks, with the navigator and `vendor/tinyxml2` compiled **from source** and a control heap-use-after-free proving instrumentation. The 17-line *"KNOWN GAP (audited, not fixed)"* comment this finding described was **replaced**, not left standing. No signature, layout, vtable or exception-specification change. |
| [SR-AUD-356](modules/collections/include/System/Collections/Generic/IEnumerator.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-356--high--collection-enumerators-dereference-invalid-current-states-instead-of-throwing) | high | remediated | `IEnumerator.hpp`, `List.hpp`, `Queue.hpp`, `Stack.hpp`, `SortedList.hpp`, `LinkedList.hpp`, ObjectModel and Concurrent enumerators | Ticket #1767 added one guarded lifecycle state and permanent before-start/after-end regressions across all ten implementations; the replacement ASan/UBSan probe is clean. Post-remediation follow-up: ticket #1787 (no `SR-AUD-*` identifier) repaired the mutation counter every one of those enumerators snapshots — fourteen UBSan-confirmed signed-overflow increments, fifteen 2^32 snapshot-reuse reproductions, and a previously unrecorded assignment-transplant defect with six ASan `heap-use-after-free`/`heap-buffer-overflow` reproductions. Every counter now uses `System::Collections::detail::BasicMutationCounter`; the lifecycle contract this finding covers is unchanged and every #1767 regression still passes unmodified. See `docs/CollectionVersionCounterSweep.md`. Second post-remediation follow-up: tickets #1792 (design) and #1793 (implementation), also with no `SR-AUD-*` identifier, closed a distinct defect on the same interface -- the non-generic `getCurrentProperty()` returned a mutable `void*` aliasing live collection storage, filled by `const_cast<T*>(&Current())`, reaching thirteen generic and eight non-generic implementations with four further `const_cast`s outside the bridge, four ASan `heap-use-after-free` reports, and a `std::unordered_map` key rewritten in place. It now returns an owning `std::any` by value, under an explicit user approval covering a public source break on both interfaces and a silent ABI break requiring a full consumer rebuild; `Current()` is unchanged at `const T&` and every #1767 regression still passes unmodified. See `docs/IEnumeratorCurrentSafetyDesign.md`. Third post-remediation follow-up: design ticket #1795 (`REMED-COLL-IDICTENUM-KEYVALUE-SAFETY-DESIGN`), again with no `SR-AUD-*` identifier and with no production change, answered the sibling `IDictionaryEnumerator` accessors that #1793 deliberately left alone. It measured that a return-type change alone does not close them: neither `getKeyProperty()` nor `getValueProperty()` performs a fail-fast version check, so on `ListDictionaryInternal` even `getEntryProperty()` and the already-migrated `getCurrentProperty()` are AddressSanitizer `heap-use-after-free` after `Clear()` -- eight such reports in all -- and `Hashtable`'s `getValueProperty()` does have a defined write path into live storage, contradicting #1794's own premise. The selected design pairs an owning `std::any` return with a mandatory `MoveNext`-time snapshot, matching .NET's `HashtableEnumerator`. Implementation ticket #1794 then landed it on 2026-07-28 under an explicit user approval covering the public source break, both `ListDictionaryInternal` parity corrections (`Current` boxes the `DictionaryEntry`, the value view boxes `void*`), and a silent ABI break through two independent mechanisms -- byte-identical mangled names with the calling convention changed, and `NodeEnumerator` growing 40 -> 72 bytes behind an `inline` `GetEnumerator()` -- both reproduced as link-clean-then-corrupt. Both accessors return an owning `std::any` by value and **no accessor on either implementation dereferences a container iterator**. Re-measurement before any source change corrected the design record's own prose: the figure is **nine** ASan `heap-use-after-free` reports, not eight -- its table listed nine. New permanent suite `DictionaryEnumeratorKeyValueSafetyTests.cpp` (+64 tests); clean-tree gate 13,602 tests across 37 executables. `MoveNext()`/`Reset()` after collection destruction remain undefined and are not claimed closed. See `docs/IDictionaryEnumeratorKeyValueSafetyDesign.md`. This finding stays `remediated`. |
| [SR-AUD-357](modules/collections/include/System/Collections/Generic/LinkedList.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-357--high--copied-linkedlistnode-handles-retain-dangling-list-iterators-after-node-or-owner-destruction) | high | remediated | `LinkedList.hpp` | Tickets #1768/#1769 replaced the raw list pointer/iterator pair with independently allocated reference-counted nodes; Remove, Clear, and owner destruction now produce a documented detached state, with 49 permanent regressions and a clean ASan/UBSan probe. Post-remediation follow-ups, neither carrying an `SR-AUD-*` identifier and neither reopening this finding: ticket #1787 repaired the mutation counter this file's enumerator snapshots, and ticket #1788 then widened that counter **and** the enumerator's snapshot from 32 to 64 bits under an explicit approval that `sizeof(LinkedList<T>)` may grow 40 -> 48 on LP64. The pre-fix 2^32 enumerator revalidation was reproduced first (`guard-fired=0` three times, `defects-observed=3`) and reads `defects-observed=0` after; `sizeof(Enumerator)` stayed 40 and no mangled name changed, so the break is binary-only and silent and every consumer must rebuild completely. The node lifetime contract this finding covers is unchanged and all 49 regressions pass unmodified. See `docs/CollectionVersionCounterSweep.md` section 19 and `docs/LinkedListNodeLifetime.md` section 10. This finding stays `remediated`. |
| [SR-AUD-358](modules/collections/include/System/Collections/ICollection.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-358--high--raw-icollection-copyto-contract-cannot-validate-destination-capacity-and-crashes-on-null-storage) | high | remediated | `ICollection.hpp`, `ArrayList.hpp`, `Queue.hpp`, `Stack.hpp`, `Hashtable.hpp`, `ListDictionaryInternal.hpp` | Raw polymorphic CopyTo had no capacity/type representation and wrote null or undersized storage directly; ArrayList nullptr was ASan-confirmed. Ticket #1770 designed and ticket #1771 landed the approved source/ABI break: `CopyTo(void*, intcs)` is removed, replaced by a validating non-virtual `CopyTo(ObjectSpan/std::vector<std::any>&, intcs)` plus one protected `copyToCore` hook, with 128 permanent regressions across every implementation and a clean ASan/UBSan/LSan probe. Follow-up ticket #1774 then corrected an over-strict corner of #1771's own validation rule: a null-pointer destination is now rejected only when paired with a positive length, so a valid empty destination (`ObjectSpan{nullptr, 0}`, a default-constructed empty `std::vector<std::any>`) for an empty collection succeeds instead of throwing. Remains `remediated`; not reopened. |
| [SR-AUD-359](modules/collections/include/System/Collections/ObjectModel/ReadOnlyDictionary.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-359--medium--readonlydictionaryempty-returns-a-mutable-singleton-reference) | medium | remediated | `ReadOnlyDictionary.hpp` | Public assignment could rebind the process-static Empty wrapper to a nonempty backing map. Design ticket #1779 selected a `const`-reference return type, matching .NET's own get-only `Empty` property; implementation ticket #1780 landed it after explicit user approval of the public signature change. Assignment through `Empty()`'s result is now a compile error; no other member, virtual, or ABI-relevant surface changed. |
| [SR-AUD-360](modules/collections/include/System/Collections/Concurrent/ConcurrentDictionary.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-360--medium--concurrentdictionary-addorupdate-loses-a-concurrent-update-instead-of-retrying-against-the-observed-value) | medium | remediated | `ConcurrentDictionary.hpp` | AddOrUpdate snapshotted outside the final lock and unconditionally overwrote an intervening write rather than compare/retry. Ticket #1778 made both overloads retry the whole operation (re-observe, re-invoke the factory) whenever the commit's `operator==` gate against the previously observed value fails, matching .NET's `TryUpdateInternal`'s `EqualityComparer<TValue>.Default.Equals` compare-and-retry; neither factory is invoked with the lock held. |
| [SR-AUD-361](modules/collections/include/System/Collections/Generic/SortedSet.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-361--medium--getviewbetween-returns-a-detached-snapshot-rather-than-the-required-live-bounded-view) | medium | remediated | `SortedSet.hpp` | GetViewBetween was a non-write-through copy, so mutations did not flow between the range view and source set. Design ticket #1782 selected a shared reference-counted `State` plus optional bounds (`docs/SortedSetLiveViewDesign.md`); implementation ticket #1783 landed it after explicit user approval of the `const` removal, the snapshot-to-live-view semantic change, and the object-layout change. `GetViewBetween` now returns a live bounded handle over the same tree: inclusive bounds enforced after construction, out-of-range `Add` throwing `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("item")`, out-of-range `Remove` returning false, range-scoped `Clear`, narrowing-only flattened nested views, one shared version counter invalidating every handle's enumerators, and bounds-enforcing write-through set algebra with a shared-state self-aliasing guard. Four adjacent defects inside the same surface are fixed with it (the `operator>` requirement, unenforced bounds, silent nested widening, and assignment defeating the version guard — the last removing an ASan-confirmed move-assign `heap-use-after-free`). 47 permanent regressions, two consumer fixtures, and a clean ASan/UBSan/LSan campaign. Post-remediation correction: ticket #1784 (`REMED-COLL-SORTEDSET-VIEW-COUNT-RACE`, P1, no `SR-AUD-*` identifier) removed a data race #1783 had introduced and reported against itself — the lazy per-view Count cache lived in two plain `mutable intcs` fields written by the `const` `getCountProperty()`, so concurrent read-only Count on one view object was ThreadSanitizer-confirmed undefined behaviour. The fields are now atomic with a release/acquire publication protocol; object layout, symbols, signatures, and every Count value are unchanged. A second post-remediation ticket, #1786 (`REMED-COLL-VERSION-COUNTER-OVERFLOW`, P3, also with no `SR-AUD-*` identifier), then repaired the shared mutation counter all of this rests on, which **predates** this finding: it was an `int32_t` incremented without bound and compared for equality only, so `++version` at `INTCS_MAX` was UBSan-confirmed signed-integer overflow, a wrapped counter silently revalidated stale iterators and stale cached view Counts, and the `-1` "never computed" cache sentinel was itself a reachable counter value that made a cold cache read as warm. The counter and the iterator snapshot are now 64-bit unsigned and the Count cache's 32-bit tag is stored biased by one and compared widened, so it identifies a counter value exactly; signatures, mangled symbols, `sizeof`, `alignof`, and every member offset are unchanged. This finding stays `remediated` and was reopened by neither ticket. A fourth post-remediation ticket, #1785 (`REMED-COLL-SORTEDSET-NESTED-EXCEPTION-ORDER`, P3, `design`, also with no `SR-AUD-*` identifier), then settled the one divergence #1783 had recorded against itself in `docs/SortedSetLiveViewDesign.md` §30.4: a nested `GetViewBetween` call that is simultaneously widening and inverted selected the inverted-range `ArgumentException` here, where .NET's `TreeSubSet.GetViewBetween` checks widening against the current view bounds first and throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("lowerValue"/"upperValue")` before delegating. Under explicit user approval the port adopted .NET's order; exactly 7 of 32 measured outcome rows changed, all doubly-invalid nested calls, with every success, every widening-only failure, every inverted-only failure, and every top-level call byte-identical. Signatures, mangled symbols, `sizeof`, `alignof`, every member offset, ownership, live write-through, Count caching, and iterator invalidation are all unchanged. This finding stays `remediated` and was reopened by none of the four. |
| [SR-AUD-362](modules/collections/include/System/Collections/Frozen/FrozenDictionary.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-362--medium--frozendictionary-create-silently-overwrites-duplicate-keys) | medium | confirmed | `FrozenDictionary.hpp` | Factory input with duplicate keys is accepted and last-value-wins instead of rejected. **Correction (ticket #1778/#1779):** the current .NET `FrozenDictionary.Create`/`ToFrozenDictionary` documents last-value-wins as intentional; this premise does not hold. Left `confirmed` (no `not-a-defect` status exists) but not an active fix target — see the per-file report's Correction section. |
| [SR-AUD-363](modules/collections/include/System/Collections/Hashtable.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-363--medium--hashtable-accepts-null-keys-and-returns-null-instead-of-the-public-keysvalues-collections) | medium | remediated | `Hashtable.hpp`, `IDictionary.hpp` | Null keys were stringified to the address text `"0"` (which aliased the ordinary string key `"0"`) and IDictionary Keys/Values were null pointers, so a contract-following consumer null-dereferenced while the sibling `ListDictionaryInternal` answered identical caller code correctly. Ticket #1775 made `toKey()` the single validating conversion site every raw-key path passes through, so `getItem`/`setItem`/`Contains`/`Add`/`Remove` (and `Remove(const char*)`, which previously terminated on `std::string`'s null construction) reject a null key with `ArgumentNullException("key")`; and both view properties now return a live, caller-owned `MemberCollection` reusing the ticket #1771/#1774 copy boundary. 70 permanent regressions parameterised over both non-generic IDictionary implementations, clean ASan/UBSan/LSan evidence, and no source or ABI break. |
| [SR-AUD-364](modules/collections/include/System/Collections/BitArray.hpp.audit.md#sr-aud-364--medium--bitarray-enumerator-exposes-current-outside-its-valid-lifecycle-and-ignores-mutation) | medium | remediated | `BitArray.hpp` | Ticket #1767 added guarded Current states and fail-fast version tracking for every mutator, with permanent lifecycle/mutation regressions and clean ASan/UBSan evidence. Post-remediation follow-up: ticket #1787 (`REMED-COLL-VERSION-COUNTER-OVERFLOW-SWEEP`, P3, no `SR-AUD-*` identifier) repaired the counter that tracking rests on. `BitArray` was missing from ticket #1786's inventory of counter-carrying types and is the one whose counter was already `std::uint32_t`, so it never had the signed-overflow UB the other fourteen did — but its implicitly declared copy/move assignment transplanted the source's counter into the destination, so an enumerator over the destination stopped seeing changes. It now holds `detail::NarrowMutationCounter`, whose assignment advances the destination. A **32-bit** counter was retained deliberately at that point: widening it grows `sizeof(BitArray::Enumerator)` from 32 to 40 bytes, arithmetically unavoidable in any member order, which needed an explicit approval — ticket #1789. **That approval was granted and #1789 landed on 2026-07-29** (also with no `SR-AUD-*` identifier): the counter is now `detail::MutationCounter` and the enumerator's snapshot `detail::MutationVersion`, both 64-bit, closing the 2^32 horizon. The pre-fix revalidation was reproduced first (`guard-fired=0` for `MoveNext`, `Reset` and at seven laps, `defects-observed=3`) and reads `defects-observed=0` after. Measured: `sizeof(BitArray::Enumerator)` 32 → 40, `sizeof(BitArray)` unchanged at 48, 0 `BitArray` symbols added, removed or renamed — so the break is binary-only and silent and every consumer must rebuild completely. The lifecycle and Current contracts this finding covers are unchanged and every regression passes unmodified. See `docs/CollectionVersionCounterSweep.md` section 20. This finding stays `remediated` and was not reopened. |

**Post-audit text-subset correction (#1929 rows 5–6, 2026-08-01).** The
historical SR-AUD-007/009 rows above preserve #1879's then-approved `.1234`
rejection. Exact later approval in `docs/TextSubsetCompatibilityDecision.md`
§6.5 item (3) now accepts one through seven digits at 100-nanosecond resolution,
along with surrounding whitespace and DateTime's one/two-digit clock fields.
Eight digits, unpadded dates and short offsets remain rejected. SR-AUD-007,
008, 009 and 061 remain remediated; no audit identifier was added and the table
still contains exactly 364 findings.

Severity is assessed by reachable impact: **critical** (data loss, security,
or systemic integrity), **high** (crash, corruption, major contract break),
**medium** (observable incorrect behavior or substantial coverage gap), and
**low** (maintainability, diagnostics, or narrow robustness issue).
